## 2026-11-22 — G02 preliminary independent CQ zone candidates

The original selector discarded every later QSO once its entity limit was reached, even when the later QSO supplied a new CQ zone. It now retains a separate deterministic preliminary zone candidate list, checks zone values 1–40, and keeps the existing entity `selected` JSON array. A zone-only candidate is not reported as wholly excluded. The local JSON adds `zoneCandidates` and `officialRulesVerified: false`; the Awards preview shows entity and zone candidate counts. No schema migration and no change to existing QSO data. English and Italian strings added; other languages fall back to English pending translation.

Official 2026 rules checked 2026-11-22: https://www.dxmarathon.com/rules/2026/ — one point per distinct country and zone, one QSO can count for both, single operating location; program country and zone lists are authoritative. The selector still does not validate those lists, location, all bands/modes, classes, or official submission format. Counts are candidates, not score or official credit. JSON remains an internal format.

A/B: `tst_cqmarathon selectsZonesIndependentlyOfEntity` before the change failed (0 zone candidates versus expected 2); after the change it passes. Fixture includes two zones in the same entity, duplicate zone, missing and out-of-range zone, and checks unchanged entity selection. A separate in-memory SQLite integration test loads two CQ zones for one entity. `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target tst_cqmarathon tst_cqmarathon_db tst_qmlpopups decolog -j 4` passed; `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir /tmp/decolog-evolution-build -R '^(cqmarathon|cqmarathon_db|vucc|qmlpopups)$' --output-on-failure` passed 4/4. macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2. UI rendering, real submission, other platforms and full rules verification remain open.

Next: make scope/location explicit in the UI and evaluate authoritative entity/zone lists and accepted submission format; separately test import undo with intervening local QSO and restart. Do not mark G02 complete.

## 2026-11-22 — G02 mode classification and station scope

Marathon previously treated AM and USB as digital and rejected CW-R for CW. The selector now uses the shared mode grouping, handles DSB as phone, and excludes an empty mode from single-mode category filters. A/B fixture: before, phone selected 0 instead of AM and USB; after, phone selects both, digital selects FT8 only, and CW selects CW-R. The existing general mode grouping treats unrecognised non-empty modes as data, so official eligibility is still not established.

The Awards preview now requires selection of a specific station profile in the existing filter, passes it to both selection and export, and warns that a profile does not prove a single operating location. Export retains the preview's edition/category/entity limit/profile and is disabled if those controls change. `QSaveFile` protects an existing JSON export from partial replacement. A synthetic in-memory SQLite fixture compares all-profile selection (two QSO) with one profile (one QSO and one zone), and confirms the exported scope ID. This verifies the underlying selector and persistence, not the QML/controller interaction or file write failure. The selected scope still needs operator confirmation and does not verify locations, rule lists, band restrictions, or organiser acceptance; JSON remains internal.

Build and local checks: `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target tst_cqmarathon_db decolog -j 4` passed; `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir /tmp/decolog-evolution-build -R '^(cqmarathon|cqmarathon_db|vucc|qmlpopups)$' --output-on-failure` passed 4/4 on macOS arm64, Qt 6.11.2. The first database test run failed because synthetic station profiles lacked mandatory callsigns; with realistic callsigns the fixture passed. English and Italian UI text is provided; the other locales fall back to English pending translation. No visual or end-to-end UI test, other operating system, external submission, hardware, or performance claim.

G05 inspection revealed undo is exposed as a controller method but no corresponding QML call was found; its ID list is memory-only and is cleared on partial success. Do not assume restart-safe undo. Next: design a per-log durable import journal with identity and explicit partial-failure recovery, then exercise import/undo after intervening QSO and restart in isolated databases; do not delete data using IDs from another log. Also complete G02 scope/UI A/B runtime verification and authoritative rule-list/format work.

## 2026-11-22 — G01/G05 import transaction integrity

Before: failed SQLite commit was ignored and the report included uncommitted IDs. A synthetic deferred-foreign-key fault reproduced `inserted=1` despite failed commit. Separately, a QSL-write failure returned an error but left an unreported QSO in the batch; A/B fault injection reproduced `qsoCount=1` instead of 0.

After: imports require their own batch transaction and stop explicitly if it cannot start; failed commits roll back the current batch, remove its IDs and inserted/duplicate counts from the report, preserve previously committed batches, and report an error. A per-record savepoint rolls back partial QSO/QSL writes; failure of savepoint handling rolls back the batch. The controller marks reports containing errors as warnings. Per-record savepoints add overhead; large-log A/B latency measurements are still required. Read/missing-UTC/QSL counts refer to attempted records, including a batch whose commit failed; they are not committed-row counts.

Synthetic tests exercise commit rejection, retry and reopen of a file-backed log; a later batch failure with an independently inserted QSO between batches; refusal of an already active transaction; and QSL write failure. A/B failures above pass after the fix. `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target tst_database tst_migration decolog -j 4` succeeded; `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir /tmp/decolog-evolution-build -R '^(database|migration|qmlpopups)$' --output-on-failure` passed 3/3 on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2. Fault injection is local, not a live database crash or hardware test. No schema migration. The work does not implement persistent import undo: the in-memory ID list, partial undo recovery and cross-log identity remain open. Next: benchmark batch overhead against the same pre-change revision, then implement a transactional per-log journal and UI with restart, partial-failure and concurrent-QSO tests.

## 2026-11-22 — G05 durable import journal foundation

Schema 12 adds `import_batch` and `import_qso`. For imports with source `import`, each newly inserted QSO is journalled with the batch UUID, local ID, persistent QSO UUID and insertion revision inside the same per-item savepoint and batch transaction. Preview imports and independent radio QSO are not journalled. A journal-write failure rolls back the corresponding QSO; a rejected batch commit leaves no journal entries from that batch, while earlier committed batches remain recorded. Empty or duplicate-only imports create no new journal batch. This is a foundation: no user-facing persistent undo, recovery of old imports, or proof of safety for edited/QSL-modified records is provided yet.

A/B: before schema change the file-backed restart fixture failed with `no such table: import_qso`; after, it finds the correct imported QSO UUID/revision and excludes a later independent QSO. Additional synthetic tests inject failure into the journal, reject a later batch commit, and reopen a simulated schema-11 database containing a QSO: version advances to 12 and ID/UUID/data remain. Existing older-schema migration tests also pass. `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target tst_database tst_migration decolog -j 4` and `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir /tmp/decolog-evolution-build -R '^(database|migration|qmlpopups)$' --output-on-failure` passed on macOS arm64 / Apple M4 / Qt 6.11.2. Simulated v11 was created by removing the new tables and version marker from a fresh database; it is not a captured customer log. The v12 migration is transactional; downgrade is not supported as a tested procedure, so use a verified pre-upgrade backup to return to an earlier application.

Performance A/B: alternating six subprocess runs each of `tst_database aSelectionExportsLikeTheWholeLog` on the same machine, Release build, offscreen, in-memory SQLite, 1,203 synthetic QSO plus full and selection ADIF exports. Before transaction fix `e6e9ba3`: 307.4, 102.1, 102.1, 102.6, 101.7, 100.7 ms (median 102.1); transaction fix `f54300c`: 190.3, 108.7, 109.0, 108.6, 108.5, 107.1 ms (median 108.6). A separate alternating run comparing `f54300c` with schema-12 journal: 109.4, 107.2, 108.5, 108.8, 107.2, 107.5 ms (median 108.0) versus 122.2, 122.2, 121.8, 121.9, 124.5, 120.8 ms (median 122.0). These include process startup, import and export, not UI latency, isolated import timing or large-log throughput. Outliers and the difference between runs limit interpretation; measure p50/p95 and concurrent logging on representative larger, file-backed datasets before acceptance.

Next: implement durable undo against the journal using UUID plus ID and revision checks, preserve independently added/edited QSO and handle already deleted/missing records and partial errors; expose a review/confirmation path in the UI and prove restart and fault recovery. Record migration and rollback instructions before release.

## 2026-11-22 — G05 guarded recovery backend

Schema 13 adds an import-time fingerprint of the complete QSO row, per-service QSL state, attachment metadata and enrichments. It is calculated and recorded within the QSO import savepoint. The durable `undoLatestImport` operation opens a transaction per journalled QSO, checks its local ID, persistent UUID, revision and fingerprint, and soft-deletes only unchanged records. Already-deleted, missing/replaced, changed and failed records are counted separately; a later retry does not repeat completed deletions. Schema-12 journal entries have no baseline and are conservatively skipped for review. A profile change or service update that modifies the stored row can produce a conservative skip. Fingerprint comparison is not a substitute for review of all possible future linked tables.

The existing controller's `undoLastImport` now uses the current log's durable journal instead of an in-memory ID list; a completed background import no longer applies its report to a different open log. There is still no user-facing undo button or review UI. This controller method is synchronous and is not suitable for a large batch from the UI until moved to a cancellable background job with progress. The journal continues to retain historical entries; the implementation does not automatically proceed to an older batch after retrying the latest one.

A/B: before fingerprint population the restart journal fixture failed because the stored hash was empty; after, it passes. On a file-backed synthetic log after restart, undo soft-deletes one unchanged QSO while protecting a later edited QSO, a QSO with QSL state changed without a revision increment, and an independently added QSO; the second run reports the previously deleted item. Injected SQL failure leaves the failed QSO and history unchanged and a retry succeeds; another test proves an identical local ID in a different log is untouched. A simulated v12 upgrade preserves a legacy entry and skips it without a hash. These exercise the core and do not prove UI interaction or real process crash recovery. Build `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target tst_database tst_migration decolog -j 4` and `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir /tmp/decolog-evolution-build -R '^(database|migration|qmlpopups)$' --output-on-failure`: passed 3/3 on Apple M4/macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. Schema migration 12→13 is additive/transactional; restore a verified pre-upgrade backup for a rollback.

Performance requires attention: alternating six runs of the same 1,203-record synthetic in-memory import+export subprocess test, median f54300c 107.8 ms, schema-13 journal+fingerprint 214.7 ms; an initial snapshot-based fingerprint measured 248.9 ms. This includes startup/export and is not a UI or large-log latency measure. The near doubling of this local workload is an unresolved regression, not an acceptance result. Next: optimise and benchmark fingerprint generation on representative file-backed datasets with concurrent logging; implement asynchronous cancellable undo, review UI, and real UI A/B/restart testing. English and Italian messages are present; other locales fall back to English.

## 2026-11-22 — G05 asynchronous import undo UI

The File menu offers “Undo last import…” with explicit confirmation. The operation uses a worker-owned SQLite connection, progress in the logbook header and cancellation between individual QSO; retries preserve earlier committed deletions and continue with remaining items. The controller reports changed/unavailable/failed items and avoids applying a completion to a different open log. An import and its undo cannot run simultaneously. The dialog warns that changed and legacy records remain for review, but it does not yet list individual exclusions or provide a full per-QSO review workflow. UI activity reports require examination for partial results. No schema change in this milestone.

A/B runtime fixture `tst_journeycolumn importUndoDialogAcceptAndRejectAcrossRestart` launches the actual app twice offscreen against separate synthetic file-backed logs: rejecting the confirmation leaves the imported and independently added QSO active; accepting it moves only the imported QSO to recycle history, confirmed by reopening the database. The probe invokes the dialog's accept/reject methods programmatically; it does not demonstrate mouse/keyboard input, layout quality, the cancellation button, or real hardware. Core test `cancelUndoKeepsUnprocessedItemsForRetry` stops after one item and verifies a later retry completes the rest. `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target tst_journeycolumn tst_database decolog -j 4` passed; `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir /tmp/decolog-evolution-build -R '^(database|migration|qmlpopups|journeycolumn)$' --output-on-failure` passed 4/4 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. English and Italian strings added; other languages fall back to English pending translation.

Remaining: complete per-record preview/review and clear completion feedback, test actual button/keyboard/cancellation with long imports and process interruption, verify other platforms, optimise fingerprint performance with compatibility for existing v13 baselines, and perform representative file-backed p50/p95 under concurrent logging. The G05 goal and wider roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-22 — G05 fingerprint query reuse

The import and guarded undo now prepare the four fingerprint queries once per operation and reuse them. The two journal write statements are also prepared once. The schema-13 JSON structure and SHA-256 calculation remain unchanged; failed reads/writes still fail closed. No schema migration.

A/B compatibility: before changing the reader, `DECODXLOG_FINGERPRINT_MODE=create DECODXLOG_FINGERPRINT_FIXTURE=/tmp/decolog-fingerprint-compat-20261122.sqlite QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen tst_database fingerprintCompatibilityFixture` created a file-backed schema-13 journal using the previous code, with synthetic QSL and extra fields. The same fixture with `DECODXLOG_FINGERPRINT_MODE=verify` after rebuilding the prepared-query reader recovered both unchanged QSO, with no changed or failed items. The final writer optimisation did not change the fingerprint representation. The offscreen app's accept/reject undo path also passed again after the final change. This does not cover every possible field/value or production database.

A/B timing: eight alternating runs of the existing 1,203 synthetic QSO in-memory import plus exports subprocess fixture. `c5c4084`: 216.9, 219.5, 225.2, 219.4, 219.6, 220.7, 219.4, 221.1 ms, median 219.5 ms; prepared statements: 186.4, 185.0, 184.3, 187.4, 185.2, 185.8, 184.5, 187.5 ms, median 185.5 ms; pre-journal f54300c: 112.0, 108.6, 109.2, 108.5, 108.9, 108.9, 109.3, 110.5 ms, median 109.0 ms. This narrows but does not close the regression. Process startup and both exports are included; results are not UI or file-backed p95 evidence.

On Apple M4/macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, Release build of database/migration/app succeeded, `ctest -R '^(database|migration|qmlpopups)$'` passed 3/3 after final change and the actual offscreen app `tst_journeycolumn importUndoDialogAcceptAndRejectAcrossRestart` passed. Earlier in this milestone all four `database|migration|qmlpopups|journeycolumn` targets passed 4/4 after the reader change. Per-QSO review UI, file-backed p50/p95 under concurrent logging, remaining performance overhead and Windows/Linux tests remain open. A future schema change affecting `SELECT * FROM qso` must preserve the old fingerprint format or version it; otherwise unchanged legacy items can be conservatively skipped.

## 2026-11-22 — G05 per-QSO undo result

The undo worker now returns bounded per-record issues with QSO ID, callsign when the identity is trustworthy, and a reason: missing legacy baseline, later revision, changed QSO/QSL/attachment, unavailable/replaced identity, or deletion failure. The result window presents these rows and the exact moved/already-deleted/changed/unavailable/failed totals; cancellation is explicit. At most 200 issue rows and 20 underlying error strings are returned to bound memory/UI work; the window explicitly reports the number of omitted issues. A missing or replaced identity does not display the current occupant's callsign. This is an outcome review, not a pre-undo candidate list or an exhaustive paginated history.

A/B runtime: the offscreen app's confirmation rejection leaves all records unchanged and shows no result; acceptance on an isolated file-backed log moves an unchanged imported QSO to recycle, preserves an edited imported QSO and an independent QSO, and reports the edited QSO's ID. The probe observes the result window visible with a rendered row containing the affected callsign. It invokes confirmation programmatically and is not evidence of physical keyboard/mouse navigation. Core tests distinguish edited, QSL-changed, missing-baseline and SQL-failure reasons; a 205-item synthetic fixture confirms 200 rows, five omitted, and exact counts with no deletion. Build `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target tst_database tst_migration tst_journeycolumn tst_qmlpopups decolog -j 4`, focused offscreen app test, and `ctest -R '^(database|migration|qmlpopups)$'` all passed on Apple M4/macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. No schema migration. English and Italian strings added; other translations fall back to English.

Next: implement a cancellable pre-operation preview with stable batch identity and paginated/exportable issue details, recheck every item transactionally after confirmation, and test physical UI navigation, cancellation and crash/restart. Performance overhead, Windows/Linux and real source exports remain open; no G05 completion claimed.

## 2026-11-22 — G05 pre-undo preview and batch identity

The File menu now starts a cancellable, read-only background preview before opening the confirmation dialog. It shows eligible, previously deleted, changed, unavailable and failed counts and the first 200 issue rows, with an explicit omitted count. Preview uses the same fingerprint and identity checks as undo; it does not modify QSO. It is an advisory per-record scan, not a single coherent snapshot: the interface says data can change and each item is checked again during the actual transaction. Preview can be cancelled between records; unprocessed rows are not counted.

The UI passes both the reviewed batch ID and its persistent UUID. The controller also binds the review to the open log path, and the worker rejects a different latest batch ID or UUID. On confirmation it independently rechecks each QSO, so changes after preview are not silently deleted. Legacy no-argument controller calls remain available and select the latest recorded batch; the UI uses the guarded path. No schema migration.

A/B offscreen app fixture: before this change the confirmation opened without candidate counts or a preview; after, both acceptance and rejection receive a preview showing one eligible and one changed QSO. Rejecting leaves the database intact; accepting moves the eligible QSO to recycle, preserves the changed and independently inserted QSO, and presents the changed item in the result. The probe invokes dialog methods programmatically, not mouse/keyboard. Core synthetic tests verify cancellation leaves QSO untouched, modification between preview and confirmation is protected, a newly inserted import causes rejection, and a different batch UUID is rejected even where numeric batch IDs coincide in different logs. `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target tst_database tst_migration tst_journeycolumn tst_qmlpopups decolog -j 4`, the focused offscreen `tst_journeycolumn importUndoDialogAcceptAndRejectAcrossRestart`, and `ctest -R '^(database|migration|qmlpopups)$'` passed on Apple M4/macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. One initial UI assertion falsely matched the new preview probe prefix; it was corrected to match the result probe exactly and then passed.

Open: issue rows are capped at 200 and lack pagination/export; no consistent snapshot, crash/restart during undo, actual keyboard/mouse or Windows/Linux UI verification. The scan initially loads all journal entries into memory before the first progress/cancellation callback, which needs bounding for large logs. Fingerprint import performance remains above pre-journal baseline; real producer exports and the remaining desktop roadmap remain open. English/Italian messages added; other locales fall back to English.

## 2026-11-22 — G05 bounded journal scan

Preview and undo no longer materialise the entire journal before reporting progress. The core calls the cancellation callback before counting, then reads at most 128 journal entries per keyset page in `(qso_id, qso_uuid)` order. It records the initial count and maximum ID as an upper bound, checks cancellation before each QSO, and reports an error if the expected remaining page is absent. The preview remains advisory rather than a coherent cross-page snapshot. Counting and an individual SQL operation cannot currently be interrupted mid-query; the initial callback uses `total=-1`, and UI progress remains at zero until a total is known. Schema 14 adds index `idx_import_qso_page` to avoid a full journal sort on each page; it does not alter QSO columns or schema-13 fingerprint bytes. The migration is transactional; a verified pre-upgrade backup is needed for rollback to older software.

A/B: before the change the immediate-cancel fixture first received total=1 after loading its journal; after, it receives `total=-1` and cancels before the scan. A 260-QSO synthetic fixture previews without mutation, stops after 129 deletions across the 128-record boundary, then retries: 129 already deleted and 131 newly deleted. A simulated schema-13 file upgrades to 14, retains a journalled QSO, uses the new index according to `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN`, and successfully undoes that QSO. An offscreen app accept/reject test, using the previously built test executable to create its schema-13 fixture and the rebuilt schema-14 app to open it, also passed. Build `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target tst_database tst_migration decolog -j 4`; focused tests and `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir /tmp/decolog-evolution-build -R '^(database|migration|qmlpopups)$' --output-on-failure` passed 3/3 on Apple M4/macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. The first A/B failure was observed before the change; after it passes.

The 128-item bound is established by the query and the page-boundary test, not by a measured peak-memory benchmark. The added index may affect import write cost; repeat file-backed A/B p50/p95 and concurrent logging measurements. Journal membership may change between pages if another process writes the same batch, and a coherent snapshot or membership token remains to be designed. The UI still shows only the first 200 issue rows, and manual keyboard/mouse, real crash/restart, Windows/Linux and real-source interoperability remain open. Next: exhaustive paginated review plus consistent/stable membership, then continue independent desktop goals.

## 2026-11-22 — G05 journal membership guard

Schema 15 adds a transactional `journal_revision` to each import batch, updated by SQLite insert/update/delete triggers on its journal rows. New databases start from the v14 resource schema and apply the same v15 migration as existing logs. The preview carries batch ID, UUID and revision into the UI confirmation; the worker checks the reviewed revision before starting and within each QSO transaction. A changed journal stops the scan with an error; if an undo is interrupted by a later change, already committed soft deletions remain and the rest need a new preview. Trigger updates roll back with the transaction. This makes journal membership changes detectable without retaining the full journal in memory. It does not make QSO state across the entire advisory preview a single snapshot; each record is still rechecked before deletion.

A/B: before the change, a synthetic write to the same journal between preview records went undetected; the new test failed because no error was reported. After, it reports a changed journal and performs no deletion. Additional tests cover a same-batch update after preview, an update during undo after one committed deletion, rollback of the revision trigger, and a change from a second SQLite connection to the same temporary file. The offscreen UI accept/reject path passes with the revision token. A genuine v14 test database with two synthetic QSO, QSL and extra fields was created by the separately built cffbc61 executable, opened and migrated by the new executable, and both unchanged records were recoverable; this is not a customer log or a real crash test. Build `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target tst_database tst_migration decolog -j 4`, targeted fault/connection tests, the offscreen `tst_journeycolumn importUndoDialogAcceptAndRejectAcrossRestart`, and `ctest -R '^(database|migration|qmlpopups)$'` passed on Apple M4/macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. Restore a verified pre-upgrade backup for downgrade; it is not tested.

Performance A/B: eight alternating Release subprocess runs of the existing 1,203-record in-memory import+export fixture: schema14 cffbc61 396.5, 190.9, 189.7, 189.7, 187.8, 189.0, 188.7, 189.4 ms (median 189.6); schema15 196.0, 196.7, 197.8, 196.3, 196.2, 197.8, 197.4, 195.8 ms (median 196.5). Includes process startup and export; this is not file-backed or UI p95. Existing fingerprint/index overhead and the additional trigger cost remain to be measured on larger representative logs under concurrent logging.

Open: exhaustive issue pagination/export, cancellability inside slow SQLite queries, actual keyboard/mouse and crash recovery, real source exports and Windows/Linux. Continue an independent G08 Net Manager audit and implementation alongside these G05 checks; initial text search alone is not proof that a radio-net workflow is absent. All other desktop streams and G13 remain open.

## 2026-11-22 — G08 initial local radio-net roster

Inspected the existing activation/contest session and ContestNet paths: they serve activation/contest logging and computer networking, so the new radio round-table roster is separate. Schema 16 adds local session, participant and event tables transactionally. The desktop menu opens a radio-net window: start/select/close/reopen sessions, check in and return a callsign without duplication, maintain name/notes and present/waiting/absent/departed state, reorder participants, advance the current turn, and view the latest 200 history events. Events preserve check-in name/note snapshots. The station profile is associated with the session. Participation does not create a QSO or transmit. See `docs/RADIO-NET.md` for use and explicit limitations.

A/B baseline: before implementation `radioNetSessionStorageDoesNotCreateQsos` failed with `no such table: radio_net_session`; after migration it passed and QSO count remained zero. Core synthetic tests cover case-insensitive repeat check-in/re-entry, similar calls, name/note history, statuses, order, skipping absent/departed participants, no eligible turn, close/reopen, restart and an injected event-write failure that rolls back the participant. A second test uses two SQLite connections to the same local file to check the same callsign remains one participant and that a closed session rejects later check-in; it is not a LAN or live multi-computer test. The real app was launched twice offscreen against an isolated file-backed log: a programmatic UI probe started a session and used the check-in control, rendered K1AAA, reopened with the same roster and verified zero QSO. This does not establish mouse/keyboard accessibility, RF or hardware behavior.

A real prior-code fixture was produced by a separate build of commit 452f73b (schema 15) with synthetic QSO, QSL and extra fields. The new schema-16 executable opened/migrated it and successfully recovered both existing imported QSO using their journal. A separate schema-15 simulation and older schema tests pass. `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target tst_radionet tst_database tst_migration tst_journeycolumn tst_qmlpopups decolog -j 4`; the two focused app probes (radio-net restart and existing import undo) passed, as did `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir /tmp/decolog-evolution-build -R '^(radionet|database|migration|qmlpopups)$' --output-on-failure` 4/4 on Apple M4/macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. The first new core run exposed null strings in NOT NULL event fields; that was fixed, then all tests passed. No real user log or RF used. Restore a verified pre-upgrade backup for downgrade; downgrade not tested.

G08 is partial: add a reviewed explicit QSO-entry linkage, full session export and summary, optional timers/reminders, history beyond the displayed 200, and tests of manual keyboard/focus, long sessions, clean restore, and Windows/Linux. The new tables are local and cloud/web sync is not implemented or claimed. English/Italian strings are supplied; other locales fall back to English. All other goal streams and G13 remain in scope.


## 2026-11-22 — G08 session export and explicit QSO draft

The selected radio-net session can be exported locally as versioned JSON with its participants and complete history. A worker-owned connection streams a SQLite read transaction into `QSaveFile`; cancellation or write failure preserves the previous destination, and the open log and SQLite sidecar paths are refused. The UI reports asynchronous results. A participant's **New QSO…** action opens the existing QSO entry dialog with callsign, name and station profile; it does not save a contact, and a second request raises the existing radio-net draft instead of replacing it. No schema migration was added. English and Italian strings are supplied; remaining locales fall back to English.

A/B: before implementation the export API check failed because `exportSession(QUrl)` was absent; after, it passes. A synthetic file-backed session exports 207 events while the on-screen history is limited to 200, preserving Unicode and quotes; tests reject the database and WAL destinations, report an unwritable destination, and cancel a 10,000-event export without replacing an existing file. The offscreen application probe creates two participants, opens K1AAA as a draft, attempts K1BBB and confirms that K1AAA remains in the open draft and the log contains zero QSO. Existing radio-net restart and import-undo accept/reject probes also pass. Build: `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target tst_radionet tst_database tst_migration tst_journeycolumn tst_qmlpopups decolog -j 4`. Focused journey tests passed 3/3, and `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir /tmp/decolog-evolution-build -R '^(radionet|database|migration|qmlpopups)$' --output-on-failure` passed 4/4 on Apple M4/macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2.

The UI probes invoke controls programmatically; physical keyboard/mouse, final QSO submission and saved-QSO/session association remain unverified or unimplemented. Cancellation during a slow individual SQLite operation, concurrent-write snapshot behavior, export interoperability, complete history browsing, timers, cloud sync, RF/hardware, LAN and Windows/Linux remain open. G08 and the other desktop streams remain in scope.

## 2026-11-22 — G08 paginated participation history

The history window now navigates the complete stored event history with Older and Newer controls. Returning to newest refreshes it, so events appended while browsing become visible without moving the older page boundaries. Changing the selected session resets navigation. ## 2026-11-22 — G08 persisted visual reminder

The selected active session can set a reminder from 1 to 86400 seconds. The window displays a countdown and then a visual due message; clearing or replacing the reminder is explicit. Deadline and cancellation are recorded as session events, so reopening the file or application retains a pending or overdue state without a schema migration. Each session has its own latest reminder. A closed session cannot schedule a new reminder but can clear one. UTC deadlines use the system wall clock, so clock adjustments affect them. The existing session export includes the reminder events. No QSO or radio command is created.

A/B: before implementation the reminder test failed because `setReminder(int)` was absent. After, a one-second file-backed deadline survives database close/reopen, becomes due, clears and remains cleared after another reopen. A trigger-induced event-write failure preserves the previous deadline. Independent-session tests cover replacement, switching, closed-session rejection and clearing. An isolated offscreen app probe sets the reminder through its control and observes the visible due state; existing history navigation and explicit QSO-save probes also pass. Build `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target decolog tst_journeycolumn tst_radionet tst_database tst_migration tst_qmlpopups -j 4`; core and focused app tests ran on Apple M4/macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2.

This is a visual reminder in the selected manager window, not a sound, background alert or operating-system notification. Physical input, suspend/wake, multiple processes, Windows/Linux and RF/hardware remain unverified. Saved-QSO/session association and the rest of the desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-22 — G09 automatic split band consistency

When the manual QSO form contains the connected rig's RX frequency, RX is blank, and CAT reports an active split, the existing controller substitutes the rig's TX frequency. Previously it retained the form's RX band as the QSO's TX band even when the rig reported a different TX band. It now derives the band from the substituted TX frequency when that frequency maps to a known band; an unknown TX frequency retains the operator's supplied band. Explicit RX entry and ordinary non-split behavior continue through their existing paths.

A/B used the same built app and a loopback-only rigctld protocol fixture: RX 435.250 MHz/70cm, TX 145.900 MHz/2m, active split. Before the fix the recorded row was `(145.9, 70cm, 435.25, 70cm)`; afterwards it is `(145.9, 2m, 435.25, 70cm)`. The fixture checks the commands received and observed no CAT frequency, split, mode or PTT write; it exercises polling and a programmatic form submission, not physical radio or RF. The test is registered as `rig_split_manual` when Python is available. Explicit satellite RX entry also passed in the offscreen app. `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target decolog tst_journeycolumn tst_maplayers -j 4`; `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir /tmp/decolog-evolution-build -R '^(rig_split_manual|newqsotuning|database|migration|radionet|qmlpopups)$' --output-on-failure` passed 6/6 on Apple M4/macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2.

This is not a tracker, Doppler, real CAT hardware, RF or cross-platform verification. G09 and the other incomplete desktop streams remain open.


## 2026-11-22 — G09 read-only PREDICT snapshot core

Added an asynchronous, read-only PREDICT UDP client core, not yet connected to the desktop UI. It requests tracking mode, selected satellite position/next event, normalized 100 MHz Doppler and TLE, then checks tracking mode again before publishing a snapshot. It reports the TLE epoch and local receipt time separately, validates the response shape and position, rejects command-injection characters, accepts numeric IPv4/IPv6 addresses, bounds response size, uses separate request sockets, and clears the result on timeout or protocol failure. The client does not send rig, rotor or PTT commands. Source contract: [PREDICT socket command specification](https://github.com/kd2bd/predict/blob/master/clients/samples/README) and [server implementation](https://github.com/kd2bd/predict/blob/master/predict.c).

A/B: before the increment `tst_predicttracker` was an unknown build target because no client existed; after, the local UDP fixture verifies the five read-only commands, position, Doppler, TLE epoch, a stopped server, mode change during a sample, invalid selected name, busy request and timeout. The normal CTest run skips the optional real-server case; `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir /tmp/decolog-evolution-build -R '^(predicttracker|radionet|database|migration)$' --output-on-failure` passed 4/4 groups on Apple M4/macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. Separately compiled upstream PREDICT 2.2.5 at commit `517fccf421909b4974151b6ca30810586ca658a3` in `/tmp`, ran it in a temporary home on UDP port 34219, and `DECOLOG_TEST_PREDICT_PORT=34219 tst_predicttracker actualPredictServerWhenConfigured` passed using its bundled OSCAR-7 2018 TLE. Server stopped after the test. The bundled TLE is deliberately obsolete and this does not establish usable current predictions.

PREDICT documents that it may return its last calculated position when tracking stops; two mode checks mitigate but do not eliminate races or prove sample age. No UI integration, live mode monitoring, selection/listing, pass planning, stale-age policy, Doppler scaling/QSO handoff, actual rig/rotor/RF, Windows/Linux or other tracker interoperability is claimed. Continue G09 and all remaining desktop streams.


## 2026-11-22 — G09 desktop PREDICT snapshot

The main menu now opens a desktop window for a manually requested read-only PREDICT UDP snapshot. The operator supplies a numeric IP, port and exact satellite name. Position, next event, normalized 100 MHz Doppler, local receipt time and TLE epoch are shown. A TLE older than 14 days or dated in the future receives a warning; the threshold is a caution rather than proof of validity. The window states that a response cannot guarantee current tracking; it hides the previous sample on a new request and displays errors. ## 2026-11-22 — G09 PREDICT satellite selection

The PREDICT window can now load tracked satellite names from the configured server and select one for a subsequent manual snapshot; exact manual entry remains available. The core asks `GET_MODE`, `GET_LIST`, then `GET_MODE` again. Multi mode exposes the returned names; single mode exposes only the tracked satellite. Stopped mode, an empty or malformed list, a mode change or timeout clear the choices. Editing host, port or satellite hides any previously displayed snapshot; changing the endpoint also invalidates the old list. Names are bounded printable ASCII without control characters, supporting names with parentheses. The operation sends no CAT, rotor or PTT command and creates no QSO. ## 2026-11-22 — G09 optional repeated PREDICT refresh

The PREDICT window offers opt-in repeated reads, scheduling the next request 2.5 seconds after each successful response. Endpoint and satellite inputs are locked during the cycle. ## 2026-11-22 — G09 PREDICT next and following pass

The PREDICT window now requests a pass from the current time and can request the following pass after the displayed LOS. It displays UTC AOS/LOS, maximum sampled elevation and the TLE epoch with the existing old/future-data caution. The asynchronous planner obtains the TLE before and after the prediction, rejects changed elements, bounds the stream and time, reconstructs lines split over datagrams, and rejects invalid, unordered or obviously incomplete points and horizon endpoints. Cancellation stops the local request; the PREDICT server may continue its computation. The server uses the station coordinates configured in PREDICT. No CAT, PTT, rotor command or QSO is generated. English/Italian text is supplied; other locales fall back to English.

A/B: the new planner test initially had no build target. The first run against real PREDICT then failed with `Invalid pass prediction`, revealing that upstream splits long text lines at 79 bytes into separate UDP datagrams. After stream reassembly, synthetic fixtures pass fragmented lines, invalid ordering, incomplete endpoints, changed TLE, unsafe names and cancellation. The offscreen app fixture displays two consecutive passes and checks only `GET_TLE` and `PREDICT` commands, with zero QSO. Separately, the offscreen app and core connected to local upstream PREDICT 2.2.5 at commit `517fccf421909b4974151b6ca30810586ca658a3`: both next and following passes displayed, and the app marked its bundled 2018 TLE old. The obsolete fixture does not validate current orbital predictions. `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target decolog tst_predictpass tst_predicttracker -j 4`; `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir /tmp/decolog-evolution-build -R '^(predict_ui|predicttracker|predictpass|database|migration)$' --output-on-failure` passed 5/5 before the final wording change, and the UI group passed again after it on Apple M4/macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2.

PREDICT 2.2.x uses a shell for the prediction helper; selected names are restricted to safe ASCII characters, and the server and its configuration must be trusted. The stream checks cannot prove that UDP packets were not lost; a 180-second maximum point gap can conservatively reject slow valid passes, and maxima are sampled rather than continuous. Physical input and separate-window rendering, up-to-date orbit validation, multi-day planning, Doppler/QSO handoff, rotor/CAT hardware, RF and Windows/Linux remain open.


## 2026-11-22 — G09 Doppler preview and explicit satellite QSO draft

The PREDICT window accepts operator-entered nominal uplink TX and downlink RX MHz and previews Doppler-corrected values using PREDICT's documented normalized 100 MHz convention: scale the downlink shift by downlink MHz/100 and apply the opposite sign to uplink. An explicit action opens the existing manual QSO dialog with satellite, SAT propagation and the corrected TX/RX values. It does not save a QSO or send tuning/PTT/rotor commands. An already open satellite draft is preserved. The draft warns its frequencies are a fixed snapshot requiring review; the dialog can scroll to expose its controls.

The calculation refuses invalid/nonpositive frequencies, an absent or busy tracker, a reading over 10 seconds old, and TLE epochs in the future or over 14 days old. These are local cautionary limits, not evidence that a tracker is advancing or an orbit or frequency is valid. The operator remains responsible for transponder frequency mapping, satellite mode, station configuration and reviewing the draft. English/Italian text is supplied; other locales fall back to English.

A/B: before the change the new test did not compile because `correctedFrequencies` did not exist. After, a local UDP fixture verifies positive and negative 961.742249 Hz normalized shifts against 146 MHz uplink and 435 MHz downlink, plus invalid input, an actual 11-second expiry and a 2018 TLE rejection. The offscreen app refuses the old TLE; with a current-dated synthetic TLE it opens a draft showing TX 145.998596 MHz / RX 435.004184 MHz and preserves it on a second request; neither case saves a QSO. A QML test with a fake connected radio observes zero `tuneTo` calls when the draft is populated, and verifies SAT/TX/RX values passed to the existing submit path. Existing satellite split and radio-net save app tests passed. `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target decolog tst_predicttracker tst_maplayers -j 4`; `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir /tmp/decolog-evolution-build -R '^(predict_ui|predicttracker|newqsotuning)$' --output-on-failure` passed 3/3 after final changes; earlier database, migration and pass groups also passed on Apple M4/macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2.

A further isolated offscreen run connected to actual locally built PREDICT 2.2.5 and opened/preserved a corrected draft with zero QSO. Its source was the bundled 2018 OSCAR-7 TLE with its epoch artificially changed to yesterday and checksum recomputed solely to exercise the integration; it is synthetic, not current or valid orbital data, and the displayed frequencies are not operational evidence. No actual radio, RF, hardware CAT, other tracker, physical input or Windows/Linux was tested. The draft's eventual saving with a real external tracker and transponder, automatic tuning, pass workflow, rover and the remaining desktop goals stay open.

## 2026-11-23 — G02 VUCC reported boundary grids

VUCC candidate preview now reads `VUCC_GRIDS` from retained ADIF extras, including records with no primary GRIDSQUARE, and expands valid adjacent 2- or 4-locator reports into distinct four-character candidates for the same QSO. It checks 4/6-character Maidenhead validity, uniqueness, cardinality, adjacency including the antimeridian, and consistency with a supplied primary locator, including mixed precision when the reported areas share the relevant boundary. A malformed extended single locator is no longer accepted just because its first four characters look valid. Duplicate GRID4 candidates remain deduplicated across QSO. `MY_VUCC_GRIDS` remains in ADIF and is exposed as reported provenance in the preview/JSON, without interpreting it as a verified station location. The UI label now says candidate and retains `external rules verified: no`. No schema change is involved. The relevant [ADIF 3.1.8 specification](https://www.adif.org/318/ADIF_318.htm) describes 2/4 adjacent 4- or 6-character lists and their relationship to the primary grid.

A/B: before the change a confirmed synthetic boundary QSO reporting EM98/FM08/EM97/FM07 produced one candidate; after, it produces four. A separate mixed-precision fixture initially returned zero for adjacent EM98/FM08AA and passed with two after rectangle-based adjacency validation. Tests also cover diagonal, duplicate, nonadjacent, three-item, malformed mixed precision and inconsistent grids; valid mixed-precision edges and corners; two adjacent six-character subsquares that collapse to one GRID4; antimeridian adjacency; invalid longer locators; malformed stored field types; and persistence of both VUCC grid fields through file reopen and ADIF export. The actual offscreen app controller returns four candidates and `officialRulesVerified=false` without changing its single QSO. `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target decolog tst_journeycolumn tst_vucc -j 4`; `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen /tmp/decolog-evolution-build/tst_journeycolumn vuccBoundaryPreviewInApplication`; `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir /tmp/decolog-evolution-build -R '^(vucc|awards|cqmarathon|cqmarathon_db|database|migration)$' --output-on-failure` passed 6/6 on Apple M4/macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. Existing tests with invalid subsquare letters were corrected to valid fixtures before testing their independent evidence/site cases.

This is not an official VUCC credit determination. Confirmation-source acceptance, geographic/account constraints, terrestrial versus satellite eligibility, own-location history and multi-grid operating eligibility, award submissions, physical keyboard/mouse and other platforms remain open. The existing preview's broad confirmation evidence policy was not adjudicated in this increment. G02, G09 and the other desktop goals remain in scope.

Added local rover sessions and time-stamped position history in schema 17, with one active session per log, historical browsing and an optional manual-QSO own-locator field. ## 2026-11-23 — G08 explicit radio-net QSO association

A manual QSO draft opened from a radio-net participant now captures its session and member. On explicit save, the controller checks that the session is open, the callsign still matches that member and the session profile matches the effective station profile. The QSO stores session UUID and member call as `APP_DECOLOG_RADIO_NET_SESSION` and `APP_DECOLOG_RADIO_NET_CALL` in its existing ADIF extras, within the QSO insertion. Roster and draft actions still create no QSO. Metadata survive local ADIF export/import; they are reported provenance, not a foreign key or proof that an imported record belongs to a local session. Existing radio-net session JSON does not yet include or list associated QSO; concurrent instances are not serialized between the session check and QSO insertion.

A/B: the same two explicitly saved, repeated synthetic contacts initially had empty session metadata; after the change both have the session UUID and member call. Programmatic offscreen app tests also reject a different participant call and a session closed while a draft was open, preserve an existing draft, and verify local ADIF export/import. The import retains one of the two identical synthetic contacts under the existing duplicate policy. `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target decolog tst_journeycolumn tst_radionet -j 4`; the three focused radio-net/rover app scenarios passed, and `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir /tmp/decolog-evolution-build -R '^(radionet|roversession|newqsotuning|database|migration|qmlpopups)$' --output-on-failure` passed 6/6 on Apple M4/macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2.

Physical input, real RF, third-party ADIF handling, cross-process races and Windows/Linux remain unverified. The session UUID metadata does not provide session-side QSO browsing or export. G08 and the full desktop roadmap remain open.


## 2026-11-23 — G08 reported session QSO browsing and export

The radio-net dialog can switch between session history and paginated QSO whose stored metadata report that session UUID. It displays each current QSO call, time, band, mode and source, plus the reported participant call; the UI explicitly warns that imported metadata are not evidence of local participation. Session JSON adds `reported_qsos` with the same distinction. Soft-deleted records and malformed metadata are excluded. Pagination uses an indexed UUID expression and includes only the selected session, with no automatic QSO creation. Schema 18 builds the partial index; the existing minimal legacy migration fixture skips it when required columns are absent. English and Italian strings are provided; other translations fall back to English.

A/B: the new page API was absent before implementation. Afterwards, 205 synthetic QSO page without overlap and a second session is empty; soft deletion and malformed JSON do not break the index or leak into the selected results. An imported record with a different reported participant remains labelled with its actual call, reported participant and source in JSON. The offscreen app navigates 202 results across pages, saves explicit repeated contacts and retains the original event-history navigation. A synthetic schema-17 database with malformed extras migrates to 18 without losing the QSO. The first full database regression exposed that the minimal v1 fixture lacks indexed columns; after guarding the migration, it passed. `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target decolog tst_radionet tst_database tst_migration tst_journeycolumn tst_roversession tst_vucc tst_awards -j 4`; `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir /tmp/decolog-evolution-build -R '^(radionet|roversession|database|migration|vucc|awards|newqsotuning|qmlpopups)$' --output-on-failure` passed 8/8, plus the three focused application scenarios, on Apple M4/macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2.

A separate Python SQLite 3.54.0 synthetic measurement created the partial index over 100,000 small records with 1,000 matching in 0.008 s on this machine; this is not a benchmark of opening an actual user log, another machine, Qt's SQLite build or a large production database. UI probes invoke controls programmatically. Real user-log migration, physical input, concurrent writers, external JSON/ADIF consumers, RF and Windows/Linux remain open; session membership still rests on reported metadata rather than a guarded relational association.


## 2026-11-23 — G12 initial local QSL OCR reader

Added a read-only OCR action to the existing QSO detail's received-QSL section. The operator explicitly chooses a local image and runs separately installed Tesseract with the English model; raw text and low-scoring words are shown for human review. No image is sent to a service, and OCR does not change the QSO, attach an image or change confirmation status. Existing attachment remains a separate action. Results are temporary, not a persistent acquisition workflow. The process is asynchronous and cancellable, with input dimensions/size, output and runtime bounded; a cancelled job can be started again. Installation, interface, scope and licensing are documented in [QSL-OCR.md](../QSL-OCR.md).

A/B: before this increment no local OCR engine implementation existed; after it, actual Tesseract 5.5.4 and `eng` read a callsign and date from two synthetic printed layouts and leave source bytes unchanged. A synthetic TSV process checks low-score handling and line grouping, and a deliberately slow process is killed and successfully restarted. The offscreen application opens the QSO detail OCR dialog, reads a separate synthetic image, and leaves the one existing QSO and its attachment list unchanged. `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target decolog tst_tesseractocr tst_database tst_migration tst_qslcards tst_journeycolumn -j 4`; `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir /tmp/decolog-evolution-build -R '^(tesseractocr|database|migration|qslcards|qmlpopups|newqsotuning)$' --output-on-failure` passed 6/6; focused OCR and radio-net application scenarios also passed on Apple M4/macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. UI controls were invoked programmatically.

The separate Tesseract installation for this test used Homebrew and upgraded some existing Homebrew dependencies; it is not bundled with the application. The current reader does not extract fields, offer candidate QSO, support PDF, link multiple QSO, persist or undo acquisition, or implement rapid historical entry. No real, faded or handwritten QSL, accuracy benchmark, physical input, Windows/Linux or package distribution was tested. G12 and the full desktop roadmap remain open.


## 2026-11-23 — G12 conservative OCR field suggestions and candidate search

The local OCR reader now proposes whole-word callsigns, dates, times, bands and modes with raw-word provenance and uncertainty. Numeric dates with two valid interpretations retain both marked ambiguous; invalid dates are omitted. Compact times require a TIME/UTC neighbour. Suggestions are heuristic and capped at 100 per category, with a visible truncation warning. The operator can select a callsign and explicitly run an asynchronous, cancellable, read-only exact-call query against the open file-backed log. It returns at most 100 non-deleted entries, indicates truncation and no matches, and does not apply date/time/band/mode filters, modify the log, attach the card or confirm any correspondence. Each result can become stale if the log changes. English/Italian labels are provided; other locales fall back to English.

A/B: the `suggestions` property was absent before the change. The synthetic word fixture now retains K1ABC and K1ABO separately, both interpretations of 03/04/2026, an ISO date, a labelled time, band and mode; it omits an invalid date and unlabelled year. The read-only search returns only the exact similar call, reports no results, caps a 103-contact fixture at 100, and cancels/restarts. Real Tesseract reads synthetic printed layouts; an offscreen app searches a log containing K1ABC and K1ABO and returns only K1ABC, and a second app fixture with only K1ABO displays no exact match. No records or attachments change. An offscreen screenshot was visually inspected for the no-match and suggestion-count layout. `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target decolog tst_tesseractocr tst_journeycolumn tst_database tst_migration tst_qslcards -j 4`; `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir /tmp/decolog-evolution-build -R '^(tesseractocr|database|migration|qslcards|qmlpopups|newqsotuning)$' --output-on-failure` passed 6/6, plus the two OCR and existing radio-net app scenarios, on Apple M4/macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2.

The heuristic can both miss text and propose non-callsigns; it is not a full structured parser or a matching decision. Cross-field ranking, manual correction and approval workflow, multi-QSO attachment, persisted acquisition, undo, handwriting/faded real-card accuracy, physical input, concurrent file replacement and Windows/Linux remain open. No real card or RF was used.


## 2026-11-23 — G12 immutable OCR input snapshot

OCR now asynchronously copies the selected, bounded image to a private temporary directory, computes SHA-256 over the copied bytes and feeds that exact copy to Tesseract. The dialog shows the beginning of the digest. The copy persists for the current OCR result, is removed on the next attempt or normal destruction, and can remain after abnormal termination. Cancelling during copying or OCR discards the result. The existing QSO attachment action still reads a separately selected file and is not yet linked to the OCR snapshot, so the user must verify that file independently. No QSO or attachment changes in this increment.

A/B: before the change there was no snapshot digest property and Tesseract was passed the original selected path. Afterward a real OCR fixture verifies the digest against the original and byte-for-byte snapshot preservation after the original is overwritten; a fake engine reports that it received the temporary snapshot path. A slow fake process is allowed to start before cancellation, then the reader successfully restarts. Real Tesseract recognizes both existing synthetic layouts; two offscreen QSO-detail scenarios still perform exact-call search and no-match handling. `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target decolog tst_tesseractocr tst_journeycolumn tst_database tst_migration tst_qslcards -j 4`; `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir /tmp/decolog-evolution-build -R '^(tesseractocr|database|migration|qslcards|qmlpopups|newqsotuning)$' --output-on-failure` passed 6/6, plus both focused app scenarios, on Apple M4/macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. The OCR tests use synthetic images; UI invocation was programmatic.

Multi-QSO attachment from the verified snapshot, persisted provenance, reversible acquisition, realistic card evaluation, crash cleanup, physical input, Windows/Linux and all other outstanding roadmap items remain open.


## 2026-11-23 — G12 explicit multi-QSO OCR acquisition

The OCR dialog now lets the operator select up to 100 exact-call candidates, including candidates from successive searches, and explicitly approve attachment of the OCR snapshot. A background, cancellable worker verifies the snapshot SHA-256 and each selected QSO's UUID, revision and live state inside a SQLite write transaction. It reuses an existing attachment by content hash, preserves pre-existing QSO links, and atomically saves any new links with an acquisition journal containing the snapshot hash, source basename, recognized text and selected QSO identities/revisions. Schema 19 adds the journal. OCR or searching alone still creates no attachment or QSO; approval does not alter confirmation status. The user is told that recognized text is stored. The snapshot is used even if the original file changes after OCR. Cancellation can stop the operation before commit but cannot undo an already committed transaction.

A/B: the acquisition table was absent before this increment. The offscreen application, using actual Tesseract and a synthetic card, explicitly approves two same-call QSO and stores one byte-identical image plus two links and OCR text; existing no-write and no-match app scenarios also pass. Core fixtures reuse a pre-existing link, repeat an acquisition without duplicating links, reject duplicate selections, a changed QSO, a modified snapshot and cancellation, and inject a journal-write failure to verify rollback of both links and a newly inserted image. A separate test overwrites the original after OCR and verifies that the approved attachment retains the original snapshot bytes. An offscreen screenshot exposed unreadable default checkbox text; the delegate was corrected and the result visually inspected. File paths containing spaces, `#` and `?` are exercised by the SQLite URI fixture. `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target decolog tst_tesseractocr tst_database tst_journeycolumn tst_migration tst_qslcards -j 4`; `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir /tmp/decolog-evolution-build -R '^(tesseractocr|database|migration|qslcards|qmlpopups|newqsotuning)$' --output-on-failure` passed 6/6, plus the three focused app scenarios, on Apple M4/macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. Tests use synthetic images and programmatic UI.

A simulated schema-18 database containing an existing QSO and attached bytes was reopened through schema 19 and retained both; this is not a real user log. The journal is not yet exposed for review or guarded undo, and schema migration has not been tested on a real user log. Concurrent replacement of the database file at the same path, actual photo/QSL OCR accuracy, physical input, Windows/Linux and desktop release packages remain unverified. G12 and the full roadmap stay open.

## 2026-11-23 — G12 acquisition history and guarded undo

QSO detail now opens a paginated OCR acquisition history showing source basename, image SHA-256, a 4096-character OCR preview and every affected QSO, distinguishing newly created from previously existing links. The operator must select and explicitly confirm an undo. Schema 20 records a unique token and revision for newly created links. Current ordinary attachment and later OCR reuse increment the link revision. A background undo validates acquisition ID/UUID, QSO UUID/revision and each created link's token/revision in one write transaction; any mismatch, missing link or untracked legacy link aborts the entire operation. It removes only links created by that acquisition, keeps pre-existing links, the image blob and the audit record, and records the undo time. Cancellation is effective before commit. Acquisitions from schema 19 with created links have no tokens and remain review-only for automatic undo.

A/B: the guarded undo API was absent before this increment. Synthetic tests show an acquisition involving two QSO can undo its one new link while retaining the pre-existing link and image; repeated undo is rejected. Edited QSO, later OCR reuse, and ordinary reuse through a second database connection independently block destructive undo. A trigger aborting the second link deletion leaves both links intact; a legacy null-token fixture is refused. An indexed history fixture traverses 52 acquisitions in two pages. A synthetic schema-19 log retains its existing attachment and OCR journal after migration. The offscreen application opens history and explicitly confirms undo across two QSO, retaining the prior link and blob; the acquisition and no-write app scenarios also pass. A screenshot revealed insufficient selected-row contrast, which was corrected and visually rechecked. `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target decolog tst_tesseractocr tst_database tst_journeycolumn tst_qsl tst_migration tst_qslcards -j 4`; `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir /tmp/decolog-evolution-build -R '^(tesseractocr|database|migration|qsl|qslcards|qmlpopups|newqsotuning)$' --output-on-failure` passed 7/7, plus the three focused app scenarios, on Apple M4/macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. UI operations were programmatic.

Older application versions or direct SQL can reuse links without incrementing the new revision, and same-path database replacement is not protected. The blob and OCR text are deliberately retained, not erased. No real user-log migration, actual cross-process contention, physical input, real QSL image quality or Windows/Linux was tested. Editing saved acquisition data, historical entry and the remaining desktop roadmap remain open.


## 2026-11-23 — G12 explicit historical QSO entry

Added a separate desktop historical-entry dialog reusing the manual QSO path. Date and time must be explicitly entered; the dialog starts without the current rig frequency, mode or signal reports. Log & keep retains the entered date, band/frequency, mode and programme references but clears the next callsign, per-contact values, reports and time. An earlier time on the same date is blocked unless the operator explicitly confirms it or selects Next UTC day. Date advance is explicit, not inferred; profile details are opt-in. Historical entries carry `APP_DECOLOG_ENTRY_CONTEXT=HISTORICAL` and do not inherit current rover/activation, current split rig frequencies, callbook completion or live QSO broadcasts. Cloud sync may still process the new manual records and existing QSL service settings can act later. See [HISTORICAL-QSO.md](../HISTORICAL-QSO.md).

A/B: the historical mode did not exist before this increment; its first QML test failed on the missing property. Afterwards a fake connected-rig QML test shows zero tuning and blank initial time/frequency; a rollover fixture verifies a blocked same-date earlier time, explicit override, year boundary and leap day. The actual offscreen app rejects missing time even through the controller, saves four synthetic QSO while rover and activation are active, requires the rollover choice, and only the explicitly opted-in fourth QSO inherits the station profile. Local ADIF export/import retains historical provenance. A loopback rigctld fixture with split RX 435.25/TX 145.9 MHz records four historical QSO at the manually entered 14.05 MHz with no inherited RX and zero CAT writes/PTT across 37 queries. The existing ordinary split fixture on the same fake setup still stores TX 145.9/RX 435.25 MHz and zero writes. `cmake --build /tmp/decolog-evolution-build --target decolog tst_journeycolumn tst_maplayers tst_database tst_migration tst_radionet tst_roversession -j 4`; three focused app scenarios and `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir /tmp/decolog-evolution-build -R '^(historical_rig|rig_split_manual|newqsotuning|qmlpopups|database|migration|radionet|roversession)$' --output-on-failure` passed 8/8 on Apple M4/macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. A screenshot of the initial dialog was visually inspected.

These are synthetic QSO, programmatic UI and virtual rig protocol tests, not physical input, radio or RF. Actual historical logs, large sessions, external service interaction and Windows/Linux remain unverified. Manual duplicates remain allowed and the rollover guard compares only the immediately previous successful save in the open dialog. Full G12 and all other desktop goals remain open.


## 2026-11-23 — G06 independent additional programme references

The existing activation session keeps its primary kind/reference and now accepts additional operator POTA, SOTA, WWFF and IOTA references. Additional POTA permits a comma-delimited list; the others permit one reference each. New entries receive the separate `MY_POTA_REF`, `MY_SOTA_REF`, `MY_WWFF_REF` and `MY_IOTA` fields without overwriting fields already present; contacted-station references remain untouched. Legacy `MY_SIG`/`MY_SIG_INFO` continue to represent the primary kind. Additional values are normalized and checked structurally, reject duplicate/empty list items or conflicts with the same primary kind, and do not claim catalogue or geographic validity. Existing primary-reference validation is unchanged. The active session's map persists and a change affects only later records. Existing callers that update a session without a `programs` key retain its current additional values. The dialog displays validation errors and warns that its generic export is not partitioned by programme/reference/day; the progress label explicitly says local count and not verified eligibility. See [MULTI-PROGRAM-ACTIVATION.md](../MULTI-PROGRAM-ACTIVATION.md).

A/B: before the change, an active POTA session with additional SOTA/WWFF data produced an empty `MY_SOTA_REF`. The new core test stores two POTA references plus one SOTA and WWFF reference, preserves separate counterpart references, rejects malformed/duplicate additional data, reopens the active settings and shows that a changed summit affects only the subsequent QSO. Local ADIF export/import retains these fields. The actual offscreen app rejects an invalid additional reference and invalid update, saves two QSO with different operator summit references and retains counterpart park/summit/WWFF values. Inputs and UI operations were synthetic/programmatic.

This is not the completed G06: the legacy session count is time-based, closed sessions/site/operator periods are not durably modelled, and programme eligibility, catalogue update, programme-specific partitioning/preview, midnight and multi-operator handling are not implemented here. POTA's public documentation calls for separate park logs, and WWFF uses `MY_SIG_INFO`; no official importer, physical UI, real radio/RF, Windows/Linux or external submission was tested. All desktop goals remain in scope.


## 2026-11-23 — G06 durable activation identity and configuration journal

New sessions receive a generated UUID. Applying the activation to a QSO fills `APP_DECOLOG_ACTIVATION_UUID` only when absent, and the value survives local ADIF export/import. Schema 21 adds a durable run and ordered start/update/stop configuration snapshots. Transitions and the active setting commit in one database transaction; an error leaves the active state unchanged. Replacing an active tracked session closes the previous run in the same transaction. UUIDs supplied by callers on start/update are not trusted. Old active sessions remain untracked; no history is inferred.

A/B: the new stable-identity test failed before this change because the UUID was empty; it passes after. Core tests cover preserved incoming identifiers, rejected start/update/stop via SQLite triggers and transaction rollback, identity through a reference update and ADIF round trip, restart, closed journal and legacy session handling. `tst_activation`: 18 passed; `tst_database`: 72 passed and one explicit compatibility fixture skipped, including the minimal schema-1 migration. The desktop application rebuilt on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2; the focused offscreen multi-program application scenario and CTest activation/database passed. The complete journeycolumn suite was interrupted and is not claimed as passed. These are synthetic local tests.

Reported ADIF metadata can be imported, copied or edited and is not proof of membership; incoming network records may bypass activation application. Existing counts, duplicates, scoring and generic exports still use their previous time-based selection; the journal has no browser and site/operator periods and programme/day/reference partitions remain to be implemented. No real user-log migration, physical UI, radio/RF or external programme importer has been verified. G06 and the complete desktop roadmap remain open.


## 2026-11-23 — G06 reported-QSO grouping preview

The active-session dialog can now request a read-only grouping preview of QSO whose stored metadata reports the active session UUID. It splits operator references by POTA/SOTA/WWFF/IOTA, reference, UTC QSO date, operator and grid; one QSO can appear in several groups without duplication in the log. Counterpart reference fields are not used. The preview uses a consistent read transaction, refuses an untracked or inconsistent session, reports missing/invalid date and missing operator-programme references, shows unknown operator/grid explicitly, and invalidates its displayed result on local session changes. Schema 22 adds an index for this metadata lookup, with guards for minimal old schemas. Results are capped at 1000 reported QSO and 100 displayed groups; exceeding the QSO cap yields an error rather than a partial result.

A/B: the grouping test could not compile before the feature because no preview API existed. Afterward, two parks and a summit across midnight, two operators and two grids form six expected groups, the counterpart park and an unrelated QSO are excluded, a missing-reference QSO is counted as excluded, and a legacy session and nested transaction are refused. A 1001-QSO fixture verifies that the cap produces no partial groups. The actual offscreen desktop probe invokes the preview button after two synthetic QSO and verifies five groups; `tst_activation` passed 20 tests, `tst_database` passed 72 with one explicit fixture skipped, and the focused desktop probe passed on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. The minimal schema-1 migration passed.

This is an informational grouping, not a file preview, an eligibility/duplicate calculation or an official submission. A UUID is editable/importable metadata, so it cannot prove membership; network records may bypass assignment. Grouping runs synchronously and must be replaced with an asynchronous, scalable preview before G06 can be complete. Operator/grid are the values on each QSO, not validated station/site periods; missing operator/grid remain visible in groups. No real-log migration, physical interaction, official importer or RF test has been performed. Programme-specific partition export, catalogues and all other outstanding desktop requirements remain open.


## 2026-11-23 — G06 background programme grouping

The session dialog now calculates its reported-QSO grouping on a separate read-only SQLite connection in a worker, within a read transaction and using the existing metadata index. The prior 1000-QSO rejection is removed. Groups are accessible in pages of 100, and the operator can cancel. Session/log notifications cancel or invalidate the pending result; completion checks the open connection, path, session identity and generation before publishing. This remains an informational preview, not a programme-specific export or eligibility check.

A/B: a 1001-QSO test failed before this increment because the previous implementation rejected the size. After the change the synchronous reference and background worker return identical counts and groups from a file-backed fixture. Tests exercise completion, a simultaneous second start, cancellation and invalidation while a worker is pending. `tst_activation` passed 21 tests; the desktop offscreen multiprogram probe invoked the preview button and reported the expected two QSO/five groups. The application rebuilt on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. All records and interactions were synthetic.

The worker holds groups in memory and has not been benchmarked on very large or high-cardinality logs; concurrent writes during an active scan and replacement of a database file at the same path remain untested. The database snapshot is an implementation property, not evidence of a concurrent-write runtime test. Program-specific exports, eligibility, reference catalogues, site/operator periods and official importer/RF verification remain open, as do the other desktop roadmap requirements.


## 2026-11-23 — G06 POTA and WWFF partition file copies

The session grouping preview now offers an ADIF export for a selected POTA or WWFF group, partitioned by operator reference, UTC date, operator and grid. The worker recomputes that group from a read-only transaction and compares its ordered QSO UUID/revision fingerprint and count with the preview. It rejects stale membership/revisions and writes the output with `QSaveFile`; cancellation is best effort before file commit. It refuses the current database and SQLite sidecars as destinations. The copy assigns the selected operator reference and corresponding `MY_SIG`/`MY_SIG_INFO`, preserving counterpart references and the original personal-log records. For POTA, an ADIF `@` location suffix is retained in `MY_POTA_REF` and omitted from `MY_SIG_INFO`.

The [POTA technical guide](https://docs.pota.app/docs/activator_reference/ADIF_for_POTA_reference.html) describes the programme fields and required contact fields; [WWFF's guidance](https://wwff.co/rules-faq/confirming-and-sending-log/) specifies `MY_SIG=WWFF` and the activated reference in `MY_SIG_INFO`. These sources inform the file mapping, not a claim of importer acceptance or programme eligibility.

A/B: the partition export API did not exist before this increment. Afterward a file-backed synthetic test exports two-park/WWFF data from one UTC day/operator and excludes another day/operator; parses both resulting ADIF copies; checks POTA/WWFF fields, counterpart reference and unchanged original; rejects the database path; and verifies an edited QSO makes the old preview stale without replacing the existing file. The offscreen desktop probe requested and parsed a POTA partition containing the two expected QSO. `tst_activation` passed 22, `tst_database` passed 72 with one explicit fixture skipped, and the focused application probe passed on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2.

There is no formal required-field, duplication, catalogue, location or eligibility validation; fingerprints rely on UUID/revision and cannot detect direct database edits that bypass revision tracking. Changes after the export snapshot and QSL-only changes may not be reflected. Group data and export documents are held in memory; very large and high-cardinality logs, concurrent writes, cancellation at every timing boundary, Windows/Linux and replacement of a file at the database path remain untested. SOTA/IOTA-specific files, closed-session access, site/operator periods, official importer acceptance and physical/RF tests remain open; G06 and the full desktop roadmap are not complete.


## 2026-11-23 — G06 SOTA and IOTA ADIF partition copies

The same selected-group, background export and stale-preview checks now also support SOTA and IOTA. Their file copies set `MY_SOTA_REF` or `MY_IOTA` to the selected operator reference, retain `SOTA_REF`/`IOTA` for the contacted station and preserve any existing `MY_SIG` fields. [ADIF 3.1.8](https://www.adif.org/318/ADIF_318.htm) defines these separate fields; this is not a claim that a recipient accepts the resulting file.

A/B: a synthetic four-programme session with separate counterpart summit/island references could not start SOTA/IOTA exports before this change; afterwards both files are parsed and their own/counterpart fields and existing `MY_SIG` are checked. The two-date/two-operator fixture still checks POTA/WWFF and stale-preview rejection. `tst_activation` passed 22 tests.
