The offscreen desktop probe produced POTA, SOTA and IOTA files, verified two different summit groups and parsed the resulting files, on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2.

No official importer, actual island activation, RF, large-log performance or Windows/Linux test was performed. Formal programme eligibility, duplicates, catalogue validity, detailed site/operator periods, complete file review and the other open G06 requirements remain outstanding; the same snapshot and metadata limitations documented above apply. All desktop roadmap streams remain in scope.


## 2026-11-23 — G06 review and export closed tracked sessions

The activation dialog now lists closed tracked sessions in pages of 100 and allows an operator to run the existing background grouping and POTA/WWFF/SOTA/IOTA partition export for a selected session. The scanner checks that the selected journal exists, and the selected UUID and preview generation are retained independently of the currently active session; the existing revision fingerprint and consistent export snapshot checks still apply. Legacy sessions without a tracked UUID are not reconstructed or attributed.

A/B: before this change the closed-session listing and preview entry point did not exist. A synthetic fixture closes a multi-programme session, previews and exports it, then opens an independent connection and previews it again; another fixture lists 102 sessions across two pages. The offscreen application probe closes a session, activates its history-row Preview button, and produces and parses a two-QSO POTA file from that closed session. `tst_activation` passed 23 tests and the focused desktop probe passed on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. No physical UI or external service was used.

The history only covers sessions created with tracking; editable/imported session identifiers are not proof of membership. The history does not yet expose site/operator periods or catalogue/eligibility validation. The grouping, output snapshot and importer limitations recorded in preceding milestones remain. Other desktop roadmap requirements remain open.


## 2026-11-23 — G06 station and operator presence in group review

The grouping preview reports, for each programme/reference/day/operator/grid group, how many QSO have an empty `STATION_CALLSIGN`, an empty `OPERATOR`, or both empty. These counts overlap and are advisory: records are not removed and callsigns and programme eligibility are not validated. The UI explains that POTA asks for at least one station/operator callsign, while WWFF distinguishes station callsign and operator, as described by the [POTA ADIF guide](https://docs.pota.app/docs/activator_reference/ADIF_for_POTA_reference.html) and [WWFF log guidance](https://wwff.co/rules-faq/confirming-and-sending-log/).

A/B: the previous grouping gave zero for the missing station-call field because it did not report it; the revised synthetic fixture reports one while recognizing the available operator. A second fixture compares records with neither identity, station only, and both, and verifies the counts separately. `tst_activation` passed 24 tests, and the focused offscreen desktop multiprogram/closed-session probe passed after rebuilding on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. The test input and interactions were synthetic.

These warnings do not replace field-format checks, duplicate and programme eligibility rules, catalogue validation, individual-QSO exclusion explanations, or importer acceptance. No external importer, physical UI, radio/RF or other platform was tested. The outstanding G06 and other desktop roadmap work remains open.


## 2026-11-23 — G06 review of ungrouped reported QSO

The grouping preview retains QSO ID, call, stored UTC timestamp and a reason for reported-session records omitted from all groups because the date is missing/invalid or no operator programme reference is available. The UI displays these rows in pages of 100. This explains the existing grouping exclusions; it does not infer untagged contacts, explain exclusions from an individual programme, or judge eligibility. A damaged date takes precedence in the displayed reason, consistent with the existing counters. Export scans do not retain the exclusion list.

A/B: before this increment the preview returned the exclusion count but no individual rows. The synthetic fixture now identifies the reference-free K1DDD record and its reason, then deliberately corrupts another QSO's stored date and identifies K1EEE with the date reason. `tst_activation` passed 24 tests; the existing offscreen desktop multiprogram and closed-session probe passed after rebuilding on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. That desktop probe has no excluded rows, so the rendered exclusion rows and their page buttons have not yet been exercised.

The result retains excluded rows in memory; large exclusion sets and concurrent writes were not benchmarked. Programme-specific exclusion details, duplicate and eligibility rules, local catalogues, review of all included records, importer acceptance and physical/RF operation remain open, along with the other desktop roadmap work.


## 2026-11-23 — G06 exclusion review desktop verification

A new offscreen app fixture creates a separate synthetic log containing one closed tracked session and 101 reported contacts: 100 without an operator programme reference and one with a deliberately invalid stored UTC date. The test opens the closed session from the desktop dialog, checks the displayed delegate text and reason on the first page, activates Next, checks the final contact and date reason, then activates Previous and verifies the original first row. It does not use a user log.

A/B: before this increment the new desktop probe did not run and its baseline surfaced two `ActivationDialog.qml` TypeErrors caused by reading `.length` on undefined preview arrays before a result existed. Those bindings now use empty-array fallbacks. The revised desktop exclusion probe and existing multiprogram/closed-session probe passed; `tst_activation` passed 24 tests. Tests ran offscreen with programmatic actions on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. This checks the QML delegate text and controls, not physical interaction or visual inspection of the screenshot.

Grouping exclusions remain limited to invalid date and missing operator references; per-programme eligibility, catalogues, concurrent writes, large-log performance, official importer acceptance, RF and the remaining desktop roadmap work are still open.


## 2026-11-23 — G06 local reference syntax warning

Each group now applies the existing programme-specific local structural syntax check to its operator reference and shows an advisory warning when the local pattern does not recognize it. The group remains visible and exportable and the underlying QSO is not altered. The warning is not proof of invalidity: the local patterns can be narrower than real programme formats, and they do not consult any reference catalogue. [ADIF 3.1.8](https://www.adif.org/318/ADIF_318.htm) defines separate programme data types, but this incremental warning is not a complete ADIF or programme validation.

A/B: an imported-like `MY_SOTA_REF=not-a-summit` was previously grouped without a syntax warning; the new test confirms the warning while retaining a valid POTA group and the counterpart SOTA reference. The offscreen application fixture includes a malformed reported summit and checks that the group delegate displays the warning. `tst_activation` passed 25 tests, and both existing desktop probes passed; the modified warning fixture was rerun and passed on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. All data and interactions were synthetic.

The warning does not validate reference existence, timing, location, eligible QSO, duplicates or recipient acceptance. Programmatic UI inspection is not physical operation; catalogues, official importer testing, RF and the remaining desktop roadmap work stay open.


## 2026-11-23 — G06 local SOTA summit catalogue

The desktop activation dialog links to the [SOTA summit list](https://www.sotadata.org.uk/summitslist.csv), accepts a locally selected CSV, imports it in a cancellable background worker and provides an offline reference/date lookup. The importer first snapshots the file, computes SHA-256, parses the dated SOTA header and required columns, checks each row and date interval, rejects duplicate references and malformed data, and builds an indexed temporary SQLite catalogue. A committed catalogue is installed through an atomic save; failure or cancellation before commit preserves the previous catalogue. Existing unrelated destination files are refused using a SQLite application marker. The UI shows the CSV's declared list date, imported filename, local import time, row count and snapshot SHA-256, explicitly identifying its local origin as unverified. The lookup reports presence and listed dates, not activation eligibility.

A/B: the catalogue API and desktop lookup did not exist before this increment; a second A/B test exposed an initial implementation that could overwrite an unrelated existing destination, which is now rejected and preserved. Synthetic tests cover CSV quoted commas, valid/outside/missing lookups, invalid date, SHA-256, cancellation, invalid and duplicate inputs preserving the old catalogue, a successful newer update, offline lookup after the source CSV is removed, and refusal to use a catalogue whose provenance metadata was deliberately damaged. A programmatic offscreen desktop probe imports a synthetic CSV and checks a reference/date. `tst_activation` passed 25 tests; CTest for catalogue, activation and database passed 3/3, and desktop catalogue and existing multiprogram probes passed on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2.

For an additional real-data compatibility check, a CSV was downloaded manually via HTTPS from the SOTA list on 2026-11-23, redirected to `storage.sota.org.uk`; the source returned 25,202,937 bytes. The snapshot header declared 2026-11-22, SHA-256 `630cca5669d6f3cec488b022c66097cf9933f63f2a81541133954aaaff4413a5`; the importer processed 182,282 rows and found `3B8/MU-001` within its listed date range. This verifies that one observed file was parsed, not that the source or future files are complete, signed or authoritative for eligibility.

Automatic download/update inside the application, catalogue use during group review, POTA/WWFF/IOTA catalogues, reference status beyond the dates in the imported file, updates under concurrent readers or power loss, Windows/Linux and physical UI testing remain open. The CSV parser requires UTF-8 and one record per line, with a 128 MiB input limit; future upstream format changes can require an update. The broader desktop roadmap and all other G06 requirements remain active.


## 2026-11-23 — G06 SOTA catalogue checks in group review

The background grouping preview now checks each SOTA reference and its UTC day against one read-only SQLite transaction on the installed catalogue. A prepared query is reused across groups. The UI distinguishes listed for that day, outside the imported date interval, not found in that file, and catalogue unavailable, and identifies the imported list date and SHA-256. A catalogue import notification invalidates the prior preview. These checks are advisory and neither remove contacts nor certify location, eligibility or authenticity. The log snapshot and catalogue snapshot are individually consistent but are not a single cross-database atomic snapshot.

A/B: previously the grouping contained no catalogue results. The new isolated fixture imports a small SOTA list and checks one reference across 2026-12-31/2027-01-01, a missing reference, an absent catalogue, background preview, and invalidation when a catalogue import begins. The offscreen desktop probe imports a synthetic list, previews a closed synthetic session and verifies the displayed group status and version. CTest passed catalogue, activation and database 3/3; the focused desktop catalogue and multiprogram probes passed on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. These are local programmatic tests, not official importer, physical UI or RF tests.

The file's origin, completeness and the validity of an activation remain unverified; future or historical changes can postdate the imported list. Concurrent external catalogue replacement during a scan, high-cardinality performance, automatic online updates and POTA/WWFF/IOTA catalogues remain open, as do the other desktop roadmap requirements.


## 2026-11-23 — G06 online SOTA catalogue update

The activation dialog now offers a user-initiated SOTA catalogue update. It downloads in the background from the configured HTTPS URL, follows at most five redirects only to the exact permitted SOTA hosts and list path over HTTPS, rejects other redirects, enforces a 128 MiB streaming limit and a transfer timeout, and supports cancellation. The existing validated temporary build and atomic replacement retain the previous catalogue on failed download, invalid CSV or cancellation before commit. Metadata records the final HTTPS endpoint, source type, list header date, import time, row count and SHA-256. The UI distinguishes HTTPS download from manually imported, unverified-origin files and states that the list is not signed. This is user-initiated, not a scheduled refresh.

A/B: the network update entry point did not exist before this increment. A loopback HTTP fixture, explicitly marked `network-unverified`, exercises success, same-server redirect, disallowed external redirect, HTTP 503, truncated body, over-limit Content-Length, malformed CSV and cancellation; every failed update leaves the prior catalogue usable. An opt-in live test through Qt networking reached `https://storage.sota.org.uk/summitslist.csv` via the configured SOTA redirect, installed 182,282 rows dated 2026-11-22 with SHA-256 `630cca5669d6f3cec488b022c66097cf9933f63f2a81541133954aaaff4413a5`, and passed on 2026-11-23. A separate opt-in offscreen desktop test activated the online-update button and observed success using a temporary application data directory. CTest catalogue, activation and database passed 3/3 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2; the regular test run intentionally skips opt-in live tests, which were separately run as described.

HTTPS provides transport protection to the contacted host, not a signature, completeness guarantee or proof of programme eligibility. Cancellation cannot reverse a completed atomic commit; mid-stream size overflow without Content-Length, network interruptions at every timing boundary, concurrent multi-process replacement, other platforms and physical UI remain unverified. POTA/WWFF/IOTA catalogues, scheduled refresh and the rest of the desktop roadmap remain open.


## 2026-11-23 — G06 offline POTA snapshot and group review

The activation dialog now imports a user-selected POTA park CSV in a cancellable background worker, preserves an unrelated destination file, builds an indexed temporary SQLite catalogue and replaces a prior catalogue atomically only after validation. The imported file is snapshotted and its filename, local import time, count and SHA-256 are recorded; origin is explicitly unverified. Offline lookup and the grouping preview distinguish active/inactive in the imported snapshot, missing reference, and a specified `@` location listed, not listed or unknown. The preview shows the snapshot provenance and labels its status as non-historical. It does not decide the park's status on the QSO date, actual boundaries or eligibility. POTA data attribution: Parks on the Air, [pota.app](https://pota.app/). The CSV has no source version field used here, so no historical version date is invented.

A/B: the POTA catalogue and group comparison did not exist before this increment. A second A/B fixture found that an unquoted stray quote was accepted by the shared CSV reader; it is now rejected without replacing the existing snapshot. Synthetic tests cover quoted commas, active/inactive/missing references, listed/not-listed/unknown locations, absent catalogue, cancellation, unrelated destination protection, invalid input preservation and offline lookup after deleting the CSV. A 2020 QSO fixture explicitly checks that the label is a current snapshot rather than a claim about 2020. The offscreen desktop probe imports a synthetic CSV and checks both manual lookup and a group; existing SOTA and multiprogram probes also passed. CTest passed POTA catalogue, SOTA catalogue, activation and database 4/4 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2.

For a real-data compatibility check, the [published POTA CSV](https://pota.app/all_parks_ext.csv) was downloaded manually over HTTPS on 2026-11-23: 10,631,104 bytes, 106,094 rows, SHA-256 `472a0594e7a649a3c29c82dead30780e520715f4396e1c9ceeb564517b62fea2`. The importer processed it and confirmed a listed location for `US-0015@US-MD`; 2,203 source rows had no location description and remain unknown. The same shared CSV parser successfully re-imported the previously observed 182,282-row SOTA file. These observations do not establish completeness, authenticity, licence terms, historical validity or recipient acceptance. POTA's own [site announcement](https://parksontheair.substack.com/p/new-pota-dot-app-now-live) says an officially accessible external API is still on its roadmap; this increment uses a manually supplied CSV, not an undocumented API.

Automatic POTA downloads, historical status, true site/operator periods and boundary checks, POTA/WWFF/IOTA eligibility and WWFF/IOTA catalogues remain open. Multi-process replacement, very large data, other platforms, physical UI, official importers and RF remain unverified; the overall desktop roadmap is not complete.


## 2026-11-23 — G06 activation site and incoming identity precedence

Manual entry now applies existing explicit QSO/rover fields first, then the active session, then the selected station profile. This allows a changed activation locator to apply to later manual QSO instead of being silently shadowed by the profile's locator, while preserving explicit QSO values and historical-entry behaviour. For a QSO received via UDP during an activation, missing station callsign, operator and locator are filled from the selected profile only when all identities supplied by the sender agree with that profile. A mismatch leaves the profile unassigned, retains the sender's fields and emits an activity warning. It does not infer rig, antenna or TX power from the profile for UDP QSO. Activation fields and UUID are still applied on a conflicting incoming record; the warning requires the operator to review session membership. Existing QSO are not rewritten.

A/B: the manual desktop fixture previously stored profile grid `JN71DC` instead of the selected activation grid `JM75`; the revised test covers the corrected grid, explicit `JN70`, a later site/profile/operator change to `JM76`, preserved first records and stable session UUID. A separate offscreen application test uses two synthetic local WSJT-X/Decodium-protocol UDP clients: before the change a record with missing identity lacked the selected profile's station call, a conflicting sender could receive an invented operator and profile assignment, and the first proposed fix also incorrectly filled default TX power. The final test verifies those three failures are corrected, explicit source fields and TX power/rig survive, a structured QSOLogged-only packet works, and the conflict warning is emitted. These are synthetic senders, not two real Decodium sessions or RF.

The existing two-client UDP routing and Decodium QSOLogged/LoggedADIF pairing probes passed without an activation; the UDP fixture now isolates app data in its temporary directory. Historical-entry and rover override desktop probes passed, along with CTest database, rover, new-QSO tuning, QML popups, historical rig and activation 6/6 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. No actual radio, two real Decodium sessions, external sender interoperability, Windows/Linux or physical UI was tested. The historical recovery path still chooses the current activation state based on time rather than reconstructing the exact operator/site period, and formal site/operator period modelling, catalogue/eligibility work and the remaining desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G06 recovered QSO and recorded activation periods

Recovery from Decodium or watched ADIF now consults the tracked activation journal. When the complete reported QSO interval lies within one intact, non-overlapping recorded configuration period, the missing activation references, grid and session UUID are taken from that historical snapshot; source fields remain authoritative. No contest serial is allocated. Intervals crossing a change, overlapping tracked sessions or lacking sufficient time within a possible session are retained with `APP_DECOLOG_ACTIVATION_REVIEW=PERIOD_UNCERTAIN` and a recovery warning, without inferring the activation fields or an unknown operator/profile. A matching explicit source identity can still select a current profile; it does not establish that profile's historical contents. Existing duplicate suppression for a resolved QSO in the currently active session remains in place. Existing ordinary database duplicate detection still applies.

A/B on the same synthetic ADIF and closed-session journal: the previous controller recovered the first QSO without its historical `MY_POTA_REF` and failed the new app test; the revised controller recovered the first and third QSO with their respective period references and grids and marked the QSO spanning the change for review. The focused offscreen app test passed with three synthetic contacts; the unit fixture covers change boundaries, minute precision, missing and invalid time, overlapping sessions, conflicting UUID and a nested database transaction. `tst_activation` passed 28 tests; targeted CTest database, Decodium log, rover, new-QSO tuning, QML popups, historical rig and activation passed 7/7. The ordinary Decodium-log recovery app test also passed. These runs used macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 and synthetic files, not real Decodium instances or radio signals.

The journal captures application-recorded activation changes, not independent station-profile edits or the actual physical operator/site. Legacy untracked sessions cannot be reconstructed. The review marker has no dedicated review interface, source timestamps and pre-existing metadata may be wrong, and concurrent cross-process changes between lookup and insertion are unverified. Active-session duplicate suppression after this change, Windows/Linux, real Decodium interoperability and RF remain to be tested. Formal site/operator periods, programme eligibility and the other desktop roadmap tasks remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G03 award contribution and QSL planning core

The award calculator can optionally collect QSO-to-award-item contributions during its existing filtered pass. A new read-snapshot planning core uses those contributions and the calculator's confirmed items to count each outstanding award/item once, identify each contributing QSO and its alternatives, retain each award's accepted confirmation services, and expose the recorded QSL manager, matching enrichment history and sent-but-unconfirmed service states. The core does not contact services or change the log. It uses the existing calculator's rules and filters, so its output remains a potential plan subject to those rules' limitations; a matching enrichment entry is historical evidence, not proof of the route's current validity.

A/B: before this increment there was no `tst_qslplanner` target or QSO-to-benefit API. The new synthetic fixture checks three contacts with overlapping DXCC/WAZ benefits, equivalent alternatives, shared and absent managers, a pending paper confirmation and a received confirmation that removes the corresponding redundant benefits. A file-backed fixture changes station profile and reopens the database before rebuilding the plan. CTest `qslplanner`, `awards`, `database` and `qsl` passed 4/4 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. No actual QSL request or external award verification was performed.

This is a core increment, not a completed planner: there is no desktop UI, selected-goal persistence, route/channel grouping, user-declared or verified costs, route-age presentation, priority explanation, automatic invalidation or reuse of existing queues and label/email workflows. Current output includes all contributing QSO rather than a globally optimised selection; alternatives are a union across benefits and do not mean they replace every benefit of a multi-benefit QSO. The calculator's optional custom rule packages and DXCC validity callbacks are not yet injected into this planner; do not present its results as official or assume equivalence with a calculator configured with those inputs. Per-QSO lookups and the in-memory contribution map have not been benchmarked on large logs; cross-process writes, Windows/Linux and physical UI remain unverified. The complete G03 and all other desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G03 desktop potential plan and configured rules

The Awards window now opens a potential QSL plan, with multi-award selection, a background calculation, unique benefit and candidate counts, per-QSO benefits and accepted services, alternative QSO IDs, recorded pending services, manager and matching enrichment timestamp/source, and an explicit unknown cost. An operator can open a candidate's existing QSO detail. No request or transmission is made. The worker receives a copy of the same award calculator configuration used by the application, including local rule packages and the installed DXCC validity callbacks, and the current award filters. Application log/award-change notifications invalidate the displayed result and require rebuilding; a changed goal selection also hides the old result. This is a snapshot, and changes outside this application's notification paths are not detected automatically.

A/B: the configured-rules test initially failed to compile because the planning API could not accept the application's configured calculator; it now checks a locally defined award and an excluded DXCC entity and passes. In a separate offscreen desktop A/B, the previous Awards dialog could not perform the new planner probe; with the updated dialog, programmatically activating its open and build controls displays a synthetic K1PAA QSO with two distinct DXCC/WAZ benefits and its manager, and changing the award band marks the result stale. The probe inspected the rendered delegate text and status, not physical mouse or visual appearance. `tst_qslplanner` passed all five test cases, and focused CTest database, awards, qslplanner, qsl and QML popups passed 5/5 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2.

The interface is a first review surface, not the completed G03 workflow. It does not persist selected goals, automatically recalculate, group by manager/channel, optimise a set of requests, accept costs, warn based on route age, link to the existing queue/label/email/OQRS actions, or show individual alternatives per benefit. The displayed alternative list is the union of alternatives for its benefits and must not be interpreted as a full substitute for every benefit. An enrichment match does not prove present route validity. QSL notifications, stale results from external database writers, large-log memory/latency, shutdown during a long scan, physical keyboard/mouse, Windows/Linux and actual external services remain unverified. No official credit or success probability is asserted. G03 and the remaining desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G03 alternatives per benefit and paper queue link

Each candidate now lists alternatives separately for each award/item, so an alternative for one credit is not implied to cover the candidate's other credits. For each benefit it also identifies alternative QSO with a recorded sent-but-unconfirmed state on a service accepted by that award; an upload or sent status is not represented as proof that an actual request was made. The desktop shows matching route evidence and warns when its recorded observation is more than 180 days old; absent evidence remains unknown and the threshold is an advisory application rule, not a success estimate. A button opens the existing paper QSL queue; it does not select, enqueue or transmit any QSO.

A/B: the preceding planner exposed only a union of alternatives and could not provide per-benefit or sent/unconfirmed alternative information; the new test initially failed to compile because these data did not exist. The synthetic fixture confirms that one QSO has a single DXCC alternative but two WAZ alternatives, identifies the relevant sent paper-card alternative, and excludes an eQSL state not accepted by the selected award filter. The offscreen desktop test checks the rendered benefit-specific alternative, a dated synthetic manager enrichment and age warning, the status invalidation, and programmatically opens the existing paper queue. CTest database, awards, qslplanner, qsl and QML popups passed 5/5 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. No real service, mailing, mouse or keyboard operation was performed.

The 180-day threshold is currently fixed in code and observation provenance is only a matching stored enrichment, not proof of current routing. Actual request states beyond the existing service sent/received fields, individual queue handoff, grouped routes, editable or verified cost, persisted goals and automatic recomputation remain open. Contribution and alternative sets are materialised per QSO and may grow quadratically for very large sets of equivalent contacts; they have not been performance tested. External database writes, real award/route accuracy, other platforms and physical UI also remain unverified. G03 and the wider desktop roadmap remain incomplete.

## 2026-11-23 — G03 goal persistence and notified refresh

The selected award IDs are stored in the application's QSettings and restored on restart. Opening the QSL planner, changing its selection, or receiving an in-app log/award-change notification schedules a new snapshot calculation after a 500 ms debounce while the planner is visible; the old result remains hidden when stale. A clear button permits removing saved goals, including a local award that is no longer installed. Planning uses a dedicated single-worker pool, clears queued obsolete work and discards results from earlier generations; a running scan is not interrupted. On shutdown the app waits for that scan.

A/B on the same isolated app fixture: the preceding dialog left the existing plan stale after the relevant QSL workflow and failed the new confirmation/rebuild probe; the updated dialog records a synthetic received card via the existing card controller and recalculates from three to one distinct potential benefits. A subsequent band change invalidates and recalculates to zero. After the process exits, a second app launch restores the DXCC/WAZ selection and automatically rebuilds the plan. The fixture also checks the benefit details, route-age text and opening the existing paper queue. Focused CTest database, awards, qslplanner, qsl and QML popups passed 5/5 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. All records and UI actions were synthetic and programmatic; no actual QSL was sent or received.

External processes editing the database without application notifications do not trigger refresh. The saved goal list is application-wide rather than per-log or per-award-account, and uninstalled rule packages must be deselected or cleared. Long running scans are not cancellable and can delay shutdown; debounce, high-volume rapid updates, database replacement, large logs and notification delivery for every external confirmation service remain unverified. Grouping routes, costs, individual queue handoff, operational acceptance and remaining G03/desktop requirements stay open.

## 2026-11-23 — G03 recorded paper-route grouping

The planner now groups paper-eligible candidates by their recorded `QSL_VIA` text (case and whitespace normalised) and their explicitly recorded paper channel, bureau or direct. It lists member QSO IDs and counts distinct potential award/item benefits within each group. Candidates missing either value remain ungrouped and are counted separately. The view explicitly notes that benefits may overlap between groups, shows cost as unknown and does not enqueue or send anything. Matching text is not validation that it names a manager or that the route is current.

A/B: the previous planner had no route groups and the new test initially failed to compile. The synthetic test now checks two contacts sharing a manager and bureau route, a third with the same text but direct route, and contacts with a missing manager or channel; it verifies the two benefits are counted once within the shared group. The offscreen desktop probe displays the shared group and its QSO IDs, exercises automatic refresh after a synthetic confirmation, and reopens the saved selection after restart. CTest database, awards, qslplanner, qsl and QML popups passed 5/5 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. There was no external QSL request or physical UI interaction.

This grouping does not infer absent channels, parse or validate free-text manager values, establish current route validity, select optimal QSO, or assign an estimated or verified cost. It includes sent/unconfirmed candidates for review rather than assuming a request should be repeated. It does not group electronic channels or provide direct selected-group handoff to queues. Long-log performance, external source provenance, route accuracy, Windows/Linux and actual programme acceptance remain unverified. G03 and the complete desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G03 operator-supplied paper-route costs

A paper-route group can now store, edit or clear a non-negative decimal amount, three-letter currency code, and either an operator estimate or an amount accompanied by an operator-supplied source and observation date. The value is stored in the current log, keyed by normalised recorded QSL-via text and channel, and is read into the same planning snapshot. The UI labels the basis and explicitly says sources are not independently verified; missing cost remains unknown. It does not convert currencies, calculate a total or claim a price per QSO. The editor checks that the same log path is still open before saving or clearing.

A/B: before this increment the route-cost API and editor were absent and the new core fixture failed to compile. The updated test checks route/channel isolation, persistence after reopening, invalid negative values, missing sourced evidence, a forced SQLite write failure preserving the previous cost, and clearing. The offscreen desktop fixture enters and saves a synthetic EUR 12.50 estimate through the editor, checks its rendered label and persistence, then verifies confirmation-driven recalculation and restart. CTest database, awards, qslplanner, qsl and QML popups passed 5/5 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. No actual price, route, payment or external service was verified.

The three-letter format does not validate an ISO currency catalogue; a sourced entry is operator-provided evidence, not app verification or an endorsement that the price is current. Observation dates may become stale and there is no automatic refresh, exchange rate, cost aggregation, per-QSO cost, or audit history. The path guard does not detect replacement of a database file at the same path. Real price sources, concurrent edits, large-log performance, physical UI and other platforms remain unverified. G03 and all other incomplete desktop requirements remain active.

## 2026-11-23 — G03 explainable marginal coverage priority

The planner now assigns an advisory priority rank using a greedy coverage pass over distinct award/item benefits. Each step selects the QSO with the greatest number of benefits not covered by earlier selections; equal coverage favours QSO whose relevant accepted services are already marked sent and unconfirmed, then the lower local QSO ID. It records and displays the specific marginal benefits and the number already sent/unconfirmed. Other candidates remain visible as alternatives. The view explains the tie-break and that it does not rank cost or predict success; it remains in QSO-ID display order. A sent status is not proof that a request was actually made. No request or upload is initiated.

A/B: the previous planner had no marginal ranks and the new test failed to compile. The revised synthetic fixture checks that the first QSO covers two awards, an equivalent QSO adds no new coverage, and another QSO adds the remaining DXCC benefit. Marking the equivalent QSO's accepted card service sent/unconfirmed changes the tie-break to favour it without double-counting; an unaccepted eQSL status does not. The offscreen app test checks the alternative explanation and priority data alongside route cost, paper queue access, confirmation refresh and restart. CTest database, awards, qslplanner, qsl and QML popups passed 5/5 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2.

Greedy coverage is a heuristic and is not guaranteed to minimise requests, cost or time. The result is conditional on recorded QSO, current filters and award calculator rules; pending states and route details may be incomplete or inaccurate. Costs, channel availability, likelihood and physical feasibility do not affect the rank. The existing per-QSO alternative materialisation can grow quadratically; large-log performance, real services, physical UI and other platforms remain unverified. The full G03 workflow and all other outstanding desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G03 shared alternatives and paged desktop review

Alternative QSO IDs and their sent/unconfirmed subset are now held once per award/item and shared by its candidates, instead of copying and modifying a set per candidate. The desktop model carries a five-ID preview and counts; a separate dialog retrieves all alternatives in pages of 50 from the current in-memory plan, identifies sent/unconfirmed entries on an accepted service and opens an existing QSO detail. The dialog closes and the snapshot becomes inaccessible when the plan changes or becomes stale. The candidate and grouping models remain in memory and the scan itself is not cancellable.

A/B: a standalone Qt microbenchmark modelling the old per-candidate copied QSet versus a shared list, with 3,000 equivalent candidates, measured maximum RSS of 141,983,744 bytes and 12,926,976 bytes respectively on this macOS arm64 machine; elapsed times were 0.41 s and 0.01 s. These are one-off structure measurements, not end-to-end application or representative log benchmarks. A 125-QSO synthetic core fixture checks shared object identity, every alternative across page boundaries and exclusion of the current QSO. The offscreen desktop test navigates three pages, checks a sent/unconfirmed item on the second page and verifies invalidation on filter change. The earlier cost, routing, confirmation and restart desktop probe also passed. CTest database, awards, qslplanner, qsl and QML popups passed 5/5 using Qt 6.11.2.

The overall planner still materialises one row per candidate and contributions per applicable award/item, with per-QSO database reads; actual large-log memory, latency, concurrent writes and shutdown remain unbenchmarked. The microbenchmark does not establish application-wide performance. Paging and invalidation were programmatic offscreen tests, not physical interaction; external services, other platforms and the remaining G03/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G03 guarded single-QSO paper queue handoff

A candidate with a paper-accepted benefit can now open an explicit review dialog, choose bureau or direct and confirm adding that one QSO to the existing paper queue. Cancel leaves the queue alone; this action does not send a QSL. The planner carries the QSO UUID and revision from its snapshot. The queue worker checks the selected log path, UUID, revision, deletion status and current paper-QSL sent/received/channel state inside its transaction; already queued, sent, confirmed or conflicting-route records are not overwritten. The asynchronous card controller reports completion or rejection in its existing status. A pending sent state is still only recorded status, not proof of an actual request.

A/B: the previous card controller lacked the guarded planner entry point and the new test initially failed to compile. Synthetic tests cover an eligible QSO, repeat queue, already sent and received states, conflicting channel, wrong UUID/revision/log, file-backed worker and a concurrent transaction recording a sent status before the worker completes. Existing paper rollback, cache/log-switch and original-log/replacement-QSO email-result tests passed. The offscreen app fixture activates the candidate's review button, cancels once, reopens and confirms, observes the queue status, and checks the QSO's persisted `Q` state; it also repeats the existing cost, confirmation refresh, alternative pagination and restart probes. Focused CTest database, awards, qslplanner, qsl and QML popups passed 5/5 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. No real QSL, email or external service was used.

The selected route is a user choice, not verified QSL routing; the action queues one QSO, not an automatically optimised group. A changed or confirmed award benefit on another QSO is not recomputed atomically with queue insertion, and database replacement at the same path and arbitrary direct SQL edits bypassing revisions remain unverified. The concurrency fixture exercises one controlled ordering, not all SQLite races. Physical UI, real services, large logs and Windows/Linux remain open, as do the other G03 and desktop roadmap requirements.

## 2026-11-23 — G04 local campaign and period matrix core

A new core stores independent campaign records in the current log's `app_setting` table under UUID-based keys, with a title, one or more calls, inclusive UTC operating dates, source and reported observation time, chosen band/mode matrix, optional station profile, archive flag and optimistic revision. Existing schema and QSO are unchanged. Updates use a compare-and-swap on the stored JSON so a stale editor cannot silently replace a newer version. The matrix counts matching exact calls and QSO dates within the campaign period, optionally scoped to a station profile, and marks locally recorded LoTW/card confirmations. These counts are local observations, not verified award eligibility; calls and dates are operator/source assertions, and overlapping campaigns can count the same QSO.

A/B: the previous build had no `tst_dxcampaign` target or campaign core; the new synthetic file-backed test creates two separate periods sharing a callsign, includes a second callsign, checks an out-of-period QSO and inclusive midnight boundaries, records a later confirmation, changes dates, rejects a stale revision, archives/reopens and reloads after closing the log. Another fixture checks two station profiles and preserves stored data on invalid duplicate calls. CTest database, awards, dxcampaign, dxassistant and dxcalendarcache passed 5/5 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. No existing user log, calendar feed or external award service was modified or verified.

This is backend foundation only: there is no desktop campaign UI, linkage to calendar events, editing workflow, operator goal selection, fresh spot filtering, DX Assistant ranking integration, watchlist or alerts. A campaign is stored locally with its log; cloud synchronization has not been implemented or tested. Matrix computation is synchronous and has not been benchmarked on large logs; malformed/non-UTC QSO timestamps are skipped, and confirmation policy currently counts stored LoTW/card states only. The source and observation date are reported metadata, not authenticated or independently verified. G04, all other incomplete desktop streams and G13 remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G04 calendar-to-campaign desktop workflow

The DXpedition calendar now offers a campaign editor and a per-announcement “Plan campaign” action. It pre-fills the announced entity, call, inclusive dates and feed URL, using the cache/download observation timestamp when available; these are editable reported data and do not establish independent verification. The operator can edit multiple calls, dates, source/time, bands, modes and station-profile scope, save with the core's revision guard, archive or reopen, and select saved campaigns. The worked/confirmed/missing matrix runs on a separate worker connection; notified log changes trigger a fresh calculation. A changed log path invalidates the selection, and the editor refuses to save into a different open path.

A/B: the prior calendar panel lacked the campaign action and could not perform the new synthetic desktop probe. The updated offscreen app opens a synthetic calendar row's action, reviews and saves two calls, shows worked and locally confirmed cells, archives and reopens, records another synthetic card confirmation and observes matrix refresh, then restarts and loads the saved campaign and rendered confirmed cell. CTest database, awards, dxcampaign, dxassistant, dxcalendar, dxcalendarcache and qmlpopups passed 7/7 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2; the calendar parser/worker tests retained their existing synthetic feed. The probe used programmatic controls, not physical interaction or a live DXpedition feed.

Campaign creation from an announcement is an editable copy, not a durable source-event ID or an automatic date-update link; the current feed parser does not expose stable event identity. Dates from later announcements require manual review. The initial matrix is a local exact-call/time count using stored LoTW/card confirmation states, not official eligibility or protection from overlapping same-call periods. Source timestamps may be absent or stale, and no authenticity is implied. Spot freshness, DX Assistant ranking, watchlist/alerts, richer objectives, sync, external database writes, large-log latency, physical UI and Windows/Linux remain unverified or unimplemented. Running scans are not cancellable and may delay shutdown. G04 and all remaining desktop roadmap goals remain active.

## 2026-11-23 — G04 explicit calendar date review

Each calendar entry now offers a date-review dialog. The operator explicitly chooses an existing campaign, compares its calls, current period and source with the announcement, feed source and observation time, and may cancel or apply the proposed dates. Applying changes only the chosen campaign's dates and reported source/observation; it retains calls, other settings and all QSO. The existing revision compare-and-swap rejects a campaign changed after selection. The matrix is recalculated after an accepted update. No association is inferred from a shared callsign.

On 2026-11-23 a direct HTTPS observation of the configured [DXCAL feed](https://www.danplanet.com/dxcal.ics) returned 12,827 bytes containing 57 VEVENT records and zero UID properties after unfolding; SHA-256 was `1ac79b4883e678f91fc80cb4adb7c82890a239a66eb7f8a74c2450b10c126d56`. This is one observed feed response, not a promise about later responses or feed authenticity. Without a stable event identifier, this increment deliberately requires manual selection and does not claim a durable source-event link or automatically match date changes.

A/B: the previous calendar lacked this review path and failed the new offscreen probe; the revised probe compares a synthetic announcement against two saved campaigns sharing the same callsign, cancels once, demonstrates rejection of a stale revision, then reselects and applies the dates to only the intended campaign. It checks the updated matrix, unchanged second campaign and preserved QSO. The existing calendar-to-campaign/restart fixture also passed. CTest database, awards, dxcampaign, dxassistant, dxcalendar, dxcalendarcache and qmlpopups passed 7/7 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. All UI actions and campaign/QSO records were synthetic and programmatic; only the separate feed observation used live network data.

There is still no stable source-event identity, automatic update detection, authenticated source, automatic propagation or cross-device sync. Manual selection can associate the wrong event if the operator chooses incorrectly; the displayed comparison is a review aid. Feed timestamps are retrieval times, not proof of operational dates. External database replacement, real user-log migration, physical UI, Windows/Linux and all other G04 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G04 fresh campaign spots and DX Assistant ranking

The saved-campaign view now lists cluster-visible spots whose exact reported call, UTC spot date, band and specific mode match the campaign and whose timestamp is no more than 30 minutes old; it rejects missing/local-time/future timestamps, ambiguous generic data modes, archived campaigns and calls with unlisted portable suffixes. USB/LSB are matched to SSB when no exact mode is selected; FM remains distinct. The same distinction now applies to matrix cells while preserving exact selection for existing campaigns containing both SSB and USB. The view refreshes on cluster changes and every five seconds, shows the observation time, source, score, reasons and limitations, and uses the existing DX Assistant ranking and cluster filters. It performs no tuning, QSO or transmit action.

A/B: the preceding campaign core had no fresh-spot filter and the new test initially failed to compile. Synthetic core cases cover the inclusive freshness boundary, old, future, outside-period, unknown-mode, wrong-band/call and archived cases, plus USB/SSB versus FM. A cluster-controller test passes only its visible spots to the unchanged ranking policy, checks rank order and existing band filtering; the existing cluster/Decodium-protocol alert test also passed. The offscreen app injects synthetic cluster lines, displays the matching ranked 3Y0J spot, excludes an unrelated call and removes the spot after changing the cluster band filter. CTest database, awards, dxcampaign, dxassistant, dxcalendar, dxcalendarcache and qmlpopups passed 7/7 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2.

The rank uses the cluster's existing global log index and assistant preferences, not campaign-profile-specific award credit; the UI says so. Spot sources, operating dates and on-air identity are not independently verified, and aggregation may reflect the latest report for a call/band/mode. Visible data can remain on screen for up to the five-second refresh interval after ageing out. The view does not maintain a campaign watchlist or alerts, choose user award objectives, automatically tune, synchronize campaigns or prove external/real-radio interoperability. Actual feed, real cluster/RF, large-spot performance, other platforms and remaining desktop goals remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G04 opt-in watchlist and activity alerts

Campaigns now persist an optional watch flag, defaulting to off for existing records. The editor exposes “Watch spots” and marks watched campaigns in the saved list. For newly received reports passing the cluster's effective filter, watched, non-archived campaigns add a local activity entry only when the exact call, band, specific mode, UTC operating period, 30-minute freshness and active station profile match; campaigns scoped to all profiles use the current profile without restriction. Repeated campaign/call/band/mode alerts are suppressed for ten minutes in the current process. The alert does not tune, transmit, send messages, or change QSO. The watched campaign revision is rechecked against the log before emitting; a changed record refreshes the cache and waits for a later report.

A/B: the previous controller produced zero campaign activity entries in the synthetic two-profile app fixture; the updated controller produced the two expected entries, once per matching profile. The fixture also injects a repeated spot, a filtered-out band, a non-watched campaign and an archived campaign, switches profiles, and repeats after app restart. Another desktop probe verifies that date review and an update omitting the new field preserve an existing watch selection. Core tests cover opt-in, active period/profile, archive and old stored records without the flag; a cluster test checks the effective band filter. Existing cluster/Decodium-protocol alert behaviour passed, as did CTest database, awards, dxcampaign, dxassistant, dxcalendar, dxcalendarcache and qmlpopups 7/7 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. All app spots were synthetic injections, not live cluster, RF or two real Decodium sessions.

Alerts are activity entries only, not system notifications or sound, and source/call identity is not independently verified. The ten-minute cooldown is process-local and resets on restart; at 4,096 simultaneous recent keys additional new alerts are suppressed until old keys expire. External changes to an already cached watched record are detected when it next matches, causing that report to be skipped and the cache reloaded; newly added watches in another process are not automatically discovered. Direct database edits bypassing campaign revisions, database replacement at the same path, high-rate performance and all race orderings remain unverified. Watchlist sync, campaign-specific award objectives, physical UI, real cluster/RF and the other desktop goals remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G04 per-cell campaign objectives

Campaigns persist explicit per-band/mode objectives to work or confirm a contact. The desktop matrix lets the operator choose each target, save it, and see whether it remains open using the campaign's existing local QSO and LoTW/card counts. Saved goals outside edited band/mode lists are retained and flagged, rather than silently discarded; existing campaigns without goals load empty. Saving through an older caller that omits goals preserves them.

A/B: before this increment the new goal API and fields did not compile; afterwards synthetic core tests passed, distinguishing a worked QSO from a confirmation, rejecting duplicate goals and reopening the stored record. The offscreen app selected a confirmation goal in the actual matrix control, saved it, observed it open, archived and reopened the campaign, recorded a synthetic card confirmation, observed the goal close, and restarted to check persistence. CTest database, awards, dxcampaign, dxassistant and dxcalendarcache passed 5/5 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. UI actions and QSO were synthetic and programmatic.

Goals currently annotate the matrix only: spot ranking and watch alerts do not yet consider them. Counts are local observations, not official award credit; overlapping campaign calls/periods may count the same QSO. No live cluster, RF, external award service, physical interaction or Windows/Linux verification was performed. G04 and the other desktop roadmap milestones remain active.

## 2026-11-23 — G04 goal-aware spot ordering

The saved campaign spot list now places open local per-cell objectives first, cells without a selected objective next, and met objectives last; the existing DX Assistant ordering is stable within each group. Rows identify an open or met target. The matrix's exact-mode-first sideband mapping is shared for spot-to-cell selection. When the matrix is loading, unavailable or failed, the existing assistant ordering is retained without asserting goal status.

A/B: the pre-change offscreen fixture received both synthetic spots but emitted undefined goal state and failed the new goal-status assertion. The updated fixture starts with a worked 20m objective and an open 40m objective, injects both reports, observes the open target first and the met target second, and verifies the existing cluster-band filter still removes them. Core tests cover exact USB/LSB selection and SSB fallback. CTest database, awards, dxcampaign, dxassistant, dxcalendarcache and qmlpopups passed 6/6 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. UI interaction and cluster reports were synthetic and programmatic.

This ordering uses the last completed local matrix, which is refreshed by application events; arbitrary external database edits and all races are not verified. Local QSO and card/LoTW states do not establish official award credit. Watch alerts still include all matching campaign cells. No live cluster, RF, official service, physical UI or Windows/Linux interoperability was tested. G04 and the other desktop roadmap milestones remain active.

## 2026-11-23 — G04 optional alerts for open objectives

A watched campaign can opt into alerts only for explicitly selected, locally open band/mode goals. Existing records default to the previous all-matching-cells behaviour; older update callers that omit the new flag preserve it. Cells with no selected goal do not alert in this mode. The cell scan runs in a dedicated single-worker pool. Before emitting, the application rechecks the current effective cluster filter, profile, freshness, campaign revision and its local log-change generation. Duplicate in-flight spot keys are coalesced, recently alerted keys bypass the scan, and at most 128 scans may be pending. The existing ten-minute alert cooldown remains in force.

A/B: the pre-change campaign core lacked the flag and goal-watch evaluator, so the new test failed to compile. Afterwards the synthetic core test verified legacy default, persistence, open versus met goals and exact USB versus SSB fallback. An offscreen app fixture with worked, unselected and confirmation goals emitted only one alert for the open goal, suppressed repeats and the met/unselected cells, recorded a synthetic card confirmation, suppressed a different matching call thereafter, and emitted no alert after restart. Existing two-profile/filter/restart alert and campaign editor/restart scenarios passed, as did date review including an update omitting the flag. CTest database, awards, dxcampaign, dxassistant, dxcalendarcache and qmlpopups passed 6/6 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. UI actions and cluster spots were synthetic and programmatic.

Each eligible goal-only report may scan the campaign QSO matrix; large-log latency and high-rate behaviour have not been measured. When the 128-request limit is reached, or the database scan fails, affected reports are skipped rather than queued indefinitely; reports invalidated while a scan runs are also skipped and require a later report. Shutdown waits for a running scan. Direct changes from another process that do not generate this process's log-change signal can race a scan. The activity alert is not a system notification, and local confirmations are not official award credit. Live cluster/RF, physical UI, other platforms and other desktop roadmap work remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G04 focused goal-alert lookup

Goal-only alert workers now evaluate the matching cell directly and stop when an eligible worked or locally confirmed QSO is found, rather than constructing and counting the complete campaign matrix for each spot. The query retains the same calls, period, profile, deleted-record, mode and local confirmation rules. The existing asynchronous queue, revalidation, limits and alert behaviour are unchanged.

A/B: the new focused API did not exist before the change. A synthetic in-memory log explicitly checked to contain 4,000 QSO compares the focused answer with the full matrix for worked, open confirmation, confirmed, absent goal, different station profile and USB/LSB/SSB cases. On one macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 run, ten full-matrix checks for a worked goal took 24,775 microseconds versus 140 microseconds for focused checks; for an open confirmation the figures were 24,872 versus 4,876 microseconds. These are local in-process measurements on one synthetic dataset, not throughput or user-facing latency guarantees. The actual offscreen application again produced and suppressed goal-only alerts as expected. CTest database, awards, dxcampaign, dxassistant, dxcalendarcache and qmlpopups passed 6/6.

Worst-case scans can still traverse many QSO; database size, disk I/O, concurrency, cross-process edits, shutdown delay and high-rate queue saturation remain unmeasured. The existing 128-request limit can skip reports and real cluster/RF and physical UI were not tested. Other G04 and desktop roadmap work remains open.

## 2026-11-23 — G12 reviewed UTC date/time candidate search

The OCR review dialog now accepts a manually corrected callsign and optional UTC date and minute, or an explicit selection from OCR date/time suggestions. Manual date/time entries override the corresponding suggestion; ambiguous suggestions are not selected automatically. The exact-call query applies the chosen criteria before its existing 100-result limit and reports the criteria used. Band and mode remain review suggestions and do not filter. Search still runs asynchronously, supports cancellation, and neither changes QSO nor marks confirmations. An image can still be attached only after explicit QSO selection and approval.

A/B: the previous backend lacked a date/time search and the new test initially failed to compile. The new file-backed fixture has 110 same-call QSO: a selected older date/minute finds its single matching QSO even though the unfiltered most-recent limit would exclude it; another date/time produces 100 results and truncation, invalid dates/times are rejected, and an unmatched combination returns none. In the actual offscreen app Tesseract read a synthetic QSL, the operator controls selected the suggested UTC date/time, and a manual date override selected a different same-call QSO; no QSO or attachment was changed. Existing unfiltered match, no-match and explicit attachment to two QSO scenarios passed. CTest tesseractocr, database, migration, qsl, qslcards and qmlpopups passed 6/6 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. UI operations were programmatic.

Filtering is exact to the UTC minute: uncertain or incorrectly interpreted image times can exclude the intended contact, so filters remain optional and are displayed with results. SQLite date functions interpret stored timestamps and skip values they cannot parse; unusual legacy timestamps and external writers have not been comprehensively verified. Candidate search does not rank similar calls, infer tolerances, use band/mode as filters or save a reviewed transcription. Large-log query latency, physical interaction, real cards, Windows/Linux and the remaining G12 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G12 saved operator transcription

Acquisition history now offers an optional editable operator-reviewed transcription, separately from the retained raw OCR text and original image. Saving is explicit, asynchronous and cancellable, appends a timestamped revision, and uses the acquisition UUID and latest review ID to reject a stale editor. A review cannot be added to an undone acquisition. A migration adds an indexed review table at schema 23 without rewriting earlier acquisition records. The interface displays the latest review; a truncated raw OCR preview is not silently used to seed a review. The action does not update the QSO or any QSL/award status.

A/B: the previous acquisition API had no review-save operation and the new test initially failed to compile. Synthetic file-backed tests append two corrections, preserve the original OCR, reject a stale revision, wrong acquisition, empty text and cancellation, and exercise existing guarded undo. A schema-22 fixture migrates with its original acquisition text intact and no fabricated review. The offscreen app saves a corrected transcription, reloads it in history, restarts and retrieves it again while retaining the image, QSO and unconfirmed state. CTest database, migration, tesseractocr, qsl, qslcards and qmlpopups passed 6/6 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. UI actions were programmatic and the card synthetic.

Earlier reviewed revisions are retained in the database but the desktop currently exposes only the latest; no author identity, structured field-level correction or export is provided. Reviews are limited to 65,536 characters. Database replacement at the same path, all cross-process race orders, cancellation during commit, physical UI, real cards and other platforms remain unverified. Schema migration should be exercised on a copy of any valuable real log; the remaining G12 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G12 reviewed-transcription history

The acquisition history dialog now opens a read-only, paginated list of saved operator transcriptions, newest first, with timestamp and text. Selecting an earlier revision does not overwrite the current editor or alter a QSO; creating another correction remains an explicit separate save. The worker verifies the acquisition ID and UUID, reads up to 21 rows to show 20 and detect the next page, supports cancellation and rejects results after a log-path change. Individual displayed revisions are bounded to 65,536 characters and a larger externally written value is labelled truncated.

A/B: before this increment there was no review-history reader, and the new test failed to compile. Synthetic file-backed tests read two revisions in order, reject a wrong acquisition UUID, and navigate 24 revisions across two pages without losing the oldest. The offscreen application saved and reopened a review, added another correction, opened the new history viewer and observed both texts in newest-first order. CTest database, migration, tesseractocr, qsl, qslcards and qmlpopups passed 6/6 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. UI actions were programmatic; no real card was used.

The viewer is read-only and does not compare or restore versions automatically. Individual reviewers are not identified. Database replacement at the same path, all concurrent external changes, large-volume history, physical UI and Windows/Linux remain unverified; other G12 and desktop roadmap requirements remain active.

## 2026-11-23 — G07 typed New QSO custom fields

The existing custom ADIF field settings can now declare text, number, date, boolean or list, an optional unit and list choices. In manual New QSO entry, configured non-empty values are checked before saving: numbers use the current Qt locale with group separators rejected, dates use YYYY-MM-DD, booleans use Y/N and lists use an exact declared choice. The field still stores the operator's entered ADIF text. Existing name protection and legacy text definitions remain in place; configuration without a type continues to mean text. The settings editor and New QSO labels expose the new metadata and format hints.

A/B: the prior core lacked typed definition/value validation, so the new test initially failed to compile. Synthetic tests exercise all five types, Italian decimal comma, invalid dates and values, duplicate choices, legacy settings and a local ADIF export/import retaining `12,5`. The offscreen desktop settings editor configured a numeric field and unit, rejected an invalid value before writing, saved a valid value and exported it as ADIF. CTest adif, database, newqsofields, newqsotuning and qmlpopups passed 5/5 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, followed by the expanded typed-field unit test. UI actions were programmatic.

This increment applies validation to configured custom fields in manual New QSO entry only; import, other QSO editors, filters, award packages and existing stored values are not retroactively validated. Types, units and choices live in application settings, not in the ADIF export or per-log schema. A locale change can change how a previously entered numeric string is interpreted; no canonical numeric value or locale provenance is recorded yet. Choices are comma-separated in this first editor and cannot contain commas. Conditional requirements, deterministic formulas, reports, physical UI, other platforms and the remaining G07 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G07 conditional custom-field requirements

A configured custom field can now be required when a different configured custom field exactly equals a specified non-empty value. Both names and values are reviewed in the settings editor; invalid, self-referential or missing dependencies are rejected. The New QSO panel displays the condition, and validation checks all configured definitions even when the dependent value is absent from the submitted map. Existing definitions without a condition remain optional, and the original ADIF value is preserved.

A/B: the prior implementation had no conditional validation API, so the new core test initially failed to compile. Synthetic tests cover matching and nonmatching values, absent fields, case-normalized field names, settings serialization and invalid references. In the offscreen app, a numeric field value of 12 required a second custom field; saving without it failed, and supplying it created one QSO containing both values. Invalid numeric input still failed. CTest adif, database, newqsofields, newqsotuning and qmlpopups passed 5/5 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, plus the focused app scenario. UI interactions were programmatic.

Conditions currently compare trimmed text exactly and refer only to configured custom fields; they do not evaluate formulas, ranges, standard fields or normalized numeric equivalence. Mutual conditions are possible and do not execute recursively. Validation applies to manual New QSO entry, not imports, other editors or existing records; settings remain application-wide rather than per-log. Physical UI, other platforms and the other G07 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G07 recorded station activity report

Statistics now includes an asynchronous report grouping live QSO by their own recorded `MY_ANTENNA`, band and `MY_GRIDSQUARE`, optionally filtered by UTC year and band. Blank or malformed per-QSO metadata stays unrecorded; current station-profile values are not inferred. The report is one read-only SQLite SELECT, labels its generated snapshot and filters, and can save one set of filters in the current log. The complete result can be exported as quoted UTF-8 CSV; reports exceeding 10,000 groups are marked truncated and cannot be exported. Export refuses the current database and its conventional SQLite sidecar paths.

A/B: before the increment no activity-report test target or report existed. Synthetic core tests independently expect counts and CSV quoting, cover missing/malformed metadata, deleted QSO and 10,001 groups. A file-backed WAL fixture pins a read snapshot, inserts a QSO through another connection, observes the old count inside the snapshot and the new count after commit. The offscreen application displays the expected grouped row, saves filters in the log, exports the expected CSV, and rejects export over the database. CTest activityreport, adif, database, newqsofields, newqsotuning and qmlpopups passed 6/6 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. UI interactions and data were synthetic and programmatic.

This is one fixed report and one saved filter set, not the general report designer. It does not support custom dimensions, operator/club groupings, confirmation turnaround, PDF, arbitrary saved report definitions, or infer missing historic equipment or location. The snapshot test uses an explicitly pinned SQLite transaction and controlled write ordering; large-log runtime under sustained concurrent insertions and all race orders remain unmeasured. An already displayed snapshot is not automatically invalidated by later edits. Same-path database replacement, filesystem alias edge cases, spreadsheet interpretation of CSV cells, physical UI, other platforms and remaining G07 and desktop goals remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G07 observed confirmation-time report

The report view now also groups recorded `rcvd=Y` states by service and counts usable receipt dates, missing dates, invalid dates and receipt dates earlier than the UTC QSO day. Its mean uses only valid non-negative elapsed calendar days; a group without usable dates has no mean. A QSO may appear under more than one service. Year and band filter the QSO, not the receipt date. The selected report kind joins the saved per-log filter set, and older activity-only saved filters load as the activity report. Preview and CSV identify the separate counts; the report reads local recorded states and does not assert official award credit.

A/B: the preceding report had no confirmation-time evaluator; the new test initially failed to compile. Synthetic fixtures independently verify a two-day sample mean of one day, another service with ten days, missing/invalid/earlier dates, a service without a computable mean, an unconfirmed status, and exclusion by year and band. The offscreen application renders a dated and undated card-confirmation group, saves its report choice and writes the expected CSV. The prior station activity report and legacy saved-filter compatibility also passed. CTest activityreport, adif, database, newqsofields, newqsotuning and qmlpopups passed 6/6 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. UI interactions and records were synthetic and programmatic.

Receipt dates may reflect service sync or manually entered data rather than actual delivery; no median, uncertainty interval, per-QSO detail, independently verified dates, eligibility decision or official status is inferred. The scan is not cancellable, may hold a read snapshot and delay shutdown on a very large log, and has not been measured under sustained writes. The current report UI saves one filter set and offers CSV only; club activity, general report building, PDF, external service interoperability, physical UI and other platforms remain open, along with the remaining desktop roadmap.

## 2026-11-23 — G07 annual recorded club-station activity

The report view now offers annual activity grouped by UTC year, the QSO's recorded station callsign and recorded operator. An optional station filter allows the operator to select a club callsign; an empty filter includes all recorded stations. Missing station/operator values are shown as unrecorded rather than filled from current profiles. The report runs through the existing asynchronous read-snapshot path, can save its filters in the log and exports a complete result as quoted CSV. The interface explicitly states that recorded calls do not prove club membership.

A/B: the preceding report core had no annual club evaluator; the new test initially failed to compile. A synthetic fixture checks two QSO for one operator, a missing operator, a different year and an unrelated station, and compares the group counts to the known input. The offscreen app filters the recorded club station, renders the expected year/station/operator/count row, saves the report and station filters, and exports the expected CSV. Existing station activity, confirmation-time and legacy saved-filter scenarios also passed. CTest activityreport, adif, database, newqsofields, newqsotuning and qmlpopups passed 6/6 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. UI actions and QSO were synthetic and programmatic.

Callsigns are reported from QSO metadata, not verified membership or station ownership; casing differences in stored values can appear as separate groups, even though the station filter ignores case and surrounding whitespace. Other historical station calls require separate review or an unfiltered report. The fixed report is not a general report designer, offers CSV rather than PDF, and does not provide custom dimensions or aggregates. Large-log latency, physical UI, other platforms and the remaining G07 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G07 PDF export for fixed reports

The three existing report previews can now be exported to a paginated A4 landscape PDF. The exported document uses the already calculated snapshot and identifies its report type, UTC filters, generation time and applicable limitations. Page headers and numbering repeat; long cells are abbreviated at 160 characters and line breaks are flattened, with the PDF directing readers to CSV for complete values. Export runs on the existing background export pool, writes through QSaveFile, waits for accepted exports at shutdown, rejects incomplete/truncated reports and protects the active database and conventional SQLite sidecars as destinations.

A/B: the previous app had no PDF export probe; the new multipage desktop test failed before implementation. The updated offscreen application exports each of the three synthetic report types; the 100-group activity fixture produces a five-page PDF, includes the last group, repeats headers and rejects using the database as the PDF destination. Poppler `pdfinfo` and `pdftotext` checked page count and extracted text; rendered first and last pages of the multipage output and first pages of the confirmation and club PDFs were visually inspected, including a wrapped/abbreviated long cell. CTest activityreport, adif, database, newqsofields, newqsotuning and qmlpopups passed 6/6 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. UI controls and records were synthetic and programmatic.

PDF labels are currently English and cell shortening is intentional; CSV retains the full data. No printer, physical UI, Windows/Linux, unusual fonts/complex scripts, very large PDF, disk-full or shutdown-during-export scenario was tested. This is PDF output for three fixed reports, not a general report designer; the remaining G07 and desktop roadmap requirements stay open.

## 2026-11-23 — G07 configurable QSO report

The report view now supports one to three distinct grouping dimensions selected from UTC year, band, mode/submode, recorded station, operator, own grid, antenna and configured custom ADIF fields. Aggregation is either QSO count or distinct non-empty recorded calls. Year and band filters, the single saved per-log report definition, asynchronous read-snapshot execution, the 10,000-group limit, and CSV/PDF export are reused. Saved custom field names remain selectable on reload even if the field is subsequently removed from the New QSO panel. Custom ADIF names are validated and their SQLite JSON paths are bound parameters; arbitrary SQL or expressions are not accepted.

A/B: the previous report core lacked configurable grouping and the new test initially failed to compile. Synthetic tests compare known QSO and distinct-call counts, exercise three dimensions including two separately bound ADIF paths, missing/malformed metadata, invalid/duplicate dimensions, rejected SQL-like input, filtered results and 10,001-group export refusal. A pinned WAL read snapshot returned the pre-insert count while another connection inserted a QSO, then returned the new count after commit. The offscreen application grouped a configured custom field, saved the definition, exported expected CSV and PDF, and after restart restored and reran it even after removing the field's panel configuration. The three fixed report scenarios also passed. Poppler text extraction and a rendered custom PDF page were inspected. CTest activityreport, adif, database, newqsofields, newqsotuning and qmlpopups passed 6/6 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. UI operations and records were synthetic and programmatic.

Only counts and distinct calls are available, with year/band filters and one saved definition per log; there is no named-report library, arbitrary predicates, sums/averages of typed fields or derived expressions. Distinct calls and groups use stored text and SQLite comparison, not callsign alias or award eligibility logic. Custom fields are grouped as ADIF text without locale-aware numeric conversion or inferred missing values. PDF labels remain English and long cells are abbreviated; report scans and large PDF exports are not cancellable. Sustained concurrent inserts, large real logs, physical UI, other platforms and the remaining G07 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.
