## 2026-11-23 — G07 named report definitions

The report window now maintains multiple named definitions in the current log. An operator can save a new copy, load a definition, update the explicitly loaded revision or remove a selected definition after an in-app confirmation. Each saved record has an independent UUID and revision; updates and removals compare the previously read value so stale changes are rejected. The existing single saved-filter key is left intact and its prior controls remain available. At most 1,000 named records can be created through this path; invalid stored records produce a visible list error rather than being silently replaced.

A/B: the prior core had no named-definition API, so the new test initially failed to compile. File-backed tests create independent custom and club definitions, edit one through a second revision, reject stale updates and removals from another connection, preserve the legacy filter key, reject invalid definitions and surface corrupt saved records. An atomic bounded-insert test checks the 1,000-record limit. The offscreen app creates two named reports, restarts, loads and runs one with its stored dimensions, explicitly confirms removal of the other, and retains the original legacy filter and QSO. The existing custom report, CSV and PDF scenario also passed. CTest activityreport, adif, database, newqsofields, newqsotuning and qmlpopups passed 6/6 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. UI actions were programmatic and data synthetic.

Named definitions currently cover only the existing four report kinds and their limited filters/aggregations. They are local to a log; sync, import/export of definitions, recovery UI for corrupt records, same-path database replacement and all concurrent writer orderings remain unverified. Physical UI, Windows/Linux, richer expressions and the remaining G07 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G07 bounded derived numeric fields

A numeric custom field can now define a bounded arithmetic formula using numeric constants, `{CUSTOM_FIELD}` references, parentheses and `+ - * /`. Configuration validation rejects unsupported syntax, nonnumeric/unknown references, self-reference and dependency cycles. Evaluation follows dependencies deterministically; a missing input leaves dependent values empty, while division by zero and non-finite results prevent logging. Input numbers use the current Qt locale with grouping disabled; computed values use C-locale decimal text with 15 significant digits. The New QSO panel displays the formula and makes the computed field read-only. Explicitly supplied derived values are rejected.

When at least one result is computed, the QSO stores its original custom inputs, computed values and `APP_DECOLOG_DERIVED_V1` metadata containing each applied formula, unit, input locale and output format. A user-supplied value colliding with that metadata field is rejected for that QSO instead of being overwritten. The metadata is stored as ADIF extra data and travels in the local ADIF export/import; it is not an authenticated statement.

A/B: the prior core had no derived-value API and the new test initially failed to compile. Synthetic tests cover dependent formulas, precedence, unary minus, Italian decimal comma, canonical decimal output, missing inputs, conditional requiredness, supplied derived values, unknown fields, cycles, excessive nesting, unsupported syntax and division by zero. The QML test checks the derived input is read-only and not submitted as manual input. The offscreen application configures a numeric formula, rejects an attempted manual result and a provenance-field collision, saves the computed value and source fields, and round-trips the formula metadata through ADIF. CTest activityreport, adif, database, newqsofields, newqsotuning and qmlpopups passed 6/6 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, plus the focused app scenario. UI operations were programmatic.

Formulas currently reference numeric custom fields only and support arithmetic rather than arbitrary functions, unit conversion or standard ADIF fields. They run only during manual New QSO entry; edits through other paths do not automatically recompute results or update provenance, and imported or externally edited metadata is not authenticated. Existing results are not rewritten when a formula or locale changes. Complex floating-point precision analysis, all possible malformed-input combinations, physical UI, Windows/Linux and remaining G07 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G07 derived-field staleness review

New calculated QSO now record version-2 provenance under the existing ADIF extra field: each computed result includes its formula, saved output and exact input values in addition to locale and output format. The QSO detail editor compares current values with that recorded snapshot as the user edits and after reload. It warns when an input or output differs, propagating the warning to derived fields that depend on an affected result; older version-1 or malformed provenance is labelled uncheckable. Saving an edited QSO still preserves the operator's values and the recorded provenance; it does not silently recalculate or block ordinary edits. A matching snapshot is a local consistency check, not authentication or independent mathematical verification.

A/B: the preceding core lacked a provenance inspection API and the new test initially failed to compile. Synthetic core tests cover unchanged and unrelated fields, changed inputs and outputs, transitive dependencies, malformed/incomplete/cyclic metadata and older provenance. The offscreen QSO editor shows the warning before saving a changed input, preserves both derived values and the original trace, and shows the warning again after restarting. A fresh manual entry records version-2 inputs and preserves the full metadata through ADIF export/import. The existing OCR-history undo scenario passed, and CTest activityreport, adif, database, qsl, qslcontroller, newqsofields, newqsotuning and qmlpopups passed 8/8 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. UI actions and QSO were synthetic and programmatic.

This does not automatically recompute or repair stale values, authenticate provenance, or detect edits that remove/forge the provenance field. Version-1 traces cannot establish whether inputs changed and require manual review. Other editor, import, cloud and award paths do not universally display the warning; in-place log changes are noticed when the detail is inspected. Physical interaction, large/adversarial logs, other platforms and the remaining G07 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G07 explicit derived-value recalculation

QSO detail now offers a preview of recalculated derived values using the formulas, units and numeric input locale recorded with that QSO, rather than the current global field settings. The preview shows each old and proposed value and expression. Cancel makes no change; explicit application changes the draft only, and the operator must separately save the QSO. Application is refused if the QSO revision or draft changes while the preview is open. A successful calculation updates the provenance snapshot with the current inputs and computed results. Missing inputs, unsupported/legacy provenance, unknown numeric locale and arithmetic errors are reported without writing; the QSO history retains the previous record on save.

A/B: the previous core had no recalculation preview API and the new unit test initially failed to compile. Synthetic core tests use Italian decimal input and recorded locale, check dependent results, verify preview leaves input values untouched, and reject missing inputs, legacy provenance, invalid locale and division by zero. The offscreen QSO editor previews two changed results, cancels without changing the database, rejects a changed draft and a separately edited QSO revision, applies a fresh preview to the draft while the database still holds the old result, then saves and checks the new values, trace and previous history. A captured preview's text and controls were visually inspected. Existing manual-field and OCR-history undo scenarios passed. CTest activityreport, adif, database, qsl, qslcontroller, newqsofields, newqsotuning and qmlpopups passed 8/8 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. UI operations and QSO were synthetic and programmatic.

This is an explicit recalculation of version-2 recorded formulas, not authentication of imported provenance or an automatic update of every editor/import/cloud/award path. The revision check happens when applying the preview to the draft; the existing later QSO save is not an atomic compare-and-swap, so another writer between application and save remains a race. Version-1 traces require manual review. Physical interaction, other platforms, large/adversarial metadata and the remaining G07 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G07 guarded recalculation save

After applying a derived-value preview, the QSO editor now saves through a guarded transaction: the log path, QSO UUID and revision must still match. The database acquires a SQLite write lock before checking the QSO and merging current untouched QSL fields, then writes history, the QSO and its QSL state in the same transaction. A conflict leaves the draft available and asks the operator to reload and review. Explicitly touched QSL fields remain operator-controlled. Ordinary QSO edits continue through their existing path. This closes the apply-to-save race described in the preceding entry for the guarded path.

A/B: an offscreen QSO-editor regression reproduced the earlier defect: a separate QSO edit after applying the preview was silently overwritten and the calculated value was saved. With the guard, that save is rejected with the original calculated value intact; after reload and a fresh preview the operator can save the calculation while retaining the external edit. File-backed two-connection tests also reject an obsolete revision without adding history, preserve a concurrent eQSL confirmation that does not increment QSO revision, and honor an explicitly edited eQSL status. CTest adif, database, activityreport, qsl, qslcontroller, newqsofields, newqsotuning and qmlpopups passed 8/8 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2; the focused offscreen editor scenario passed. UI operations and QSO were synthetic and programmatic.

The guard is restricted to drafts with an applied recalculation; other editor paths retain their existing concurrency behaviour. The tests cover representative two-connection orderings, not every possible concurrent writer, same-path database replacement or crash during commit. External QSL services, physical interaction and other desktop platforms were not exercised. Version-1 provenance and the remaining G07 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 shared-serial allocator correction and warning

When the local next number exceeds the in-memory distributor's counter, allocation now advances the distributor before issuing the actual number. That number is recorded as issued and subsequent grants start above it. The New QSO panel no longer presents the local counter as the number that will necessarily be assigned for a shared serial: it says the number is assigned on save and warns the operator to check the saved QSO before transmission. The activation configuration also warns that network splits, restarts and lost replies can produce duplicate numbers. Existing local and shared workflows remain available.

A/B: a new synthetic allocator test failed on the preceding implementation: assigning local number 100 left the recorded highest value at 1. After the change it records 100, grants 101 next and detects a remote collision involving 100. Existing local, shared, and split-network collision cases pass. The application and test targets built on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2; CTest contestnet, qmlpopups, activation and contestrules passed 4/4. QML compilation was verified; the new warning was not observed through physical interaction or an offscreen UI probe.

This does not implement persistent reservations, request idempotency, lost-reply recovery or a safe distributor election. The current shared serial can change on save, and uniqueness is not guaranteed even while the network appears connected; do not interpret the warning as a guarantee. No packet-loss/reordering, three-node partition, restart or real LAN/SO2R test was performed for this increment. All of those and the remaining G10 requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 persistent serial reservation foundation

Schema 24 adds a persistent per-log serial reservation journal. A reservation is keyed by explicit scope, requester and request ID and records both the requested minimum and allocated number. Under `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, a new request allocates above both its minimum and the highest number already reserved for that scope; a repeat with identical parameters returns the original number, while a reused identity with changed parameters fails. Independent scopes have independent sequences, numbers cannot overflow the supported integer range, and a failed insert rolls back. This is a core foundation: ContestNet does not yet call it and the existing shared-serial workflow remains volatile and explicitly unguaranteed.

A/B: the new synthetic test initially failed to compile because the persistent reservation API did not exist. With the implementation, a file-backed log returns the same number after a second connection and restart, rejects changed retry parameters, advances for another requester, separates scopes and rejects exhausted numbers. Two threads using separate SQLite connections obtain distinct consecutive numbers. An injected SQLite trigger failure leaves no reservation and a retry succeeds. A simulated schema-23 database migrates to 24 and retains its QSO. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app and test targets built; CTest database, migration, contestnet, contestrules, activation and qmlpopups passed 6/6. The migration fixture is synthetic, not a user log; use a verified backup before upgrading and for any rollback to older software.

No shared scope or authority has yet been negotiated between stations, and there is no client-side durable pending request, network retry/ack integration, lease or partition protocol. Separate distributor databases can issue the same number. Three-node loss/reordering, process crash during commit, actual LAN, real hardware and other platforms remain untested. This journal alone does not provide globally unique contest serials, and all other G10 requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 local callsign queue

The New QSO panel now offers a local queue of up to 100 full or partial calls, with duplicate rejection, selection, reordering, removal and explicit loading of the first entry. Letters, digits, slash and `?` are accepted; the normal QSO validation still applies when logging. Loading is refused while the callsign field contains text, so an operator cannot accidentally replace the current call. Queue entries do not create QSO, tune the radio or send network messages. The queue lives in the controller for the current application process and is not persisted or shared with other stations.

A/B: the new controller test initially failed to compile because no queue API existed. It now verifies normalization, duplicate/invalid rejection, reordering, loading and removal. An offscreen app probe queues two calls, rejects a duplicate, refuses to overwrite an existing callsign, moves the second entry first and loads it after the field is cleared; the log still contains zero QSO. A captured 1440x900 desktop image exposed an initially compressed queue selector; after arranging controls across two rows a second image showed the selected call legibly. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app and test targets built, the focused offscreen probe passed, and CTest database, contestnet, netcontroller, qmlpopups and contestrules passed 5/5. Operations were synthetic and programmatic; the final placeholder text was changed after the visual capture and the app probe rerun.

The queue is volatile and not part of ContestNet, the full New QSO dialog or a separate contest-desk stack; it does not store band, frequency or notes. Keyboard-only navigation, narrow layouts beyond the captured panel, physical operation, Windows/Linux and long-running sessions were not tested. Shared serial integration and the remaining G10 and roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G11 local API discovery and request framing

The authenticated local API now exposes `GET /api/v1/capabilities`, optionally accepting `apiVersion=1` and rejecting other requested versions. It advertises the existing status, worked-before and QSO read/write operations and explicitly identifies the shared-token permission model. It reports per-extension permissions, events, TX control and durable atomic idempotency as unavailable. No existing endpoint or authentication method was removed. HTTP parsing rejects invalid, negative, duplicated or oversized Content-Length, unsupported Transfer-Encoding and headers larger than 16 KiB, and bounds each socket's Qt read buffer. Valid multipart-arrival request bodies continue to work. API documentation now states that the existing idempotency response and QSO are not written atomically and must not be treated as an exactly-once guarantee.

A/B: five new raw TCP framing cases previously reached the authenticated handler and returned 200; after the change all return 400. Existing token variants, valid POST and split-body tests pass. The application integration probe initially received 404 for capability discovery; after implementation it checks version 1, explicitly unsupported version 2, unsupported POST, the shared-token model and advertised read/write and unavailable capabilities. This probe also exercises existing QSO logging, retry across app restart and anonymous 401. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app and tests built; the targeted real-app API scenario passed, and CTest localapi, database and qmlpopups passed 3/3. HTTP clients and data were local and synthetic.

This is discovery, not a per-extension permission system or process isolation. The shared token grants all listed operations including writing QSO; clients with that token are trusted. The idempotency journal still has a crash/error window between QSO and response writes. No events, extension lifecycle, sandbox, adapter or developer harness has been delivered in this increment. Fuzzing, resource-exhaustion under many simultaneous sockets, other operating systems and all remaining G11 and roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G11 revocable local read-only API credential

The API can now have a second, separately generated or revoked read-only token in addition to the existing full-access token. At the HTTP server boundary, the read-only token permits only GET of capabilities, status, worked-before and recent QSO; all other paths and methods are denied before reaching the handler. Capability discovery identifies the credential's effective operations and omits QSO write for a read-only request. A matching full and read-only token fails closed. Existing full-token clients and their three authentication forms remain supported. The desktop settings provide create/replace and revoke actions and label the full token's write access. Both tokens are stored in local application settings; they are not OS-keychain credentials.

A/B: the new server test initially failed to compile because the read-only credential did not exist. It now exercises allowed read, denied QSO write and unknown path, continued full-token write, revocation and conflicting-token refusal. A real offscreen application using separate synthetic keys reports read-only capabilities, reads QSO, returns 403 on attempted logging and leaves the test callsign absent; its pre-existing full-token logging and restart retry still pass. The app built, the focused integration test passed and CTest localapi, database and qmlpopups passed 3/3 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. The settings controls were compiled but not exercised through a physical UI or a dedicated UI probe.

This is one shared read-only token, not independent credentials or permissions per extension. A local process that can read the settings can use the full token, so this is not process isolation or a security sandbox. In-flight requests are not cancelled by revocation; new requests are denied. Token storage, per-extension lifecycle, events, authenticated isolation, crash-safe atomic QSO idempotency, other operating systems and the remaining G11/desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G11 standalone read-only report example

A Python 3 standard-library example runs as a separate process and reads the existing local API using the read-only token from an environment variable. It negotiates API version 1, refuses credentials that advertise QSO write, disables proxy and redirect handling, applies a request timeout and response-size bound, and returns a JSON count by stored band and submode/mode. The output explicitly labels itself a recent sample of at most 500 QSO, not a complete-log statistic or award report. It uses no SQLite file and does not transmit or alter QSO. Instructions and limitations are in `docs/API.md`.

A/B: the new actual-app integration scenario initially failed because the example did not exist. It now starts the application and example in separate local processes with a synthetic read-only token, observes a nonempty sample with a 20m result and unchanged QSO count, then verifies that the full-access token is refused without printing it. Independent Python tests compare sample counts against a hand-calculated four-record fixture and reject incompatible permissions/version, missing token, invalid limits and malformed responses. The test runner registers the Python test when Python 3 is available; the integration probe reports if Python is missing. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the focused app scenario passed and CTest recent_activity_extension, localapi, database and qmlpopups passed 4/4. No external service or physical radio was used.

This is an executable example, not managed extension installation or process sandboxing, and the token is shared with other read-only clients. The two API calls are not a coordinated snapshot, the API cannot page beyond the latest 500, and no full-log or award inference is made. Python availability, environment-variable secrecy against other privileged local processes, unusual data volumes, real logs, Windows/Linux, the tracker adapter and the rest of G11/desktop requirements remain to be verified or implemented.

## 2026-11-23 — G11 experimental manifest and developer harness

The read-only report example now includes a versioned JSON manifest with extension identity, version, Python entrypoint, API version and declared permission. A standalone Python developer harness checks strict known fields, duplicate keys, schema/API version, entrypoint confinement to the manifest directory, and a read-only permission allowlist that excludes TX and QSO writing. It can validate the manifest alone or execute the example with a read-only token in a separate process, reduced environment, temporary working directory, timeout and monitored output size. It validates the expected sample-report output and refuses to echo a token found in the output. The manifest is advisory: the API read-only token, not the manifest, enforces access.

A/B: the new developer-harness test initially failed because the harness was absent. Synthetic tests now reject unsupported schema/API, TX, traversal, duplicate manifest fields and missing entrypoint; they exercise a successful child, crash, timeout, oversized output and credential echo. The real offscreen app integration starts the harness and report as separate processes, verifies a sample and unchanged QSO count, and refuses a full-access token. In a separate scenario an intentionally blocked child is timed out while the logger concurrently accepts and retains a new synthetic QSO. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, the focused app scenario passed and CTest extension_harness, recent_activity_extension, localapi, database and qmlpopups passed 5/5.

The harness is developer tooling for the example, not an in-app installer or OS sandbox. It does not enforce manifest permissions, isolate filesystem/network access, stop descendant processes, or provide a strict disk/output quota against hostile code; it must only run trusted code. The output-size check is polling rather than a hard quota. Extension authentication remains a shared token; event delivery, managed lifecycle, independent revocation, atomic logging idempotency, an operational tracker adapter, other platforms and remaining G11/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 CQ WW log-checking report reader foundation

Added a bounded UTF-8 parser for the organiser's CQ WW text LCR layout. It recognises the report identity and the exact `Not In Log`, `Incorrect Call` and `Incorrect Exchange Information` section headings, validates each recognised row's date, time and station identity, and exposes the recorded calls/exchanges and any organiser-supplied correction. It preserves every original line, including unsupported sections, and marks nonempty unrecognised rows inside supported sections for review. Informational sections about other stations copying the entrant are deliberately not treated as errors in the entrant's log. Unrelated reports and files without a supported section are rejected; no QSO is modified or matched automatically.

A/B: the new parser test initially could not configure because the parser files did not exist. The synthetic fixture now verifies three recognised entries, corrections, unknown-row retention, separation from other-station information, unrelated files and the 2 MiB input bound. A separate optional test was run against the actual public CQ WW CW 2024 4O4A report from https://cqww.com/publiclcr/2024cw/4o4a.rpt: 14 NIL, 20 incorrect-call and 6 incorrect-exchange records, matching its published summary, with no unknown rows in those sections. The external report was downloaded only to `/tmp` and is not included in the repository. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, CTest contestreport, contestrules, database, cabrillo and contestnet passed 5/5; the public-file test passed when explicitly supplied.

This is a parsing foundation, not yet a desktop import/review workflow or a persisted report. It does not match findings to local QSO, interpret unsupported sections, handle ARRL/other LCR or UBN formats, calculate score penalties, or establish correctness across editions. The public sample is one report and the other fixture is synthetic; no private report or hardware was used. UI, unknown-format review/export, broader source examples and the remaining G10/desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 CQ WW report desktop review

The single contest window now offers a CQ WW text report picker and an asynchronous, read-only review. It shows total NIL, incorrect-call, incorrect-exchange and unrecognised-row counts, followed by the original text and line number for recognised findings and unknown lines from those supported sections. To keep UI work bounded, at most 500 findings and 200 unknown rows are displayed, each truncated at 1,024 characters; the dialog discloses these limits. The file is opened read-only with a 2 MiB cap, read failures are reported, and neither it nor the QSO database is changed. The contest entry shortcuts are disabled while the picker or review is visible.

A/B: the new controller test initially failed to compile because no review API existed. Synthetic file-backed tests now verify one recognised and one unknown row, an unchanged existing QSO and byte-identical source file; a second fixture verifies exact totals of 501 findings and 201 unknown rows while bounding displayed lists to 500 and 200. An offscreen application probe opens the contest window, reviews a synthetic file, observes both rows and confirms the source and zero-QSO log remain unchanged. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the focused app and controller scenarios passed; CTest contestreport, activation, contestrules, database, cabrillo and qmlpopups passed 6/6 before the final wording/read-error change, and the focused app/controller scenarios passed again after it.

This is transient review, not a persisted import, QSO association, correction, or complete interpretation of all CQ WW report sections. Unsupported sections remain solely in the original file; long rows are only previews. The file picker and rendered layout were not verified through physical interaction or a captured review image, and Windows/Linux, real private reports, other organisers' LCR/UBN formats and the remaining G10/desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 CQ WW report archive foundation

Schema 25 adds a per-log archive for complete CQ WW report bytes and their source filename, contest, callsign, SHA-256 and import timestamp. The storage method validates the supported format first, performs the insert in an immediate transaction and deduplicates identical content by hash while comparing bytes before accepting an existing entry. The original bytes, including CRLF and unsupported text, are returned unchanged on retrieval. Bounded metadata listing and retrieval do not modify QSO. The existing preview window is not yet connected to this archive; there is no user-facing save or history action in this increment.

A/B: a synthetic archive test initially failed to compile because the storage API was absent. It now stores a CRLF report, reopens the log, verifies exact byte equality, deduplicates a second filename and rejects unsupported input. A simulated schema-24 database with an existing QSO migrates to 25 without changing the QSO; an injected SQLite insert failure rolls back and a later retry succeeds. The app and affected test targets were rebuilt, and CTest database, migration, contestreport, activation and qmlpopups passed 5/5 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. The migration and QSO are synthetic; use a verified backup before upgrading and for any rollback to older software.

Archive save/history UI, an explicit reviewed-snapshot handoff, protection when the selected source or active log changes, asynchronous writes and cross-log/replacement scenarios are not implemented or tested. The backend currently only validates CQ WW layout; other LCR/UBN formats, QSO association, external report compatibility beyond the previously checked public sample, Windows/Linux and remaining G10/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 archive reviewed CQ WW copy

The contest report review now offers an explicit save of the exact byte snapshot read for that preview into the active file-backed log. Parsing and the archive write run on a worker; successful completion identifies the target log filename and report ID. Duplicate bytes reuse the existing archive entry. The original report file and all QSO remain unchanged. A per-`LogDatabase` open generation and path are captured at preview start and checked before accepting the save; switching logs or closing/reopening even the same path makes that preview ineligible for saving. If the source file changes after the preview, the saved bytes remain the reviewed snapshot, not the changed file.

A/B: the new controller test initially failed to compile without the save API. It now previews a synthetic report, replaces the source contents, saves and checks that the original snapshot was archived. It also switches to another log and back and verifies the old preview is rejected. The offscreen app probe opens the review, invokes save, observes success and reopens the log to compare stored bytes and confirm zero QSO. Existing read-only review and bounded-row scenarios passed. The app and affected targets built on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2; CTest database, migration, contestreport, activation and qmlpopups passed 5/5. UI actions were programmatic.

Archive history and reopening from the desktop are still missing. The log-generation check covers changes made through this `LogDatabase` instance before the save begins, not external replacement of a database at the same path, a file-removal/replacement race while the worker opens it, or all filesystem failure modes. Once accepted, the worker targets the reviewed log path even if the UI later changes log. Source content beyond the bounded preview is preserved but not displayed. No physical interaction, Windows/Linux, real operator log or other LCR/UBN formats were tested; the remaining G10 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 archived report history and reopening

The contest window now lists archived CQ WW reports from the current log in pages of up to 100, with previous/next navigation. A selected item is reopened asynchronously from a separate read-only database connection and reviewed through the existing bounded viewer. The controller binds the selection to the displayed page, log path, open generation, report ID and recorded SHA-256, then hashes the retrieved bytes again before parsing. A missing, changed, oversized or damaged archive record is reported; switching or reopening the log invalidates pending history/selection. Opening a report never changes QSO or rewrites the archived bytes.

A/B: the new controller scenario initially failed to compile because history and reopen APIs were missing. Synthetic tests now reopen an archive, reject a wrong selection, detect altered bytes and reject a log switch while history loads. A 101-entry fixture verifies 100/1 page boundaries and rejects a selection from the previous page. An offscreen app probe starts from a previously archived report, opens history after a fresh app launch, reopens the report and observes the recognised and unknown rows while retaining exact bytes and zero QSO. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the targeted controller and app scenarios passed; CTest database, migration, contestreport, activation and qmlpopups passed 5/5. UI operations were programmatic.

The UI does not yet export the original archived bytes, delete archive entries, associate findings with QSO or interpret unsupported report sections/formats. The previously documented external same-path database replacement and save-worker file race remain; hash verification protects selected report bytes but is not authentication. Physical navigation, visual capture of the new history dialog, other desktop platforms, real operator logs and remaining G10/desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 export archived report bytes

Each report in the paged contest history now has an explicit export action. The chosen report is bound to the currently reviewed history page, log open generation, ID and recorded SHA-256; a background reader verifies the archived bytes again before `QSaveFile` atomically writes the selected destination, with direct-write fallback disabled. The log database and its SQLite WAL/SHM/journal paths are rejected as destinations, including existing filesystem aliases and canonical parent aliases. Export preserves the original bytes, including unsupported sections and line endings, and does not change the archive or QSO.

A/B: a new controller test initially failed to compile because the export API did not exist. It now compares an exported CRLF fixture byte for byte, rejects the log, WAL and a symlink alias, rejects a mismatched report identity, and confirms that an altered archive cannot replace an existing destination. An offscreen app probe opens history after restart, invokes export and verifies the output bytes, one retained archive and zero QSO. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the focused controller and actual-app scenarios passed; CTest database, migration, contestreport, activation and qmlpopups passed 5/5. The save dialog and controls were invoked programmatically rather than physically.

Filesystem checks cannot guarantee safety against adversarial path changes between validation and commit; external replacement of the log at the same path remains unverified. Disk-full, permissions, interrupted commit, Windows/Linux, physical navigation and real operator reports remain untested. Deletion of archive entries, association of findings with QSO, unsupported LCR/UBN formats and the remaining G10/desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 archive target identity guard

Schema 26 adds a persistent random log identity. The report preview captures both that identity and the existing open generation. When the operator requests archival, the worker opens the selected path using SQLite URI `mode=rw` with Qt's URI option, so a missing database is not created. The archive transaction compares the reopened log's identity with the reviewed identity before writing. This prevents a different independently created log substituted at the same path before worker open from receiving the report. The existing byte-snapshot and changed-open-generation checks remain.

A/B: an isolated two-database replacement test reproduced the previous bug: after preview, a different database renamed to the same path received the report. After the change, the write fails and the substitute has no archive. A second test renames away the selected database and verifies that the worker fails without recreating the missing file. Database tests verify distinct identities, mismatch rollback, successful matching identity, URI paths with spaces/`#`/`?`, and a simulated schema-25 migration retaining the archived report and a stable identity across reopen. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, the affected app and tests built, three offscreen archive/reopen/export scenarios passed, and CTest database, migration, contestreport, activation and qmlpopups passed 5/5. The test uses synthetic files and switches SQLite to DELETE journal mode before the external rename; it is not evidence that moving a live WAL database is safe.

Qt URI option: https://doc.qt.io/qt-6.8/sql-driver.html ; SQLite `mode=rw`: https://www.sqlite.org/uri.html . A copied/restored database retains its identity, so the check identifies a database lineage rather than a physical file; replacement by a clone with the same identity is not detected. External rename/replacement while SQLite is actively writing, adversarial identity modification, crash/power loss, Windows/Linux, real operator logs and other archive workflows remain unverified. Schema rollback requires a verified pre-upgrade backup; the migration fixture is synthetic. G10 and the broader desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 possible local QSO for CQ WW findings

Each recognised CQ WW finding can now start an asynchronous, read-only search of the current file-backed log. Candidates must share the callsign as logged in the report, the band derived from report frequency, a supported CQ WW mode family and the same UTC minute. CW, phone and RTTY report modes map to explicitly listed ADIF modes; unsupported modes or frequencies are reported instead of guessed. At most five candidates are displayed, with a warning when more exist. The list includes QSO ID, UTC and station call, marking whether the call came from the QSO, the current station profile or is unknown. It does not apply the report's suggested correction, assert identity, or write/link any QSO. The worker checks the log identity and open generation before and after searching.

A/B: the new core and controller tests initially failed to compile because the candidate APIs were absent. Synthetic fixtures check same-minute calls/band/mode, exclusion of deleted and different-minute QSO, ambiguous candidates, a five-result limit, UTC midnight rollover, unsupported mode and profile-versus-QSO station provenance. A file-backed controller test confirms a log switch invalidates an in-flight search and preserves the QSO. The offscreen app probe opens a report, searches a synthetic finding and observes one candidate while the log remains unchanged. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the focused app and controller scenarios passed; CTest database, migration, contestreport, activation and qmlpopups passed 5/5, and the final focused provenance test passed after being added.

These are bounded suggestions, not a verified association: exact frequency, exchange, contest identity, station validity and time corrections are not checked, and a profile is current data rather than a historic snapshot. The search can miss valid QSO logged in another minute, with unsupported modes or legacy timestamp representations; it can also return multiple unrelated contacts. Results can become stale after the query. The dialog is programmatically exercised but not physically or visually inspected, and no real operator log, Windows/Linux, other LCR/UBN formats or remaining G10/desktop requirements are verified here.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 candidate comparison and guarded QSO opening

The CQ WW finding's candidate list now shows the logged QSO frequency, received exchange and contest ID alongside the report's frequency and exchange; missing fields remain explicitly unknown. These are recorded values for operator comparison, not a validation of contest credit or a proposed edit. The operator can explicitly open a candidate in the existing QSO detail dialog. Before opening, the controller verifies that the candidate came from the current search and that the log path, open generation, log identity, QSO UUID and revision still match; changed or deleted QSO require a fresh search. The action does not automatically change a QSO or create a report association.

A/B: the added comparison and candidate-guard tests initially failed to compile because these fields and guard were absent. Synthetic tests check recorded frequency/exchange/contest values, preserve missing fields as unknown, distinguish station data from current profile data, and reject a candidate after a QSO revision changes. An offscreen application probe searches a finding, opens the candidate in the actual existing QSO detail dialog and confirms the QSO count remains one. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the focused app/controller/core tests passed and CTest database, migration, contestreport, activation and qmlpopups passed 5/5; the final focused missing-field test also passed.

This is a comparison and navigation aid, not automatic correction or a verified match. Frequency equality, exchange interpretation, contest equivalence and eligibility are not inferred; the operator must review them. The guard precedes opening and is not a lock against every external writer; the detail dialog loads the then-current record. Physical navigation, visual inspection, real operator data, other platforms, additional LCR/UBN formats and remaining G10/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G09/G11 read-only PREDICT status adapter

The authenticated local API now offers `GET /api/v1/tracker` and advertises `tracker.read` to both existing credentials. It returns the most recent PREDICT snapshot, when present, with source, satellite, position, Doppler, timestamps, `available`, request `busy`, 10-second reading freshness and a separate 14-day TLE freshness flag. This is an observation, not a live-connection guarantee or a prediction that a contact is possible. The endpoint does not start tracker requests or control CAT, rotor or TX. A standalone Python process and versioned manifest consume this API with the shared read-only token, reject the full token, return `freshForReview` only when both freshness checks pass, and can be run through the experimental developer harness. The manifest remains advisory and the read-only token also permits the other read endpoints.

A/B: the new tracker-status core test initially failed to compile and the real-app API test initially found no advertised tracker capability. The updated core fixture checks no snapshot, fresh data, expired reading and old TLE; the existing deterministic UDP fixture still checks Doppler on approach/recession and verifies its exact read-only command sequence. Python tests reject privileged/incompatible credentials and malformed status and distinguish fresh, stale, old-orbit, busy and unavailable states. An isolated integration starts a simulated PREDICT UDP server, the actual offscreen app, the separate tracker adapter and harness; it checks fresh then expired readings, read-only POST rejection, exactly the five GET commands and zero QSO. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app and test targets built, the focused API integration passed and CTest predict_extension_integration, tracker_extension, extension_harness, recent_activity_extension, predicttracker, localapi and database passed 7/7.

This is a read-only snapshot adapter, not a process sandbox, live subscription, tracker control, pass planner, RF test or proof of satellite visibility. The UI may have closed or changed selection after the last observation; consumers must inspect the satellite and timestamps, and stale data can remain visible with `fresh=false`. No independent per-extension token, authenticated orbital provenance, complete TLE validation, real PREDICT/Gpredict device in this increment, radio/rotor hardware, Windows/Linux or full G09/G11 roadmap completion is claimed.

## 2026-11-23 — G09 TLE line integrity before tracker use

The PREDICT snapshot and pass planner now share a TLE inspection step. Both 69-column lines must have valid modulo-10 checksums, matching nonempty catalogue identifiers and a valid epoch; malformed characters, short lines and inconsistent identifiers are rejected before a snapshot or pass is published. The snapshot's read-only API includes the checked catalogue identifier. This deliberately rejects non-standard or truncated server responses. It does not authenticate the source, verify that the server's satellite name corresponds to the catalogue number, validate all orbital elements, or establish that a current-looking orbit is accurate.

A/B: a synthetic PREDICT server sent a TLE with an invalid checksum; before the change the tracker published a successful snapshot, and after it reports failure and clears the snapshot. The shared inspector also tests the CelesTrak reference example, corruption, short lines and two different satellite numbers with individually valid checksums. A planner fixture confirms a mismatched TLE stops before any PREDICT pass command. Dynamic-epoch test TLEs were changed to recompute checksums rather than relying on malformed fixtures. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app and tests built; CTest predict_ui, predict_extension_integration, predicttracker, predictpass, tracker_extension, localapi and database passed 7/7. After adding the catalogue field, focused tracker/planner tests and CTest predict_extension_integration, tracker_extension and localapi passed again. UI/extension and PREDICT protocol tests used local synthetic UDP servers; optional actual-server tests were skipped in this increment.

Format reference: https://celestrak.org/NORAD/documentation/tle-fmt.php ; PREDICT GET_TLE contract: https://github.com/kd2bd/predict/blob/master/clients/samples/README . A previously tested actual PREDICT 2.2.5 installation is not a new verification of this change. RF, radio/rotor control, current orbital data, untrusted servers, unusual valid formats, Windows/Linux and remaining G09/G11/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G09 coherent PREDICT snapshot checks

A tracker snapshot now requests and validates TLE before and after the position/Doppler requests, refusing publication if the orbital lines change. The initial and final tracking modes must also agree, including when each mode would independently have been supported. The recorded receipt time is captured when the position response arrives rather than after the later TLE response. Doppler correction and the read-only API now both apply the 10-second reading limit at millisecond precision. The client sends six read-only commands for a successful snapshot; it does not command CAT, rotor or PTT.

A/B: a synthetic server changed a valid, checksummed TLE during one snapshot; the old client published the snapshot and the new client rejects it. A second A/B test waited 10.5 seconds: the previous Doppler calculation still accepted the reading while the API marked it stale; after the change it is rejected. Further UDP tests reject a change from multi to single tracking, verify position-receipt timing under a delayed Doppler reply, exercise unchanged TLE, Doppler signs and the exact six-command sequence. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, CTest predict_ui, predict_extension_integration, predicttracker, predictpass, tracker_extension, localapi and database passed 7/7; the final focused delayed-response test passed separately. Test servers and UI actions were synthetic and local.

Matching checks cannot detect a TLE that changes and changes back between requests, guarantee that PREDICT's returned position was freshly calculated, authenticate the server, or validate propagation or radio frequencies. An extra UDP round trip can increase latency or timeout sensitivity. No new actual PREDICT-server run, physical interaction, radio/rotor/RF hardware, Windows/Linux or completion of the remaining G09/G11/desktop roadmap is claimed.

## 2026-11-23 — G09 manual linear-transponder pairing

The PREDICT window retains independent nominal TX/RX frequencies as its default, suitable for the existing FM/independent workflow. It also offers operator-selected ideal 1:1 linear inverting and non-inverting models: the operator enters uplink and downlink centres, a signed uplink offset and half bandwidth; the downlink offset has the opposite or same sign respectively. An invalid/non-finite input, unsupported model, non-positive bandwidth or offset outside the supplied range is rejected. The paired nominal frequencies go through the existing fresh-snapshot Doppler preview and explicit QSO draft; no CAT, PTT or rotor command is sent. The UI says the model is not a satellite catalogue and the operator must verify actual transponder limits and mode.

A/B: the new core test initially failed to compile because manual pairing did not exist. Synthetic core tests cover independent frequencies, positive/negative offsets in both directions, invalid model, NaN, invalid resultant frequency and out-of-band offset. The offscreen actual-app UDP fixture now checks the existing independent CW and FM paths and both linear directions, rejects a 51 kHz offset with 50 kHz half bandwidth without opening a draft, then explicitly saves synthetic QSOs with distinct Doppler-adjusted TX/RX, band, satellite and SAT propagation fields and round-trips them through ADIF. The simulator verifies only the expected read-only tracker commands. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, CTest predict_ui, predict_extension_integration, predicttracker, predictpass, localapi and database passed 6/6; after a final invalid-selection guard, the focused pairing test and predict_ui passed again. The linear fixture values and orbital data are synthetic, not operating instructions.

The relationship is an ideal fixed 1:1 approximation based entirely on operator-supplied centres and range; it does not obtain, validate or update satellite transponder data, sideband, passband, activity or Doppler continuously. No real FM or linear transponder, radio/rotor/CAT, RF, physical input, other desktop platform or current on-air interoperability was tested. AMSAT describes both mappings at https://www.amsat.org/live-linear-satellites/ . Remaining G09/G11 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G09 reject expired pass responses

The read-only PREDICT planner rejects a server response whose loss of signal is earlier than the requested start, clearing the result and reporting an error. A pass already in progress is still accepted. This prevents an old response from being shown as the requested next pass; it does not establish that the server selected the earliest possible pass or that its orbital prediction is correct.

A/B: a synthetic UDP server returned the same finished pass for a request one second after LOS. The old planner accepted it; the updated planner rejects it. The same fixture requests a time within that pass and verifies acceptance. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app and planner test built; CTest predict_extension_integration, database, localapi, predicttracker and predictpass passed. The first predict_ui run failed in the ADIF round-trip because tst_journeycolumn had not been rebuilt with its current expectations; after rebuilding that auxiliary target, the complete predict_ui test passed, including simulated next-pass and draft paths. This was a local synthetic UDP test, not a real PREDICT-server or RF test. Multi-pass scheduling, physical radio/rotor tests and other desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G09 bounded multi-pass schedule

The tracker window can request one to ten consecutive passes from the selected numeric PREDICT server and satellite. It displays each sampled maximum elevation, AOS/LOS and TLE epoch, shows progress and allows cancellation. Each prediction uses the existing read-only, timeout-bounded planner; the next search begins one second after the preceding LOS. A SHA-256 fingerprint of the validated TLE response must match throughout the sequence. If a request fails, is cancelled, returns a previously ended pass, or the TLE changes between passes, the sequence stops and labels retained results incomplete. Input selection is held while planning, and changing it afterwards hides the old schedule.

A/B: the new schedule test first failed to compile because no schedule API existed. After implementation, a local UDP fixture verifies two consecutive passes and the exact read-only command sequence, rejects an invalid count, and cancels an in-flight plan. Another fixture changes a valid checksummed TLE between predictions and verifies an incomplete result with just the first pass retained. The offscreen actual-app probe verifies three rendered passes and separately a changed-TLE failure with one rendered partial pass; both keep the log empty and only send GET_TLE/PREDICT commands. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, CTest predict_extension_integration, database, localapi, predicttracker, predictpass and predict_ui passed 6/6. After a final progress-label adjustment, predict_ui passed again.

The list is count-limited rather than a time-window search; it does not establish that the server returned every possible pass, authenticate TLE data, validate orbital propagation or guarantee on-air availability. The 14-day warning remains advisory, and PREDICT timeouts can make a multi-pass request slow. The app probe uses a synthetic UDP server and offscreen programmatic interaction, not a new actual PREDICT/Gpredict, RF, radio, rotor, visual usability or Windows/Linux test. Remaining G09 and other desktop roadmap work is open.

## 2026-11-23 — G09 explicit selection of planned passes

Each row of the bounded PREDICT schedule now has a Select control. Selection shows that row in the existing pass summary and lets the operator request the following pass from its LOS; the selected row is labelled. Starting a new schedule, changing the server or satellite, or closing the window invalidates the selection. Selecting a predicted pass is for review and navigation: it does not create a QSO, associate a future pass with a QSO, control a radio or command TX. Following-pass requests now start one second after LOS instead of 60 seconds, avoiding an unnecessary gap.

A/B: the offscreen app test first failed because selecting a schedule row and using it as the basis for the following prediction were absent. After the change, the synthetic UDP fixture exercises the actual row button, selects the second of three passes, observes its AOS in the selected summary, and requests the third. It checks the following PREDICT command starts at the selected LOS plus one second, allows only GET_TLE/PREDICT, and confirms no QSO was created. It also retains the incomplete-schedule fixture and the independent single/following-pass and QSO draft scenarios. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 predictpass and predict_ui passed 2/2; predict_ui passed again after the exact timestamp assertion was added.

The selected pass is not persistent and is not associated automatically with a logged contact. There is no proof of orbital accuracy, earliest available pass, real PREDICT/Gpredict interoperability, on-air activity, RF, radio/rotor control, physical interaction or other desktop platforms in this increment. Remaining G09 and desktop roadmap work is open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 candidates for corrected calls

For a CQ WW incorrect-call finding, the local QSO search now includes both the call as reported and the organiser-suggested corrected call, provided the latter has a bounded callsign-shaped value. Each candidate shows its actual logged call and whether it matched the reported call or the report's correction. Reported-call matches are ordered first; the existing five-result bound and more-results warning apply to the combined list. An incorrect-exchange correction is never treated as a callsign. These remain read-only suggestions: the correction is not applied, a candidate is not identified as the actual QSO, and the existing log-generation, UUID and revision checks still guard opening.

A/B: the added core test initially failed to compile because candidate calls and match provenance were not exposed. With the change, a synthetic report finds both a QSO with the reported call and one already using the corrected call, distinguishes their basis, avoids duplicate matching when both calls agree and does not interpret an exchange correction as a call. A file-backed controller test confirms the corrected candidate is marked and passes the existing opening guard. An offscreen actual-app test opens a report where only the corrected call exists in the log, observes the corrected-match marker, opens that QSO and verifies the log still has one QSO. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 these focused tests passed and CTest contestreport, database, migration, qmlpopups and activation passed 5/5.

This does not verify that a suggested correction is right, make a report association, search time offsets, guarantee that a corrected-call match appears among the first five if reported-call matches fill the bound, or cover other report formats. Tests used synthetic reports and local data; physical UI inspection, real operator reports, other desktop platforms and remaining G10/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 operator-selected report time window

The CQ WW candidate dialog still searches the report's UTC minute by default. The operator may explicitly repeat the read-only search with zero to five additional whole minutes on each side; the displayed result records the window actually used and each candidate retains its logged UTC timestamp. Reported/corrected call, band and mode-family criteria, the five-candidate bound, truncation notice and guarded QSO opening remain in force. The query covers `[report minute - N minutes, report minute + (N+1) minutes)`, so the whole reported minute is included. Nothing is corrected or associated automatically.

A/B: the new test initially failed to compile because the candidate search had no configurable window. A synthetic in-memory log then verified the default finds only the exact UTC minute, a one-minute expansion includes entries on both sides of midnight, invalid negative and over-five values are rejected, and QSO count is unchanged. An offscreen actual-app test finds no match at the default setting, uses the dialog's search-again control with one extra minute, finds and opens the adjacent-minute QSO and confirms the log remains unchanged. Existing offscreen exact-minute and corrected-call tests also passed. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, CTest contestreport, database, migration, qmlpopups and activation passed 5/5.

The broader window can increase ambiguity and does not establish that any candidate is the report QSO. Results remain ordered by call basis then timestamp rather than proximity; the five-result cap may omit later candidates, which is disclosed. Legacy timestamp representations, real reports, physical UI interaction, other desktop platforms, other report formats and remaining G10/desktop requirements are not verified here.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 closest report candidates before truncation

The CQ WW candidate search orders matches in the reported UTC minute first, then other matches by distance from that whole minute; reported-call matches precede corrected-call matches only at equal time distance, followed by logged time and QSO ID for a stable order. The dialog describes the ordering. The same five-result cap and more-results warning remain. Proximity is a navigation aid, not an assertion that a candidate is the report QSO.

A/B: with six earlier matching QSO and one in the report minute, the previous search returned five earlier records and omitted the exact-minute record. The new core fixture returns the exact-minute QSO first and still reports truncation. Another fixture checks both sides of UTC midnight. An offscreen actual-app test widens the search through the dialog, sees five candidates and the truncation flag, verifies the first has the report minute, opens it and confirms the seven-QSO log is unchanged. Existing window-expansion and corrected-call app tests passed. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, CTest contestreport, database, migration, qmlpopups and activation passed 5/5. The optional public-report test was not supplied in this run.

This ordering cannot establish identity or ensure that the desired QSO is among five candidates. SQLite time parsing and the existing lexical filter can omit or rank unusual legacy timestamp representations differently; physical UI, real operator reports, Windows/Linux, other LCR/UBN formats and remaining G10/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 recoverable contest audio file foundation

Added a separate contest-audio PCM file writer, independent of the DVK and without radio/PTT access. It creates uniquely named timestamped `.wav.part` files in an existing directory, writes fixed 16 kHz mono 16-bit little-endian samples, retains a split sample between writes and enforces a caller-specified even-byte limit up to 1 GiB. Normal finalization writes the WAV sizes and renames without overwriting an existing target. Explicit recovery accepts only this writer's filename and PCM header, validates size and alignment, then updates the header and renames the partial file; invalid inputs remain untouched. A checkpoint flushes userspace buffers but does not claim power-loss durability.

A/B: the initial test could not compile because no contest audio file writer existed. The synthetic tests verify split samples, an eight-byte limit, normal finalization, invalid recovery rejection, and recovery after a separate writer process flushes and exits abruptly without destructors. A local ffmpeg process decoded the generated WAV back to the exact synthetic PCM bytes. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, CTest contestaudio, database and migration passed 3/3.

This is file infrastructure only: there is no contest-window control, audio-device capture, periodic checkpoint scheduling, storage-budget UI, association with QSO/session, playback navigation, recovery discovery or hardware recording yet. Abrupt-process recovery does not prove power-loss durability; samples not flushed before a crash may be lost, malformed or odd-length partial data require separate handling, and rename/filesystem races remain. The ffmpeg test uses synthetic PCM, not a microphone, radio or two real sessions. Windows/Linux and the remaining G10/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 optional contest audio capture

The contest window now opens a separate session-audio dialog. The operator chooses a local folder, input and per-recording limit from 1 to 1024 MiB, then explicitly starts and stops capture. The controller uses Qt Multimedia at 16 kHz mono signed 16-bit PCM; it reports a missing or unsupported input rather than silently changing the device. It checkpoints every five seconds, stops at the limit, finalizes on normal stop or orderly application shutdown, and leaves a partial file on an unexpected stream or write failure. The selected input is tracked by device ID during this run. Recording continues when the dialog closes. No rig, CAT or PTT control is connected to this controller.

A/B: the controller test initially failed to configure because capture did not exist. A synthetic QIODevice fixture then checks split samples, explicit stop, automatic size-limit stop, stream destruction and retained partial files; an incomplete-sample test verifies explicit recovery of complete samples and a subsequent new recording. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, a Qt audio generator and recorder connected through BlackHole 2ch produced a WAV containing the generated signal. A separate offscreen actual-app probe selected that virtual input, started and stopped recording through the dialog controls, produced a nonempty WAV and left the QSO log empty. CTest contestaudio, database, migration, contestaudiocontroller and qmlpopups passed 5/5; the configured virtual-loopback test and final incomplete-sample test also passed separately.

This is virtual-audio evidence, not RF, a physical input, two Decodium sessions or a real contest. Input formats other than 16 kHz mono Int16 currently fail explicitly, and the device selection is not persisted. The limit applies to one recording, not total folder usage; disk-full and physical unplug need further testing. File timestamps approximate capture start and are not yet associated with a QSO or contest session. Recovery discovery/UI, playback and QSO navigation, automatic splitting, Windows/Linux and the remaining G10/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 QSO-time audio review

Recent contest QSO now expose their full stored UTC timestamp and an Audio action. The operator selects a completed contest WAV and playback output; the application checks the filename timestamp, exact PCM WAV header and data length, and rejects a QSO outside the recording. Playback uses the approximate elapsed time from the recording filename and starts up to ten seconds before the QSO timestamp. It uses a selected Qt audio output, does not control PTT or CAT, and does not create a persistent QSO/audio association. Recording and playback cannot be started together through these controls.

A/B: the initial location test could not compile because timestamp lookup did not exist. The synthetic fixture verifies an in-range offset and duration, out-of-range timestamps, and a damaged WAV header. An initial QMediaPlayer implementation remained in LoadingMedia and produced no virtual-loopback audio; it was replaced with direct PCM playback through QAudioSink. A Qt capture on BlackHole 2ch then observed the generated playback signal, and a separate offscreen app probe exercised Play and Stop with the selected virtual output. The existing app capture probe also passed, and an activation test confirms the recent-QSO row carries the full UTC value. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest contestaudio, database, migration, contestaudiocontroller, qmlpopups and activation passed 6/6, plus both configured virtual-audio tests and focused app scenarios.

The QSO-to-file relationship is operator-selected and approximate: filename times and log timestamps can be wrong, microphone latency, dropped audio or clock changes can shift the actual sound, and imported QSO may have only minute precision. The app probe supplies a synthetic QSO row and does not physically click a real recent-QSO row. There is no persistent recording index, automatic QSO association, timeline, recovery UI, audio-device fallback, physical audio/RF, two-Decodium-session or Windows/Linux proof. Recording-file replacement between validation and playback is not locked. Remaining G10 and desktop requirements are open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 explicit audio recovery in desktop

The contest audio dialog now lets the operator select a local `.wav.part` file for explicit recovery. The existing filename, header, data-size and alignment checks are reused; recovery is unavailable while recording or playback is active. On success the resulting WAV path is shown for review. A pre-existing destination is now detected before rewriting the partial header, preserving both files in that conflict case. No QSO is created or changed.

A/B: the controller recovery test initially failed to compile because no desktop recovery action existed. The revised synthetic fixture rejects recovery while recording, checks an existing destination and the original partial remain byte-for-byte intact, then recovers the partial once the conflict is removed. An offscreen actual-app probe recovers a selected synthetic partial through the dialog's recovery function, verifies the PCM bytes in the resulting WAV and confirms an empty QSO log. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, the configured BlackHole 2ch capture and playback tests passed, and CTest contestaudio, database, migration, contestaudiocontroller and qmlpopups passed 5/5. The previous separate-process abrupt-exit recovery fixture also ran in contestaudio.

The app probe invokes the dialog function programmatically; it does not physically navigate the file picker. Recovery does not discover partials automatically, reconstruct unflushed or incomplete samples, guarantee power-loss durability or guard all adversarial filesystem races; a failure after header update can still leave a modified partial. Physical audio/RF, two Decodium sessions, Windows/Linux, automatic session/QSO indexing and remaining G10/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 native-rate contest audio

Contest capture still requests 16 kHz mono Int16 when supported; otherwise it uses the selected input's preferred supported sample format, converts UInt8/Int16/Int32/Float multichannel PCM to mono Int16, preserves incomplete input frames between reads, and writes a WAV at the input sample rate from 8 to 192 kHz. An incomplete final frame is reported and leaves a recoverable partial. WAV recovery, QSO-time lookup and playback now read and validate the stored sample rate; playback asks the selected output for that rate and reports unsupported formats. The size limit continues to apply to converted mono WAV bytes, not source bytes.

A/B: new tests initially failed to compile because native sample rates and source formats were unsupported. A fragmented 48 kHz stereo-float fixture now produces the expected mono samples and a 48 kHz WAV; a recovered 48 kHz partial retains its rate and gives the expected one-second duration and QSO offset. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, the BlackHole 2ch playback fixture observed a generated 48 kHz WAV on the virtual output, the existing 16 kHz virtual capture still passed, and the offscreen app played a 48 kHz file and exercised capture and recovery. CTest contestaudio, database, migration, contestaudiocontroller and qmlpopups passed 5/5.

The preferred-format fallback and stereo conversion were tested using a synthetic input stream, not a physical device that rejects 16 kHz; the virtual capture device accepts the initial format. No resampling or playback output-format conversion is implemented. Clock drift, audio dropouts, physical hardware, RF, two Decodium sessions, Windows/Linux, persistent session/QSO indexing and remaining G10/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 find recordings for a QSO

The contest audio dialog can now search the operator-selected folder asynchronously for completed contest WAV files whose validated filename start and PCM duration cover the selected QSO's UTC timestamp. It shows start, duration and offset for each possible recording and requires an explicit Select action; playback validates the selected file again. The search checks at most 1,000 matching filenames, returns at most 50 results and discloses truncation. It is non-recursive, excludes symlinks and unreadable files, ignores nonmatching or invalid WAV files, and discards results in the dialog if the folder or QSO time changed while the worker ran. It does not persist an association or modify QSO/audio files.

A/B: the initial test could not compile because the search API did not exist. Synthetic fixtures check two overlapping recordings, an outside recording, an unrelated WAV, a missing folder, and both scan and result caps. A controller fixture verifies asynchronous delivery, single in-flight search and unchanged file. An offscreen app probe searches a synthetic folder, selects a result using the row control and leaves the QSO log empty. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, CTest contestaudio, database, migration, contestaudiocontroller and qmlpopups passed 5/5; after the last cap assertion the focused search test passed, and BlackHole 2ch capture and playback passed again.

Results are only time-based possibilities and depend on file names, audio duration and clock quality. The first 1,000 filesystem entries are not guaranteed to be chronological, unreadable files are omitted, and partials/subdirectories are not searched. Files can change after search; playback revalidates structure but cannot authenticate content. The app probe uses a synthetic QSO row and programmatic UI interaction. Physical audio/RF, two Decodium sessions, persistent index/association, Windows/Linux and remaining G10/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 audio review navigation

The contest audio dialog now shows approximate playback position and duration and provides ten-second backward/forward controls. Navigation clamps to the selected WAV's bounds, seeks on a PCM sample boundary and restarts the selected audio output; reaching the end still permits seeking back. Stopping playback clears the position. The existing QSO-time starting point and no-PTT behaviour remain unchanged.

A/B: the new virtual-output test initially failed to compile because position, duration and navigation were absent. With the change, a 48 kHz synthetic WAV plays through BlackHole 2ch, moves forward and back within its bounds, and its generated signal is observed on the virtual input. An offscreen actual-app probe exercises the forward/back controls, observes the position change and stops playback; the recording-search app probe also passed. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, the configured virtual capture/playback controller tests passed and CTest contestaudio, database, migration, contestaudiocontroller and qmlpopups passed 5/5.

Position is estimated from Qt audio's processed time and can differ from audible output because of buffering, device latency or clock drift. The app probe uses a synthetic QSO row and programmatic controls; physical UI/audio, RF, two Decodium sessions, session indexing, Windows/Linux and remaining G10/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 remember audio folder and search on QSO review

The contest window now remembers its chosen audio folder in local window settings. When an operator opens Audio for a recent QSO and a folder is available, the existing bounded asynchronous time-coverage search starts automatically; the operator still selects a candidate and playback revalidates it. If the QSO time or folder changes while searching, the old result is discarded and a new search is requested for the current selection while the dialog remains open. An absent or moved folder is reported by the existing search error path. This stores a folder preference, not a recording index or QSO association.

A/B: an offscreen two-launch test initially failed because the folder and automatic search were absent. After the change it selects a synthetic folder, closes the app, reopens with the same isolated settings and finds the synthetic WAV for a QSO time without selecting the folder again. Another app probe changes the QSO while a search is in flight and observes only the new time's result. Both keep the audio file unchanged; the two-launch test confirms no QSO was added. The existing BlackHole 2ch app capture/playback probes passed, as did CTest contestaudio, database, migration, contestaudiocontroller and qmlpopups 5/5 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2.

The setting is global to the contest window, not specific to a log or station. Search remains bounded and time-based; filesystem state can change, and automatic results do not prove QSO identity. App probes use synthetic QSO rows and programmatic interaction, not physical UI, real contest logs, physical audio/RF, two Decodium sessions or Windows/Linux. Persistent audio indexing/association and other G10/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 persistent contest audio devices

Contest audio input and playback output selections now persist in local settings as explicit system-default choices or raw device identifiers. On reopening, a selected device is resolved by ID; an absent or empty saved ID remains missing, the dialog displays a notice, and capture or playback refuses to start until an available device is chosen. A system default is used only when it was explicitly selected. This setting is independent of DVK device choices and does not operate PTT or CAT.

A/B: with saved IDs for absent input and output, the previous controller reopened both selectors as system default. The new isolated-settings fixture observes both as missing, verifies capture and playback fail, then explicitly chooses defaults and observes them restored in a new controller. A separate offscreen application test uses three launches with isolated settings: it chooses BlackHole 2ch for capture, chooses it for playback, and confirms both names are restored after restart. The virtual capture/playback controller tests passed, as did the folder-restoration app test and CTest contestaudio, database, migration, contestaudiocontroller and qmlpopups 5/5 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. A final focused test also checked missing playback output.

Device IDs may change after OS or hardware changes; the user must reselect in that case. The missing-ID tests use synthetic settings rather than physically unplugging a device, and app interaction is programmatic. Physical audio/RF, two Decodium sessions, Windows/Linux, audio session indexing and remaining G10/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 active audio-device loss

Contest audio capture and playback now retain the actual device ID used at start, including when the operator selected the system default. When Qt reports an input/output inventory change, the controller compares the appropriate active ID with that inventory. A missing active input stops capture as a failure and leaves the partial file for explicit recovery; a missing active output stops playback and reports the disconnection. Another available device is not substituted. Empty backend IDs cannot be reconciled by this mechanism; the existing Qt audio error handling remains in place.

A/B: the new loss fixture initially could not compile because active-inventory reconciliation was absent. With real BlackHole 2ch Qt streams running, the fixture supplies an unchanged synthetic inventory and verifies both streams continue, then supplies an empty inventory and verifies capture stops, its nonempty partial is recovered, and playback stops with a disconnected message. This simulates inventory changes through the same controller methods used by Qt notifications; it does not physically unplug the device or prove that a particular OS emits its notification.
