On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, the complete configured virtual-audio controller test and both offscreen app capture/playback probes passed; CTest contestaudio, database, migration, contestaudiocontroller and qmlpopups passed 5/5.

Capture may lose buffered samples at disconnection and timing is still approximate. OS notification delivery, physical unplug/replug, default-device changes, RF, two Decodium sessions, Windows/Linux, persistent audio association and remaining G10/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 folder audio budget and writer coordination

The contest audio setting now limits total bytes of this feature's `contest-audio-*.wav` and `.wav.part` files in the chosen folder, including their headers, rather than only the next file. Before starting, the controller counts up to 1,000 such regular files, reserves the next WAV header and limits its PCM data to the remainder. It refuses to start if the scan is incomplete or the budget is exhausted; it never deletes recordings. Recording and explicit recovery acquire the same nonblocking QLockFile in that folder, preventing cooperating DecoDXLog processes from writing/recovering concurrently. The lock is released on stop or failure; a dead local lock-holder can be detected by Qt. The UI labels the limit as a folder limit.

A/B: the folder-budget test initially failed to compile because accounting did not exist. Synthetic files verify WAV and partial sizes, unrelated-file exclusion, exhausted budget, scan limit and no deletion. Two controller instances using BlackHole 2ch verify that a second recording and recovery of an active partial are refused, then recording succeeds after release; an oversized existing audio file prevents a new recording. A separate helper process holds the lock, then releases it; a second run is killed and the next recorder reclaims the stale local lock. Offscreen app capture and recovery probes and the complete configured virtual-audio controller tests passed. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, CTest contestaudio, database, migration, contestaudiocontroller and qmlpopups passed 5/5.

The budget is for matching regular files in one folder, not all disk usage, subdirectories, symlink targets or external writers. Locking is cooperative and filesystem-dependent; noncooperating processes can exceed the limit or alter files, and network filesystems and power-loss behaviour were not tested. The scan refuses folders over 1,000 matching entries. Physical audio/RF, two Decodium sessions, Windows/Linux and remaining G10/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 discover interrupted audio recordings

Opening the contest audio dialog with a chosen folder now starts a bounded asynchronous search for `contest-audio-*.wav.part` files. A separate interrupted-recordings window lists up to 50 entries with filename, size and a preliminary format/size check; invalid files are labelled and cannot be recovered from the list. The scan inspects at most 1,000 files and reports truncation. Recovery remains an explicit per-file action using the existing revalidation, destination conflict check and folder lock; the search itself changes no files. Results for a previously selected folder are discarded and rescanned for the current folder while the dialog is open.

A/B: the initial core test could not compile because partial discovery did not exist. Synthetic fixtures distinguish a valid partial from a damaged header, test scan and result limits and confirm files remain unchanged. An asynchronous controller test verifies discovery alone neither renames nor recovers the file. An offscreen actual-app probe discovers a synthetic partial, explicitly activates the list's Recover control, verifies recovered PCM bytes and an empty QSO log. Existing explicit recovery and folder-restoration app probes passed. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, the configured BlackHole 2ch controller suite passed and CTest contestaudio, database, migration, contestaudiocontroller and qmlpopups passed 5/5.

A preliminary valid label is not proof of complete or accurate audio, and another process may still hold the folder lock; recovery rechecks. Symlinks and subdirectories are excluded, the bounded first entries are filesystem-order dependent, and files may change during or after scanning. Physical UI navigation, power-loss recovery, physical audio/RF, two Decodium sessions, Windows/Linux, persistent QSO/audio association and other G10/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 compare recorded contest activity across periods

The contest window now compares two explicitly selected inclusive UTC date ranges for one recorded `CONTEST_ID`. A read-only worker counts live QSO and displays A, B and B−A by recorded band, operator, own radio (`MY_RIG`) and UTC hour. Missing recorded values are shown as unknown; the remote station's `RIG` is not treated as the operator's radio. Overlapping dates count in both periods. This is a log-activity comparison, not scoring, credit, contest eligibility, automatic edition selection or an error-rate analysis. Each range is limited to 367 days; requests with over 100,000 matching records or over 4,000 groups fail visibly rather than returning partial totals.

The worker queries a separate read-only connection and checks log path, identity and open generation before returning. The dialog discards a result if its contest/date inputs or observed log revision changed while it ran, and clears displayed results on a subsequent application log-change notification. This is a snapshot and cannot observe every external database writer.

A/B: the first core test could not compile because the comparison API did not exist. Synthetic fixtures compare 2 versus 1 QSO, check band/operator/hour and own-radio grouping, missing operator, other-contest and deleted-QSO exclusion, overlapping periods, malformed ADIF extras, fractional UTC midnight and a malformed timestamp. A file-backed controller fixture rejects an in-flight result after a log switch. The offscreen actual-app test displays 2 versus 1 with four groups, rejects a result after the contest selection changes and verifies three QSO remain. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, CTest contestcomparison, contestreport, database, migration, qmlpopups and activation passed 6/6; the app probe passed again after the final UI guards.

The filter depends on a text `CONTEST_ID` and canonical ISO UTC timestamps in the selected ranges; untagged, malformed or legacy representations can be missed. Invalid UTC timestamps encountered by the bounded query are counted, but records excluded before that query are not enumerated. Operator and own radio are recorded fields, not authenticated identities; no QSO deduplication, score, historical profile inference, objective comparison, operating-time compliance or LCR error analysis is performed. The UI probe is programmatic and synthetic. Real logs, visual/physical UI, Windows/Linux and remaining G10/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 optional QSO goals in period comparison

The contest-period comparison now accepts optional operator-entered QSO-count goals for A and B (zero means unset). After a valid read-only comparison it shows the recorded count, goal, rounded percentage and nonnegative remaining QSO; results above target can exceed 100%. Changing a period clears that period's goal, and changing the contest ID clears both. Goals are temporary UI inputs, do not alter the log and are not contest scores or eligibility decisions.

A/B: the revised offscreen app test first failed because goal results were absent. It then verified synthetic totals A=2/B=1 against goals 5/1 (40% and 100%, three and zero remaining), an exceeded goal showing 200%, an unset goal hiding its result, and the existing stale-selection guard. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app probe passed and CTest contestcomparison, contestreport, database, migration, qmlpopups and activation passed 6/6.

Goals are not stored or shared and apply only to the currently displayed counts. They are QSO-count targets, not validated score targets, and inherit the selected contest-ID, UTC timestamp and log-data limitations of the comparison. Physical UI, real contest logs, Windows/Linux, persistent goals and remaining G10/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 persist per-period contest QSO goals

The comparison dialog now offers explicit Load goals and Save goals actions. Each optional QSO target is stored in the active log's existing settings table under a key derived from the normalized contest ID and exact inclusive UTC date range; zero remains unset. Both periods save in one immediate transaction, with log-identity and per-goal revision checks. The controller requires a successful load for the same log generation and selection before saving, rejects stale revisions and reports that a pending save may have completed in the previous log if the user switches logs. Identical periods must have identical targets. Application log-change notifications invalidate the dialog's loaded-save state. No QSO is changed, and targets remain activity goals rather than contest scores.

A/B: the initial goal persistence test failed to compile because load/save APIs did not exist. File-backed two-connection tests verify exact contest/period isolation, restart-style reload, stale revisions, atomic rollback when only the second period is stale, wrong log identity, duplicate periods and a bounded 1,000-goal limit. A controller test requires load before save and rejects saving after a log switch. An offscreen app test explicitly loads and saves 5/1, restarts with the same isolated log, loads 5/1 again and confirms zero QSO; the comparison/goals and stale-selection app probe also passed. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest contestcomparison, contestreport, database, migration, qmlpopups and activation passed 6/6; the final app tests passed again after the log-change guard.

Goal records use the log's settings table and are included only where that database is retained; they are not synchronized as contest-network reservations or official targets. Saving zero leaves a versioned unset record, and there is no goal-library deletion UI; the 1,000-record cap therefore needs management in future work. A save already accepted can complete against its original log after a UI switch, which is reported; external file-replacement races and noncooperating database edits are not fully excluded. Real logs, physical UI, other desktop platforms and remaining G10/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 remove saved contest goals safely

After explicitly loading goals, the comparison dialog can remove the saved A and B goals for the selected contest/date ranges. Removal deletes their records in one immediate transaction and releases their slots; identical periods are handled once. A transactionally maintained per-log goal generation is read with the goals and checked on every save/removal, in addition to per-goal revisions and log identity. This rejects a stale client even if a goal was created, deleted and recreated after its earlier read. Since the generation covers all contest goals in the log, a concurrent change to an unrelated goal also requires reload. The dialog clearly names both targets and keeps QSO untouched.

A/B: the new test initially failed to compile because removal and generation tracking were absent. Two file-backed connections then verify deletion, actual removal of both setting records, rejection of stale pre-creation and pre-deletion snapshots, safe recreation, stale deletion rejection and unchanged QSO count. Existing tests verify duplicate periods, revision conflict and transaction rollback. A controller test removes only after loading the current log; an offscreen app test saves 5/1, restarts and loads them, removes them via the control, restarts and observes both unset. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, CTest contestcomparison, contestreport, database, migration, qmlpopups and activation passed 6/6; the final focused removal test also passed.

The generation protects cooperating versions using this protocol; older binaries or direct database edits that do not update it are outside that guarantee. There is no full searchable goal library or independent deletion of just A or B in this action. A mutation already accepted can finish in the previous log after a UI switch, and the controller reports this possibility. Physical UI, real logs, Windows/Linux and remaining G10/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 compare selected CQ WW report findings

The contest-period comparison can now open a separate comparison of two explicitly selected local CQ WW reports, one per inclusive UTC date period. A bounded background reader parses each file without writing either the report or the log. It requires a recognised CQ WW CW, SSB or RTTY report header matching the selected contest ID and a station call, then counts recognised NIL, incorrect-call and incorrect-exchange findings in the corresponding period. It displays each source filename, contest header and station call, plus unrecognised lines and recognised findings outside the period. Reports and periods can overlap; each side is independently counted. Changing the active log invalidates the asynchronous result; changes to contest, dates or selected files discard stale results.

A/B: the new comparison test initially failed to compile because report comparison was absent. Synthetic 2024/2025 reports then verified independent category counts, distinct station calls, unrecognised lines, rejection of a mismatched contest and overlapping periods. A controller test verified unchanged report bytes and QSO count and rejection after a log switch. An offscreen actual-app probe selected the two synthetic files, observed NIL A=1, incorrect call B=1 and one unrecognised A line, and verified a changed selection discards a pending result; both source files and the empty log remained unchanged. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest contestcomparison, contestreport, activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 6/6.

This is a comparison of three parser-supported sections, not a complete error rate, score, eligibility decision or association with local QSO. Unrecognised lines are counted only in supported sections, and unsupported sections are not counted. Reports are snapshots read from local files and may change externally; header and dates do not establish that the station or edition is appropriate, so the operator must check them. The app probe is programmatic and synthetic; physical UI, additional organiser report formats, real user reports, Windows/Linux and remaining G10/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 compare contest activity by UTC day

The contest-period comparison now also groups recorded QSO by their relative UTC day within each selected range. Day 1 is the first selected date on each side, allowing different editions to be aligned; the row displays both actual dates, counts and difference. Only days with at least one QSO on either side are listed. If a range is shorter, its missing date and difference are displayed as unavailable. Overlapping ranges remain independent. These are QSO activity counts, not measured operating time, off-time compliance or a score.

A/B: a fixture for QSO around UTC midnight initially failed because no daily rows existed. The implemented comparison then verified midnight boundaries, separate editions, unequal-length ranges, overlapping ranges and unchanged QSO count. An offscreen actual-app probe verified the displayed day label and A=2/B=1 for two different dates, alongside the existing goal and stale-selection checks. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the focused core and app tests passed; CTest contestcomparison, contestreport, activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 6/6.

The comparison inherits the existing contest-ID and canonical timestamp filters. Aligning by the selected start date does not prove that operating schedules or contest rules match, and missing zero-activity days are not shown. This does not calculate duration or compliance. Tests use synthetic logs and programmatic offscreen UI; physical UI, real contest logs, other desktop platforms and remaining G10/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G11 extension harness process-group cleanup

The developer harness now starts each checked extension in a separate POSIX session on macOS/Linux and kills its original process group when the direct process exits, crashes, times out or exceeds the monitored output limit. Cleanup occurs before reading the result and before the temporary working directory is removed. Windows retains direct-process termination. The API documentation describes the platform distinction and explicitly continues to require trusted extension code.

A/B: a synthetic extension spawned a child which wrote a marker after its parent timed out; the new test failed before the change because the marker appeared. Afterward, child processes did not write their delayed marker after timeout, normal parent exit, parent crash or oversized output. Existing manifest, permission, output, timeout and credential-leak tests passed. The separate-process actual-app API scenario again ran the read-only example and tested a blocked extension while accepting a synthetic QSO. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, CTest extension_harness, recent_activity_extension, localapi, database and qmlpopups passed 5/5.

This is process-group cleanup for trusted code, not an OS sandbox or a managed in-app extension lifecycle. A descendant can escape by creating a new session or process group, the polling output limit is not a strict disk quota, and Windows descendants are not covered. Process-group behavior on Linux, Windows job-based cleanup, per-extension credentials and permissions, events, atomic idempotent logging, installation/removal and remaining G11/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G11 decoded extension-output credential check

The developer harness checks the parsed JSON tree as well as the raw output for the exact read-only token, including object keys and nested string values. This closes a case where an extension could spell part of the token using JSON Unicode escapes and the harness would subsequently print the decoded token. The traversal is iterative; malformed excessively nested JSON is reported as an error rather than an uncaught traceback. Documentation states the boundary of the check.

A/B: a synthetic child emitting an escaped token nested in a JSON value passed the old raw-byte check and the new test failed. After the change, both escaped nested values and escaped object keys are rejected, and a separate CLI invocation exits unsuccessfully without printing the token. Existing success, crash, timeout, excessive-output, process-group and manifest cases passed. The actual-app local API scenario again exercised the separate read-only example and a blocked extension while logging a synthetic QSO. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest extension_harness, recent_activity_extension, localapi, database and qmlpopups passed 5/5.

This detects the exact token in the parsed output, not arbitrary encodings, transformed or different secrets, stderr, external files or network transmission. The harness remains for trusted code and is not an OS sandbox or managed extension runtime. Independent credentials and permissions, event delivery, managed lifecycle, atomic logging idempotency, Windows/Linux verification and remaining G11/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 real CQ WW reports and uncorrected calls

The CQ WW reader now accepts the organiser's explicit `incorrect call` annotation in the Incorrect Call section when no corrected call is supplied. It preserves the finding with an empty correction, so candidate search uses only the reported call; it never invents a replacement. The same annotation in another section and other unknown annotations remain unrecognised and visible for review.

A/B: a focused synthetic test failed before the change because that real-format row was classified as unrecognised. Afterward it recognises the row, retains an empty correction, still recognises a supplied correction, and rejects unexpected annotations or use in the wrong sections. The offscreen application compared two synthetic reports and counted this variant while retaining an unknown line and source files unchanged. Two actual public CQ WW reports for P33W were downloaded to temporary files: [2022 CW](https://cqww.com/publiclcr/2022cw/p33w.rpt), SHA-256 `1390dda4a4b5c72729d362ee98936f813fcd69d2e2b78671832f8ac93e371cc9`, and [2023 CW](https://cqww.com/publiclcr/2023cw/p33w.rpt), SHA-256 `2f80b624294b764a9f22f19d0f0b6aa29f1df4a801637e7f16bac83c9532d6b9`. The parser/comparison returned 75/79/7 and 83/100/11 NIL/incorrect-call/incorrect-exchange findings respectively, matching the reports' own summaries, with zero unrecognised or out-of-period lines in those supported sections. The existing optional public 2024 4O4A report also passed. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest contestcomparison, contestreport, activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 6/6.

The two live samples prove only these particular public CW report files and their supported sections; they do not establish compatibility with all editions, SSB/RTTY layouts or other organisers. The real samples were tested by the core comparison, while the app probe used synthetic files and programmatic UI interaction. No association, score, eligibility, private report, physical UI or other desktop platform is verified. Remaining G10 and desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G07 bind report snapshots to the open log

All four activity-report workers now start a read transaction, verify the current log UUID inside that transaction and run the query on the pinned SQLite snapshot. The result is accepted only if the controller's log path, identity and open generation still match the request. CSV and PDF export also require the report's original identity and open generation. A worker opening a different database at the same path is rejected rather than producing a report attributed to the original log. Existing report filters, grouping and snapshot export formats are retained.

A/B: the new test initially failed to compile because verified report snapshots were absent. With the implementation, two distinct file-backed logs reject a mismatched identity, and a valid snapshot continues to report one QSO while a second connection inserts another; after closing the snapshot, a fresh report sees two. Empty identities are rejected. Four offscreen actual-app scenarios for activity, custom, confirmation and club reports and their existing exports passed. An initial test invocation used a nonexistent custom-test name and was corrected; the subsequent named test passed. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest activityreport, adif, database, newqsofields, newqsotuning and qmlpopups passed 6/6.

The identity mismatch and concurrent-write tests exercise the core snapshot directly; they do not simulate replacing a live database file during an actual app run or race a log switch against PDF export. A PDF export already started writes its captured snapshot even if the active log later changes. External tools that copy the same log UUID, filesystem replacement races, physical UI, Windows/Linux and remaining G07/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G03 recorded QSL waiting dates

The QSL planner now carries the recorded sent date for every service marked sent and not confirmed. For a valid ADIF `YYYYMMDD` date it displays the ISO date and elapsed whole UTC dates at plan generation; missing, malformed and future dates are labelled separately and do not produce a fabricated wait. Existing pending-service lists, priority, paper routing and explicit queue approval are unchanged. The date is a stored assertion, not proof that a request was sent or received.

A/B: the new test initially failed to compile because pending-date details did not exist. Deterministic tests then verified 39 days between 2026-10-15 and 2026-11-23, missing/invalid/future dates, an actual planner result and removal after confirmation. The offscreen actual-app planner probe displayed a recorded card date, retained its existing cost and queue workflow, refreshed after confirmation and survived restart. Its first run exposed that the QML engine did not provide `flatMap` for the model; the probe was corrected to iterate normally and the full scenario then passed. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest database, awards, qslplanner, qsl and qmlpopups passed 5/5.

Elapsed dates are calculated once when the plan is built, using the system UTC date; an open unchanged plan does not automatically age at midnight. `sent=Y` and a date are local records and do not establish an actual request, expected response time or eligibility; only the ADIF date format is interpreted. UI verification is programmatic and synthetic; external services, physical UI, other desktop platforms and remaining G03/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G05 import the reviewed data snapshot

The interactive import preview now retains the converted ADIF bytes actually parsed and estimated, and accepting the dialog imports those same bytes instead of reopening the selected source. The dialog tells the operator a reviewed snapshot will be imported; cancelling releases it and invalidates any pending preview. Preview and import workers additionally check the active log's identity, and preview acceptance checks its open generation, profile and log-change version. Existing direct `importAdif` calls that did not request a preview continue to read the selected file, preserving that API's previous behaviour.

A/B: in an offscreen actual-app test, the source initially contained synthetic K1OLD; after preview the test replaced its contents with K1NEW. Before this change, confirming imported K1NEW and the new assertion failed. Afterward the same test imported K1OLD; a second run removed the source before confirmation and still imported K1OLD, while cancelling imported no QSO. The existing accept/reject import-undo app scenario passed. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest database, migration and qmlpopups passed 3/3.

The snapshot is held in memory while the dialog is open, so very large converted imports can increase memory use; this change does not add a size limit or benchmark large files. Estimates remain advisory, and later QSO changes may alter actual deduplication results. The test uses synthetic ADIF and programmatic offscreen interaction; CSV/N1MM conversion, Club Log download, changed identity races, physical UI, Windows/Linux and real producer interoperability were not newly verified. The direct no-preview API deliberately has no reviewed snapshot. Remaining G05 and desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G05 accurate duplicate-key import preview

The import preview now copies the live log's stored duplicate keys into its private simulation without re-importing and deduplicating the existing log through ADIF. The copy runs from one SQLite read transaction, preserves every live QSO's call, band, mode, submode and exact timestamp, and uses the current deduplication windows before simulating the reviewed ADIF. It leaves the source untouched. Failure to create a complete simulation is reported instead of silently showing zero estimates; normal invalid input records remain counted as rejected.

A/B: a synthetic log had two already-existing contacts of the same station one minute apart and a 60-second duplicate window. The former ADIF reconstruction dropped the second contact and incorrectly estimated a new contact two minutes after the first; the revised preview and actual import both reported one duplicate and no new QSO. A second mixed batch checked one new QSO, one within-batch duplicate and one invalid date against the real import; source count stayed unchanged during preview. The offscreen actual-app test reproduced the overlapping-window case with a two-minute window and observed zero estimated new and one duplicate, then confirmed the real log still had its two QSO. The reviewed-snapshot modified/deleted/cancel scenarios also passed. An initial mixed-record test exposed that ordinary invalid records were being treated as a simulation failure; this was corrected and retested. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest database, migration and qmlpopups passed 3/3.

The dry run reproduces current duplicate-key logic, not award, profile or external-service semantics, and the estimate can become stale after preview if the log changes; the existing UI version check covers notified in-process changes before acceptance, not arbitrary external writers. The source scan and in-memory copy are not cancellable or benchmarked on large logs. Tests use synthetic ADIF and programmatic UI; real producer exports, external concurrent writers, physical UI and other desktop platforms remain unverified. Remaining G05 and desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G05 invalidate import preview after external commits

Import preview now captures SQLite `PRAGMA data_version` on the active log connection before the background estimate. The same connection is checked when the preview returns and again before the operator's accepted snapshot is sent to the import worker, alongside the existing log identity, open generation, in-process change version and station-profile checks. A failed token query fails closed. Because SQLite reports commits from other connections, any such commit invalidates the preview even if unrelated to QSO; the operator is asked to preview again. The existing activity warning reports a stale acceptance.

A/B: an offscreen actual-app test paused at the import preview, used a second database connection to insert a synthetic K2EXT QSO, then accepted. Before the change both K2EXT and the reviewed K1OLD were present; afterward only K2EXT remained, so no reviewed import occurred. A focused database test verified that the token is stable for a write made by the observing connection and changes after another connection commits. The app tests for overlapping duplicates, changed/deleted source files and preview cancellation also passed. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest database, migration and qmlpopups passed 3/3.

This is invalidation, not an atomic lock spanning confirmation and import: another process can still write after the final check or between committed import batches, and deduplication is re-evaluated at insertion. `data_version` is meaningful only on the same open connection; the separate generation/identity checks remain required. Unrelated external commits can conservatively force a new preview. Physical UI, live external logging, network filesystems, Windows/Linux and remaining G05/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G05 cancellable import preview

While an import preview is being prepared, a desktop dialog now exposes Cancel preview. Cancellation or a replacement preview sets a per-request atomic flag and invalidates the old callback; shutdown also requests cancellation. The private duplicate-key copy checks the flag while scanning, rolls back both temporary transactions on cancellation, and the simulation checks between input records in 128-record batches. The general import helper's optional cancellation rolls back the current batch and reports previously committed batches; normal accepted imports do not supply that option and keep their existing behaviour. Cancelling discards the pending reviewed snapshot and does not change the source log.

A/B: the new cancellation test initially failed to compile because the estimator had no cancellation contract. Synthetic tests now cancel after 130 source-scan checks and during simulated input processing, verify the source log is unchanged, then rerun successfully. A separate 300-record import fixture cancels in its second 50-record batch and verifies the first 50 remain committed while the current batch is rolled back. An offscreen actual-app probe opens and rejects the working dialog, observes busy state clear, starts another preview and imports one QSO; the cancelled request publishes no preview. Existing actual-app tests for external changes, duplicate estimates, modified/deleted source and cancellation passed. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest database, migration and qmlpopups passed 3/3.

Cancellation is cooperative: file conversion and reads, ADIF parsing, an individual SQL operation and the Club Log temporary-file preparation already in progress cannot be interrupted immediately. The app probe rejects the dialog programmatically and requests cancellation immediately; mid-scan and mid-simulation cancellation were exercised in the core tests, not through physical UI on a large log. Memory use, latency under sustained incoming QSO, Windows/Linux, real producer exports and remaining G05/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G02 VUCC confirmation-source filtering and review

The VUCC candidate preview now accepts locally recorded confirmation status only from `lotw` or `card`; an eQSL, QRZ or other service alone is excluded with its source named. When an accepted and another confirmation coexist, the accepted source and other recorded sources remain separately visible in the candidate and JSON. Duplicate grids are described as already selected, not credited. A desktop review dialog lists selected grid/QSO/evidence and exclusion reasons, and explicitly warns that local status is not an ARRL award credit. Changing preview filters or an in-process log notification invalidates the displayed result.

The filter follows the ARRL's published [VUCC LoTW and paper-credit guidance](https://lotw.arrl.org/lotw-help/vucc-award-credit/) and [electronic QSL policy](https://www.arrl.org/e-qsl-policy). LoTW itself requires a qualifying confirmation containing grid information; a local `lotw` status alone does not establish this, and a local card status does not prove that a card has been checked.

A/B: before the fix, a synthetic eQSL-only QSO was included and the new test obtained four candidates instead of three. Afterward eQSL-only was excluded, paper and LoTW rows remained, and a mixed paper/eQSL row retained `card` as evidence and `eqsl` as other recorded evidence. Tests also reject a similarly named fake source, preserve mixed-case legitimate sources and existing boundary-grid behavior, and confirm the log is unchanged. An offscreen actual-app probe opened the review and observed the accepted card, other eQSL status and eQSL-only exclusion; the earlier boundary-grid app test also passed. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest vucc, database, awards and qmlpopups passed 4/4.

This is still a preliminary local candidate filter: it does not prove LoTW's confirmed grid, physical card validity, station/account/geographic eligibility, complete band and terrestrial/satellite rules or official award submission/credit. Selection remains dependent on recorded data, and external database changes without an application notification may leave a displayed preview stale. UI interaction was programmatic and synthetic; real LoTW or paper submissions, physical UI, Windows/Linux and remaining G02/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G02 separate satellite and non-satellite VUCC previews

The VUCC preview now has explicit non-satellite and satellite categories. Satellite candidates require recorded `PROP_MODE=SAT` and are selected across TX bands; their satellite name and recorded TX/RX bands are shown. Non-satellite previews exclude recorded satellite contacts and recorded cross-band contacts. A satellite name combined with absent or non-SAT propagation is excluded as inconsistent. A non-satellite candidate with no propagation field is retained for compatibility but explicitly labelled as an assumption requiring review; a SAT record without a name is likewise flagged. Category, recorded propagation, satellite name, RX band and classification are included in the local JSON. The four-argument preview API retains non-satellite as its default.

The [ARRL VUCC rules](https://www.arrl.org/files/file/Awards/VUCC_Rules.pdf) describe Satellite separately and permit cross-band for that category. These filters use recorded ADIF values; they are not a determination that a contact meets all award conditions.

A/B: the new satellite-category test initially failed to compile because category and propagation support were absent. Synthetic records then verified separate non-satellite and satellite selection, satellite contacts on different TX bands, cross-band satellite handling, rejection of inconsistent satellite metadata and recorded non-satellite cross-band, and explicit unreported-propagation classification. An offscreen actual-app probe selected Satellite, displayed an AO-91 2m/70cm row and excluded a non-satellite contact; the evidence-source and boundary-grid app tests also passed, with no QSO changes. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest vucc, database, awards and qmlpopups passed 4/4.

These are candidate filters, not a verified VUCC application. Missing propagation can conceal a satellite QSO; missing `BAND_RX` can conceal cross-band operation. The preview does not validate satellite operation, LoTW's confirmed grid, active repeaters, dates, allowed bands, operating-location distance, account linkage or official credit. The satellite category aggregates recorded SAT contacts without checking band eligibility. Tests used synthetic records and programmatic offscreen UI, not RF, real LoTW data, physical interaction or Windows/Linux. Remaining G02 and desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G02 VUCC date and supported-band checks

VUCC candidate selection now requires a valid timestamp with an explicit UTC offset and excludes QSO before 1983-01-01 UTC. Non-satellite previews also require an ADIF band represented by the currently supported VUCC band list; unsupported selections such as 20m or 4m return a visible validation error. The satellite category remains separate and does not apply this terrestrial band list. The original timestamp is retained in candidate JSON and shown in desktop review. `submm` remains a broad ADIF band and is only a candidate category, not proof of a qualifying optical contact.

The cutoff and listed frequencies are taken from the [ARRL VUCC rules, January 2026](https://www.arrl.org/files/file/Awards/VUCC_Rules.pdf). Other eligibility requirements remain unverified.

A/B: the new date/band fixture initially failed to compile because the VUCC entry did not carry its timestamp. After implementation synthetic QSO at 1982-12-31 23:59:59 UTC and 1983-01-01 00:00:00 UTC were respectively excluded and selected; explicit positive/negative offsets crossing the boundary were converted to UTC, while a malformed or timezone-less timestamp was excluded. Tests reject unsupported HF and 4m band selections and retain supported 2m, 1.25m and submm and the satellite scope. An offscreen actual-app review showed the eligible 1983 timestamp and pre-1983 exclusion; satellite, confirmation-source and boundary-grid app scenarios also passed. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest vucc, database, awards and qmlpopups passed 4/4.

The list maps broad ADIF bands to the published VUCC categories and does not validate the QSO's exact frequency, licence privileges or optical operation. Satellite-band eligibility, repeater use, aeronautical mobile, station-location distance, award account, proof and official credit are not established. These are synthetic records and programmatic offscreen UI; real LoTW/card submissions, physical UI, Windows/Linux and remaining G02/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G02 VUCC initial thresholds

The VUCC preview displays the initial locator threshold for the selected supported category under the [ARRL January 2026 rules](https://www.arrl.org/files/file/Awards/VUCC_Rules.pdf): 100 for 6m, 2m and satellite; 50 for 1.25m and 70cm; 25 for 33cm and 23cm; 10 for 13cm; and 5 for the remaining individually mapped SHF bands. The operator's target is not silently changed when the band or scope changes. An explicit control copies the initial threshold to the editable target, and the threshold is included in the local preview JSON. Unsupported selections return no suggestion. The broad `submm` ADIF band remains available for review but has no suggested threshold: it does not by itself establish a qualifying laser contact.

A/B: the initial mapping test failed to compile before the threshold API existed. The new deterministic test checks every mapped category, the satellite and unsupported cases, and confirms that a custom target of 123 is retained while the 70cm reference is 50. An offscreen actual-app probe shows a custom 125 target, explicitly applies 50 and verifies the editable field, changes to 13cm without overwriting the target, disables the action for 4m, and shows the satellite reference of 100. Previous satellite/date app scenarios and CTest vucc, database, awards and qmlpopups passed; after the final submm distinction the VUCC suite, threshold app probe and four CTest suites passed again on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2.

These numbers are initial-award reference thresholds, not automatic endorsements or proof of confirmed and accredited grids. The mapped ADIF bands do not prove exact qualifying frequencies, and the underlying preview still does not establish all ARRL conditions or official credit. UI interaction and logs were synthetic and programmatic; real award submissions, physical UI, other platforms and remaining G02/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G02 CQ DX Marathon ADIF review export

The CQ DX Marathon panel now offers an asynchronous ADIF review export for one explicitly selected station profile and UTC year. It includes every live QSO in that profile/year, including alternative QSO, bands and modes, with recorded ADIF fields and extras, so the operator can inspect the file and choose candidates in the organiser's tool. A read-only worker checks log identity and uses a single read transaction for selection and export; it reports invalid/unspecified UTC timestamps skipped and refuses missing required fields, an unavailable profile, an empty selection or the database/SQLite sidecars as destination. QSO start and end timestamps with offsets are converted to UTC in the exported fields. No external upload or submission occurs. The existing JSON export is unchanged.

The [official data-format documentation](https://dxmarathon.com/resources/data-formats/) states that the submission tool accepts ADIF and recommends retaining the year's QSO and alternatives for operator review. It also identifies the minimum ADIF fields. A separate selector fix permits a valid CQ zone candidate without a recorded DXCC; it does not infer the missing entity.

A/B: the new zone test initially failed because a later QSO was selected in place of a valid zone with missing DXCC. The export test initially failed to compile because no review-ADIF function existed. Afterwards synthetic tests verified independent zone selection, all alternatives, year/profile isolation, preserved custom fields, UTC offset across the year boundary, invalid timestamp reporting, missing-field refusal and wrong-log identity rejection. An offscreen actual-app scenario produced and re-parsed two alternatives, preserved an extra field, excluded another year/profile and refused to overwrite its database. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest cqmarathon, database, awards and qmlpopups passed 4/4.

The output is an unsubmitted review file, not a score or verified entry: the local preview does not implement the complete official CQ entity list, operating-location, frequency, propagation, maritime/aeronautical or edition rules. The ADIF includes potentially private stored fields, which the UI asks the operator to inspect. Real organiser-tool import, physical UI, large-log memory/latency, file replacement races and Windows/Linux were not tested. A job already accepted may complete using its original log snapshot after the active view changes. The organiser's actual processing and all remaining G02/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G02 CQ DX Marathon 2026 candidate review

The local CQ DX Marathon preview now applies the documented 2026 band list (160m through 6m, including the listed WARC bands), excludes calls ending `/AM`, and holds calls ending `/MM` out of the preliminary selection for location review. The `/MM` decision is deliberately conservative because the recorded suffix alone cannot establish the operator's actual location. Other edition years retain their prior preliminary behaviour; no 2026 rule is silently extended to them. Missing or timezone-less QSO timestamps are excluded instead of being interpreted in the computer's local timezone. A new review dialog displays entity candidates, independently selected zone candidates and reasons for exclusion, and invalidates its in-process snapshot when the log or award settings change. The review ADIF still deliberately contains all profile/year QSO and alternatives; the operator must review it in the official tool.

The edition-specific checks derive from the [2026 DX Marathon rules](https://www.dxmarathon.com/rules/2026/). A/B: before this change a synthetic 2026 fixture selected all six zones including 2m, `/AM` and `/MM`; the new test expected three and failed. Afterward it selects the valid 20m, 6m and 60m zones, flags the others, preserves a call merely ending in letters `AM` without a slash, and does not apply the 2026 band filter to a 2025 fixture. It also rejects a timezone-less timestamp. An offscreen actual-app probe displayed a zone candidate with unknown DXCC and the 2m exclusion; the ADIF-export app scenario passed. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest cqmarathon, database, awards and qmlpopups passed 4/4.

An optional test hook generated `/tmp/decolog-marathon-synthetic.adi` from the actual exporter using synthetic data for external validation. The official [tool documentation](https://dxmarathon.com/resources/data-formats/) says ADIF is supported and permits review before submission, but this session's browser access host returned unavailable and no upload or official-tool import was performed. An external test still needs an available browser, appropriate approval for uploading the synthetic file, and review without submission. The local filter remains incomplete: it does not validate the official CQ country/zone lists, exact frequency/licence, repeaters, single-location requirement, all `/MM` circumstances, past-edition rules or official scoring. The dialog does not yet implement local candidate replacement. Physical UI, Windows/Linux, real logs and the remaining desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G02 CQ DX Marathon special entities

The 2026 preliminary entity selector now recognises the six special WAE entities in the [CQ DX Marathon entity list v1.1](https://dxmarathon.com/resources/countries/) using the recorded ADIF DXCC and REGION together: 206/IV = 901, 279/SI = 902, 248/AI = 903, 248/SY = 904, 259/BI = 905, and 390/ET = 906. The original DXCC and REGION are retained; the computed entity code and its basis appear in local JSON and the desktop review. No callsign-based inference is performed, and other edition years retain their prior behaviour. The organiser-review ADIF is unchanged. Where REGION is absent or does not match, the recorded DXCC remains a preliminary fallback and the review flags the need to check special regions.

A/B: after rebuilding the previous selector with the new database fixture, three synthetic Italian QSO (ordinary Italy, SY and AI) produced one entity candidate rather than the expected three. The new selector produces three. Focused tests cover all six mappings, lowercase REGION, unrelated DXCC and a 2025 comparison; the DB fixture verifies persisted ADIF REGION and JSON. CTest cqmarathon, cqmarathon_db, database, awards and qmlpopups passed 5/5 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. An offscreen actual-app scenario displayed entity 904, DXCC 248 and REGION SY, alongside the independent zone and unsupported-band exclusion. One initial incremental build was run while the entry header was being edited and its test crashed; rebuilding after edits completed and the subsequent focused and suite runs passed.

This is not a complete official entity-list or scoring implementation. Missing, ambiguous or incorrect REGION values and any direct special codes still require operator review; the application does not validate the full list or reconcile conflicting source data. Official organiser-tool import, real QSO, physical UI, Windows/Linux and the other outstanding G02/desktop requirements remain unverified. The prior external browser prerequisite remains unresolved.

## 2026-11-23 — G02 CQ DX Marathon published entity-code check

The 2026 preliminary selector now checks entity codes against the 346 combined ADIF codes in the organiser's [entity list v1.1](https://dxmarathon.com/resources/countries/). An unknown code is held out of entity candidates and reported explicitly, while an independently valid CQ zone remains a zone candidate. Other years retain their prior preliminary behaviour. JSON and the desktop review identify the snapshot as v1.1 and as of 2023, as described by the organiser; the source CSV SHA-256 is recorded beside the compiled codes. No recorded QSO or review ADIF is changed.

A/B: with the previous selector a synthetic code 999 was counted as a second entity; the new test expected one and failed. After the change it is excluded from entity candidates while its zone remains selectable; code 2 is excluded, special code 906 is accepted, and the same unknown code retains prior 2025 behaviour. A separate Python comparison verified that all 346 compiled codes exactly match the downloaded official CSV. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the cqmarathon, cqmarathon_db, database, awards and qmlpopups CTest suites passed 5/5; after the final snapshot label change the complete cqmarathon suite passed again. An offscreen actual-app probe showed the code 999 warning and its independent zone candidate; the preceding review scenario also passed.

The snapshot is described by its publisher as current as of 2023 and may not reflect later entity changes. This check does not establish validity for a particular QSO date or callsign, validate CQ-zone/entity combinations, or resolve missing and conflicting source data. Official organiser-tool import, physical UI, real logs, Windows/Linux and remaining desktop requirements remain open. The operator must inspect the alternatives in the official tool; no submission was made.

## 2026-11-23 — G02 CQ DX Marathon entity/zone review warnings

The 2026 preliminary review compares a recorded CQ zone with the zones associated with its candidate entity in the organiser's [published list v1.1](https://dxmarathon.com/resources/countries/). A mismatch produces a separate warning in local JSON and the desktop review. Because the data alone cannot establish which field is wrong, both entity and zone remain preliminary candidates; neither the QSO nor the review ADIF is rewritten. Unknown or missing entity codes and invalid or missing zones do not generate a misleading entity/zone mismatch. The rule is not extended to other years. The list is a 2023 snapshot according to its publisher and is not proof of QSO validity.

A/B: a synthetic Italy/DXCC 248, CQ zone 5 QSO produced no warning before this change and the new test failed. Afterwards it produces a warning while retaining both candidates; a valid US/zone 3 pair produces none. Tests also cover unknown and missing DXCC, the African Italy special entity with matching and mismatching zones, JSON and 2025 behaviour. An independent script compared every one of the 346 compiled code/zone masks to the downloaded source CSV. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest cqmarathon, cqmarathon_db, database, awards and qmlpopups passed 5/5; three actual-app offscreen scenarios passed for a mismatch warning, unknown entity and existing entity/zone review.

Warnings do not independently validate the operator's callsign, physical location, region, date, an authoritative zone boundary or official credit; list errors or changes can yield false warnings or missed conflicts. The preview still chooses the first eligible QSO for an entity or zone rather than offering local replacement, and an official organiser-tool import remains untested. Physical UI, real logs, Windows/Linux and the remaining desktop scope remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G02 CQ DX Marathon local candidate replacement

The preliminary selector exposes eligible alternative QSO separately by Marathon entity and by CQ zone, after the applicable scope, year, category, band and other existing filters. The review dialog can cycle to a different candidate within the same entity or the same zone without changing the other list. An entity already represented by multiple selected QSO does not select the same QSO twice. This is a temporary in-process review: refreshing resets it, the existing JSON export still recomputes the automatic selection, and the organiser-review ADIF still exports all profile/year alternatives. The UI states those boundaries; the original QSO are not changed. Exclusion messages refer to the automatic selection.

A/B: a focused test initially failed to compile because the selector exposed no alternative lists. After implementation three eligible synthetic QSO for one entity and two zones were available as alternatives, while an ineligible 2m QSO was not. An offscreen actual-app probe changed the entity choice from QSO 1 to 2 while the zone stayed at 1, then changed that zone choice to QSO 3; the database still held all three QSO. The interaction was programmatic through the same replacement function used by the buttons, not physical mouse/keyboard input. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest cqmarathon, cqmarathon_db, database, awards and qmlpopups passed 5/5; five app scenarios passed, including previous review warnings and review-ADIF export.

The manual choices are not persisted or represented in the current JSON export and are not automatically carried into the external submission; the operator must make final choices in the official tool from the alternatives ADIF. Large-log performance of passing all alternatives to QML, physical UI, actual organiser import, real data and Windows/Linux remain unverified. Other G02 and desktop scope remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G02 CQ DX Marathon reviewed JSON export

The JSON work export now records the user's independently reviewed entity and zone candidates, together with eligible alternatives, the selection kind and the fact that exclusions describe the automatic selection. Before writing, the controller reloads the current log, checks the preview fingerprint (including log identity, scope, selected data and alternatives), and validates that choices are still eligible, have no duplicates within a list, and preserve the original entity and zone coverage. An invalid, changed or unavailable preview is refused with a visible error. The original automatic-export API remains available, and both JSON paths now reject the open database and its SQLite sidecars as destinations. The organiser-review ADIF remains the separate all-alternatives format.

A/B: the new unit test initially failed to compile because the selector could not apply reviewed choices. After implementation it exported a different entity and zone QSO, and rejected duplicate, missing, ineligible and coverage-changing IDs without partial mutation. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest cqmarathon, cqmarathon_db, database, awards and qmlpopups passed 5/5. An offscreen actual-app probe exported and re-parsed reviewed JSON with entity QSO 2 and zone QSO 3; it also refused a deliberately incorrect snapshot, the log database as destination through both APIs, and a changed year after the preview, preserving the original database. Three app scenarios including replacement and ADIF export passed; the final protection and year-guard scenario was rerun after its changes.

The JSON is a local working format, not a verified organiser submission. The snapshot test used an intentionally incorrect token and a changed UI year; an external concurrent database edit during export was not exercised. Database path protection compares absolute and canonical paths but does not prove safety against hard links or replacement races. Manual choices are preserved in exported JSON, but import/resume of that JSON is not yet implemented; the in-process choice resets on refresh. Real organiser-tool import, physical UI, large logs, Windows/Linux and remaining desktop scope remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G02 CQ DX Marathon reviewed JSON restore

A reviewed JSON export now includes the preview fingerprint. After generating a fresh preview for the same log, profile, edition and settings, the operator can reopen the JSON and restore entity and zone choices. The controller recomputes the fingerprint from current selector data and the log identity, validates the file format and scope and applies only eligible unique IDs preserving entity/zone coverage; the view is changed only after complete validation. Files from the preceding implementation without a fingerprint are refused because QSO IDs alone cannot identify the log safely. Automatic JSON files are not treated as reviewed files. A 128 MiB import limit bounds file reading.

A/B: the round-trip unit test initially failed to compile because reviewed choices could not be restored. The first full run then exposed an incorrectly scoped synthetic fixture (entries had profile 0 while the test selected profile 7); after fixing the fixture, CTest cqmarathon, cqmarathon_db, database, awards and qmlpopups passed 5/5 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. The unit test also refuses missing/mismatched fingerprints, a different profile and a fractional QSO ID without changing the result. An offscreen app test saved choices for entity QSO 2 and zone QSO 3, refreshed and restored them, then restarted after an actual SQLite change to a selected alternative; import was refused and automatic choices remained unchanged. Existing reviewed-export and replacement app scenarios passed. The final malformed-ID assertion was rerun separately and passed.

The fingerprint covers selector inputs represented in the preview, not every field in the log, and is a consistency check rather than an authenticated signature. Changes to other QSO fields can remain undetected. Reviews are restored from a chosen work file, not automatically persisted; imports above 128 MiB are refused. The JSON is not an official submission format. Physical UI, very large logs, real organiser import, Windows/Linux and remaining desktop scope remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G02 CQ DX Marathon operating-location evidence

The preliminary review collects per-QSO `MY_GRIDSQUARE` values for contacts passing the current edition, profile, category and other general filters. It displays distinct valid four-character squares and counts missing and invalid own-grid data. Multiple squares produce a review warning, not a disqualification: grid boundaries, data errors and actual locations require human checking. Matching squares also do not prove a single physical location. The [2026 rules](https://www.dxmarathon.com/rules/2026/) require operation from one location. Data are read from the QSO ADIF extras, not inferred from the current station profile; the QSO and organiser-review ADIF are not changed.

A/B: the new DB fixture initially failed to compile because no location evidence was exposed. After implementation, synthetic QSO with JM75FV and JN58TD produced two grid squares and a review warning; a third missing grid was counted separately, and a non-string field was counted invalid. CTest cqmarathon, cqmarathon_db, database, awards and qmlpopups passed 5/5 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. An offscreen actual-app probe displayed both squares and the missing count; the previous zone review and saved-review round trip passed. A subsequent app run tested the final compatibility path: a synthetic file reconstructed with the immediately preceding fingerprint representation was restored with current evidence rechecked; after an actual QSO change the same file was rejected. This reconstruction is not a test using a binary from the prior commit.

Previous reviewed files containing the immediately preceding fingerprint remain restorable when their old selector inputs still match; the UI asks the user to review current location evidence because that old fingerprint did not include it. Older files without a fingerprint remain refused. New fingerprints include recorded grids and location summaries. This evidence does not interpret MY_VUCC_GRIDS, prove exact coordinates, check distant antennas or remote receiver location, or establish an official rule violation. Missing data remain unresolved, and physical UI, real logs, official organiser import, Windows/Linux and remaining desktop scope remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G02 CQ DX Marathon single-band preview

The desktop Marathon preview now offers all bands or a specific supported 160m–6m band, independently of the mixed/CW/phone/digital mode filter. The core validates the filter, reports out-of-band QSO as excluded from that preview and computes entity and zone alternatives only within the chosen band. Reviewed JSON preserves and validates the band when present; all-band JSON omits the optional field so existing all-band preview fingerprints and older reviewed work files retain their representation. The separate organiser-review ADIF continues to export all profile/year QSO for review. The [2026 rules](https://www.dxmarathon.com/rules/2026/) describe single-band and single-mode designation and say other-band contacts in a submission are ignored.

A/B: the new unit fixture initially failed to compile because the selection rules had no band field. After implementation 20m CW retained one entity and zone, excluded a 40m CW QSO and a 20m SSB QSO, while all-band CW retained two entities and 20m mixed retained two. The test rejects 2m, accepts case-insensitive band input, verifies reviewed JSON round trip and refuses opening a 20m file as an all-band review. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest cqmarathon, cqmarathon_db, database, awards and qmlpopups passed 5/5. The offscreen app produced and reloaded a 20m reviewed JSON and showed the band exclusion; previous all-band restore, export and ADIF app scenarios passed.

The band filter uses the recorded ADIF band, not exact frequency or licence privileges. It does not designate the entry on the official site, validate all category/class rules or demonstrate an official import. The all-year review ADIF deliberately remains unfiltered. The app tests were synthetic and programmatic; physical UI, real logs, organiser-tool import, Windows/Linux and remaining desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G02 CQ DX Marathon 2026 entity limit

The regular 2026 preview requires exactly one candidate per entity while retaining independent zone candidates. A request with a larger entity limit is rejected instead of producing an inflated preliminary entity count. The desktop field is disabled for 2026, displays one, and resets to one when switching to that edition; its prior adjustable behaviour for other years remains available without asserting those editions' official rules. The [2026 regular scoring rules](https://www.dxmarathon.com/rules/2026/) count each country and zone once. This is not an implementation of the separate DX Marathon Challenge, which has different per-band scoring.

A/B: before the change a synthetic 2026 request with limit two was considered valid; the new test failed. Afterwards it is rejected, while limit one selects one entity and two independently valid zones. A 2025 synthetic fixture retains the earlier two-candidate behaviour. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest cqmarathon, cqmarathon_db, database, awards and qmlpopups passed 5/5. An offscreen actual-app scenario switched from 2025/limit 2 to 2026/limit 1, observed the disabled field, one entity and two zones; the single-band and reviewed-JSON scenarios also passed.

The view remains preliminary and does not establish all eligibility or official credit. Entries in the separate Challenge, physical keyboard/mouse interaction, real organiser-tool import, Windows/Linux and remaining desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G02 CQ DX Marathon recorded propagation

For the 2026 preliminary selection, recorded `PROP_MODE` RPT, ECH and IRL are held out because they describe repeater or Internet-linked paths, and INTERNET and SAT are conservatively held for operator review because their actual route is not established by the field alone. Both entity and zone candidates are affected; other editions retain previous behaviour. The underlying QSO and the all-QSO review ADIF are unchanged. The view explains that missing propagation does not prove direct operation; direct LOS, EME and unreported propagation remain preliminary candidates. A digital voice mode alone is not assumed to mean a repeater. The [2026 rules](https://www.dxmarathon.com/rules/2026/) prohibit repeaters and relays; [ADIF propagation definitions](https://www.adif.org/315/ADIF_315.htm#Propagation_Mode_Enumeration) distinguish these recorded modes.

A/B: before the change seven synthetic QSO with different recorded propagation modes all supplied a zone, and the new test expecting two failed. Afterwards RPT, ECH, IRL, INTERNET and SAT are held out, while EME and unspecified propagation remain. A focused test covers lowercase RPT, direct digital voice with LOS and unchanged 2025 behaviour. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest cqmarathon, cqmarathon_db, database, awards and qmlpopups passed 5/5. Four offscreen app scenarios passed, including visible RPT exclusion and the existing review ADIF export. A further app run restored a synthetic prior-format fingerprint for eligible EME candidates, then changed one candidate to RPT and verified the old review was rejected; the relay exclusion scenario passed again. This compatibility fixture reconstructs the old fingerprint and is not a run of the old binary.

The conservative INTERNET/SAT decision is not an organiser ruling for every possible path. Empty or wrong propagation can hide relay operation, while a direct digital mode may be legitimate; no network, RF, satellite or repeater path was observed. A previous reviewed JSON is accepted only if its earlier selector representation still matches and the choices remain eligible; otherwise a new review is required. Actual organiser-tool import, physical UI, real logs, Windows/Linux and remaining desktop scope remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G02 CQ DX Marathon recorded frequency review

The Marathon preview now reads the recorded `FREQ` in MHz and compares it with the recorded band using the application's existing ADIF band edges. A conflicting or invalid frequency generates a review warning without deciding which field is correct or removing either entity or zone candidates. Missing frequency remains unknown. The candidate review shows the recorded frequency when available, and current reviewed JSON fingerprints include it. The [2026 rules](https://www.dxmarathon.com/rules/2026/) require contacts to comply with the operator's allowed frequencies; a generic ADIF band interval alone cannot establish that eligibility.

A/B: before the change a synthetic 20m QSO at 7.074 MHz produced no conflict warning and the new test failed. Afterwards it is flagged while its zone remains available; 14.074 MHz is accepted as consistent, an absent frequency is not treated as zero, the 14.35 MHz edge is accepted, 14.350001 MHz is flagged, and an invalid stored value is reported. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest cqmarathon, cqmarathon_db, database, awards and qmlpopups passed 5/5. Four offscreen actual-app scenarios passed, including visible frequency conflict, single-band selection and restoration of synthetic pre-frequency and pre-propagation review fingerprints. Restoring an older fingerprint displays a notice to recheck current frequency, propagation and location evidence; these fixtures reconstruct prior representations rather than running old binaries.

The check is preliminary for all years and does not validate national allocations, 60m channelisation, licence class, transmission bandwidth or the actual RF frequency. Its broad band edges are not proof of authorised operation. Reviewed JSON from older versions can be restored only if the corresponding earlier snapshot and current eligible choices still match; the organiser-review ADIF remains unchanged. Real logs, physical UI, organiser import, Windows/Linux and remaining desktop scope remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G02 CQ DX Marathon unknown-mode handling

The 2026 preliminary selector no longer assumes every unrecognised non-empty mode is digital. For a single-mode filter it holds such QSO out with an explicit classification-review reason; in mixed mode it retains them with a warning. A missing mode is held out. A bounded set of recognised mode names combines existing application modes and common ADIF base modes; other years keep their prior behaviour. This does not change the shared mode classifier or the all-QSO review ADIF. ADIF defines base modes and submodes, including MFSK/FT4 and DIGITALVOICE/DMR ([ADIF 3.1.7](https://www.adif.org/317/ADIF_317.htm)); the current Marathon grouping uses the recorded base MODE, not an independent validation of each submode.

A/B: a synthetic unknown mode and FT8 produced two zone candidates in the digital filter before this change; the new test expected one and failed. Afterwards digital retains FT8 only and flags the unknown mode; mixed retains both with a warning, and a missing mode is held out. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest cqmarathon, cqmarathon_db, database, awards and qmlpopups passed 5/5. The offscreen app showed the digital exclusion and mixed warning; single-band and recorded-relay scenarios also passed. A later focused DB test verified MFSK/FT4 is grouped as digital and DIGITALVOICE/DMR as phone under the existing local grouping.

The recognised list is not a complete current ADIF mode/submode registry and the local category assignment is not an organiser ruling; supported but unlisted names may need review. Image and digital-voice categorisation, submode validity, actual two-way exchange and licence privileges remain unverified. Reviews whose previous selection or warnings change may require a fresh preview. Real organiser import, physical UI, Windows/Linux and remaining desktop scope remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G03 external QSL-plan invalidation

The QSL planner now binds each asynchronous build to the open log identity, connection generation, main-connection SQLite `data_version` and in-process log generation. The worker opens a read-only connection; a changed version before delivery discards the result. While a plan is active, a one-second check marks externally committed changes stale and the existing desktop flow schedules a rebuild. Alternative-page access and paper-queue confirmation also check the current version immediately, so they can refuse a stale snapshot before the periodic UI update. The existing per-QSO queue guards remain in place.

A/B: an offscreen actual-app test built a plan for synthetic QSO K1AAA, then a separate process committed a change to K1CHANGED. Before this increment the plan stayed visible and the test timed out without a stale signal. Afterwards the plan was marked stale, alternative access returned `Plan is no longer current`, and the recalculated plan showed K1CHANGED. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest qslplanner, database, awards and qmlpopups passed 4/4; the existing QSL planner app scenario for benefits, settings, cost, guarded queue and invalidation also passed. The external-change app test was rerun after adding the immediate guard check and passed again.

SQLite `data_version` detects commits from other connections, not arbitrary filesystem replacement or uncommitted changes; the displayed plan can remain visible until the next one-second poll. The immediate check and queue's per-QSO guards do not form one transaction with every later external award/route change, so a remaining check-to-write race is not ruled out. An external commit specifically during a long-running build, large-log performance, physical UI, Windows/Linux and actual QSL service traffic were not tested. G03 and the remaining desktop scope remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G03/G05 paper QSL worker log identity

File-backed paper QSL batch operations now capture the current log identity when accepted, open an existing database without creating or migrating it, and verify that identity inside each write transaction before changing QSL state. The existing QSO UUID, revision, sent/received and route checks remain. A changed identity causes an explicit failure rather than applying the operation to that log. The separate email-completion path is not changed by this increment.

A/B: a file-backed test held an exclusive SQLite transaction, started a guarded queue operation, changed the log identity while the worker waited, and committed. Before this change the worker queued the QSO and the new test failed. Afterwards it reports the identity change and does not queue the QSO. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest qslcardcontroller, qslplanner, database and qmlpopups passed 4/4; two offscreen actual-app QSL planner scenarios covering external-change invalidation and the existing queue workflow passed. After adding a repeated identity check between multi-row transaction blocks, the focused identity-race, concurrent-sent-state and ordinary file-backed queue tests passed again.

The race fixture changes the identity row under a database lock; it does not simulate every filesystem replacement, process crash or cloned database with the same identity. The repeated multi-block identity check has not been exercised by a dedicated inter-block race. The planner's benefit calculation and the later queue operation still do not share a single transaction across all QSO; unrelated changes may occur between the plan check and queue write. Real QSL delivery, physical UI, Windows/Linux and remaining desktop scope remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G03/G05 QSL email origin identity

QSL email preparation and delayed callbook callbacks now bind the batch to the original log identity and open generation as well as its path. A changed identity stops remaining sends. A confirmed Cloud or SMTP response carries the original identity into the asynchronous sent-status update, which opens only an existing log and verifies identity inside the same transaction as the QSO UUID check. If the confirmed send cannot be recorded, the existing warning explicitly distinguishes delivery from local status. Preparation uses a read-only connection and checks the identity before consuming the snapshot.

A/B: with a loopback fake Cloud and a synthetic `.invalid` recipient, changing the log identity during delayed address lookup previously allowed the local HTTP request; the new test failed. A separate test changed identity while the fake server's response was pending: the previous code marked the QSO sent, while the new code leaves it queued and reports that the confirmed send could not be recorded. The A/B for the second test rebuilt the immediately preceding controller source with the new fixture. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest qslcardcontroller, qslplanner, database, qsl, qslcontroller and qmlpopups passed 6/6; two offscreen planner scenarios passed. After the final error-text adjustment, the two identity tests and the existing test that records a confirmed send in the original log after switching logs passed again. All network traffic in these tests was loopback; no real email was sent.

This does not establish exactly-once email delivery or resolve ambiguous network outcomes. A file replaced at the same path while the original SQLite connection remains open, a cloned identity, the interval immediately after a pre-send check, physical UI, real mail providers and Windows/Linux require further verification. The check on an already in-flight send prevents an incorrect local status update but cannot retract a delivered message. Other G03/G05 and desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G03/G05 QSL email file replacement

File-backed QSL email batches now record a native file identity in addition to the log UUID and connection generation. On macOS/Linux this uses the device and inode; the Windows implementation uses the volume serial and file index. Preparation and pending lookup callbacks compare the current path to the captured file; if it changes, remaining sends stop. A confirmed response carries that file identity into the sent-status worker, which checks the path before and after opening the existing database and still validates the log and QSO identities transactionally. An in-memory log continues to use its log identity and open generation. Failure to obtain a file identity prevents starting a file-backed email batch.

A/B: using synthetic temporary SQLite databases and a loopback fake Cloud, two tests replaced the open log at the same path with a `VACUUM INTO` clone retaining the same log and QSO UUIDs. Before this change, the delayed lookup sent a request to the fake server, and a separate already-confirmed request marked the clone as sent. Afterwards the pending lookup stops before its request, and the confirmed-send handler leaves the clone queued and reports that the status could not be recorded. The second A/B rebuilt the previous controller source with the new test. An initial fixture failed because an active PRAGMA query prevented `VACUUM`; finishing the query fixed the fixture before the baseline run. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest qslcardcontroller, qslplanner, database, qsl, qslcontroller and qmlpopups passed 6/6; two offscreen QSL planner scenarios also passed. All network traffic was loopback; no real email was sent.

This is not an exactly-once delivery guarantee: a message already in flight cannot be retracted, and path identity checks around opening are not atomic against every possible replacement race or file-ID reuse. Network filesystems and the Windows implementation were not exercised; platforms that prohibit renaming an open SQLite file skip the replacement fixtures. This increment covers email, not all paper-card operations or other workers. Physical UI, real providers, Linux and the remaining desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G03/G05 paper QSL file replacement during queued work

File-backed paper QSL operations now capture the native file identity at acceptance, check it before opening the worker database, inside the write transaction and immediately before each batch commit. A mismatch rolls back the active block and reports a file change. The same transaction-level check also applies to confirmed email status writes, which already carry their origin file identity. Existing log UUID, QSO UUID/revision and QSL state guards remain. No email or QSL is sent by the paper queue operation.

A/B: a synthetic file-backed test held the card worker busy on a loopback fake-email completion, queued a second paper operation, then replaced the open database path with a SQLite clone retaining the same log and QSO UUIDs before the worker opened it. Before this change the paper operation marked the clone queued and the test failed; afterwards the clone remains unchanged and the operation reports a file change.
