On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest qslcardcontroller, qslplanner, database, qsl, qslcontroller and qmlpopups passed 6/6. The existing identity-change, concurrent-sent-state and email-clone tests passed, as did two offscreen QSL planner scenarios. The fake Cloud was loopback only; no real message was sent.

The file token is captured when the operation is accepted. If the path was already replaced before that moment while the application's older SQLite connection stayed open, the token alone does not bind the path to that connection; this case remains open. Checks immediately before commit also cannot eliminate every filesystem replacement race or file-ID reuse. The controlled fixture tests one queued-worker ordering, not replacement during the commit itself; Windows, Linux, network filesystems, physical UI and the remaining desktop roadmap remain unverified.

## 2026-11-23 — G03/G05 QSL log replacement before acceptance

`LogDatabase` now retains the native file identity observed when each file-backed connection opens and exposes a check against its current path; closing clears that token, reopening captures a new one, and in-memory connections remain supported. QSL paper operations and email batches require the path still to match that open-connection token before accepting work. Their existing worker-side UUID, file-token and transaction checks remain. Native file identification is shared with the existing QSL checks and does not change the database schema.

A/B: two file-backed fixtures replaced the path of an open SQLite log with a `VACUUM INTO` clone retaining the same log and QSO UUIDs before invoking the action. Previously the paper queue marked the clone queued and the email path sent a request to a loopback fake server; both new tests failed. Afterwards the queue and email actions refuse the mismatched file before work starts, and the prior queued-worker, delayed-lookup and confirmed-response replacement tests pass. A DB lifecycle fixture verifies mismatch after replacement, failure after close, new token after reopening, read-only/existing read-write opens and in-memory operation. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest database, migration, qslcardcontroller, qslplanner, qsl, qslcontroller, awards and qmlpopups passed 8/8; two offscreen QSL planner scenarios passed. The final additional read-only/read-write lifecycle assertions passed in a focused rerun. Network fixtures used loopback only; no real email was sent.

The token is read from the path immediately after SQLite opens it, not obtained atomically from SQLite's actual file handle. A replacement in that narrow interval, in-place modification retaining the file ID, file-ID reuse, network filesystem semantics and races after individual checks remain possible. Other database consumers are not automatically guarded by the new API. Windows/Linux builds and behaviour, physical UI, real providers and the remaining desktop roadmap remain unverified. Platforms that prohibit replacing an open SQLite file skip the replacement fixture.

## 2026-11-23 — G03 QSL plan and replaced log file

The QSL planner now verifies that the path still names the file observed when the active `LogDatabase` connection opened. It performs that check when starting a build, in the read-only worker, before publishing its result, during periodic invalidation and on immediate plan-current checks. If the file changed, the UI requests reopening the log rather than building a plan from a different file with the same logical UUID.

A/B: an offscreen actual-app fixture built a plan, then replaced the open log path with a `VACUUM INTO` clone retaining the same log and QSO UUIDs. Before this change the plan became stale in this macOS/SQLite run but the rebuild reported only `Cannot verify the log snapshot`; the new test expected an explicit file-replacement error and failed. Afterwards it marks the plan stale, refuses alternatives and reports `Log file changed; reopen it before planning`. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest database, qslplanner and qmlpopups passed 3/3; three offscreen app scenarios passed for file replacement, an external SQL commit and the existing planner/queue flow. The baseline observation does not prove that every SQLite platform detects replacement via `data_version`.

The native token is captured from the path immediately after SQLite opens it, so this does not close the open/stat race, detect in-place content replacement with the same file ID, or guarantee correctness on network filesystems. An external commit during a long-running worker, actual QSL traffic, physical UI and Windows/Linux remain unverified. Other planner functions and the remaining desktop roadmap are still open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 durable serial requester journal

Schema 27 adds a per-log requester journal complementary to the existing authority reservation journal. A request records scope, requester, request ID, expected authority and minimum before a grant is accepted. One requester may have at most one pending or granted request per scope; repeated identical requests and grants are idempotent, while changed parameters, wrong authority, conflicting grants and reuse of the same granted serial on that authority/scope in this log are rejected. A granted serial is bound once to an explicit use ID; repeating the same use is idempotent and a different use is refused. Pending/granted requests can be queried after restart. Operations use `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` and database constraints; no serial is allocated locally merely because a response is missing.

A/B: the new requester lifecycle test initially failed to compile because these APIs did not exist. After implementation, file-backed tests covered a lost authority response, restart, retry with the same request ID, duplicate and conflicting grants, wrong authority, consumption replay and refusal to bind a grant to a second use. Three separate requester databases and one authority database simulated delayed/reordered and duplicate responses; this was a direct API simulation, not ContestNet traffic. A migration fixture upgraded schema 26 to 27 while preserving an existing QSO and authority reservation; an injected SQLite trigger left a failed grant pending and a retry succeeded. Two concurrent connections attempting different active requests for the same requester/scope produced exactly one accepted request. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller and qmlpopups passed 5/5; the final requester-concurrency fixture passed separately. The desktop app built, but does not yet use this journal.

This is a local foundation, not a network guarantee: ContestNet still uses its existing volatile serial protocol, does not transmit these request IDs, authenticate an authority, recover a pending request, enforce partition behaviour or bind a consumed use ID atomically to QSO creation. The three-node fixture did not transmit packets or simulate LAN failures. The identity of an authority is only a recorded string until a protocol authenticates it. No previously sent serial is renumbered. Schema 27 is additive; preserve a verified pre-upgrade backup for rollback. Real LAN/SO2R, crashes during commit, Windows/Linux and remaining G10/desktop requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 explicit serial message contract

Added a separate experimental request/grant contract over the existing `DXLNET` envelope. Requests carry an explicit scope, requester, authority, request ID and minimum; the authority validates routing and commits the existing durable reservation before constructing a grant. The requester checks its stored request and the configured scope/authority before accepting a positive integer grant. Retries and duplicate grants use the durable journals. Invalid version, routing, numeric values and conflicting parameters are refused. The contract is documented in [contest-serial-protocol.md](contest-serial-protocol.md).

A/B: the new three-requester test initially failed to compile because this contract did not exist. After implementation three separate requester databases and one authority database exercised packed/unpacked messages, reordered responses, duplicates, a missing response, restart of requester and authority, and the same-number retry. Separate malformed-frame cases cover wrong scope/sender, altered minimum, fractional/zero/string/oversized numbers, wrong protocol version and conflicting responses. A loopback UDP fixture sent an actual request to `ContestNet`, deliberately dropped the first grant, reopened the authority database and accepted the same serial on retry. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest contestnet, database, migration, netcontroller and qmlpopups passed 5/5 and the desktop app built; the final malformed-version and UDP tests passed again.

This is not yet connected to `NetController`, activation/QSO entry or the existing shared-serial controls. The three-requester test uses packed messages in-process; the UDP fixture uses one requester and a test callback, not three deployed stations. Existing UDP sender IDs are unauthenticated, so matching fields are routing checks, not protection against spoofing. There is no authority election/fencing, negotiated scope, automatic retry, atomic QSO binding or tested partition/rejoin workflow. The existing volatile serial path and its warning are unchanged. Real LAN/SO2R, Windows/Linux and the remaining G10/desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 experimental authority receiver

The station network controller now offers an explicitly configured experimental authority receiver for `serial_request_v3`, disabled by default and reset at process restart. Only a station whose own ID matches the configured authority serves the configured scope. It uses a separate existing-log connection on a single background worker, commits a reservation before sending a grant, and checks the open log's generation, logical identity and native file identity before returning the result. Reconfiguration and network restart invalidate pending replies. The panel exposes session configuration with warnings; the existing QSO serial path is unchanged. A busy authority drops additional requests, which require a sender retry.

A/B: the new controller test initially failed to compile because the authority configuration and grant signal did not exist. Afterwards a loopback UDP sender reached the actual controller, retried the same request, and obtained the same grant signal after closing and reopening the authority database and controller. Wrong scope, another configured authority and disabled configuration produced no grant. This observes the controller's send path via a signal, not receipt by a second controller. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the desktop app built and CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller and qmlpopups passed 5/5. The QML test checks existing popup routes; opening the new configuration dialog was not exercised interactively.

The requester controller, automatic retry and atomic QSO binding remain unimplemented. Sender IDs are unauthenticated, multiple authorities can allocate duplicates, and requests dropped while busy are not queued. An already running worker can commit after configuration changes, although its response is suppressed; a later retry must use the same request ID. File checks do not eliminate all path replacement races. No real LAN, multiple deployed stations, SO2R, Windows/Linux or physical UI was tested; the desktop roadmap remains open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 experimental requester grant receiver

The network controller can now receive an experimental serial grant for an already-persisted request, validate it against its configured scope, requester and authority, and commit it through a worker connection. It uses the same open-log identity and generation guards as the authority receiver. The receiver is inactive unless the experimental scope and authority are configured; a packet from another sender is ignored. The controller still has no action to create or transmit the request or display/use the resulting serial.

A/B: a new controller fixture first failed to compile because the grant-acceptance signal was absent. Afterwards it persisted a pending request in a temporary SQLite database, sent a wrong-sender UDP grant over loopback and confirmed the request remained pending, then sent the configured sender's grant and verified it was committed and survived closing and reopening the database. This tests a real requester controller with a simulated sender, not two running controllers or a real LAN. The existing authority test still passes. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the desktop app built; CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller and qmlpopups passed 5/5.

Sender IDs are not authenticated; the test's wrong ID check is routing validation, not spoofing protection. There is no automatic request/retry or QSO integration, and packets received while the worker is busy are dropped. A worker already running during reconfiguration may commit to its original log while its notification is suppressed; native-file checks do not close every replacement race. Real LAN, SO2R, Windows/Linux, actual UI interaction and the other roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 experimental requester send and manual retry

The experimental panel can request a serial with a minimum. The controller opens the original file-backed log on a worker, persists a request before sending and manually retries the same pending request ID; a different minimum or authority is refused while it remains active. The displayed state distinguishes pending from a received experimental grant. No serial is generated locally when the authority is unavailable. The grant is not connected to QSO entry and retry is manual.

A/B: the new requester test initially failed to compile because the request method and send signal did not exist. Afterwards it verified persistence before the observed send, a repeated request with the same ID, refusal of a changed minimum and pending recovery after reopening the database. A two-controller loopback test dropped the first request while the authority was off, started it and delivered a retry and grant using a test relay that repacks the controllers' emitted frames onto separate local UDP ports. This exercises both controller receivers but does not test the normal broadcast path. An initial attempt to use loopback broadcast on a shared port did not complete and was interrupted; the relay fixture replaced it. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the desktop app built and CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller and qmlpopups passed 5/5; the final changed-minimum assertion passed in a focused controller rerun.

No automatic retry, grant-to-QSO atomicity, partition fencing or authenticated sender exists. A busy worker drops incoming packets; the operator must retry. Authority configuration is session-only and an already running worker may commit after reconfiguration while its callback is suppressed. Filesystem identity checks do not remove all replacement races. The panel changes were compiled but not exercised interactively; real broadcast LAN, three deployed stations, SO2R, Windows/Linux and the rest of the desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 automatic in-session request retry

While an experimental request is pending, the controller retries every two seconds, consulting its durable journal and keeping the same request ID and parameters. A matching committed grant stops retry. Reconfiguration or network restart stops the timer; a reopened or replaced log also stops it rather than sending from a different log. After an application restart, configuration is still session-only: the operator must configure and request again to resume a stored pending request. There is no fallback allocation or QSO binding.

A/B: the two-controller fixture dropped the first request while the authority was down, started the authority and waited without clicking retry. Before the change it failed with no accepted grant; afterwards the same stored request is resent, the test relay delivers it and the grant is committed, with no further retries observed for 2.5 seconds. Another fixture checks that reopening the log stops retries while preserving the pending request. Both controllers use separate SQLite files and UDP loopback through a test relay, not the normal broadcast path. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app built and CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller and qmlpopups passed 5/5.

This does not authenticate UDP, fence competing authorities, bind the grant atomically to a QSO or automatically resume after a process restart. Network setting changes require an explicit new request. The two-second retry has no backoff and an authority can still drop packets while busy; it is experimental traffic, not a proven production protocol. Log file checks do not eliminate all filesystem races. Real LAN and hardware, three deployed stations, UI interaction, Windows/Linux and the rest of the desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 transactional serial and QSO foundation

Added an explicit database operation that inserts a QSO and marks a matching granted serial consumed by that QSO's UUID in the same SQLite transaction. It rejects pending, mismatched authority, consumed and conflicting STX values; a failure to bind rolls the QSO insertion back. This is an additional API and does not change existing QSO entry or the volatile shared-serial path.

A/B: the new fixture initially failed to compile because the transactional operation did not exist. Afterwards it verified pending rejection, injected a failure in the serial update and observed no QSO and an intact grant, rejected conflicting STX, committed a QSO with the grant, refused reuse and checked persistence after reopen. A separate concurrent fixture used two SQLite connections to bind one grant to different QSOs; exactly one insertion succeeded. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the desktop app built and CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller and qmlpopups passed 5/5; the final concurrency fixture passed in a focused rerun.

The controller and UI do not call this operation yet; an operator cannot use it from QSO entry. No guarantee is made about what was transmitted before logging. Crash injection during SQLite commit, external file replacement, real LAN, SO2R, physical UI, Windows/Linux and the remaining desktop roadmap have not been verified. Existing non-transactional QSO paths are unchanged.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 explicit experimental serial in compact manual entry

The compact manual QSO panel can select one experimental grant explicitly during a serial-enabled contest. It shows the selected serial, scope and authority before logging, warns that UDP IDs are unauthenticated and multiple authorities can collide, and keeps the selected grant identity for validation at save. If that selection changed or no grant exists, save is refused. The controller bypasses volatile serial allocation for this explicit action and inserts the QSO together with the grant consumption in one database transaction. Selection resets after a successful save; requesting the next grant remains explicit. The existing normal path remains available. A database guard also refuses a purely numeric `STX_STRING` that conflicts with the grant; compound text is not parsed.

A/B: an actual-app offscreen test first logged through the manual controller with the experimental flag; before the change the grant remained `granted` rather than being bound to the QSO. Afterwards a panel probe selected the shown grant, saved the QSO and verified its STX and UUID against the consumed request. It also checked that a stale selected number and a missing grant are refused and that a subsequent ordinary manual QSO still logs. A separate A/B fixture showed that a conflicting numeric sent exchange was previously accepted and is now rejected without consuming the grant. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app built; CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, activation, newqsofields, newqsotuning and qmlpopups passed 8/8, and the focused actual-app probe passed. The first newqsotuning run could not start because its executable had not been built; building `tst_maplayers` resolved that environment prerequisite before the passing run.

The panel probe uses offscreen QML and programmatically selects the control, not physical keyboard or radio transmission. No authentication, authority fencing, automatic post-restart resume or guarantee of globally unique numbers exists. Compound sent exchanges, other manual forms, digital intake, network import and SO2R do not use or fully validate this grant path. Database file identity checks have filesystem race limits. Real LAN, hardware, Windows/Linux and the remaining desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 experimental grant in contest entry and macros

Both the contest desk entry panel and the separate contest window can explicitly select a grant for one QSO, display its serial, scope and authority, and pass the captured selection to the existing transactional manual-QSO path. The displayed sent number and contest macro context use the same selected serial. Before a contest macro send, each surface checks that the selected grant still matches the current journal; a stale selection stops the send and asks for review. The normal serial path remains available when the option is not selected. Both surfaces warn that the sender identity is unauthenticated and separate authorities can collide.

A/B: actual-app offscreen probes for the contest desk and contest window first failed because these surfaces had no experimental serial controls. After implementation each selected a grant, observed serial 10 in its macro context, attempted a stale-grant macro send and observed it blocked, then logged a QSO with serial 10 bound to the consumed grant. These probes programmatically manipulate QML; they do not transmit radio signals. The compact-manual probe also passed again. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the desktop app built and CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, activation, newqsofields, newqsotuning and qmlpopups passed 8/8.

This does not prove CW, PTT, ESM timing or SO2R on hardware. A user can transmit outside these guarded controls or abandon a contact after transmitting; the software cannot establish what was actually sent. Compound sent exchanges are not parsed, UDP is unauthenticated, multiple authorities are unfenced, retry is not automatically resumed after restart, and other QSO intake paths remain separate. Real multi-station LAN, physical UI, Windows/Linux and the remaining desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 recovery after explicit reconfiguration

When the operator configures the experimental scope and authority and the network is running, the controller checks the existing log on its worker. It resumes a matching pending request with its saved ID and minimum, displays an existing grant, and refuses to resume a request naming another authority. With no saved request it creates nothing. Configuration remains session-only and must be supplied again after restarting the application. Recovery is bound to the original log identity and connection generation.

The retry path also now requires its original request ID to remain active. This closes a case where a grant consumed through another connection caused the timer to create and send a new request unintentionally. A/B: on the previous source, a synthetic external consumption caused a second request frame; with this change the timer stops, no replacement is created and the journal remains consumed. A separate A/B fixture initially observed no automatic send after restarting with a pending request; afterwards it observed the saved ID and minimum resumed, checked a mismatched authority is refused, and confirmed an empty scope creates nothing. Traffic used loopback only. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the desktop app built; CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, activation, newqsofields, newqsotuning and qmlpopups passed 8/8, and focused compact and contest-entry app probes passed.

An additional A/B test showed that choosing the local node itself as authority previously created a request the node could not receive from itself. The controller now refuses this requester action before creating the request; serving other stations as the designated authority remains possible. Local authority allocation for its own operator is not implemented.

Recovery requires explicit reconfiguration after each application restart and does not authenticate UDP or fence competing authorities. A failed or busy packet can still be retried; checks are not atomic against every filesystem replacement race. Crash during SQLite commit, physical UI, real LAN/radio, SO2R, Windows/Linux and the remaining desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 authority's local serial request

The designated authority can now request a serial for its own operator using its local requester journal and the same durable reservation sequence used for remote stations. Request, reservation and grant are separate idempotent transactions; if a pending request and its reservation survive an interruption, recovery accepts that same number instead of allocating a new one. The action remains experimental, requires the network to be enabled and an explicit authority configuration, and uses the same QSO grant selection as a remote requester.

A/B: before this change the local authority request was refused and a saved local request could not recover. Afterwards controller fixtures allocated serial 10 locally, 11 to a simulated peer, recovered the local grant after reopening, consumed it, and allocated 12 locally. Another fixture reopened a database with a pending local request and a previously committed reservation and recovered that exact number; the next peer reservation received the following number. This models an interruption between transactions; it is not a process kill or crash during commit. Loopback was used for the enabled controller and the peer reservation was a direct database call. The desktop app built and CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, activation, newqsofields, newqsotuning and qmlpopups passed 8/8 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2; the three existing focused offscreen QSO entry cases passed.

The shared worker can drop incoming network packets while recovering a local request; remote requesters must retry. Separate authorities are not fenced and sender IDs are not authenticated. Physical UI, real multi-station LAN, hardware/SO2R, Windows/Linux, crash injection during commits and the remaining desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 three-node serial recovery fixture

An integration fixture now runs three actual `NetController` objects with separate file-backed logs: one authority and two requesters. A local UDP relay repacks their emitted request/grant frames onto separate loopback ports, allowing deterministic loss, duplication and reordering; it is not the production broadcast path. It initially drops both requests while the authority is down, forwards a duplicate request, withholds both grants, destroys and recreates the authority controller and reopens its database, then delivers one held grant twice while the other requester retries against the restarted authority. Both clients accept distinct serials with their original request IDs, and their granted states survive reopening their respective logs.

A/B: during the simulated partition both requester journals remain pending with no locally allocated number; after reconnect and authority restart the same requests become granted with distinct values. The focused fixture passed twice on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. The CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, activation, newqsotuning and qmlpopups groups passed 7/7. The test only covers serial request/grant traffic; it does not exercise QSO edits, deletions or peer convergence.

The relay makes packet delivery controllable but does not establish real broadcast routing, authentication, multi-host behaviour or hardware interoperability. Its restart destroys the controller and reopens SQLite, not an operating-system crash during commit. This is one tested loss/reordering scenario, not an exhaustive partition model or proof against multiple authorities. Real LAN, SO2R, Windows/Linux, physical UI and the remaining desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 delayed grant after consumption

The requester controller now rechecks the active journal entry before publishing a grant received from a worker, and also rechecks grants recovered or obtained by its request worker. A duplicate grant for an already consumed request remains an idempotent protocol replay but is no longer reported as a currently available grant or emitted as a newly accepted grant. This prevents an old delayed UDP packet from replacing the displayed status of a newer active grant.

A/B: a loopback controller fixture stored and consumed serial 5, stored a new active serial 6, then delivered the old grant packet. Before the change the controller emitted an accepted-grant signal and displayed the stale serial; afterwards it emits no such signal and retains serial 6 as the active grant. The three-controller loss/reorder fixture passed again. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the desktop app built and CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, activation, newqsotuning and qmlpopups passed 7/7; the focused compact and contest-entry app probes passed.

The recheck is a point-in-time read, not a lock spanning external writers and UI presentation; the QSO transaction still performs its own validation. UDP remains unauthenticated, separate authorities are unfenced, and worker contention can drop packets until retry. This increment does not implement QSO edit/delete convergence, actual multi-host LAN, hardware/SO2R, Windows/Linux or the remaining desktop roadmap.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 local QSO change journal foundation

Schema 28 adds a local, ordered journal of QSO UUID, revision and deletion-state changes. SQLite triggers record inserts and changes to UUID, revision or deleted state in the same transaction as the QSO; a UUID change also records the previous UUID. This includes changes made by existing Cloud operations, and is not itself a ContestNet message, a peer acknowledgement or a historical QSO snapshot. The new read API pages by local sequence and caps page size at 500. Existing QSO are not backfilled during migration, so a future synchronization protocol must first establish a consistent initial inventory. The migration is additive; retain a verified pre-upgrade backup for rollback.

A/B: the new tests initially failed to compile because the journal API did not exist. Afterwards file-backed fixtures checked insert, edit and soft-delete sequence/revisions, pagination, rejection of invalid cursors, rollback of both insertion and edit, persistence after reopen, and migration from a synthetic schema-27 log without invented historical entries. A Cloud UUID-adoption fixture verifies that the journal records both identities. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the desktop app built and CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner, qslcardcontroller and activation passed 8/8; the final focused journal cases passed again. A single synthetic A/B timing of 1,000 QSO inserts in one SQLite transaction measured 21 ms with the triggers removed and 27 ms with them enabled; it is not a production or multi-million-row benchmark.

The journal currently grows without retention or compaction and records metadata rather than full payloads. It does not implement snapshots, peer checkpoints, ACKs, conflict policy, replay, initial inventory, real network propagation or restoration after a database rollback. Changes made by Cloud can alter UUIDs and revisions, so journal entries cannot be interpreted as independent local edits or assumed to have monotonically increasing QSO revisions. Raw SQL changes that do not alter UUID, revision or deleted state and physical SQL deletion are not captured. No real LAN, concurrent peer edits/deletes, crash during SQLite commit, Windows/Linux or remaining desktop roadmap requirements are proven by this increment.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 consistent initial QSO inventory

Added a read-only `ContestNetInventory` that opens an existing schema-28 log and holds one SQLite read transaction while paging QSO UUIDs, revisions and deleted flags together with a journal watermark from that same snapshot. It includes QSO that predate the journal, bounds pages to 500, exposes the origin log and native-file identities, rejects an observed path replacement and releases the transaction on close or destruction. The object is thread-affine; the caller must keep the snapshot short. The local contract and limitations are in [contest-qso-convergence.md](contest-qso-convergence.md).

A/B: the new fixture initially failed to compile because this inventory did not exist. Afterwards it read the first page, edited and deleted QSO, inserted another QSO and adopted a Cloud UUID through a separate writer connection, then verified later inventory pages still represented the earlier snapshot while all four changes appeared after its watermark. Additional file-backed fixtures refused a missing log without creating it, refused schema 27 without migrating it, detected a same-log-UUID file replacement, checked page limits and included the minimum signed SQLite row ID. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the desktop app built; CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner and activation passed 7/7, and the final UUID-adoption assertion passed in a focused rerun.

This inventory contains metadata only and is not connected to ContestNet, a peer protocol, QSO payloads, conflict handling, checkpoints or ACKs. Equal UUID/revision values do not prove equal content, especially across Cloud revision changes; copied or restored logs need a separate continuity policy. A long SQLite snapshot can retain WAL pages or block writers in rollback-journal mode; cancellation is explicit, with no UI deadline yet. File-token checks do not eliminate the open/stat race, in-place replacement or network-filesystem ambiguity. Real LAN, crash recovery, Windows/Linux and the remaining desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 inventory ADIF content fingerprints

Each inventory row now includes a versioned SHA-256 fingerprint of sorted ADIF field names and their exact values as read in the inventory transaction. It includes ADIF-visible QSL state but excludes local UUID/revision, attachments, profile records and non-ADIF service metadata. A checked record reader refuses QSO/QSL query errors and malformed or non-string `adif_extra` data; the inventory discards the whole page on error. This is a comparison aid, not a content transfer or an automatic conflict decision. The exact format and scope are documented in [contest-qso-convergence.md](contest-qso-convergence.md).

A/B: the fingerprint test initially failed to compile because inventory rows had no digest. Afterwards reordered ADIF fields produced the same digest, changed content produced a different one, and a golden digest for Unicode input matched an independently constructed Python encoding. A file-backed fixture changed a QSO's content without changing its UUID or revision and observed distinct fingerprints. Another changed only QSL state and observed a changed fingerprint with the same revision. A separate A/B fixture showed malformed ADIF extras were previously ignored and produced a page; the checked reader now rejects malformed or non-string JSON, and another fixture rejects an unreadable QSL table. Snapshot isolation, the replaced-file case and the three-node serial tests still pass. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app built and CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner and activation passed 7/7.

One synthetic 1,000-QSO timing measured less than 1 ms for a direct metadata-only SQL scan versus 52 ms for the inventory snapshot with ADIF hashing; these are different operations, one warm-cache run and not a large-log or interactive performance guarantee. The current implementation makes separate QSO and QSL queries per row. The QSL-only fixture also exposed an important remaining gap: the fingerprint changes while the schema-28 journal watermark does not, so snapshot plus journal cannot yet capture every fingerprint change. No payload transfer, peer protocol, conflict policy, QSL merge policy, checkpoint, real LAN or cross-platform proof is provided.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 QSL-visible changes in the local journal

Schema 29 adds transactional journal triggers for QSL insertions, deletions and changes to service, sent/received state or date, and QSL route. A QSL-only change now advances the journal even if the QSO revision is unchanged. Moving a QSL row between QSO records both affected UUIDs; updates only to the service's local remote ID or error text, and no-op updates, do not add an entry. The inventory now requires schema 29, so it cannot present a schema-28 journal with the known QSL gap as an adequate starting point. Migration is additive and does not invent historical events; retain a verified pre-upgrade backup.

A/B: before this change a QSL update changed the inventory fingerprint without advancing the watermark and a focused journal fixture observed no event. Afterwards it observed an event with the original QSO revision, and a rolled-back QSL update produced none. Further tests covered date updates, QSL removal, moves between QSO, local-only metadata, no-op updates, migration from a synthetic schema-28 log preserving its QSO and QSL state, and read-only refusal of schema 28 without migrating it. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the desktop app built; CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner, qslcardcontroller, qslcontroller and activation passed 9/9.

Journal events are local metadata and can include conservative duplicates; QSL merge rules, payloads, initial peer synchronization, checkpoints, ACKs and conflict handling are not implemented. Arbitrary raw SQL that changes QSO content without changing its revision/identity/deleted flag, physical QSO deletion, attachments and non-ADIF metadata remain outside this journal. No process crash during commit, real LAN/radio, Windows/Linux or remaining desktop roadmap requirement is proven by these tests.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 bounded inventory ADIF payloads

Inventory pages now return a UTF-8 ADIF record alongside each snapshot fingerprint. A page is limited by both row count and returned payload bytes (1 MiB default, 16 MiB maximum). A record that cannot fit as the first item reports an error without advancing the cursor, so the caller can retry with a larger allowed budget. Each payload is parsed back by the application's ADIF reader and checked against the snapshot fingerprint; a mismatch fails the page instead of returning incomplete data. This is a local API and does not transmit the potentially private QSO or QSL data.

A/B: the new bounded-payload fixture initially failed to compile because the payload and byte budget were absent. Afterwards it verified invalid budgets, retry after an undersized budget without skipping the first QSO, page splitting without truncation, Unicode and embedded tag text, QSL state from the original snapshot despite a later write, and a payload/fingerprint round trip. Another fixture deliberately used a non-round-trippable ADIF field name and verified that the complete page is refused. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app built and CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner and activation passed 7/7. A single warm-cache synthetic run of 1,000 QSO measured under 1 ms for a direct metadata SQL scan and 55 ms for the inventory including payload serialization, parsing and hashing; the operations differ and this is not a large-log benchmark.

The payload budget limits returned ADIF bytes, not all temporary allocations. A record over the hard 16 MiB limit cannot currently be inventoried, and there is no fragmentation. Validation uses this application's ADIF parser and is not third-party interoperability proof. Journal events still lack immutable revision payloads; network transport, authorization, QSL merge policy, peer checkpoints, conflicts, real LAN and the remaining desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 read-only QSO inventory comparison

Added a paged, read-only comparison of two separately pinned file-backed inventories. It classifies each UUID as local-only, remote-only, matching ADIF fingerprint, same fingerprint with different revisions, content conflict, or deletion-state conflict. It does not apply edits or decide that a higher revision wins. Inventories can be paged in SQLite binary UUID order; the comparator uses matching UTF-8 byte order, bounds internal source pages and checks both file identities even when rows were buffered. A failed page invalidates the comparison, so a caller must discard earlier partial results.

A/B: the new comparison fixture initially failed to compile because this facility did not exist. Afterwards two synthetic logs with interleaved insertion order produced the six expected classifications in UUID order, while an edit after the pinned snapshot appeared in the later local journal and neither log was overwritten. Additional tests covered non-ASCII UUID ordering, replacement of a source file while rows were buffered, invalid page sizes and explicit refusal of an oversized source record without partial output. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app built and CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner and activation passed 7/7; the final focused fixtures passed. A single synthetic warm-cache comparison of 1,000 matching QSO in two SQLite snapshots took 115 ms, not a large-log or UI responsiveness benchmark.

This is a local API, not a network protocol or user-facing review. The two snapshots are taken sequentially; later writes need catch-up. Matching fingerprints cover ADIF only and do not prove attachment or local metadata equivalence. There is no automatic QSO or QSL merge, UUID-rename reconciliation, peer ACK/checkpoint, replay, authentication or real LAN proof. A snapshot can retain WAL pages or block writes depending on SQLite mode, and file-identity checks have open/stat and filesystem limitations. Windows/Linux and the remaining desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 offline station-log comparison UI

The station-network panel can now compare the open log with a user-selected local DecoDXLog file without enabling the network. A separate worker reads pinned snapshots, counts all categories and shows up to 200 differences with UUID, callsign, date, band, mode and revision; it never changes either log. Cancellation is checked between comparison pages and partial rows are not published after cancellation or a worker error. The result is marked stale when the open log changes or either file is replaced. It is explicitly presented as an observation, not live synchronization or a merge.

A/B: a controller test initially failed to compile because the asynchronous comparison API was absent. Afterwards file-backed fixtures verified content conflicts and one-sided QSO, preserved both source records, invalidated a result after a local edit, cancelled without publishing partial rows, detected replacement of the selected file, and counted 201 differences while showing 200. An actual-app offscreen probe opened the comparison dialog and observed a content conflict in English and Italian; it selected the fixture programmatically, not through the native file picker. The Italian translation was absent from the prior compiled catalogue and is present in the new one; XML and placeholder parity were checked. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the desktop app built and CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner, qmlpopups and activation passed 8/8.

This is a read-only local comparison, not ContestNet transfer, field-by-field review or conflict resolution. Its two snapshots are sequential; a remote file modified in place after its snapshot is not monitored, so even an unflagged result can be stale. Cancellation waits for the current page, and long SQLite read snapshots may retain WAL data or block writers depending on journal mode. Closing the dialog clears the displayed results. File-identity checks do not eliminate all filesystem races. The new comparison strings have Italian translations and English source text; other locales and some core diagnostic strings still need translations. Large production logs, physical UI/file picker, real LAN, Windows/Linux and the remaining desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 bounded ADIF field comparison

The offline comparison can now show differing ADIF fields for records present on both sides, including which side lacks a field. Each QSO preview displays at most 32 changed fields; additional fields are counted, and long field names or values are marked truncated after at most 256 UTF-16 code units without splitting a surrogate pair. The QSO data remains unchanged and the display uses plain text. Only the ADIF projection is compared; this is not a merge or conflict decision.

A/B: before the change the controller fixture reported a content conflict but could not show the different `NAME` values. Afterwards it exposed both values. A separate synthetic case checked missing fields, 41 differing fields, omission count, long names and Unicode truncation while preserving the source value. Actual-app offscreen probes observed the field text in English and Italian; the fixture selected the comparison file programmatically. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app built and CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner, qmlpopups and activation passed 8/8. In one warm-cache synthetic run, comparing 1,000 matching records took 118 ms and comparing 1,000 records with a content difference took 121 ms; this does not establish large-log or interactive responsiveness.

Previews can include private QSO/QSL values and remain local; they omit attachments and non-ADIF metadata. Truncated values and omitted fields require separate full-log inspection. The comparison does not monitor subsequent in-place changes to the selected file and cannot apply decisions, exchange data over ContestNet, reconcile UUID changes, establish peer checkpoints or authenticate a peer. Other translations, physical UI/file picker, real LAN/radio, Windows/Linux and the rest of the desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 selected-log change detection in comparison

The offline comparison now keeps a separate read-only SQLite watcher for the selected log. It captures that connection's `data_version` before starting the worker, checks it before publishing, and polls it while the result is displayed. A commit to the selected log, including a QSL-only update without a QSO revision change, marks the result stale; a file replacement and changes to the open log retain their existing guards. The watcher is closed on cancellation, failure, stale result or dialog close. The UI stale message was updated in English and Italian.

A/B: before the change a file-backed selected-log edit left the comparison unflagged. Afterwards tests observed staleness after a same-file QSO edit, a QSL-only commit and a commit during the comparison, and retained the existing file-replacement and cancellation checks. Actual-app offscreen probes of the comparison dialog passed in English and Italian. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the desktop app built and CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner, qmlpopups and activation passed 8/8.

The check is periodic, so changes can remain unflagged until the next poll; it detects SQLite commits from other connections, not arbitrary in-place binary edits or every filesystem race. Unrelated commits may conservatively invalidate the result. Opening the read-only watcher occurs on the UI thread and has not been benchmarked on slow or network filesystems. This remains an offline, read-only preview: no peer transfer, merge, checkpoint or conflict resolution is implemented. Physical UI, real LAN/radio, Windows/Linux, other translations and the remaining desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 experimental fragmented QSO wire contract

Added an experimental, standalone encoder and single-transfer receiver for an inventory ADIF record. It divides at 512 bytes, validates bounded routing metadata and exact fragment lengths, accepts reordering and identical duplicates, and exposes the record only after SHA-256 of the received bytes and the existing ADIF fingerprint both match. Conflicting duplicate fragments and malformed, oversized or misrouted fragments are refused; an integrity failure requires a new receiver. The sender and transfer IDs constrain routing but do not authenticate a peer. This code does not listen on the production controller, transmit automatically, apply a QSO, or change a database.

A/B: the new focused test initially failed to compile because the transfer contract was absent. Afterwards tests passed for reverse ordering, duplicate and missing fragments, later retry, wrong sender, malformed and fractional sizes, invalid base64, conflicting duplicate, corruption, and rejection of a payload over 4 MiB. A file-backed inventory fixture encoded and reconstructed a real snapshot including Unicode and checked that the source record was unchanged. A local UDP fixture used ContestNet framing and an actual loopback socket to omit one fragment, repeat another, and subsequently deliver the missing fragment; it is not a multi-host or radio test. The desktop app built on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2; CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner and activation passed 7/7. The packet size assertion used short test identities and is not a universal MTU guarantee.

The 4 MiB transfer limit is lower than the inventory's 16 MiB record limit; larger records fail explicitly. Memory is bounded per receiver, but concurrency, expiry, cancellation, congestion control, retransmission protocol and aggregate memory limits do not yet exist. Hashes do not authenticate a sender or prevent a malicious peer from replacing both data and hashes. Replay persistence, peer authorization, production networking, initial inventory exchange, journal checkpoints, ACKs, conflict resolution, QSL merge, database application, real LAN, hardware and cross-platform checks remain open. ADIF can contain private data, so future sending needs explicit user authorization and peer security.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 bounded missing-fragment requests

The experimental single-record receiver can now produce a request for up to 32 missing fragment indices after at least one valid fragment has established its transfer metadata. The encoder checks transfer ID, payload hash, index bounds, ordering, duplicates and request size before returning the corresponding fragments. A caller can request another page or repeat a request; completed or failed receivers do not request more. This remains an explicit, in-memory contract: it does not schedule retries or send packets itself.

A/B: the new recovery fixture initially failed to compile because the missing-fragment request API did not exist. Afterwards, a 40,000-character synthetic ADIF record was reconstructed across multiple bounded requests, and invalid hash, duplicate, fractional and out-of-range indices were rejected. A separate test exchanged the missing request and its response fragments through two UDP loopback sockets using ContestNet framing, then verified the reconstructed ADIF bytes. The app and contestnet test target built on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2; CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner and activation passed 7/7. The loopback test sends requests and replies directly and is not a production-controller or multi-host test.

Requests and sender IDs are unauthenticated and can be spoofed; the hash identifies bytes but does not prove origin or authorize disclosure. No production receiver, request routing or peer permissions, automatic retry/timer/backoff, rate or aggregate memory limits, durable resume, completion ACK, initial-fragment discovery, QSO application or conflict handling is implemented. The caller must choose and manage a peer and transfer lifecycle before using this on a network. Real LAN/radio, Windows/Linux and the remaining desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 guarded insertion of a received missing QSO

Added a standalone operation to insert one completely reconstructed, integrity-checked QSO into an explicitly identified, file-backed destination log. It starts an immediate SQLite transaction, refuses deleted records, verifies the target identity and file token, uses the existing duplicate and ADIF/QSL insertion logic, preserves the received UUID and revision, and checks that the stored ADIF fingerprint matches before committing. It removes this transaction's temporary generated UUID from the local change journal and leaves one event for the received identity. An exact existing QSO is an idempotent no-op; a changed revision/content, deletion or likely duplicate under another UUID is refused for review. No existing QSO is overwritten. The caller is responsible for explicit review and peer authorization; this operation is not connected to the controller or UI.

A/B: the new file-backed test initially failed to compile because guarded insertion did not exist. Afterwards it transferred a real inventory snapshot into a second log, verified the UUID, stored fingerprint and one journal event, and replayed it without a second insertion. Wrong destination, incomplete receiver, locally edited QSO and a duplicate under another UUID were refused. An injected SQLite trigger rejected UUID assignment: neither the QSO nor journal events survived, and retry after removing the fault succeeded. This injection is not an OS crash or commit-failure test. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app built and CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner and activation passed 7/7.

This is not automatic synchronization: peer authenticity, user-facing review and authorization, a destination-profile policy, updates/deletions, three-way conflict handling, acknowledgements, checkpoints, crash-during-commit testing and production network wiring remain open. The ADIF round-trip guard can conservatively reject valid records normalized by the existing importer. It copies ADIF-visible QSL fields but not attachments, local service metadata or station profiles; review of QSL and Cloud interaction on real logs remains necessary. File-token checks cannot eliminate every filesystem replacement race. The 4 MiB transfer limit, real LAN/radio, Windows/Linux and remaining desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 guarded reviewed QSO edits and deletions

Added a standalone review token for an existing local QSO and a fully reconstructed remote QSO. It pins the destination log/file identity, local UUID, revision, deletion flag and ADIF fingerprint, plus remote source identity, revision, deletion flag, ADIF fingerprint and payload hash. An explicit subsequent call starts an immediate transaction and checks the token again. If current local and received state still match, an operator-selected remote edit uses the existing history-preserving update, retains the local station profile, and checks the stored ADIF fingerprint before commit; a selected remote deletion uses the existing soft-delete and QSL remote-deletion behavior. Local revision advances according to existing local edit/delete rules; it is not replaced by the peer's revision. Restoring an already deleted local QSO is refused. The API does not itself display the comparison, authenticate a peer or obtain operator consent; it is not wired to UI or networking.

A/B: the new test initially failed to compile because review/apply operations were absent. Afterwards it transferred an initial QSO between two local file-backed logs, rejected an edit after a concurrent local change, rejected a QSL-only change with no QSO revision increase, applied an explicitly selected remote edit after a fresh review, and then applied a reviewed remote deletion. An injected SQLite trigger caused the update to fail and preserved the local QSO; retry after removing the trigger succeeded. This is a synthetic local fixture, not a real process crash, LAN, or multi-operator session. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app built and CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner and activation passed 7/7.

The token covers ADIF-visible state and deletion/revision, not attachment changes or QSL service remote IDs/errors that are outside the ADIF fingerprint. An operator choosing the remote ADIF can replace ADIF-visible QSL values; review and merge policy for QSL, local-only metadata and actual service synchronization remain necessary before UI/network exposure. There is no three-way merge, peer authentication or authorization, replay-resistant network protocol, user-facing review, automatic convergence, checkpoint or restoration workflow. Concurrent changes to untracked local-only metadata, file replacement races, crash during commit, real LAN/radio, Windows/Linux and the rest of the desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 review guard for local metadata and attachment links

The local review token now includes a transactionally read fingerprint of the complete QSO row, QSL service state including remote ID and error, enrichment rows, and attachment metadata and link token/revision. The write transaction recomputes it and refuses changed or unreadable state before applying a selected remote change. Rows are hashed incrementally; attachment binary data is not loaded into the fingerprint. This is an ephemeral local guard, not a wire fingerprint or a persisted compatibility format. Conservative invalidation is possible when unrelated columns change.

A/B: before the change the focused fixture showed that changing only a QSL remote ID/error after review did not block application. Afterwards it blocks that application; separate checks also block an added attachment and a repeated attachment link that changes its link revision. Renaming the enrichment table makes both review and application fail closed until it is restored. The earlier local edit, QSL-visible change, injected SQL failure and explicit remote edit/deletion scenarios pass again. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the desktop app built and CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner and activation passed 7/7.

The guard records attachment metadata, stored hash and length but does not hash attachment bytes, so in-place byte changes that preserve those values are not detected. It does not cover every possible future linked table or filesystem replacement race. This remains a core-only primitive without user-facing review, peer authentication, automatic convergence, QSL merge policy, actual network controller integration, real LAN/radio, cross-platform or crash-during-commit proof. The remaining desktop roadmap stays open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 direct UUID lookup in a pinned inventory

A pinned `ContestNetInventory` can now look up one QSO by UUID without scanning or advancing its page cursor. It uses the same SQLite read transaction, payload budget, ADIF round-trip and fingerprint checks as paged inventory, and refuses an observed file replacement. A missing UUID returns an empty successful result; an oversized record reports an error without invalidating the snapshot, allowing a larger-budget retry. This supports future selected-record review and transfer but is not yet connected to the comparison UI or production network controller.

A/B: the new test initially failed to compile because direct lookup was absent. Afterwards a file-backed fixture paged the first record, edited the second through a writer, then fetched that UUID from the pinned earlier snapshot and confirmed the old data; a subsequent page still returned the second record. It also covered an undersized budget, retry, absent UUID and lookup after close. Existing bounded payload and malformed ADIF fixtures passed. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, the app and database, contestnet and netcontroller targets were rebuilt; CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner and activation passed 7/7. The rebuilt netcontroller suite took 22 seconds; earlier 0.04-second results should not be treated as equivalent coverage without checking the previously built executable.

The lookup retains the existing snapshot, filesystem identity and 16 MiB payload limitations; a long snapshot can retain WAL data or block writers. It does not provide peer authentication, user approval, application of changes, conflict resolution or network synchronization. UI integration, real LAN/radio, Windows/Linux and the rest of the desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 inspect individual offline comparison records

The offline station-log comparison now offers “Inspect all fields…” for a displayed difference. A worker reopens both pinned log snapshots, fetches that UUID, verifies each record's fingerprint, revision and deletion state against the comparison, and returns the full ADIF field set without changing either log. The controller checks both file identities, the current log change stamp and the selected log's SQLite data version before and after the worker. A changed log rejects or clears the review; results are discarded when a comparison is cancelled or replaced. The dialog shows current and selected field values in plain text and marks values longer than 2,048 UTF-16 code units as truncated. It is read-only and does not apply any remote change.

A/B: the controller fixture initially failed to compile because individual review was absent. Afterwards it compared two file-backed logs, inspected a 400-character value on both sides without the comparison preview's 256-character truncation, verified neither source changed, and refused another review after a selected-log commit. The first implementation's focused fixture failed to find a field because of a temporary ADIF document lifetime; after retaining the parsed document it passed. The actual app's offscreen probe opened the comparison and inspection dialogs and received fields in English and Italian; it invokes the selection programmatically, not with mouse or keyboard. The app built on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner, qmlpopups and activation passed 8/8; netcontroller and qmlpopups passed again after the UI probe changes. Italian catalogue XML parsed successfully.

This inspection supports records up to 4 MiB on each side and only rows among the first 200 displayed differences. The controller retains full values in memory; the visual dialog truncates long values and does not show attachments or non-ADIF metadata. It does not prove physical input, visual layout on all screens, selected-file in-place binary changes, all filesystem races or behaviour on slow/large logs. The UI does not apply QSO edits/deletions, reconcile conflicts, authenticate peers or provide network synchronization. Other translations, real LAN/radio, Windows/Linux and the remaining desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 confirmed offline import of one missing QSO

From a current offline comparison, an operator can inspect a non-deleted QSO found only in the selected local log and then explicitly confirm its import into the open log. The review worker reconstructs the inspected record using the bounded QSO transfer contract; immediately before import the controller checks current comparison/file identities and SQLite change tokens, and the guarded insertion rechecks the destination inside its transaction. The source's captured reviewed content is used; the operation refuses existing divergent or likely duplicate QSO. On success it preserves the UUID, invalidates the comparison, updates the log model and invokes the normal Cloud logging notification. The dialog explains that ADIF fields and QSL states are copied, but attachments, local service metadata and station profile are not, and that normal Cloud sync may include the QSO. No network peer is contacted by this workflow.

A/B: the controller fixture initially failed to compile because the operation did not exist. Afterwards it rejected import without a review, rejected it after the selected log changed, succeeded after a fresh comparison and review, notified the model callback once and refused a repeated action. A focused actual-app offscreen test cancelled confirmation in English and verified an empty target log; it accepted in Italian and reopened the target to verify one QSO with the original UUID. The test invokes selection and confirmation programmatically, not physical mouse or keyboard. After the final explanatory text change, both app cases passed again. The macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 desktop app and relevant test targets built; CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner, qmlpopups and activation passed 8/8. Italian translation XML was checked.

The import itself currently runs synchronously on the UI thread and has not been benchmarked with the maximum 4 MiB QSO or slow disks. The confirmation's source change check is point-in-time; it imports the captured reviewed record if a source write occurs after that check. File-identity checks do not eliminate all replacement races. QSL values may participate in configured service/Cloud workflows; interoperability with real accounts and duplicate-policy behaviour on production logs remain unverified. Existing-QSO edits/deletions, full conflict review, attachments, profile mapping, physical UI, real LAN/radio, Windows/Linux and remaining desktop roadmap work remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 confirmed offline edits and deletions

The offline comparison can now prepare a guarded review of a QSO present in both logs and explicitly apply the selected log's ADIF fields, or soft-delete the current QSO when the selected copy is deleted. The worker binds the reconstructed selected record to a local review token and verifies it matches the comparison snapshot. Confirmation rechecks both log change tokens and applies through the existing transactional guard, which verifies local revision, ADIF, QSL service metadata, attachment links and enrichment state. A changed current QSO is refused. The edit path keeps the local station profile and attachments, refreshes the model, queues normal remote/QSL edit handling and handles a changed callsign; the deletion path keeps history, removes the model row and schedules configured deletion services. Both invalidate the comparison and refresh the application state. The dialogs explicitly describe the chosen operation and possible service updates. A locally deleted QSO is not restored by this path.

The UI inspection limits displayed field values to 2,048 UTF-16 code units. An A/B fixture demonstrated that import was previously offered for an unseen longer value; import and apply are now disabled if any field name or value exceeds that limit. Full values are retained by the read-only controller result, but no action is offered until a full-review UI exists.

A/B: the new controller fixture first failed to compile because the UI-facing operation was absent. Afterwards it refused application before review and after an intervening local edit, then applied the remote edit only after a fresh comparison and review, and applied a later reviewed deletion. The actual-app offscreen test rejected and accepted an edit, and rejected and accepted a deletion, verifying the reopened database each time. It invokes confirmation programmatically, not with physical input. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app and test targets built; CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner, qmlpopups and activation passed 8/8. Italian strings were added and catalogue XML parsed, but the new edit/delete dialogs were not separately exercised in Italian.

This is explicit offline conflict resolution, not automatic three-way merging or network convergence. ADIF-visible QSL state is replaced on an accepted edit; external QSL/Cloud behaviour and callsign changes have not been proven against live accounts. Attachment binary changes preserving metadata are not covered by the review token; other future linked tables and filesystem races remain possible. Apply runs synchronously on the UI thread and has not been benchmarked for large fields or slow disks. The source check is point-in-time and uses captured reviewed data if the source changes immediately afterwards. Restore, values exceeding the visual limit, physical UI, peer authentication, real LAN/radio, Windows/Linux and the rest of the desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 review guard for attachment bytes

The local review fingerprint now reads the bytes of every linked attachment within the same SQLite transaction as its metadata, using 1 MiB SQL slices, and hashes them into the ephemeral review token. Missing attachment rows, non-blob data, short reads and SQL failures refuse the review or application. A same-length change to attachment bytes therefore invalidates a reviewed edit or deletion even when the stored attachment hash and metadata were not updated. This does not change attachment data or the stored attachment hash and does not assert that an existing hash is correct.

A/B: before this change a fixture modified an attachment's bytes through SQL without changing its length or metadata, and the guarded application incorrectly succeeded. Afterwards it is refused. Additional tests changed bytes on a 1 MiB slice boundary and at the end of a 2 MiB-plus-one-byte attachment; both were detected. Earlier QSL, link-change, unavailable-table and rollback scenarios passed again. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app built and CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner, qmlpopups and activation passed 8/8.

Performance A/B was a separate warm-cache Python/SQLite synthetic file with one 16 MiB blob, not an application/UI benchmark: three runs hashing 64 KiB SQL slices took 489.22, 496.92 and 493.88 ms; 1 MiB slices took 35.81, 36.97 and 36.80 ms. A metadata-length-only query was about 0.01–0.05 ms and is a different, weaker operation. The Qt-visible returned slice is bounded, but SQLite's own memory usage was not measured. Application of the guarded change still runs on the UI thread and rereads all linked attachment bytes; large/many attachments, slow disks and concurrent logging need representative latency and memory tests and likely asynchronous execution. New linked tables, filesystem races, actual network synchronization, external services, real LAN/radio and cross-platform proof remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 paged review of long QSO fields

The offline QSO inspection can now open a long field name or current/selected value in 2,048-UTF-16-unit pages. The controller requires the pages of every long component to be requested sequentially before it enables import or application; restarting a completed component disables the action until its pages are traversed again. Page boundaries do not split a UTF-16 surrogate pair. Both log change checks remain in force, and a changed or closed comparison invalidates review. The normal confirmation and transactional guards still apply; merely opening pages does not change QSO. This replaces the previous blanket prohibition on actions involving long fields.

A/B: the controller test initially failed to compile because the long-field paging API was absent. Afterwards it rejected a direct jump to a later page, preserved an emoji across a boundary, held import disabled until two separate long fields were traversed, disabled it again on restart, and finally imported the complete original QSO. The actual app offscreen opened and traversed a long field and confirmed import; reopening the target verified the complete Unicode value. Selection, page traversal and confirmation were invoked programmatically, so this is not proof of physical input or reading comprehension. The prior import accept/reject and edit/deletion accept/reject app fixtures passed again. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, the app and test targets built and CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner, qmlpopups and activation passed 8/8. Italian strings were added and XML parsed; the new long-field window was not separately exercised in Italian.

The guard records that pages were requested, not that an operator actually read or scrolled every line; physical keyboard/mouse accessibility and layout need verification. Full ADIF fields are retained in controller memory and transfer remains limited to 4 MiB per record. Applying changes remains synchronous on the UI thread, with no representative slow-disk/large-attachment benchmark. Peer authentication, automatic network synchronization, real service interoperability, real LAN/radio, Windows/Linux and the remaining desktop roadmap stay open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 three-log QSO loss and conflict fixture

Added an integration fixture combining the experimental QSO fragment contract, real UDP loopback sockets, three separate file-backed logs and the existing guarded insertion/review operations. The fixture deliberately sends fragments in reverse order, duplicates one, omits another and sends a bounded missing-fragment request. It closes and reopens the source database before reconstructing the missing fragment for the original unchanged transfer. A second destination stays partitioned until the first has received the QSO, then receives and inserts it. Later an origin edit and deletion are delivered to both destinations. In each case the second destination is edited after its review: the stale application is refused and its local content remains; a newly obtained review is then explicitly applied. After the deletion both destination databases are closed and reopened and retain the same QSO UUID and deletion state.

A/B: before reconnect the partitioned destination has no QSO, after transfer and explicit insertion it does. Before fresh review the concurrent edit/deletion application is refused, after fresh review and an explicit choice it succeeds. A separate request fixture confirms that an encoder reconstructed from changed ADIF content refuses a missing-fragment request bound to the old payload hash. The focused three-log fixture passed twice; after the additional old-payload assertion both focused cases passed again. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner, qmlpopups and activation passed 8/8; this regression run preceded the final additional assertion, which was run in the focused rerun.

This is deterministic core integration with loopback sockets and direct fixture calls, not three running NetControllers, production broadcast, an OS/process crash, automatic journal replay or a live operator workflow. Source restart means closing and reopening its SQLite connection while the test process and UDP socket remain alive. It tests one QSO and one loss/reorder/concurrency sequence; QSL service merge, attachments, multiple simultaneous transfers, real LAN/radio, authentication, ACK/checkpoint protocol and cross-platform interoperability remain unverified. No application behavior was added in this milestone; production QSO network convergence and the remaining desktop roadmap stay open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 manual experimental fragmented QSO exchange

Added an explicit experimental single-QSO exchange to the station-network panel and `NetController`. The operator enters the other station ID, a shared transfer ID and a QSO UUID; the receiver must be armed first, and the sender explicitly starts a read-only snapshot and sends paced 512-byte fragments. One outgoing and one incoming transfer can be held, each record is capped at 4 MiB, state expires after five minutes, and closing the experiment or restarting the network clears it. A receiver with at least one fragment can manually request up to 32 missing fragments from the held outgoing snapshot; the sender validates the request using the existing transfer ID/hash/index checks. A received complete record shows a summary but is not inserted or applied to any log. Source log identity, file token and change stamp are checked after background preparation.

A/B: the two-controller fixture initially failed to compile because the controller interface was absent. Afterwards two actual `NetController` objects on separate loopback UDP ports used a local test relay that withheld one fragment, delivered the others backwards, injected a wrong-sender packet, and forwarded a manual missing request/retry. The receiver reported incomplete before retry and complete afterwards; neither source nor destination log was changed by reception, and disabling the network cleared transfer state. The actual app's offscreen probe opened the new warning dialog in English and Italian; it did not exercise physical input or two application processes. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the desktop app and relevant targets built; CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner, qmlpopups and activation passed 8/8. The earlier offline comparison, import, edit/delete and long-field offscreen app fixtures also passed. Italian catalogue XML parsed.

This is a manual experimental path, not production-grade synchronization: the sender ID and transfer ID do not authenticate a peer, UDP content is unencrypted and broadcast to network listeners even when a logical recipient is specified. The UI warns about both. There is no access control, replay persistence, rate limiting of requests, automatic retry/backoff, complete-transfer ACK, inventory or journal exchange, checkpoint, automatic conflict detection or application of received data. If all initial fragments are lost, the sender must manually send again; repeated missing requests may be needed. Preview is a summary, not a full field review. The five-minute timer and single-transfer bounds are in-memory only. A source change after the final preparation check can make the transmitted snapshot older than the current log. Real broadcast routing, two separate app instances, real LAN/radio, Windows/Linux and remaining desktop roadmap requirements remain unverified.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 automatic fragment retry with bounded responses

An explicitly armed experimental receiver now requests the first fragment if none has arrived, or up to 32 missing indices once a fragment establishes the snapshot hash. It retries every two seconds until completion, a fatal integrity failure, network shutdown or expiry. Automatic requests alone do not extend the receiver's five-minute expiry. The sender serves a valid first-fragment or missing-fragment request from its retained snapshot at most once per second, sharing the limit between request types; the expected logical sender and transfer ID must match. A fatal integrity failure stops retry and requires the operator to rearm. The manual retry button remains available. The UI text was updated in English and Italian.

A/B: before this change the two-controller fixture timed out when every initial fragment was dropped.
