Afterwards, using a local UDP relay and no manual retry, the receiver requested the first fragment, requested the remaining fragments and completed without changing its database. A burst of five first-fragment requests yielded one response within the observed rate-limit interval. Another fixture injected a corrupted transfer, observed integrity failure and no automatic retry for 2.2 seconds, then rearmed and completed a separate loss/reorder/manual-retry sequence. Both focused fixtures passed after the final test change. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, the app built, the experimental dialog opened in English and Italian offscreen, and CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner, qmlpopups and activation passed 8/8 before the final additional retry-stop assertion, which passed in the focused rerun. Italian translation XML parsed.

The limit bounds responses for one active outgoing transfer; it is not authentication, encryption or comprehensive denial-of-service protection. Logical peer IDs and transfer IDs can be spoofed, and broadcast content remains visible to listeners. Retry uses a fixed interval without congestion control or exponential backoff; no complete-transfer ACK, durable restart, automatic journal synchronization or application to the receiver's log exists. The five-minute timeout and behaviour under sustained hostile traffic were not tested over their full duration. The relay fixtures use two controllers within one process, not two physical hosts, real LAN/radio or proof on Windows/Linux. The remaining desktop roadmap stays open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 optional shared-key protection for experimental QSO frames

The manual experimental QSO transfer now optionally accepts a random 32-byte shared key as 64 hexadecimal characters, generated locally or supplied by the operator and exchanged separately. XChaCha20-Poly1305 from the vendored Monocypher library encrypts and authenticates the fragment and retry payloads, binding normalized network group, logical sender and recipient as associated data; every packet gets a random 24-byte nonce. The controller rejects clear experimental packets while the key is active, checks authenticated nonces against a bounded 32,768-entry in-memory replay set, and stops accepting new protected packets when the set is full. Keys and replay state are session-only; clearing the transfer, closing its window, network restart or expiry removes the current key. The UI disables transfer commands if its entered key no longer matches whether a key is active and warns that legacy QSO and other network messages are not protected. The original clear experimental mode and its 512-byte fragments remain available explicitly.

Protected transfers use 256-byte raw QSO fragments, with an optional chunk-size field checked consistently by the receiver. The controller checks the fully serialized protected UDP envelope against 1,472 bytes before sending; oversized envelopes report an error instead of silently sending. This threshold matches a common 1,500-byte IPv4 link and is not a path-MTU guarantee. In the same synthetic fixture, the largest protected datagram was 1,709 bytes with 512-byte chunks and 1,277 bytes with 256-byte chunks; long station identifiers caused the new guard to reject sending. Legacy frame encoding remains unchanged.

A/B: the new secure-frame and controller fixtures initially failed to compile because the APIs did not exist. Afterwards wrong keys, altered ciphertext, routing/group changes, malformed tags, replay within one session and a complete valid clear transfer injected into protected mode were rejected; a matching key completed transfer, including automatic recovery after all initial fragments were dropped. A five-request encrypted burst produced one response in the observed interval. The underlying vendored AEAD decrypted the published [IRTF XChaCha draft-03 test vector](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-irtf-cfrg-xchacha-03#appendix-A.3.1); this is an expired Internet-Draft and is a primitive test, not a protocol audit. The [Monocypher AEAD manual](https://monocypher.org/manual/aead) specifies a secret random 32-byte key and unique 24-byte nonce. Actual-app offscreen probes opened the clear and protected dialogs in English and Italian and simulated key removal to check the UI prevents accidental reuse; no physical input was tested. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app built and CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner, qmlpopups and activation passed 8/8.

This protects only the experimental messages and proves possession of a shared key, not individual station identity: any key holder can impersonate another. Group, station IDs/name, outer session/sequence, timing and sizes remain visible; outer session/sequence/name are not authenticated. Replays are blocked only within the current in-memory key session, so reuse of a key and transfer ID after restart is unsafe; use a new random key and transfer ID for each session. Qt/OS may retain memory copies despite best-effort clearing. The custom envelope has not had an independent security audit, and its denial-of-service resistance, RNG failure handling, key exchange, lower path MTUs, separate-process/multi-host interoperability, real LAN/radio, Windows/Linux, automatic QSO application and full journal convergence remain open. The remaining desktop roadmap stays open.

## 2026-11-23 — G10 explicit review and application of received QSO

A complete experimental network QSO can now be compared against the open local log and reviewed field by field before an explicit confirmation. A missing, non-deleted QSO can be inserted; a conflicting active local QSO can be updated or soft-deleted according to the received snapshot. Matching records and restoration of a locally deleted record are not applied through this path. The review is prepared on a worker using a copy of the completed receiver, a pinned local inventory and, for an existing record, the same local-state token used by offline review. The controller rechecks the open log's path, identity, file token, generation and SQLite change stamp, plus the identity of the still-active received transfer, before applying the existing transactional guards. Rearming, clearing, expiry or network shutdown invalidates a pending network review. Long fields use the existing paged-review gate. Existing model, QSL/service and Cloud notifications are used after confirmed changes; no QSO is applied automatically on receipt.

The UI shows the logical sender and source log ID and distinguishes an unauthenticated clear transfer from shared-key possession, warning that a key holder can impersonate a station and that the received snapshot may no longer match the sender's current log. Its confirmation describes QSL/service effects and omitted attachment/profile data. The received snapshot is not a live lock or a guarantee that the remote log has not changed since sending.

A/B: the new controller fixture initially failed to compile because network review was absent. Afterwards two real controllers with a local UDP relay exchanged protected QSO: reception alone left the target unchanged, an unrelated local write after review caused import to be refused, and a new review allowed explicit import. A separate protected controller fixture checked an existing QSO, blocked a concurrent local edit, applied a fresh reviewed edit, then blocked a pending deletion after transfer/key state was cleared and applied it only after a new transfer and review. It also traversed long fields before allowing the actions. The clear-mode controller path likewise required review and explicit import.

The actual app was exercised offscreen with a separate test sender thread transmitting only to localhost; a test-only environment guard routed the app's network target to loopback before enabling it. Eight cases covered accept/reject of protected insertion, edit and deletion and accept/reject of clear insertion, using English and Italian, checking sender/source attribution, trust and stale-snapshot warnings, reopened database state and the QSO model count. Selection and confirmation were invoked programmatically, not via physical input. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app and relevant test targets built; CTest database, migration, contestnet, netcontroller, cloud, qslplanner, qmlpopups and activation passed 8/8. The earlier offline comparison, import, edit/delete, long-field and transfer-dialog app probes passed again; Italian translation XML parsed.

This is explicit one-record reconciliation, not automatic multi-peer convergence, a three-way merge, durable peer checkpoint, automatic journal exchange or complete-transfer ACK. Shared-key holders can impersonate peers, clear traffic is unauthenticated and visible, and the protected envelope has not received an independent security audit. Source state can change after its snapshot; attachment/profile data are not transferred and QSL effects have not been tested with real services. Application still runs on the UI thread, long-field traversal records pages requested rather than comprehension, and lower MTUs, real LAN/radio, two actual app processes, Windows/Linux and the remaining desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G11 idempotent retry request context

New local API idempotency entries retain the effective JSON/ADIF parser choice and normalized X-App provenance alongside the body and response. Reuse with a different effective format or app returns 409, including after restart. Schema 30 adds nullable context fields; legacy entries keep their previous body-only replay behavior because their original context is unavailable. The source-app value uses the same default and length limit as logging, and equivalent content-type spellings selecting the same parser remain compatible.

A/B: before the change, the real desktop application running offscreen returned 201 after restart when the same key and ADIF body were retried as JSON; the new fixture expected 409. After the change that fixture passed, also checking a different X-App, a successful identical replay, one stored QSO and a different-body conflict. The database suite passed including migration of a schema-29 legacy row, persistence of a new context across reopening and rejection of a changed app; CTest database, migration and localapi passed on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2. The format mismatch was demonstrated on the original app; the separate X-App mismatch was checked after the change, not independently demonstrated before it.

The QSO and response are still not written atomically, and failure to persist the response is not an exactly-once guarantee. Legacy entries cannot validate format or app; clients should use a fresh key when context changes. This check is not individual app authentication: clients share credentials and can declare the same X-App. No crash-injection, multi-process concurrency, other desktop platform, hardware or external service verification was performed. Remaining G11 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G11 report idempotency journal failure

When a keyed local API QSO request has been processed but the idempotency response cannot be saved, the API now returns HTTP 503 with `retrySafe: false`, an explicit warning that QSO may already have been saved, and the per-record results available to help reconciliation. Existing successful replies and the unkeyed path remain unchanged. The API guide describes the uncertain outcome and the need to inspect the log before retrying.

A/B: an actual app running offscreen on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 used a separate SQLite connection to install a test-only trigger that rejects idempotency inserts. Before the change the keyed request returned 201 even though the journal write failed; afterwards it returned 503 and included the logged result and warning, while the QSO was present once. After removing the trigger, retry returned the ordinary duplicate response and the QSO count stayed at one. The same app fixture covered the existing keyed replay after restart and context conflicts. CTest database, migration and localapi passed 3/3; this is a fault-injection test, not a crash or power-loss test.

The QSO write and response journal remain non-atomic; process death or a lost HTTP reply can leave the client without this warning. Per-record results describe local processing, not completion of external QSL services. The duplicate check is not a general exactly-once guarantee, and an automatic client retry without reconciliation can still be unsafe. This change does not establish cross-process serialization or test other platforms or real services; remaining G11 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G01 QSO insert rollback within an outer transaction

`LogDatabase::insertQso` now uses a SQLite savepoint around the QSO and its QSL status writes. This works both on its own and within a caller's existing transaction: a QSL write failure rolls back this insert rather than leaving a partial QSO for the outer transaction to commit. Savepoint creation and release are checked, failed results no longer expose a newly allocated QSO ID after a QSL or release failure, and the error is retained. The surrounding transaction remains the caller's responsibility.

A/B: a database fixture began an outer transaction and injected a failing QSL insert trigger. Before the change, `insertQso` returned an error but left one QSO visible in that transaction. Afterwards the count remained zero; after removing the trigger, the same outer transaction inserted the QSO with its LoTW status and committed successfully. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app and relevant targets built, CTest database, contestnet, migration and netcontroller passed 4/4, and the actual app's offscreen local API fixture including journal-failure injection passed.

This does not make the API's QSO and idempotency-response writes atomic or defer external side effects until the caller's outer commit. Duplicate lookup still precedes the savepoint and is not a cross-process uniqueness guarantee. Savepoint release failure, power loss, physical hardware, external services and Windows/Linux were not independently fault-injected or verified in this increment. Remaining G01, G11 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G01 QSO edit rollback within an outer transaction

`LogDatabase::updateQso` now protects its history entry, record and revision update and QSL rewrite with one SQLite savepoint, including when called inside an existing transaction. It checks savepoint creation and release and reports QSL write failure explicitly. A failed edit rolls back the operation's own writes while leaving the outer transaction under its caller's control.

A/B: a database fixture first inserted a QSO with LoTW status, began an outer transaction and injected failure on QSL insertion during an edit. Before the change the failed edit still exposed the changed name; afterwards the old name, LoTW status and revision remained intact and no history entry remained. After removing the fault, an edit succeeded and the outer transaction committed. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app and affected targets built; CTest database, migration, netcontroller and qslcontroller passed 4/4, and the offscreen actual-app local API fixture passed.

This does not make external notifications transactional, protect unguarded concurrent edits from stale reads, or make the API idempotency journal atomic. Trigger-forced transaction-wide rollback, savepoint release failures, crash/power-loss recovery, real QSL services and Windows/Linux were not independently tested. Remaining G01 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G01 QSO delete rollback within an outer transaction

`LogDatabase::softDeleteQso` now uses a checked SQLite savepoint to group the history entry, soft deletion and queuing of supported remote deletions, even inside an existing transaction. Failure of any SQL step rolls back the delete's own changes and retains the SQL error; the outer transaction remains controlled by the caller.

A/B: a database fixture inserted a QSO with a remotely identified CRX confirmation, began an outer transaction and injected an error while marking that confirmation for deletion. Before the change the operation returned false but left the QSO marked deleted. Afterwards the QSO, revision, remote service state and history remained unchanged; after removing the fault, deletion succeeded and the remote state became `D`. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app and affected targets built, CTest database, migration, netcontroller and qslcontroller passed 4/4, and the actual-app offscreen local API fixture passed.

This checks the local queue only, not deletion by a real remote service. It does not make notifications transactional, eliminate races in unguarded concurrent operations or cover transaction-wide trigger rollback, savepoint release failures, crash/power loss and Windows/Linux. Other QSL read-error handling and the remaining desktop roadmap require further work.

## 2026-11-23 — G01 preserve QSL state on read failure

`writeQsl` now stops if reading existing service state fails, including an error while iterating rows, before deleting any existing QSL entries. The edit savepoint then rolls back the QSO and history changes. This preserves non-ADIF service information such as remote IDs when the prerequisite read fails.

A/B: with the macOS Qt SQLite driver verified to link the system SQLite library, an opt-in database fixture used SQLite's native authorizer to deny reading only `qsl_status.last_error`. Before the fix, an edit returned success despite the failed read; after the fix it returned an error and preserved the original QSO name and CRX remote ID and sent status. The native-authorizer fixture is skipped unless explicitly enabled on macOS because it requires the Qt driver and the loaded SQLite API to use the same library. The targeted fixture passed without skips with `DECOLOG_TEST_SYSTEM_SQLITE_AUTHORIZER=1`; the app and affected targets built, CTest database, migration, netcontroller and qslcontroller passed 4/4, and the offscreen app local API fixture passed on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2.

This injects an authorization error, not disk corruption, mid-stream I/O failure or power loss. Snapshot and other database read paths need separate error audits, and actual remote-service behavior, Windows and Linux remain unverified. Remaining desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G01 reject incomplete QSO history snapshots

Before editing or soft-deleting a QSO, the history snapshot now uses the existing checked record reader and the operation refuses to continue if the record, QSL states or metadata cannot be read. This avoids committing a history entry that silently omits inaccessible QSL fields. The existing operation savepoints roll back and expose an error. The checked reader also rejects malformed or non-string ADIF-extra JSON, so affected legacy records must be repaired before they can be edited or deleted through these paths.

A/B: using the opt-in native SQLite authorizer fixture on macOS, denying a QSL column read previously allowed deletion with an incomplete history snapshot. Afterwards deletion was refused, the active QSO and its LoTW status remained, and no history entry was written; after removing the fault, deletion succeeded and its history retained the LoTW status. The targeted fixture passed without skips with `DECOLOG_TEST_SYSTEM_SQLITE_AUTHORIZER=1`. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app and affected targets built, CTest database, migration, netcontroller and qslcontroller passed 4/4, and the offscreen actual-app API fixture passed.

The authorization injection covers a deterministic read error, not disk failure, corruption recovery or power loss. It relies on the verified system SQLite driver and is opt-in; Windows/Linux and real services remain unverified. Concurrent unguarded edits and atomic API idempotency remain separate open issues, along with the other desktop roadmap requirements.

## 2026-11-23 — G11 refuse keyed API requests on journal lookup failure

The local API now distinguishes a missing idempotency entry from an error during expiration cleanup or lookup. If the journal is unavailable, it returns HTTP 503 before parsing or processing a keyed QSO request. Successful and unkeyed operations are unchanged; the API guide distinguishes this case from a failure to save the result after processing.

A/B: an actual macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 app running offscreen used a temporary test log. A second SQLite connection temporarily renamed the idempotency table before a keyed request. Before the change the request returned 503 only after inserting the QSO; afterwards it returned 503 with no QSO inserted. Once the table was restored the same request returned 201 and one QSO appeared. The same fixture also covered successful replay after restart and a journal-write failure after processing. CTest database, migration and localapi passed 3/3.

A lookup can still succeed immediately before another process or a crash changes the journal: QSO and response remain non-atomic and simultaneous clients are not serialized by this change. The test simulates an unavailable table rather than disk failure or power loss; Windows/Linux, real services and the remaining G11 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G04 discard externally stale goal-alert results

Goal-only campaign alerts now capture the open log generation and SQLite `data_version` before starting the worker and recheck them, along with the open file identity, before emitting. If another connection commits, the log is reopened/replaced, or the stamp cannot be read, the pending result is discarded. Existing in-process log-version, campaign revision, profile, spot-filter and freshness checks remain. A later matching spot can start a fresh check.

A/B: a focused two-connection fixture demonstrated that the previous generation-only check remained satisfied after the second connection inserted a QSO, while the new check rejected the stale stamp and accepted a refreshed one; it also rejected a closed log. The initial fixture could not compile before the new check existed, so this is a comparison of guard behavior rather than a reproduced end-to-end app race. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the app and affected targets built; CTest database, dxcampaign and dxassistant passed 3/3, and the actual app's offscreen goal-watch fixture still produced and suppressed alerts as expected.

Any external database commit can discard an otherwise valid pending alert, and the result is not automatically retried without a new spot. A commit after the final check remains possible; there is no lock spanning the UI alert. The test did not force an actual two-process app race, use a real cluster or RF, or verify Windows/Linux. Remaining G04 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G04 actual-app external-write alert race A/B

Added an opt-in application probe and integration fixture for the pending goal-alert check. An actual offscreen app receives a synthetic cluster spot, then its UI event loop is held briefly while the separate test process writes the matching QSO to the same SQLite log. The process output and database are checked before normal shutdown. The busy-wait probe runs only when explicitly requested with the test `--show dxgoalraceprobe` path.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: with the final `watchSnapshotCurrent` recheck temporarily removed, the fixture observed one obsolete DXCAMPAIGN highlight and failed its zero-alert assertion. With the recheck restored, the same fixture passed with zero highlights; the normal actual-app goal-watch fixture also passed. CTest database, dxcampaign and dxassistant passed 3/3. The fixture uses a timed 500 ms gap rather than a worker-completion barrier: the A run demonstrates the stale alert in that run, but timing varies under load and the B run alone does not prove the precise worker schedule.

This uses two local processes and synthetic input, not two stations, a real cluster, physical interaction or RF. It does not remove the interval after the final check, test power loss, Windows/Linux, or complete the other desktop roadmap requirements.

## 2026-11-23 — G04 worker-confirmed two-process alert race

The actual-app race fixture now waits for an explicitly enabled test trace from the worker confirming `needed=1`, then writes the matching QSO from the separate test process while the app UI event loop is still held. It checks that the UI has not resumed both before and after the write. The trace is emitted only when `DECOLOG_TEST_DX_WATCH_TRACE` is set; the synthetic busy-wait remains limited to the explicit test probe. This replaces the earlier unverified 500 ms assumption about worker scheduling.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: with the callback's version recheck temporarily removed, the worker confirmed an open goal, the external write committed before UI resumption, and one obsolete alert appeared. With the recheck restored, the same ordered sequence produced no alert; the normal actual-app goal-watch fixture also passed. CTest database, dxcampaign and dxassistant passed 3/3.

This controls the tested order but uses a bounded five-second UI hold, not an unbounded interprocess barrier; the test fails if the worker or external write cannot complete in that interval. It exercises an offscreen app and a separate local test process with synthetic cluster input, not two radio stations, real cluster/RF, Windows/Linux or every possible concurrent schedule. A commit after the final version check remains possible. The desktop roadmap remains open.

## 2026-11-23 — G04 bounded retry of stale goal alerts

If an external commit invalidates a goal-only alert result while the same log, file, profile and spot remain valid, the controller now performs at most one fresh check for that campaign. The background lookup opens the log read-only: the prior read-write open could run SQLite optimization and itself change the observer's data version, causing the fresh result to be discarded. Campaign revision and all existing filter and freshness checks still apply.

A/B with the actual offscreen app and a second local process: the worker first confirmed the objective was open, and the other process committed while the UI event loop was held. Before the change a QSO satisfying the objective correctly produced no alert, but an unrelated QSO also suppressed the still-valid alert. After the change the matching QSO produced zero alerts and the unrelated QSO produced exactly one; the normal goal-watch app fixture passed. During development, an initial retry using the existing read-write worker failed because its own open advanced `data_version`; switching to the read-only connection resolved that observed failure. CTest database, dxcampaign and dxassistant passed 3/3 on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2.

A second external commit during the one retry still discards the result; no unlimited retries or queueing guarantee is claimed. Changes of campaign, log, active profile or filters can cancel the alert, and a commit after the final check remains possible. The test uses synthetic cluster input and a bounded UI hold with a worker completion signal, not real cluster/RF, physical input or other desktop platforms. The full release gate has not been repeated after this change; remaining G04 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G03 QSL goal selection per log and station profile

The QSL planner now stores the selected award IDs in each log's existing settings table, separately for the current award station profile, including the all-profiles selection. Opening the planner, switching profiles or switching logs reloads the corresponding selection; an explicitly empty selection remains empty. When no selection exists for a scope, the pre-existing global preference is used as the initial value and left unchanged. Selection writes report errors, reject duplicate/oversized IDs and verify the open log's file identity; malformed saved JSON is not silently overwritten as an empty selection. This scopes the goal choices, not the award account itself.

A/B: before implementation the new scoped-persistence fixture could not compile because the API was absent; afterwards two separate logs and multiple profiles retained independent selections across reopen, including an empty selection, and invalid data/duplicates were refused. An actual app offscreen probe switched between two station profiles, saved different choices, switched back and restarted; both selections were restored, and a pre-existing global `wac` preference supplied the initial value for an unsaved profile and remained unchanged. The existing planner benefits, queue and invalidation app fixture passed after a restart-loading regression found during development was fixed. CTest database, qslplanner and qmlpopups passed 3/3; the final expanded invalid-data fixture and the legacy-fallback app check passed separately on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2.

Station profile is not the same as a verified award account; per-award/account goal scope remains open. The app-level probe covers profile switching and restart in one log; separate logs were checked at database level. Concurrent edits from another process, live account changes, physical UI, real QSL services, Windows/Linux and the full release gate after this change remain unverified. Other G03 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G05 preview of existing program-field mappings

The import preview now counts and displays changes made by the existing QRZ and eQSL program-field mapper on the parsed source records, including its precedence for already present target values. Counts refer to records read, including duplicates or records later rejected, not guaranteed inserted QSO. The QSL field-presence count now includes mapped eQSL/QRZ fields and CARD_QSL_RCVD. A present `N` is still a field presence, not a confirmation. The mapper and import behavior are unchanged; Italian UI text was added.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: before the change the actual offscreen app fixture failed because no mapping summary existed. Afterwards a synthetic two-QSO ADIF showed one mapping for each of QRZ status, QRZ date and eQSL status; reopening the log confirmed the first QSO's QRZ/eQSL states and that an explicit eQSL `N` on the second took precedence. The proprietary-field import fixture also passed. A separate pre-final screenshot showed mapped statuses while the QSL presence count incorrectly showed zero; after the correction the two-QSO fixture reports two. CTest adif, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4 after the final change, translation XML parsed and git diff --check passed. A screenshot before the final count correction showed the mapping text and controls readable at 700x430; the final screenshot was not repeated.

The displayed mapping list covers only the three existing program conversions, not all source-to-database transformations or vendor formats. Counts are based on the parsed intermediate ADIF, not fields already discarded by a converter; the additional-fields list can be long and the fixed-height dialog has not been checked with adversarially many field names. The test uses synthetic data and programmatic controls, not vendor exports, live services, physical UI or other platforms. Remaining G05 and desktop roadmap acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G05 scrollable import preview

The import preview's summary, additional fields, mappings and UTC warning now occupy a clipped, vertically scrollable region. Cancel and Import remain in a separate fixed action row. No parser, mapping or import behavior changed.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 using the same synthetic ADIF containing 150 proprietary fields: the before screenshot showed the field list clipped by the fixed dialog and the actions absent from view. After, an offscreen actual-app probe scrolled to the bottom and reported the last field present, the end reached and the action row within the dialog; the after screenshot showed the last field, mapping and both buttons. The probe uses programmatic scrolling, leaves the log empty and does not itself demonstrate wheel, keyboard or accessibility navigation. The existing program-field mapping app fixture passed as well. CTest adif, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4; git diff --check passed.

The test uses one synthetic long field list and a 700x430 offscreen dialog, not actual vendor files or all window sizes, desktop platforms, input methods or assistive technologies. Exceptionally large field inventories and conversion/mapping completeness remain open, as do the other G05 and desktop roadmap acceptance requirements.

## 2026-11-23 — G12 OCR candidate search log identity

OCR candidate searches now bind the request to the open log's UUID, file identity and open generation. The read-only worker begins a transaction and verifies the log UUID and file identity before querying; the UI accepts the result only if the original log session and file still match. A reopened or replaced log causes the advisory result to be discarded; a fresh search can run after a normal reopen. The search remains read-only and does not approve or alter a QSO.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: before the change, starting a search and synchronously closing/reopening the same log path produced a successful old-session result. The new fixture now rejects that result and a new search after reopening succeeds. A second fixture renames the still-open file and replaces its path with another database before result delivery; the result is rejected. CTest tesseractocr, database, migration, qsl, qslcards and qmlpopups passed 6/6; the actual offscreen app's OCR read/search and date/time-filter scenarios passed (four QtTest results including setup/cleanup). The timing fixtures force session/path changes before the queued completion is processed, but do not control the worker's precise opening order. git diff --check passed.

An initial replacement test also tried reopening the replacement while the old SQLite WAL was still associated with the original path and observed the old record; that assertion was removed because it does not establish safe live replacement. No safe live replacement or SQLite WAL recovery is claimed; close the database cleanly and handle its WAL/SHM files before replacement. The checks do not protect against changes after the last check, a copied identical file identity, all concurrent edits, real QSL images, physical UI or other desktop platforms. The remaining G12 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G12 OCR write origin checks

The desktop OCR attachment, reviewed-transcription save and acquisition undo now capture the open log UUID and file identity when scheduled. Each worker verifies both after acquiring its SQLite write transaction and again before commit; a mismatch rolls back and reports the log changed. The UI entry points refuse to schedule when the open file identity cannot be verified. The static core execute helpers retain optional empty identity arguments for existing direct callers; such callers do not receive this protection and must pass both values. These checks do not change the existing QSO revision/link guards or make live SQLite file replacement safe.

A/B: before the change, the new test requesting explicit origin verification failed to compile because the helpers had no origin arguments. Afterwards synthetic file-backed fixtures rejected a wrong file token for attachment, a wrong log UUID for undo, and either mismatch for review save; the corresponding valid-origin operations succeeded and existing atomicity/undo assertions passed. This demonstrates the new guard contract, not a reproduced live file-replacement write race. An intermediate build failed due to an incomplete updated undo signature; fixing the signature allowed the tests to pass. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest tesseractocr, database, migration, qsl, qslcards and qmlpopups passed 6/6. Actual offscreen app scenarios attached a synthetic snapshot to two QSO, undid an acquisition preserving a previous link, and saved reviewed text (five QtTest results including setup/cleanup). git diff --check passed.

A replacement after the final check and filesystem/SQLite WAL races remain possible; the origin file should be cleanly closed before replacement. A job started for one log can still complete there after the user switches logs, and its completion callback is not yet scoped to the current log session. Selections made before switching to a copied log with matching QSO identities need separate verification. Direct unguarded helper callers, real images, physical UI, Windows/Linux and remaining G12/desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G12 OCR write completion attributed to its origin

The completion of an OCR attachment, reviewed-text save or undo is now bound to the log path, UUID, file identity and open generation captured at scheduling. If the open session changed, the completion does not trigger the current log's success path. It retains the operation result and explicitly distinguishes a transaction already committed to the original log from a failed transaction, warning the operator to inspect the original before repeating a committed action. Italian messages were added. The worker can still finish on its original log after a switch; this change reports that outcome rather than undoing it.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: a synthetic file-backed fixture scheduled a review, polled SQLite until the text was actually committed without processing the queued completion, and reopened the same path before processing it. Before the change the callback incorrectly reported success for the new session; afterwards it reports the changed origin and `committed=true`, and the original review remains in the database. A second stale-review attempt after a session change reports failure and `committed=false`. The first fixture attempt lacked a timer include, and a subsequent attempt incorrectly expected the acquisition UUID in the creation result; these test setup errors were corrected before the A/B result. CTest tesseractocr, database, migration, qsl, qslcards and qmlpopups passed 6/6. Existing actual-app offscreen attach, undo and reviewed-text scenarios passed (five QtTest results including setup/cleanup); translation XML and git diff --check passed.

The forced session-change assertion is a core event-loop fixture, not a physical UI test or an end-to-end switch during all three write operations. Whether the warning remains visible if the corresponding dialog is closed by a log switch requires UI verification. Read-only history and revision callbacks still need equivalent session binding. SQLite live file replacement, external races after the last identity check, direct unguarded core helper calls, real QSL images, Windows/Linux and the remaining G12 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G12 OCR history tied to log session

Both read-only OCR history workers now capture the log path, UUID, file identity and open generation. They verify the UUID and file identity within their SQLite read transaction and discard their rows if the UI log session, file or identity changes before delivery. A fresh request remains possible after reopening. Existing paging, cancellation and write guards are retained.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: the file-backed test starts a history read and reopens the same path before its queued callback; before the change it incorrectly reported success. Afterwards both acquisition-history and review-revision reads are rejected in that situation with empty results, while a new revision read on the reopened session succeeds. The fixture controls completion delivery but not the worker's precise opening order. CTest tesseractocr, database, migration, qsl, qslcards and qmlpopups passed 6/6; actual offscreen app history/undo and reviewed-text scenarios passed (four QtTest results including setup/cleanup). git diff --check passed.

This does not establish safe live SQLite replacement, freshness after the final check, physical UI or all concurrent writer orders. The warning visibility after a log-switch closes the OCR window remains unverified, as do real cards, Windows/Linux and the remaining G12 and desktop roadmap requirements.

## 2026-11-23 — G12 OCR origin-change activity warning

When an OCR write completes after its originating log session or file has changed, the controller now adds the existing committed-or-failed warning and the original log path to the main activity model, independently of whether the OCR dialog handles its completion. The originating path is the path captured at scheduling and may no longer exist if the file was moved. No credentials or OCR transcription are added to this activity entry. This supplements the previous dialog-level result; it does not cancel or reverse an already committed write.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: an actual offscreen app starts a synthetic review save while an explicitly enabled probe holds the UI event loop for a bounded 3.5 seconds. The separate test process observes the committed review through read-only SQLite and renames the temporary database before the UI resumes. Before this change the completion produced no OCR entry in the activity model; after, the model contains an OCR warning with the original path and the completion is not reported as success on the current session. The first after-change probe read the model during signal delivery before the controller's handler had run; activity tracing confirmed the warning was appended afterwards, so the probe was corrected to inspect on the next event-loop turn. The existing app transcription-history scenario also passed. CTest tesseractocr, database, migration, qsl, qslcards and qmlpopups passed 6/6; git diff --check passed.

The test reads the actual activity model but does not physically open the activity tab or verify the entry's visual presentation. Activity is an in-memory, bounded model and is not a durable audit across restart; an operator may miss the warning if the activity tab is not visible. The timed UI hold fails if the worker or external rename does not finish within its window; it does not demonstrate safe live SQLite replacement, all commit races, a real card or Windows/Linux. Remaining G12 and desktop roadmap acceptance remains open.

## 2026-11-23 — G08 radio-net validation within QSO insertion

Manual QSO saves from a radio-net draft now pass the selected session and participant IDs to the database insertion. Inside the existing QSO savepoint, the insert rechecks that the session is open and its UUID, member call and station profile match the QSO and recorded radio-net metadata. The same arguments are forwarded through the existing contest-serial transaction. If the recheck fails, the insert rolls back. Ordinary inserts and imports without the explicit guard retain their existing behavior, including imported historical radio-net metadata. No schema change was made.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: in a file-backed fixture a guarded manual QSO succeeds while the session is open; a second connection closes the session, after which the guarded insert is rejected with the original QSO retained. On that same closed session the former unguarded insertion path accepts an identical record, demonstrating the difference. A separate import without a guard remains accepted. The initial new guard API test could not compile before the implementation. CTest radionet, database, migration, qmlpopups, newqsotuning and roversession passed 6/6; actual offscreen app scenarios for explicit repeated saves, preserving an existing draft and paginated reported QSO passed (five QtTest results including setup/cleanup). git diff --check passed.

The fixture forces closure before the guarded database recheck, not a precisely scheduled close between the recheck and SQLite insertion; SQLite locking/snapshot conflicts are expected to fail rather than silently accept, but that ordering was not separately forced. The guarded contest-serial combination has not been directly exercised, although existing serial database tests passed without a radio guard. Other callers that deliberately omit the guard retain prior semantics. Physical UI, RF, real multi-operator operation, Windows/Linux and remaining G08/desktop roadmap acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G08/G10 radio-net guard with contest serial

Added a file-backed, two-connection fixture covering the radio-net insertion guard together with the existing durable contest-serial transaction. A wrong reported member is rejected without adding a QSO or consuming the granted serial. A matching participant and open session save a QSO with serial 10 and session UUID, and bind the consumed grant to that QSO's UUID. After a second connection closes the session, another guarded grant remains granted and no QSO is inserted; reopening the database retains these states.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: after the same session closure, the guarded contest-serial path rejects its request while the prior unguarded path, using a separately granted serial for another requester, inserts and consumes its grant. This compares the two available API paths in the current build; it is not a rerun of the prior application version. The first fixture attempted to grant two simultaneous requests to the same requester and failed due to the existing active-request constraint; using a second requester corrected the fixture. CTest database, contestnet, migration, radionet and qmlpopups passed 5/5; git diff --check passed.

This verifies core transaction behavior with synthetic local records, not the UI route with an actual shared-serial network, a forced close between the final SQL check and insertion, hardware/RF, Windows/Linux or a release package. The unguarded API remains available for other callers. Remaining G08, G10 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G08 forced radio-net close between check and insert

Added an opt-in native SQLite authorizer fixture to force the specific ordering left open in the radio-net guard milestone. On a temporary file-backed database in WAL mode, one connection starts `insertQso`; the authorizer intercepts preparation of the QSO insert after the guard's session read, while a second thread and independent connection close and commit the session. The fixture requires the close to finish before allowing the insert to continue, and checks both the insert result and persisted QSO count.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 with the verified system SQLite driver: in separate otherwise equivalent synthetic logs, the unguarded path accepted one QSO after the close; the guarded path rejected it and left zero QSO. Both runs confirmed that the second connection successfully closed the session before insertion resumed. This compares API paths in the current build, not separate application versions. The targeted opt-in fixture passed without skips; with `DECOLOG_TEST_SYSTEM_SQLITE_AUTHORIZER=1`, CTest radionet, database, migration, contestnet and qmlpopups passed 5/5. git diff --check passed. The native hook is skipped by default and outside macOS, consistent with the existing authorizer fixtures; opt in only with a Qt driver using the same SQLite library as the resolved API.

This exercises one controlled ordering in WAL mode, not every concurrent schedule, rollback-journal mode, the guarded contest-serial path (which acquires a write lock earlier), actual-app UI, physical radio/RF or Windows/Linux. A failed guarded insert requires operator retry after reviewing the session; there is no automatic retry. Remaining G08 and desktop roadmap acceptance remains open.

## 2026-11-23 — G11 experimental local extension lifecycle

Added a standalone Python developer tool to install, list, run, update and remove trusted manifest-based read-only examples under a chosen dedicated directory. Each version is staged separately before an atomic active-pointer replacement; identical reinstall is idempotent and conflicting content under the same version is rejected. A marker prevents removal of unmanaged directories, while a damaged managed extension can still be removed. The tool copies declared Python dependencies, bounds file sizes, checks hashes before running, and reuses the existing separate-process harness and read-only API token. The tracker manifest now declares its shared `recent_activity.py` dependency. This is not an in-app installer or sandbox, and does not enforce independent extension permissions; see API.md for use and limits.

A/B: the new lifecycle test initially failed because the tool did not exist. Afterwards, isolated tests install and run the sample against a simulated local API, update and reinstall versions, reject changed same-version content, unsupported TX permission and symlink input, detect changed dependencies, remove a corrupted managed installation, preserve an unmanaged directory and confirm an unrelated SQLite log remains unchanged. The first actual-app integration exposed that the tracker example imported an undeclared helper; the manifest and lifecycle were extended to copy declared dependencies. The updated integration runs the installed tracker against the actual offscreen app and simulated PREDICT server, removes it, confirms it no longer runs and checks zero QSO and no control packets. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, CTest extension_manager, extension_harness, recent_activity_extension, tracker_extension, predict_extension_integration, localapi and database passed 7/7. Python tests also passed separately.

The manager runs trusted Python code with the user's filesystem/network privileges and a shared read-only token; the reduced environment, timeout and process-group cleanup are not a security sandbox. Concurrent management, all crash/power-loss points, hostile local filesystem races, package assets beyond Python files, GUI lifecycle, events, per-extension credentials, full-log reports, other platforms and the remaining G11/desktop roadmap requirements remain open. The integration uses simulated PREDICT and no RF or real services.

## 2026-11-23 — G11 validate extension updates before activation

The experimental lifecycle manager now uses the same capability selection as the execution harness before staging an extension, and compiles its entrypoint and declared Python dependencies without executing them. Unsupported capability combinations or syntactically invalid Python fail before changing the active pointer. The previous version remains active. This does not prove runtime behavior or remove the need for a functional harness run.

A/B: before this change an update declaring only `status.read` passed manifest validation and replaced a working `qso.read` version even though the harness could not run it; the new test failed because rejection was absent. Afterwards the unsupported update and a syntactically invalid update are rejected and version 1.0.0 stays active; a valid 2.0.0 update succeeds. A separate injected failure at the active-pointer write leaves 1.0.0 active, and repeating the already staged update succeeds. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest extension_manager, extension_harness, recent_activity_extension, tracker_extension, predict_extension_integration, localapi and database passed 7/7, including the actual offscreen app with simulated PREDICT. The manager's isolated tests passed separately; git diff --check passed.

The compiler does not execute extensions or establish correct imports, credentials, API interoperability, safe behavior or resistance to malicious code. Pointer-write injection is not a power-loss or filesystem durability test; concurrent management and rollback of a syntactically valid but malfunctioning upgrade remain open. The experimental tool is not an in-app installer or sandbox, and the remaining G11 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G11 optional functional preflight for extension updates

The experimental local extension manager now offers `install --verify-port PORT`, using the read-only token from the environment to run the staged candidate through the existing harness before changing the active pointer. It rechecks the manifest and declared file bytes after the run. A failed candidate, denied credential or changed staged file leaves an existing active version selected; a failed first installation removes only the managed directory created by that attempt. Offline installation remains available and does not perform runtime verification. API.md documents both paths and the trusted-code requirement.

A/B: on isolated directories with a simulated local read-only API, the unverified legacy install activated a syntactically valid candidate that crashed when run. With preflight, the same candidate was rejected and the previous version continued to produce the expected two-QSO report. An incorrect credential and a candidate that rewrote its own staged file were also rejected; a valid new version passed preflight and became active. The preflight test initially could not run against the old manager because the option did not exist. A separate test then exposed that a failed first installation left an incomplete directory that broke listing; cleanup was added and the test passed. The updated integration invokes the CLI preflight against the actual offscreen app and simulated PREDICT, then runs and removes the tracker. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 CTest extension_manager, extension_harness, recent_activity_extension, tracker_extension, predict_extension_integration, localapi and database passed 7/7; the five manager tests passed independently. git diff --check passed.

Preflight executes trusted code with the user's privileges and the shared read-only token. The harness checks only its bounded example output contract and cannot establish honest behavior, future reliability, independent permission isolation or absence of filesystem/network side effects. Offline updates can still activate a runtime-broken candidate; concurrent manager operations, crash/power-loss durability, graphical management, Windows/Linux, real services and the remaining G11/desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G09 reject out-of-model PREDICT Doppler

The PREDICT UDP snapshot reader now rejects a finite normalized 100 MHz Doppler value whose absolute magnitude is at least 100,000,000 Hz. In PREDICT's classical normalization, the value is the reference frequency times radial velocity divided by the speed of light; this bound only excludes responses implying a radial speed at least equal to light speed in that approximation. It is deliberately far wider than plausible satellite shifts and is not an operational transponder limit or proof that any accepted value is accurate. On rejection the pending snapshot is cleared and no subsequent TLE/mode request is issued. The calculation and valid command sequence are otherwise unchanged. Source inspected: https://github.com/kd2bd/predict/blob/master/predict.c (PREDICT 2.2.5 normalization, also inspected in the local source at `/tmp/decolog-predict-g13/predict.c:5050`).

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: with a synthetic UDP server returning `100000001`, the prior tracker published a snapshot; the updated tracker rejects it and clears the snapshot. The fixture also rejects `-100000000`, then accepts `961.742249` and verifies the normal six-command read-only sequence; the rejected request ends after GET_DOPPLER. CTest predict_ui, predict_extension_integration, predicttracker, predictpass, tracker_extension, localapi and database passed 7/7, including programmatic offscreen app scenarios and simulated trackers. git diff --check passed.

This is a gross input sanity check under the specified classical model, not relativistic modeling or a validation of actual radial speed, orbital elements, PREDICT freshness, satellite identity, band plan or live frequencies. Values within the bound can still be wrong. No real RF, radio/rotor, physical UI, other desktop platform or current orbital prediction was tested. Remaining G09 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G09 no rig tuning from tracker-prepared draft selections

In a QSO draft opened from the read-only tracker, changing band, mode or submode no longer calls the existing `tuneTo` radio/DecoLink action. The form still updates its local frequency according to its existing band/mode behavior, so the operator must review any changed draft values before saving; the warning now states that these selections do not tune the radio. Ordinary manual QSO drafts retain their existing tuning behavior. No tracker, CAT or PTT protocol code changed; Italian text was added.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: a QML fixture with a simulated connected rig activated mode selection and a band change. Before the change, the ordinary manual draft made one tuning call and the tracker-prepared draft added two more; afterwards the ordinary draft still makes one and the tracker draft adds zero. The separate RX field remains populated in that fixture. The fixture invokes QML signals/functions programmatically and counts calls to a fake controller; it is not a physical CAT or RF test. CTest newqsotuning, predict_ui, predicttracker, predictpass, localapi and database passed 6/6, including actual offscreen app scenarios with synthetic PREDICT; translation XML and git diff --check passed.

The end-to-end app test does not combine this changed selection with a live or simulated rig connection, and two communicating Decodium sessions, real radio/CAT/PTT, physical UI and Windows/Linux remain unverified. This does not certify transponder frequencies or automatically refresh the fixed tracker snapshot. Remaining G09 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G09 tracker draft with loopback rigctl A/B

Added an actual-app offscreen probe for a tracker-prepared QSO draft while the application is connected to a local split-rig simulator. A separate local synthetic PREDICT UDP server supplies a current-dated synthetic TLE and read-only snapshot. After the draft opens, the probe programmatically activates a mode selection and a band change. The rigctl simulator records requests; the test checks that the tracker uses its six read-only commands, that no QSO is saved, and that no `F`, `I`, `S`, `T` or `M` rig write/PTT command is received.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: with the guard from commit 8ab0aed, the app issued 37 rig queries and zero checked writes; with only that QML condition temporarily reverted, the same probe observed `F 144050000`, `M CW 0`, `F 14030000`, `M CW 0` and failed. The corrected source was restored and rebuilt; CTest tracker_rig, historical_rig, rig_split_manual, newqsotuning, predicttracker, predictpass and database passed 7/7. The probe and fixture are isolated under temporary paths; git diff --check passed.

The test uses one offscreen app, synthetic PREDICT and a loopback rigctl simulator, not two Decodium sessions, physical input, hardware CAT/PTT, radio or RF. It checks the listed rigctl command forms and does not prove all hardware/backend behaviors or every UI interaction. The TLE is artificially current-dated for the fixture and is not valid orbital data. Windows/Linux and remaining G09/desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G09 preserve tracker draft frequencies during selections

A tracker-prepared QSO draft no longer replaces its Doppler-adjusted TX/RX values when band, mode or submode is selected. Reopening the same dialog as an ordinary draft clears tracker state and restores ordinary tuning. CTest tracker_rig, historical_rig, rig_split_manual, newqsotuning, predicttracker, predictpass and database passed 7/7 before the final probe assertion; after it, tracker_rig, newqsotuning and database passed 3/3. Translation XML and git diff --check passed.

The UI selections are programmatic and the tracker/rig endpoints simulated; this is not physical input, two Decodium sessions, real CAT/PTT, radio or RF evidence. The corrected values remain a fixed snapshot and can become stale; the operator must review explicit edits and actual transponder data. Windows/Linux and the remaining G09/desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

Extended the actual-app tracker/rigctl fixture with a separate explicit-save scenario. With the block restored and rebuilt, it saves TX `145.998596`/`2m`, RX `435.004184`/`70cm`, SAT, OSCAR-7 and CW; no checked rig writes occurred. The app, PREDICT server and rigctl are local synthetic fixtures with programmatic UI; no two Decodium sessions, physical keyboard, actual hardware CAT/PTT, radio or RF were used.
