## 2026-11-23 — G05 LoTW interrupted TQSL outcome

A TQSL subprocess that crashes or is killed after launch now emits one completion marked as requiring reconciliation, rather than an early process-error completion followed by another completion. A process that never starts remains a distinct local failure. TQSL cancellation, unexpected server response, network failure and unknown exit codes are conservatively classified as requiring review before retry; known TQSL rejection, local library and syntax cases retain their existing handling. For a running LoTW batch, controller cancellation now pauses automatic LoTW uploads and retains the batch context and temporary ADIF until the process exits, so the single uncertain result can be processed. The automatic setting is persisted; the QSO is not marked sent merely because the process stopped. Italian messages were added. The certificate-check dependency can be injected for isolated subprocess tests; production defaults to the existing real certificate check.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: a POSIX fake TQSL process that crashed after launch previously emitted two completions; after the change it emits exactly one uncertain result. A started fake process killed by cancellation also produces one uncertain result, while a non-executable fake produces a local start failure. In a separate real QslController fixture, cancelling the running fake previously left automatic LoTW upload enabled; afterwards it is immediately paused, the batch and temporary file remain until process exit, the file is cleaned up, the QSO remains pending and the paused setting survives controller restart. Another fixture verifies the same persisted pause after a crash. Exit-code tests cover partial, rejected, network, cancelled and unknown outcomes. CTest qsl, qslcontroller, database, migration, localapi and qmlpopups passed 6/6; the targeted process/controller tests passed independently. Translation XML and git diff --check passed.

The processes and QSO are synthetic; no TQSL certificate, LoTW account, live upload or actual remote outcome was exercised. Pausing automatic upload is not a durable per-batch reconciliation journal: abrupt termination of the entire application before it receives the subprocess outcome, manual re-enabling, and detailed per-QSO/account history remain open. The controller-level fixture does not verify the physical UI, other desktop platforms or all QProcess failures. LoTW's developer documentation places responsibility on the logger to avoid resubmitting already submitted contacts: https://lotw.arrl.org/lotw-help/developer-submit-qsos/ . Remaining G05 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G05 LoTW pre-send checkpoint and review

Before launching TQSL, the controller now writes a settings checkpoint with the original log identity and path, location, per-QSO identity and revision, and a SHA-256 fingerprint of the exported ADIF. No QSO comments or credentials are included. Pending or unreadable checkpoints block further LoTW uploads across logs, including automatic uploads. A separate cross-process lock prevents another running controller from acknowledging the checkpoint during an upload. On a known local failure the checkpoint can be cleared; uncertain outcomes retain it. A successful subprocess result updates QSL state only when the original log and captured QSO revisions still match, and removes the checkpoint only after the state update succeeds. The QSL settings page lists the batch and requires explicit confirmation after checking LoTW and correcting local states; acknowledgement itself changes no QSO status. See LOTW-CHECKPOINT.md for the operator procedure and limitations.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: a previously existing checkpoint did not prevent a synthetic TQSL launch before this change; it blocks the launch afterwards. Before the flight lock, another controller could acknowledge a batch during the synthetic process; afterwards it cannot. With the revision guard temporarily disabled, a QSO edited during the synthetic upload caused the checkpoint to clear; with the guard restored, the checkpoint remains and the edited QSO remains pending. A separate child controller process was force-killed after its synthetic TQSL started; a restarted controller found the checkpoint, kept automatic upload paused and refused another launch. Tests also cover changed log identity, malformed checkpoint, lock failure, cancellation and successful synthetic completion. The actual offscreen app shows the contact list and warning, and a programmatic reject preserves the checkpoint while explicit confirmation clears it without changing QSO status; a captured screenshot was inspected. The UI probe uses programmatic interaction, not physical input.

The fixtures use synthetic contacts and subprocesses: no actual TQSL certificate, live LoTW account or remote upload was exercised. The process-crash test is not a power-loss durability test for QSettings. The cross-process lock is held by the controller, not an orphaned TQSL after its parent dies; the confirmation warns the operator to wait for TQSL to stop. A malformed checkpoint has no guided recovery, a temporary ADIF can survive abrupt termination, and every log/account is conservatively blocked by one pending checkpoint. Windows/Linux, physical UI and the remaining G05 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

After the final warning text change, the macOS build of the app and focused test targets succeeded; CTest qsl, qslcontroller, database, migration, localapi and qmlpopups passed 6/6, and the focused offscreen review scenario passed. Translation XML parsing and `git diff --check` passed.

## 2026-11-23 — G05 LoTW concurrent QSL-state changes

The LoTW batch now captures the presence and fields of each contact's LoTW QSL status in the same read transaction as its ADIF and QSO revisions. Before applying a successful synthetic TQSL result, the write transaction rechecks those states as well as the existing QSO identity and revision. A missing or changed state, or a failed read, prevents the batch update, preserves the checkpoint for operator review and pauses automatic sending. This is necessary because QSL-state writes do not increment the QSO revision. Other services' states are not compared.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: a second connection modified the LoTW QSL state while a synthetic TQSL process waited. Before the change, completion cleared the checkpoint despite that edit; after, the checkpoint remains, automatic upload is paused and the operator's QSL error remains unchanged. Targeted tests also passed for a concurrent QSO edit and a normal synthetic success/cancellation. The app and focused targets built; CTest qsl, qslcontroller, database, migration, localapi and qmlpopups passed 6/6; git diff --check passed.

The test controls a change before the final transaction, not every concurrent SQLite ordering. A SQLite snapshot upgrade can fail and retain the checkpoint; no automatic remote reconciliation is provided. The remote process and records are synthetic, with no actual LoTW account, TQSL certificate or physical UI; other desktop platforms and remaining G05 and desktop roadmap acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G06 manual review of uncertain recovered activations

A QSO marked `PERIOD_UNCERTAIN` now shows a warning in its detail window and offers a dedicated review of the local POTA, SOTA, WWFF and IOTA references, grid, station call and operator. The dialog starts from the QSO's existing values, instructs the operator to consult original records and enter only known values, and does not infer a session. Cancel leaves the draft untouched. Applying the reviewed values changes only the draft, removes the uncertainty marker and records a review timestamp; a separate QSO save uses the existing guarded UUID/revision/log-path update. Existing QSO history remains the record of the change. Review is an operator assertion, not validation of the references, a programme eligibility decision or proof of the actual site/operator.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: the new actual-app offscreen scenario initially failed because the review path did not exist. Afterwards, the test observes the warning, cancels without removing the marker, applies synthetic values to a draft and verifies that an intervening QSO edit causes the guarded save to fail without clearing the stored marker. After reloading, it applies and saves again; reopening SQLite confirms the entered POTA reference and grid, preserved concurrent comment and review timestamp, with no activation session UUID invented. The existing recovered-period app fixture also passed. The app and focused targets built; CTest database, migration, qmlpopups and activation passed 4/4; translation XML and git diff --check passed. The initial requested CTest regex also included `rover`, but no test by that exact name was selected; no rover test is claimed here.

Interaction in the UI fixture is programmatic, not physical keyboard/mouse or visual-layout verification. The guarded save covers the existing UUID/revision/log-path contract, not every file-replacement or external-write ordering. An operator can deliberately confirm blank fields, and the app does not verify the historical facts or catalogue validity. English and Italian strings are supplied, with other languages falling back to English. Actual Decodium recovery, RF, Windows/Linux and the remaining G06 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G06 exclude uncertain activation periods from partitions

A tracked-session QSO carrying `APP_DECOLOG_ACTIVATION_REVIEW=PERIOD_UNCERTAIN` is now counted as reported but excluded from programme partition groups and export selection, with an explicit reason in the exclusion list. Export rechecks grouping in its consistent read snapshot, so a newly uncertain record invalidates the earlier group. After an operator removes the review marker through the reviewed-QSO workflow, the QSO can be grouped again if it still has a matching session UUID and programme fields; this does not establish programme eligibility.

The previous review dialog said no session was assigned, which was inaccurate for a recovered source record that already carried an activation UUID. It now displays the existing UUID, asks the operator to review it and offers an explicit checkbox to remove the association; otherwise it is retained. This corrects the preceding milestone's description: the review did not invent an association, but could preserve one supplied by the source. Removal is saved through the same guarded QSO update, not applied to the database merely by checking the box.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: a synthetic QSO with both an existing session UUID and uncertain-period marker was included in a POTA group before the change; afterwards it is counted as an uncertain exclusion and becomes groupable only after the marker is removed. A separate fixture marks a QSO uncertain after preview and confirms export is refused and an existing file remains byte-identical. The actual offscreen app review scenario exercises cancellation, a rejected stale save, explicit removal of an existing source UUID and a successful guarded save. The app and focused targets built; CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4. Translation XML and git diff --check passed.

The grouping and export fixtures use synthetic records, and the app probe interacts programmatically; the new exclusion text and checkbox have not been verified with physical input or visual layout inspection. QSO without a session UUID are not associated with any tracked session by this change and require a separate assignment workflow. The operator must verify any association retained and the underlying period, site and references; the app does not establish historical truth or programme eligibility. Windows/Linux, real Decodium/RF and remaining G06 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G06 explicitly assign a reviewed QSO to a tracked session

The uncertain-activation review can now optionally associate the QSO with a session recorded in the current log: it offers the active session and pages of up to 100 closed sessions with their recorded dates. Keeping or removing an existing association remains possible. The interface tells the operator to check original records and dates; neither listing nor selection proves that the QSO belongs to the session. Applying changes the draft only. On the existing guarded QSO save, an explicitly changed nonempty activation UUID is now checked against `activation_run` within the same SQLite immediate transaction as the UUID/revision check and QSO update; a missing session fails without saving. An unchanged source UUID is not newly validated by this rule.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: before this change, a guarded QSO update accepted an explicitly assigned nonexistent session UUID; afterwards it rejects it without changing the QSO, while assigning an existing recorded UUID succeeds. In an actual offscreen app with a synthetic closed session and a QSO lacking a session UUID, a programmatic review selects that session and saves the association and POTA reference while clearing the uncertainty marker. The previous cancellation, stale-edit and explicit-removal scenario also passes. The app and focused targets built; CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4; translation XML and git diff --check passed.

The app probe selects the only listed session programmatically; pagination beyond 100 sessions, active-session selection, physical input and visual layout were not exercised. The check establishes session existence at the save transaction, not temporal overlap, correctness of the programme references or historic site/operator truth. Existing UUID/path guarded-save limits, later removal or modification of session records, Windows/Linux, real Decodium/RF and remaining G06 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G06 session pagination and removed-session A/B

Added an actual-app offscreen fixture for reviewing a QSO against 105 synthetic closed activation sessions. It checks the first page contains 100 sessions and offers a next page; the second contains the remaining five and has no next page. The probe selects the oldest session through the QML selection signal, goes back, checks the selected UUID remains recorded, applies the draft and verifies the saved UUID in SQLite. This compares first- and second-page behavior in the same build; it is not a before/after app-version comparison. Interaction is programmatic. The preceding milestone's pagination limitation is therefore narrowed to other sizes, concurrent list changes, visual layout, physical input and other platforms.

A separate file-backed two-connection fixture selects an existing synthetic session, deletes it through the other connection and attempts the guarded QSO update. A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: temporarily disabling only the session-existence guard from commit 8087333 allowed the update; restoring it rejects the update and preserves the QSO revision and empty association. The source was restored and the app and focused targets rebuilt. The new pagination scenario passed again after restoration, and CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4; git diff --check passed.

This tests a deletion completed before the save transaction, not every concurrent SQLite ordering. It does not detect a still-existing session whose dates or history changed after selection, establish historical membership, test a real Decodium session or RF, exercise active-session selection or verify visual accessibility. Remaining G06 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G06 guard selected session history at QSO save

Each listed closed activation session now carries a SHA-256 review token derived from its UUID, recorded start/end, initial and latest state, and ordered recorded change events. The active-session option obtains the same kind of token. Selecting a session retains its token across pagination; the explicitly reviewed QSO save compares it again inside the same SQLite immediate transaction as the QSO revision and session-existence checks. A missing token prevents the draft from being applied, while a changed or missing session rejects the save. A freshly reloaded and explicitly reselected session can be saved. The previous unguarded API remains available to callers that do not supply a token; keeping an existing association without explicitly selecting a session does not invoke the new token check.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: in a file-backed two-connection fixture, one connection captured the session token and the other inserted a history event. With only the new token comparison temporarily disabled, the guarded update accepted the stale assignment; after restoring it, the update is rejected with QSO revision and association unchanged. A second change to the recorded end time is likewise rejected, while a fresh token allows the update. The rebuilt actual offscreen app passed its programmatic reviewed-assignment, 105-session pagination and cancellation/stale-QSO scenarios; CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4. Translation XML and git diff --check passed.

The controlled mutation precedes the write transaction; no claim is made for every SQLite schedule, physical input, real Decodium/RF, other platforms or historical correctness. The interface does not display the full change-event history, so the token detects subsequent change but does not establish that the operator reviewed all historic states. Retrieving tokens for up to 101 listed sessions reads their histories synchronously; large-history latency and cancellation have not been measured. Existing association retention and other unguarded callers do not use the token, and remaining G06 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G06 defer activation history fingerprints until selection

The closed-session page now fingerprints only each session's recorded header and state, without reading its events. When the operator selects a row, the controller takes a consistent snapshot containing both the current header fingerprint and the complete session/history fingerprint. If the displayed header no longer matches, or either fingerprint cannot be read, the selection cannot be applied and the review window shows a warning. The full fingerprint remains bound to the draft and checked in the guarded write transaction as before. A change only to hidden events between listing and selection becomes part of the newly captured fingerprint; an event change after selection is rejected at save. The operator still cannot inspect the full event history from this dialog.

A/B synthetic in-memory benchmark on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, one run each with 100 sessions and 1,000 events per session: listing took 184 ms before and 1 ms after; calculating one complete selected-session token took 1 ms in both runs. These are single measurements with synthetic short events, not p95, file-backed, concurrent-log, UI responsiveness or large-single-session evidence. The opt-in `DECOLOG_BENCH_ACTIVATION_HISTORY=1` fixture retains the dataset for repeat measurements. Two-connection tests distinguish a changed header from an event-only change, verify deleted sessions fail snapshot retrieval and retain the stale-history rejection at save. Actual offscreen app probes for a single reviewed assignment and pagination across 105 sessions passed after the final UI warning change. The app and focused targets built; CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4; git diff --check passed.

The new warning's visual presentation and actual mouse/keyboard operation were not inspected, and the stale-list branch is tested at controller/core level rather than through a forced external change in the running UI. Reading one selected session's full history is still synchronous and unbounded by event count. Historical membership, real Decodium/RF, other platforms and the remaining G06 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G06 inspect recorded activation changes during review

A selected session in the uncertain-QSO review now has a read-only recorded-changes window. It displays event time/type, recorded programme, reference, grid, station-profile ID and additional programme references, in ascending keyset pages of 50. Invalid or oversized state is identified; display fields are bounded and the window warns that long values may be shortened. It explicitly says these records do not prove the physical site or operator and that a profile ID does not reconstruct the profile's historical contents. The operator still enters reviewed values manually. Before each page, the controller checks the selected session's full token inside the same SQLite read snapshot; changed or missing sessions return an error without rows. The existing guarded QSO save still independently checks the token.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: before implementation the new history endpoint returned no events; afterwards a file-backed two-connection fixture reads 55 synthetic events in pages of 50 and 5, checks recorded reference/grid/profile, and rejects all rows after the other connection changes the session. In an actual offscreen app, the programmatic reviewed-assignment probe opens the history window, observes a synthetic event and then completes the guarded QSO save. After the final warning-text change, the app and focused targets built, the focused offscreen scenario passed and CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4. Translation XML and git diff --check passed.

The endpoint and app probe use synthetic data; pagination navigation and changed-session warning have not been exercised by physical input or visually inspected, and the test does not verify every malformed or oversized input. Every page currently re-hashes the complete event history synchronously, so latency on a very large individual history remains open. The view is not evidence of real-world position/operator, programme eligibility, RF, real Decodium interoperability, Windows/Linux or completion of G06 and the desktop roadmap.

## 2026-11-23 — G03 guarded paper-route cost review

The paper-route cost editor now captures an opaque review token bound to the current QSL plan generation and the open log's path, persistent UUID, file identity, open generation and SQLite data version. Saving or clearing from this editor requires the same current plan and token; a guarded `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction rechecks the captured log/file/data identity before modifying the route cost and commits before reporting success. A stale editor must be reopened against a current plan. Existing lower-level unguarded save/clear APIs remain available to their existing callers; this protection applies to the reviewed desktop editor. Italian warning text was added.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: in file-backed fixtures, the previous unguarded route-cost path wrote into a replacement clone reopened at the same path. With the reviewed origin, both save and clear are rejected after replacement and leave the replacement's cost unchanged. A fresh review can save; an external database write invalidates a subsequent clear while preserving the cost, and another fresh review can clear it. The clone shares the original persistent UUID, so the check also exercises open generation and file identity. The actual offscreen planner scenario still saves a synthetic cost and completes its existing queue/confirmation checks. The app and focused targets built; CTest qslplanner, qsl, database, awards and qmlpopups passed 5/5; translation XML and git diff --check passed.

The replacement and external-change assertions exercise the core transaction with synthetic local logs, not a forced replacement while the physical editor is open. The normal editor probe is programmatic, and plan-generation invalidation in the new controller wrapper has not been isolated by a dedicated UI race test. File replacement after the final identity check, unguarded callers, Windows/Linux, physical UI and the remaining G03 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open. No external price, QSL request or service was contacted.

## 2026-11-23 — G03 cost editor after plan rebuild

Added an actual-app offscreen fixture that opens a QSL paper-route cost editor with a previously saved synthetic EUR 9.99 estimate, rebuilds the plan for the same log while the editor remains open, then programmatically invokes Save and Clear. With the plan-generation guard from commit 5c618ee, both actions retain the editor, show the stale-review warning and leave the original 9.99 cost in the database. The existing normal planner scenario also passes, including saving its reviewed cost and the paper-queue/confirmation checks.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: with only the plan-generation comparison temporarily removed, the stale Save succeeded and closed the editor; the subsequent Clear was rejected after that successful save invalidated the plan, so the A result demonstrates the Save failure mode rather than a separate unguarded Clear. After restoring the comparison and rebuilding, both actions are rejected and the original cost remains. The fixture accepts either Italian or English warning text; the initial assertion was corrected after the A run revealed the configured Italian locale. CTest qslplanner, qsl, database, awards and qmlpopups passed 5/5; git diff --check passed.

UI interaction was programmatic and the plan rebuild was requested by the probe, not by a physical user. This does not reproduce replacement of an open database file while the editor is visible, every possible concurrent write, physical UI, live services, Windows/Linux or the remaining G03 and desktop roadmap acceptance.
