## 2026-11-23 — G03 cost editor with an externally replaced log

Added an actual-app offscreen fixture that creates a synthetic QSL route and EUR 9.99 cost, clones its SQLite log with the same persistent UUID, opens the reviewed cost editor and waits for an explicit ready marker. A separate test process renames the open database and places the clone at the original path, then waits for the app to detect that its plan is no longer current. The programmatic probe invokes Save and Clear; the guarded app keeps the editor open with the stale-review warning, and the replacement clone still contains 9.99. The test skips if the platform refuses to rename the open file. The prior plan-rebuild editor fixture also passed.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: routing the reviewed calls temporarily through the old unguarded cost methods led SQLite to return a disk I/O error for both attempted writes after the live rename. Thus this A run does not demonstrate an unintended write to the clone; it demonstrates that SQLite itself rejected this specific live-file replacement and that the old UI displayed the database error. After restoring and rebuilding the reviewed guard, the same probe receives the explicit stale-plan warning for both actions and the clone's cost remains unchanged. The test intentionally invokes QML signals programmatically, not physical input. CTest qslplanner, qsl, database, awards and qmlpopups passed 5/5; git diff --check passed.

Live replacement of an open SQLite file is not a supported operational workflow; this fixture forces one failure ordering to check rejection. It does not prove every filesystem timing, other platforms, physical UI, remote QSL service, or completion of G03 or the desktop roadmap. No QSL request was sent.

## 2026-11-23 — G03/G01 fail closed when paper QSL status cannot be read

The database now exposes a checked QSL-status read that distinguishes an empty result from a failed SQL prepare, execution or iteration. Paper QSL batch operations use it before modifying a contact. A failed read rolls back the current transaction block, reports a failure and stops processing the remaining contacts; blocks already committed by the existing bounded batching policy remain committed. The existing `qslStatus` API remains available. This applies to the paper queue and other operations using the same batch worker, including its email completion path; it does not claim all other callers of the legacy read are guarded.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 using the opt-in system-SQLite authorizer fixture: denying the `qsl_status.last_error` read made the previous planner queue path report success for an already sent/received QSO with a conflicting route. A second two-QSO fixture denied the second status read and the previous worker left the first QSO queued. With checked reads restored, the planner operation is rejected and the original sent/received/direct state remains; the two-QSO current transaction block is rolled back and both original states remain. The first test compile needed a mutable QVariant for the native driver handle; that fixture error was corrected before the A/B run. The focused normal queue tests, including a file-backed log, passed. With `DECOLOG_TEST_SYSTEM_SQLITE_AUTHORIZER=1`, CTest qslcardcontroller, qslcards, qslplanner, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 6/6. The actual offscreen planner/queue scenario also passed; git diff --check passed.

The authorizer fault uses an in-memory synthetic log and is opt-in because it requires the Qt driver and resolved native SQLite library to be the same implementation. It does not test every SQL failure, interprocess ordering or a failure after an earlier batch block has committed. The offscreen app test exercises the normal path, not injection into its separate worker connection. Physical UI, real QSL services, Windows/Linux and remaining G03/G01 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open. No QSL was sent.

## 2026-11-23 — G03 reject a plan when a candidate's QSL status cannot be read

The QSL planner now uses the checked status reader from the preceding milestone for every selected candidate. If that read fails, it returns an error with an empty plan instead of presenting incomplete pending-service or paper-route information. It does not change QSO or QSL data. This protects the candidate-status stage; it does not establish that every query used by the award calculator or every other planner input reports failures explicitly.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 using the opt-in verified-system-SQLite authorizer: a synthetic candidate normally has a sent/unconfirmed card and recorded direct channel. Denying the `qsl_status.last_error` read previously produced no planner error; afterwards the plan returns an error without candidates or paper routes. With `DECOLOG_TEST_SYSTEM_SQLITE_AUTHORIZER=1`, CTest qslplanner, qslcardcontroller, database, awards, qsl and qmlpopups passed 6/6. The normal actual-app offscreen planner/queue/confirmation scenario also passed; git diff --check passed.

The injected fault is local, synthetic and in-memory; it is opt-in because the Qt SQL driver must use the same native SQLite implementation as the resolved authorizer. The offscreen app probe covers the normal path rather than an injected failure in its worker. Other failed award queries, Club Log reconciliation status reads, cross-process schedules, physical UI, Windows/Linux and remaining G03/G01 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open. No QSL was sent.

## 2026-11-23 — G05 Club Log reconciliation requires readable local QSL state

Club Log batch-result matching and presence reconciliation now require a successful checked local QSL-status read before using a contact's stored remote identity. An unreadable status leaves the result uncertain and the upload checkpoint pending. Full and partial reconciliation callbacks also re-read local QSL status inside their SQLite write transaction and reject an existing Club Log remote ID that differs from the identity being reconciled; the transaction rolls back on mismatch or read error. The result remains subject to the existing checkpoint and per-record matching rules. Italian text was added for the unreadable-status warning.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 with the opt-in verified-system-SQLite authorizer: a synthetic final batch result previously cleared its checkpoint when the `qsl_status.last_error` read was denied; afterwards the checkpoint and unsent local states remain. A second fixture changes a stored remote ID before the write transaction: the previous reconciliation accepted it, while the updated transaction rejects it and preserves the changed ID. Denying the transaction's status read also rejects reconciliation; after restoring a matching readable ID the normal synthetic reconciliation succeeds. The app and focused targets built; the focused result/rejection/reordering and loopback-query scenarios passed, and with `DECOLOG_TEST_SYSTEM_SQLITE_AUTHORIZER=1` CTest qslcontroller, qsl, database, migration, localapi and qmlpopups passed 6/6. Translation XML and git diff --check passed.

The injected failures use synthetic local logs and an opt-in native authorizer; no live Club Log account, token or upload was used. The presence-reconciliation and partial-transaction checked-read branches were exercised by existing normal simulated flows, but the authorizer fault was not separately injected at every asynchronous callback or every partial-write ordering. The check is conservative about differing nonempty stored IDs and does not prove remote identity truth. Physical UI, Windows/Linux, real service interoperability and the remaining G05 and desktop roadmap requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — User-reported callsign search and bulk paper confirmation

A complete callsign-shaped search now matches the callsign exactly, while recognised Maidenhead locators continue to use general search. Prefix `call:` searches partial callsigns; `all:` retains the former substring search across calls, locators, names, QTH and comments. Empty explicit prefixes match no rows. The search tooltip explains these choices. This intentionally changes the default for strings that look like complete callsigns; ambiguous strings can use the explicit prefixes.

The bulk-edit window remains open while the operation runs and displays changed, unchanged and failed counts plus the first error; hiding it does not cancel the operation. For QSL status fields it explains that `N` is treated as empty when the empty-only option is enabled. A new completion signal also reports an open-log change without claiming the original operation's outcome. Existing activity-log reporting remains.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 using synthetic data: the old general search for `J51A` included an unrelated `ZY0SAT` QSO whose locator was `HJ51AM`; the new exact-call search excludes it and also excludes a separate `J51ABC` call. The explicit `all:` and `call:` alternatives and empty prefixes are covered. In the old-dialog comparison, the actual offscreen app applied `QSL_RCVD=Y` to 32 selected synthetic QSO but immediately closed the dialog, so the outcome was only in the activity log. The revised dialog shows 32 changed; repeating shows 0 changed and 32 unchanged. The app fixture invokes the same selected-row menu handler programmatically, selects the field and its default value, and injects a pointer click on the actual Apply button; it verifies both unrelated contacts remain unchanged in SQLite. Core tests cover existing paper sent/via state, received date, and equivalent LoTW/eQSL bulk statuses. An initial core fixture accidentally triggered duplicate detection; using distinct bands corrected it before drawing the conclusion.

The reported failure to persist paper confirmations was not reproduced with the supplied synthetic scenarios: the old core and old app path did update those QSO. The actual reporter's database, exact pre-existing QSL values, operating system and physical interaction were not available, so the reason for their no-action observation is not proven. UI interaction was simulated offscreen; no physical mouse or remote QSL service was used. CTest qsomodel, migration, database, qmlpopups, qslcards and qslcardcontroller passed 6/6; the focused UI and core tests passed, translation XML and git diff --check passed. No release or deployment was performed.

## 2026-11-23 — User-reported intermittent cluster tuning mode

CAT frequency and mode polls issued before a cluster tune are now discarded if their replies arrive after the corresponding new set command. The new-QSO panel also applies its existing post-tune grace period to a prepared cluster QSO. For FT8, FT4 and FT2 cluster spots, the panel retains the selected digital log mode and signal frequency when CAT reports the corresponding dial frequency and USB carrier; a subsequent different dial or mode resumes following the radio. A same-dial change from digital USB to voice USB cannot be distinguished from CAT alone; the operator can select the intended mode in the panel.

A/B on macOS arm64 with local synthetic fixtures: before the change, delayed pre-tune CAT replies restored the old CW/7.030 MHz values in the real offscreen app after an FT8 spot was selected; after the change the selected J51A/FT8/14.075300 MHz remained intact. The local rigctl simulator received F 14074000 and M USB 0. A separate QML comparison showed the old panel convert FT8 to SSB when the radio reported USB after the grace period; the new panel retains FT8 and follows a later RTTY/frequency change. The offscreen app probe invokes the same cluster tune handler programmatically, not a physical double click. CTest spots, rig, modes, newqsotuning, cluster_spot_race and clustercontroller passed 6/6; the app and focused targets built successfully and git diff --check passed.

These tests reproduce concrete timing and carrier-interpretation defects but do not establish that they explain every reported occurrence or that a physical radio was previously set incorrectly. No physical rig, RF, two live Decodium sessions, other operating system or actual user station was tested. Cluster mode inference can also depend on the information in the received spot. The paused desktop roadmap remains paused.

## 2026-11-23 — Identify HRDLog pending contacts

The logbook QSL filter now includes HRDLog upload pending, using the same unsent status set as the uploader, including contacts without an HRDLog status row. The filtered view refreshes when upload status changes. Other logbook filters still apply, and the upload service's optional since-date may narrow the actual upload batch; a pending contact is not necessarily a rejected contact or the cause of an automatic-upload problem. Existing QSO detail shows a saved service error when one exists.

A/B with synthetic local contacts on macOS: the previous model had no HRDLog pending filter; the new filter showed an explicitly unsent contact and a contact without a service row while excluding a sent contact. Before the refresh fix, a contact just marked sent remained in the filtered view; afterwards it disappeared. The app and model targets built, and CTest qsl, qslcontroller, qsomodel and qmlpopups passed 4/4. No real HRDLog account, user's log, automatic-upload incident, physical UI, or remote service was tested. The user's actual blocking cause remains unknown; a transport or service error can also leave contacts pending. The desktop roadmap remains paused.

## 2026-11-23 — Cluster FT spots use CAT data mode

The cluster-specific FT8/FT4/FT2 override forced plain USB, unlike the normal CAT mapping. It now uses the shared digital mapping, PKTUSB; flrig maps that to an available USB-D or DATA-U spelling, and TCI maps it to DIGU. CW, RTTY and voice retain their existing mappings. A former code comment warned that some Hamlib backends can map a CAT data mode to a dedicated RTTY modem: the actual outcome remains backend- and rig-dependent and needs real-device verification.

A/B on macOS: the previous mode test returned USB where PKTUSB was expected; after the change the app's offscreen cluster scenario sent F 14074000 and M PKTUSB 0 to a local rigctl simulator and kept FT8 in the QSO panel. CTest rig, modes, tci, flrig, newqsotuning, cluster_spot_race and clustercontroller passed 7/7. No real radio, live flrig/Hamlib rig backend or Decodium session was tested. The previous milestone's USB command observation remains historical evidence of the old implementation, not the current behavior. The desktop roadmap remains paused.

## 2026-11-23 — TS-50 serial digital mode compatibility

For the locally managed serial Hamlib TS-50S model (2001), RigController now maps CAT PKTUSB to USB, because the installed Hamlib capability dump lists AM, CW, USB, LSB and FM but not PKTUSB. Other model IDs and external network, TCI, flrig and OmniRig connections retain their previous mapping. This applies to both cluster and ordinary radio tuning through RigController. It does not establish why the reporter's physical TS-50 selected LSB.

A/B on macOS: the previous model mapping returned PKTUSB for TS-50S; the new mapping returns USB and retains PKTUSB for IC-7300 model 3073. A local rigctl simulator observed +M USB 0 for the TS-50 mapping and +M PKTUSB 0 for the IC-7300 mapping, with the existing FT/RTTY regression test also passing. App built, CTest modes, newqsotuning, cluster_spot_race, clustercontroller and rigcontroller passed 5/5. No physical TS-50, IC-7300, live serial Hamlib backend or Decodium session was tested; on-device confirmation remains necessary. The desktop roadmap remains paused.

## 2026-11-23 — G01 preserve manually selected FT mode on USB CAT feedback

The New QSO panel now remembers the intended FT8, FT4 or FT2 mode and selected dial frequency after a connected operator selects that mode through the panel, just as it already did for a cluster spot. A matching USB or PKTUSB CAT report keeps the digital mode in the QSO draft; a different frequency or incompatible mode resumes normal radio following. On the same frequency, a change from digital USB to voice USB remains indistinguishable from CAT feedback alone; choosing another mode in the panel clears the remembered intent.

A/B using the actual QML component with a simulated connected CAT source: after manual FT8 selection and the normal post-tune delay, the previous code changed the draft to SSB when USB was reported; the revised code retains FT8 at the chosen frequency and follows a subsequent change to CW at another frequency. The application built on macOS arm64 and CTest modes, newqsotuning, cluster_spot_race and rigcontroller passed 4/4. The fixture invokes panel functions and synthetic signals; no physical radio, Decodium session, physical interaction or other platform was tested. The broader G01 reliability and integrated release acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G06/G10 scope active-session views and exports to tracked contacts

For an active session with a tracked UUID, the existing active-session count, unique calls, per-band summary, duplicate check, selected IDs, recent list, rate/multiplier summary and contest score query now require that UUID in the QSO's recorded activation field as well as the existing start-time condition. ADIF and Cabrillo selection use the same scoped ID list. An active legacy session without a UUID retains the historical time-only selection. Independent contacts and contacts assigned to other tracked sessions no longer enter these tracked-session views merely because they have a later timestamp. Unassociated contacts are not automatically assigned or altered; manual review remains necessary. The start-time condition is retained, so a deliberately assigned QSO before the recorded start is not in these active-session views even though the separate partition preview may show it.

A/B on macOS arm64 with an in-memory synthetic tracked session: before the change an associated and an unrelated QSO produced count 2; afterwards the count, recent list, rate, duplicate check and ADIF export include the associated contact and exclude the unrelated one. A legacy active-session fixture without UUID retains time-based inclusion. One existing recent-contest test fixture had inserted a QSO without applying the session and was corrected to create an associated contact. The app built; CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4 (one opt-in benchmark skipped), and git diff --check passed.

This does not establish historical membership, official programme eligibility, real importer compatibility, every contest scoring edge, physical UI, other operating systems or cross-process scheduling. UUID filtering parses ADIF JSON in these existing queries; large-log responsiveness and index use were not measured. Legacy time-only sessions can still include unrelated contacts and cannot safely infer associations. The remaining G06, G10 and release acceptance requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G06/G10 use the existing session index for active views

The tracked-session queries introduced in the preceding milestone now select a SQL predicate according to whether the active session has a UUID. Tracked sessions use the indexed JSON expression directly; legacy sessions without a UUID retain the time-only predicate. Bound parameters and the previous start-time constraint are preserved across counts, duplicates, recent rows, rate, score and export selection. This addresses the unmeasured indexing risk recorded above; it does not change session membership rules or make the synchronous operations asynchronous.

A/B on macOS using a synthetic in-memory Python SQLite database with 250,000 live QSO rows and 50 matching a session: EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN for the prior OR predicate selected idx_qso_live_time and five runs took 16.84–17.19 ms; the direct expression selected idx_qso_reported_activation and five runs took 0.01–0.05 ms, with both counts equal to 50. These are warm local measurements of a single count query, not a file-backed, Qt SQL, p95, memory-pressure or UI latency benchmark; index selection and timing may differ for other data distributions and SQLite versions. The app and activation test target built, and CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4, including tracked/unrelated and legacy-session synthetic cases. No hardware, RF, real Decodium or other platform was tested. Remaining G06, G10 and release acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G06/G10 include explicitly associated older QSO consistently

For tracked active sessions, the session UUID is now the membership criterion in active counts, lists, duplicates, score and exports, even if an associated QSO's recorded UTC precedes the session start. This matches the existing partition preview and accommodates an operator's explicit historical association without changing the QSO's timestamp. The rolling rate still uses its own 10/60-minute windows. Legacy active sessions without a UUID continue using the original start-time criterion. This supersedes the preceding milestone's statement that tracked active views always impose the start-time condition; their JSON expression remains indexable.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 with a synthetic tracked session and an explicitly associated 2020 QSO: the previous active-session count was zero while the partition preview contained the contact; afterwards count, ID list, recent list and duplicate detection include it, the preview agrees and the current ten-minute rate remains zero. The existing synthetic unrelated-QSO and legacy-session cases continue to pass. The app and activation target built; CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4, with the opt-in benchmark skipped. This tests the association and recorded UTC, not the correctness of an operator's decision or official eligibility; a prematurely or incorrectly associated contact can now appear in those views. The fixture does not exercise physical UI, real importer, concurrency or other platforms. A full per-program eligibility and historical review workflow remains open.

## 2026-11-23 — G06/G10 exclude uncertain associations from active exports

Tracked active-session views and their ADIF/Cabrillo selection now exclude contacts marked `APP_DECOLOG_ACTIVATION_REVIEW=PERIOD_UNCERTAIN`, matching the existing programme partition exclusion. This affects the count, recent list, duplicate and score/rate inputs as well as the export selection; it does not delete or modify the QSO. Once the operator completes and saves the existing review, the QSO can reappear if associated with that session. Legacy sessions without a tracked UUID retain their former time-only behaviour, since their membership cannot safely be inferred. The programme partition preview still reports the excluded QSO and reason.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 using a synthetic session-associated uncertain QSO: before the change the active count was 1 despite the partition exclusion; afterwards count and selection are empty and an ADIF export with no other contacts creates no file. After clearing the marker through a simulated stored-record update, the contact is counted and ADIF export succeeds. This fixture does not exercise the review UI or physical input, prove historical membership, validate official programme eligibility, cover all SQL failure paths or establish behaviour on other platforms. The app and activation target built; CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4, with the opt-in benchmark skipped. The added JSON check's large-log cost has not been measured. Remaining G06/G10 and release acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G06 show pending activation-period review in session summary

The active tracked session now counts its QSO marked for activation-period review separately and shows a warning explaining that they are excluded from totals and exports; it points to the programme preview to identify them and to each QSO for review. A query failure produces an unknown/error warning rather than a zero count. After a QSO detail save involving the current tracked session (or an active untracked legacy session), the session counts are refreshed. The message is translated into Italian. Unassociated uncertain QSO are not included in the session-specific count and must still be found through the existing import/recovery reporting; the review is an operator assertion, not proof of programme eligibility.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: the previous controller exposed no uncertainCount property and the synthetic one-contact fixture read zero; after the change it reports one while the QSO is uncertain and zero after the fixture removes the marker and explicitly refreshes. The app and focused target built; CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4 (one opt-in benchmark skipped). The counter's display and the automatic refresh after a QSO-detail save were compiled but not exercised through a running UI or physical input; direct external edits, bulk operations, other platforms and large-session latency remain unverified. Remaining G06/G10 and integrated release acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G06 active-session uncertainty warning actual-app A/B

An isolated offscreen application probe now creates a synthetic tracked POTA session and QSO, marks the existing QSO's activation period uncertain through the app's save API, observes the dialog, then clears the marker through the same save API. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2, A/B with only the new post-save activation refresh temporarily removed showed count 0, total 1 and hidden warning after marking; with the refresh restored it showed count 1, total 0 and a visible Italian warning, then count 0, total 1 and hidden warning after clearing. Both saves returned no error. The B source was restored, the app rebuilt, and CTest activation and qmlpopups passed 2/2; git diff --check passed.

The probe uses programmatic dialog/controller calls and an isolated synthetic SQLite log, not the guarded human review flow, physical mouse/keyboard, visual layout inspection, hardware, other platforms or external operator records. It does not cover log replacement, concurrent editors or all ways of modifying/removing QSO. The previous milestone's statement that the post-save refresh had not been exercised in a running app is superseded for this one save path. G06/G10 and integrated release acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G06 open an excluded activation QSO for review

Each exclusion in the activation programme preview now offers an Open QSO action. The preview records the local QSO UUID and revision with its ID; before opening, the dialog checks the active log path and rereads UUID and revision. A mismatch leaves the QSO closed and asks for a new preview. A matching action closes the activation dialog and opens the existing QSO card, where the operator can review an uncertain activation period explicitly. This is navigation only: it does not alter the contact or declare programme eligibility. The message is available in Italian.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: with only the added exclusion UUID/revision fields temporarily removed, the new synthetic fixture failed because the QSO identity was absent; after restoring them the focused test and CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4. An isolated offscreen app created a tracked POTA session, marked its synthetic QSO uncertain, ran the real asynchronous preview and programmatically activated the excluded row's Open QSO button; the existing QSO card opened for ID 1. The app built and translation XML and git diff --check passed.

The offscreen probe invokes the button programmatically; physical mouse/keyboard, visual layout, stale-QSO/log rejection in the running UI, concurrent log replacement and real activation data remain unverified. UUID/revision and log-path checks reduce stale selection risk but do not prove a QSO's activation history or protect all possible filesystem replacement races. Remaining G06 and release acceptance work remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G06 stale excluded-QSO navigation A/B

An isolated offscreen application scenario now captures a real excluded preview row, modifies the underlying synthetic QSO through the application's edit API and invokes the same opening function with the captured row. With only the revision comparison temporarily disabled, the stale row opened the QSO and showed no warning; with the comparison restored, opening was refused and the recalculation warning appeared. This verifies the previously untested stale-revision branch in the running application. The correct source was restored and rebuilt; CTest activation and qmlpopups passed 2/2 and git diff --check passed.

The edit and navigation are programmatic, not physical mouse/keyboard or visual-layout verification. This scenario changes one QSO in the same open log; path changes, UUID substitution, external processes and filesystem replacement races are not tested by it. G06 and integrated acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G11 isolate corrupt extension entries in the developer manager

The experimental local extension manager's list operation now reports an unreadable, invalid or unmanaged directory as an `invalid` entry and continues listing other entries. It does not print exception messages or file contents. Running still validates the selected installation, and removal still requires its ownership marker. The API guide explains that an invalid listing does not prove manager ownership; the tool remains separate from the logger, uses trusted subprocesses and is not a sandbox or a per-extension permission system.

A/B on macOS with two synthetic installations: before the change, corrupting one active pointer caused listing to raise a JSON parse exception and hid the healthy extension; afterwards the invalid entry and the healthy manifest are both returned, the corrupt managed entry can be removed, and the healthy extension remains listed. The existing changed-dependency case now expects an invalid listing while still checking that execution and reinstall reject it. Python manager and harness tests passed 11/11; CTest extension_harness, extension_manager and localapi passed 3/3; git diff --check passed. This does not demonstrate protection from hostile concurrent filesystem mutations, full in-app lifecycle, independent permissions, Windows process handling, actual extension interoperability or atomic API idempotency. Those G11 requirements and integrated release acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G11 reserve keyed API requests before processing

A keyed local QSO request now creates a persistent status-0 reservation before processing any records. A unique random owner is required to replace that reservation with the completed response. A repeat with matching body, format and source while unresolved returns HTTP 503 with `retrySafe:false`; different context returns 409. Completed responses retain the existing 30-day expiry and legacy records' comparison rules. Unresolved reservations are not automatically expired, including after restart, and the API guide states that no reconciliation/removal interface exists yet. A request that cannot reserve the key stops before QSO processing. This is a conservative durable uncertainty marker, not atomic QSO/response or exactly-once external side effects.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: in the prior post-processing journal-write fault comparison recorded above, the QSO was saved but no durable unresolved marker existed, and retry after fault removal reached ordinary duplicate handling. In the new actual-app HTTP fixture, a trigger rejects finalizing the already-created reservation after a QSO is saved: the first response is 503 with results, the app is restarted, and a matching retry is rejected as unresolved with `retrySafe:false`; one QSO remains, while a different X-App gets 409. A separate trigger rejects the initial reservation and confirms no QSO is saved; after removing it, that request succeeds. A file-backed two-connection test shows only one successful reservation, preserves an old unresolved reservation across reopen and expiry cleanup, rejects another owner, and permits its owner to finalize once. The app and focused targets built; the actual-app HTTP scenario passed, and CTest database, migration and localapi passed 3/3. The database reservation test initially failed with the unimplemented reservation path and passed after implementation; git diff --check passed.

The fault injection rejects specific SQLite statements; it is not a kill-at-every-instruction, disk-full, power-loss or simultaneous-process stress test. QSO and response remain separate commits, and network/QSL/UI side effects are not transactional. An unresolved reservation may mean no QSO was ever saved; it remains indefinitely until a future reviewed reconciliation feature is implemented, and users must not blindly resend under a new key. No GUI reconciliation, cleanup policy for resolved reviews, per-extension permission isolation, real remote service, Windows/Linux or integrated release acceptance is established. G11 and the broader goal remain open.

## 2026-11-23 — G11 inspect one idempotency key without replaying it

The local API's full-token capabilities now advertise `idempotency.inspect`. Authenticated `GET /api/v1/idempotency`, with the key in the existing `Idempotency-Key` header, reports `unresolved` with `retrySafe:false`, or `completed` with the original HTTP response status, and the recorded timestamp. It does not return the request body, original response body, reservation owner or key. An absent key gets 404, a missing/oversized key 400 and a journal read error 503. The existing read-only credential cannot access the endpoint. Inspection does not resolve or clear an entry; the API documentation describes its limits.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: the actual-app HTTP fixture previously got 404 when inspecting a completed key; after implementation it reads completed/201, distinguishes an absent key, and after an injected response-finalization error and app restart reads unresolved with `retrySafe:false`. It checks that bodies are absent, full-token capabilities advertise the operation, read-only capabilities do not, and the read-only token receives 403. The full synthetic local API scenario passed; CTest localapi, database and migration passed 3/3; git diff --check passed.

The endpoint's state is a point-in-time observation: unresolved includes both an in-flight request and one interrupted before or after QSO save. It does not provide a human reconciliation interface, unlock keys, establish remote QSL results, atomically couple QSO writes to response/side effects, or prove exactly-once behaviour. The tests inject SQLite failures but do not cover all crash, power-loss or simultaneous-process schedules, external services or other platforms. G11 and integrated acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G11 local review of unresolved API requests

Setup's Decodium link page now offers a read-only view of up to 50 unresolved keyed API requests in the current log. It shows the request timestamp, source, a SHA-256 fingerprint of the key, and up to 10 compact contact identities from ADIF or JSON requests no larger than 64 KiB. Larger requests and lists over ten contacts are marked incomplete. The raw key, reservation owner, complete request body and credentials are not returned by this view. The panel explains that a request may still be running or may have stopped before or after QSO persistence, asks the operator to check the log and does not clear or retry a reservation. A new view read is explicit; log-change notifications hide the previous result. The Italian UI and API guide describe these boundaries.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: before implementation the new synthetic database review fixture returned no rows; after implementation it returns a request with a K1ABC identity and omits the raw key, owner and body. Additional fixtures cover JSON-wrapped ADIF, oversized input and an unavailable journal table. An isolated actual app was opened once to create a temporary database, given one synthetic unresolved row directly through SQLite, then restarted offscreen; the Setup probe activated the view and observed one rendered row and one K1ABC contact. The first full build attempt failed because the new test lacked a QJsonObject include; after adding it the app and database target built. CTest database, migration, localapi and qmlpopups passed 4/4, translation XML and git diff --check passed.

This is a bounded, point-in-time read-only view, not automatic matching, a guarded resolution/removal workflow or proof that a QSO or external side effect occurred. The 50-request limit has no pagination yet. The UI probe invokes the control programmatically and does not establish physical interaction, visual layout, all locale/platform support, concurrent replacement or large-journal latency. Keys with predictable content can potentially be guessed from their fingerprints; do not treat the fingerprint as a secret. Atomic QSO/response and side effects, reconciliation and the remaining G11 and release acceptance requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G11 page through unresolved API requests

The local read-only unresolved-request view now pages through the current log in insertion order, 50 rows at a time, using a validated internal rowid cursor encoded as a string. The cursor does not contain the idempotency key or request body. Previous/Next controls keep prior cursors in the dialog, and the explicit review button restarts at the first page. Missing or invalid cursors return an error; a now-empty later page asks the operator to refresh. As before, the view neither resolves nor retries requests. It is not an immutable snapshot: a request completing or arriving while paging can change the rows visible on a later read.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 using 55 synthetic reservations: the previous single-page result had no next cursor and the new test failed; afterwards the first page contains 50 rows, the second five, their fingerprints differ, returning to the first returns its first entry, and invalid cursors are rejected. In an isolated offscreen application, Setup's programmatic controls displayed 50, then 5, then 50 rows; the fixture inserted the reservations directly into a temporary database. The final text-only clarification was rebuilt afterwards. CTest database, migration, localapi and qmlpopups passed 4/4; translation XML and git diff --check passed.

This tests neither snapshot isolation under concurrent writes nor physical mouse/keyboard, visual layout, huge-journal latency, power loss or other platforms. It does not reconcile or remove unresolved reservations, prove whether a request ran, make QSO/response/side effects atomic, or complete G11 and integrated acceptance.

## 2026-11-24 — G11 serialize keyed API processing for future reconciliation

A keyed local API QSO request now acquires a QLockFile scoped to the canonical open log path and SHA-256 of its key before looking up or reserving the key, and holds it until the request handler finishes. If the current open file identity is no longer available or the lock cannot be acquired, the handler returns 503 before processing the request. The lock path does not contain the key in clear text. The API guide documents the writable-directory requirement and limitations. This lock prepares a shared coordination point for a future guarded human reconciliation; no action to resolve a reservation has been added.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 with an isolated real app process: the test process held the target key's lock and sent a synthetic QSO to the app. Before the change the app returned 201 and saved it despite the lock; afterwards it returned 503 with `retrySafe:false` and no QSO. Releasing the lock allowed the same request to return 201 and save one QSO. The same actual-app scenario passed the existing unresolved-reservation restart, failed-insert and completed-replay cases. CTest database, localapi and migration passed 3/3; app and test targets built; git diff --check passed.

This verifies cross-process contention for the same canonical pathname, not all simultaneous-request schedules, stale PID/lock recovery, other hosts/filesystems, hard links, lock-file deletion/replacement races, older application versions, power loss or Windows/Linux. A 503 on lock contention is conservative and does not by itself prove whether another request has saved anything. QSO persistence, response journal and external effects remain non-atomic; unresolved reservations still lack a guarded review/clear workflow. G11 and integrated acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G11 guarded operator review of unresolved API requests

Setup's unresolved-request view now offers an explicit Mark reviewed action for rows whose request details fit the bounded view. A confirmation asks the operator to check the original request, log and external effects and explains that the outcome remains unknown, no QSO is changed or resent and the original key may expire after 30 days. Cancel changes nothing. Confirmation acquires the same per-key cross-process lock as API processing, checks the open log identity and a review token covering the key, timestamp, request context, bounded body and owner, then changes only the unresolved journal row in an immediate SQLite transaction. Requests still in use, changed, missing, or too large are rejected; the UI does not offer confirmation for truncated rows. This records an operator review, not a determination of success or failure.

A reviewed entry has internal status -1 and is no longer in the unresolved list. API inspection reports `reviewed-unknown` with `retrySafe:false`; a POST with the same key and context gets 409 rather than replaying or resending. Like completed entries, this reviewed marker can expire after 30 days. It is not a permanent duplicate barrier. Documentation and Italian UI explain these limits. This supersedes the prior statements that no review action exists; there is still no automatic reconciliation, no QSO/response/side-effect atomicity and no review action for incomplete request previews.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: before this increment the database review fixture had no row ID/token and failed. Afterwards a file-backed two-connection fixture rejects a held per-key lock and a changed request, records a fresh review once, preserves QSO count and refuses a second review. In the real offscreen app's HTTP fixture, a synthetic post-save journal failure leaves one QSO and an unresolved request; a second database connection records the review, after which inspection returns reviewed-unknown and retry returns 409 without adding a QSO. An isolated offscreen Setup probe exercised the actual confirmation callback: cancel retained one unresolved row; confirm removed it from the list, stored status -1 and left QSO count zero. It used programmatic callbacks and direct synthetic fixture setup, not physical interaction or a real operator assessment. App/test builds succeeded; CTest database, localapi, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4. The final confirmation wording clarification was rebuilt afterwards; translation XML and git diff --check passed.

The record is an operator assertion and cannot prove whether any QSO or remote action occurred. A request in an older app version or accessed through a different hard link may not honor this lock. All filesystem race, process crash/power loss, multi-host and platform schedules remain unverified; malformed or oversized requests cannot be marked via this UI and require a future comprehensive review workflow. G11 and integrated release acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G11 retain reviewed unknown API outcomes

Idempotency cleanup now expires only positive completed HTTP responses after the existing 30-day retention. Both unresolved reservations (status 0) and operator-reviewed unknown outcomes (status -1) remain without automatic expiry, preventing an old unknown-outcome key from silently becoming available again. The confirmation text and API guide have been updated. This supersedes the preceding milestone's 30-day expiry statement for reviewed unknown entries; it does not make them permanent across database deletion, restoration, manual edits or application versions with different cleanup logic.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: a synthetic reviewed entry with its timestamp set to 2020 was removed by the former cleanup and the new regression test failed; the updated cleanup retains it, while the existing expired-completed-response test still passes. The actual offscreen app HTTP fixture now ages a synthetic reviewed unknown entry, checks that inspection still reports reviewed-unknown and retry remains 409, and verifies the previously saved QSO count stays one. The app and focused tests built, the HTTP fixture passed, and CTest database, migration, localapi and qmlpopups passed 4/4; translation XML and git diff --check passed.

Indefinite retention also retains the stored request context and body, can grow the log, and currently has no operator-controlled archival/retention workflow. It is not transactional exactly-once processing or proof of the remote outcome; requests with a new key still require manual reconciliation. Existing backups, old app versions, power loss, other platforms and remaining G11 and release acceptance remain unverified.

## 2026-11-24 — G11 minimize reviewed unknown request bodies

When an operator marks an unresolved API request reviewed, the journal transaction now replaces the request body with a versioned SHA-256 digest while retaining the key, request format and source context. A same-body retry remains blocked as reviewed-unknown and a different body remains a conflict. Existing reviewed-unknown rows written by the previous version still compare using their original bodies; they are not migrated or automatically rewritten. Unresolved entries keep their bodies for review and completed responses retain their existing retention. This supersedes the preceding milestone's statement that newly reviewed rows indefinitely retain their full logical request bodies.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: the new file-backed synthetic review test failed before the change because the stored body still contained the full ADIF; after the change it retains a digest, matches the original, rejects a changed body and also accepts the previous raw-body representation for comparison. In the isolated actual-app HTTP test, a reviewed request still reports reviewed-unknown, an identical retry is rejected without another QSO and a different body returns the key-conflict response. App and test targets built; the focused HTTP scenario and CTest database, migration and localapi passed 3/3; git diff --check passed.

This is logical minimization, not secure deletion: prior bytes may persist in SQLite free pages, WAL, snapshots or backups. Unsalted SHA-256 of predictable request content can be guessed, and the key and source metadata remain. Existing reviewed rows retain their original bodies until a separately designed migration; new requests still retain their body while unresolved. No assertion is made about secure erasure, crash/power-loss durability, remote side effects, other platforms or the remaining G11 and integrated release requirements.

## 2026-11-24 — G09 record the tracker snapshot time on an unchanged draft

An explicitly saved PREDICT-prepared satellite QSO now records `APP_DECOLOG_TRACKER_SNAPSHOT_UTC` when its satellite, SAT propagation and TX/RX frequencies still match the generated draft. The time is the PREDICT position-response receipt time, converted to explicit UTC; it is not a measurement of the radio or proof of orbit/contact validity. The manual-save controller only stores a valid explicitly zoned timestamp for SAT propagation. If the operator changes satellite or either frequency before initial save, the timestamp is omitted. The draft displays the timestamp with a warning, and the PREDICT guide states that later edits do not automatically clear it and QSO history should be checked. The tracker continues to send no CAT/PTT commands.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: before the change the QML test could not set a tracker receipt time and the actual-app synthetic tracker/rig fixture saved no snapshot metadata; afterwards the QML test submits it for unchanged values and omits it after TX, RX or satellite edits. The offscreen app with synthetic PREDICT and loopback rigctl explicitly saves a QSO with both frequency fields and a recent UTC snapshot timestamp, while the fixture observes no checked CAT/PTT write commands. The initial baseline app fixture failed at an absent ADIF extra (its assertion was then clarified before the B run). App built; CTest newqsotuning, tracker_rig, tracker_rig_save and database passed 4/4, and after the final guard/label changes the two affected scenarios passed again. Translation XML and git diff --check passed.

The fixture uses synthetic orbital elements, a simulated tracker and rig, and programmatic UI interaction; it is not physical input, two Decodium sessions, real RF/CAT/PTT, live orbital data or other platforms. The timestamp is untrusted provenance and can remain after a later QSO edit; it does not establish present frequency correspondence. G09 and integrated release acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G09 archive snapshot provenance when local QSO frequencies change

For a local QSO edit or bulk edit, changing SAT_NAME, PROP_MODE, FREQ or FREQ_RX now removes an unchanged `APP_DECOLOG_TRACKER_SNAPSHOT_UTC` from the current QSO, while the existing QSO history preserves the prior record and timestamp. Numeric frequency strings with the same parsed value and case-only satellite/propagation differences do not cause removal; editing an unrelated comment retains the timestamp. An explicitly different supplied timestamp is preserved, as are other update paths such as cloud imports; the field remains untrusted source metadata. This supersedes the previous blanket statement that all later edits leave the timestamp unchanged. The PREDICT guide now distinguishes local edits from other update paths.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 with a synthetic satellite QSO: before the change correcting RX left the timestamp in the current record; after the change it is absent from the current record and present in the previous history entry, while a comment edit preserves it. The test also covers a bulk satellite-name change and an injected SQLite update failure, which rolls back both the QSO change and history entry. After the core change CTest database, migration, newqsotuning and tracker_rig_save passed 4/4; the targeted rollback test passed after it was added. The application and database target built and git diff --check passed.

This does not establish that the original tracker frequencies were physically used or prevent source metadata being misleading through non-local update paths, later reversion or manually supplied timestamps. Cloud/import and all concurrent SQLite schedules, actual UI editing, real radio/rotor/RF, two Decodium sessions and other platforms were not tested in this increment. G09 and integrated release acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G07 distinct UTC activity days in custom reports

Custom reports now offer a third aggregation, distinct UTC calendar days with at least one QSO, alongside QSO and distinct-call counts. It works with the existing one-to-three grouping dimensions, year/band filters, saved report definitions, filter persistence, preview, CSV and PDF headings. The UI explains that unparseable dates are excluded and the result is neither operating duration nor award eligibility. SQL's date conversion uses the UTC interpretation of stored timestamps; this does not validate that a historical timestamp was correctly recorded.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: the previous custom-report validator rejected the new `days` aggregation. Afterwards a file-backed synthetic fixture with four QSO counts two UTC days, including an offset timestamp that crosses back into the previous UTC day; CSV labels the result `distinct_utc_days`, and a second database connection reloads the saved definition. An offscreen app with three separately inserted synthetic QSO saved and restored a named report, selected `days` and displayed one 20m group with value 2. The final explanatory wording was rebuilt afterwards. CTest activityreport, database and qmlpopups passed 3/3; the focused activityreport and qmlpopups checks passed after final edits; translation XML and git diff --check passed.

This is an added aggregation, not complete configurable arithmetic metrics, arbitrary filters, official award rules or proof of club membership. SQLite date parsing and the recorded timestamps determine the result; malformed dates are not individually listed. The app probe is programmatic and synthetic, not physical UI, large-log concurrent responsiveness, Windows/Linux or integrated acceptance. Remaining G07 and the desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G07 mean recorded transmit power

Custom reports offer mean recorded TX power in watts by the existing dimensions and filters. Each group shows the number of numeric, finite, nonnegative samples and excluded QSO; zero is included and missing, invalid, negative or infinite values are excluded. A group without samples has no mean. Saved definitions and CSV include this aggregate and CSV leaves an unavailable mean blank. PDF output has mean, sample and excluded columns. The UI explains that recorded power is not measured transmitter output. An overflow resulting in a non-finite average aborts the report rather than displaying an invalid value.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: the new synthetic file-backed fixture initially failed because `mean_power` was unsupported; after implementation it returns 7.5 W from 5 and 10, with two samples and three excluded values (missing, negative and nonnumeric), and an empty mean for a separate all-missing group. It checks CSV and definition reload from a second connection. A separate isolated actual offscreen app, using three QSO directly inserted into a temporary SQLite log, saved and restored the report and returned 7.5 W, two samples and one exclusion. The initial fixture insertion omitted required timestamps and was corrected before the app scenario passed. The app and focused test built; CTest activityreport, database and qmlpopups passed 3/3; translation XML and git diff --check passed. The final generic explanatory text and Italian translation were built afterwards.

The app probe drives QML programmatically; physical UI and visual layout were not checked. PDF formatting for this aggregate, real measured RF output, large-log performance, other platforms and integrated release acceptance remain unverified. This is one numerical aggregation, not complete configurable arithmetic metrics or all G07 requirements; the desktop roadmap remains open.

## 2026-11-24 — G07 missing power in preview and PDF verification

Fixed the custom-report preview for a power group with no valid samples: the SQLite null arrives in QML as an empty string, which JavaScript previously converted to 0.00. The preview now shows a dash for an empty value or zero samples; an actual zero with samples remains numeric. The existing CSV blank and PDF `(not recorded)` representation remain unchanged.

A/B in isolated offscreen app processes on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: for a synthetic 40m group with one missing power and no samples, the same delegate rendered `40m · 0.00 · 0 · 1` before, and `40m · — · 0 · 1` afterwards. A 20m group with 5 W, 10 W and a missing value continued to render 7.50, two samples and one exclusion. The actual app exported a PDF, and text extraction plus a visual inspection of its rendered first page confirmed the two rows and column headings. The fixture uses direct synthetic database inserts and programmatic QML controls; it does not prove physical UI interaction. A repeatable CTest app/PDF probe was added when Python and pdftotext are available; CTest activity_power_pdf, activityreport and qmlpopups passed 3/3. The PDF check covers this short one-dimensional example, not multipage or all dimension/layout combinations, other operating systems or real RF. Remaining G07 and integrated acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G07 distinguish a recorded zero from missing power

The power-report regression fixture now compares a group with an actual recorded 0 W to one with no recorded value. The offscreen app preview shows 0.00 and a dash respectively, and the actual exported PDF shows 0 and `(not recorded)` respectively. This is an A/B comparison of two data cases, not a before/after change in product behavior.

The first expanded app test failed because the virtualized ListView had not instantiated the later delegates; the QML probe now scrolls to each row before reading it, and the expanded check passes. ## 2026-11-24 — G07 large file-backed report snapshot comparison

Added a reproducible two-connection test on a file-backed synthetic log containing 100,000 QSO. After the reader pins a verified snapshot, the writer adds a 20 W QSO. The report on the original snapshot still sees 100,000 samples at 10 W; after closing that snapshot, a fresh report sees 100,001 samples and the independently calculated new mean. This is an A/B comparison of the same log before and after the inserted record, not a comparison of application versions. On one macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 run the insert took 4 ms and the insert plus two report queries 80 ms after seeding; these single measurements are not latency guarantees.

The insertion occurs after the snapshot is pinned but before the report query begins. The test does not exercise an insertion while the query is actively executing, the actual app's UI responsiveness, slow disks, many groups, other concurrent schedules, other platforms or real customer logs. The focused test and CTest activityreport, activity_power_pdf, database and qmlpopups passed 4/4; git diff --check passed. Remaining G07 concurrency/responsiveness and full desktop acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G07 insertion during an active report scan

Added an opt-in, deterministic SQLite progress-hook fixture for a 100,000-QSO file-backed report. The callback runs while SQLite is executing the report query and inserts one 20 W QSO via a second connection before allowing the scan to continue. The in-flight snapshot returns 100,000 samples at 10 W; after closing it, a fresh query sees 100,001 and the independently computed updated mean. This A/B compares the pinned snapshot and a subsequent fresh snapshot of the same synthetic log. The test asserts that the callback actually ran and the insertion succeeded.

On macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2, `otool -L` confirmed that the Qt QSQLITE driver links `/usr/lib/libsqlite3.dylib`; with `DECOLOG_TEST_SYSTEM_SQLITE_AUTHORIZER=1`, the native progress-hook test passed. The ordinary CTest activityreport skips this special hook without explicit opt-in. CTest activityreport, activity_power_pdf, database and qmlpopups passed 4/4; git diff --check passed. The fixture deliberately pauses the scan and performs the write in the callback using a separate connection; it does not establish independent thread scheduling or actual application UI responsiveness, slow-disk performance, other query shapes, other operating systems or full G07/release acceptance. The previous milestone's active-query gap is narrowed to this controlled core scenario.

## 2026-11-24 — G07 offscreen app report load probe

Added a standalone synthetic actual-app probe comparing the same custom mean-power report with 100 and 500,000 file-backed QSO. A QML 10 ms timer runs between the report request and its asynchronous result; the probe checks the returned sample counts and mean and, for a report taking at least 100 ms, at least one timer tick. On the recorded macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 run, 100 QSO took 3 ms with zero timer ticks, while 500,000 took 233 ms with 14 ticks and a 21 ms maximum observed gap. A prior run measured 232 ms, 14 ticks and 19 ms gap. These are single-machine observations, not performance guarantees or p95 measurements. The script is `tests/tools/activity_report_load_probe.py` and accepts the app executable path.

The fixture inserts synthetic records directly before each app launch; it does not write during the active app report or exercise physical input, visually inspect layout, measure general event-loop responsiveness, slow disks, multiple groups or other platforms. The core snapshot test in the previous milestone covers a controlled insert during a query separately. The app was rebuilt; the saved standalone probe passed and CTest activityreport, activity_power_pdf, database and qmlpopups passed 4/4. Remaining G07 and full desktop acceptance stay open.

## 2026-11-24 — G07 saved exact mode/submode filter

Custom reports now accept an optional exact recorded mode filter alongside year and band. It matches the recorded submode when nonempty, otherwise the mode, case-insensitively after normalizing input. It does not expand a parent mode into a family. The UI explains this rule and shows the selected filter in the snapshot; PDF metadata includes it. Named definitions retain the filter in format 2, while existing format-1 definitions remain readable and unfiltered; a previous reader that rejects unknown definition formats will reject the new definition rather than interpreting it as unfiltered. The existing Save/Load filters path also retains it. CSV continues to contain report rows only, without filter metadata.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: the new core test initially did not compile because the filter API and definition field were absent. Afterwards, synthetic CW/FT8/FT4 contacts count three without a filter and one with FT8; an unsafe filter string is rejected, and a saved named definition reloads with FT8 from a second connection. An isolated offscreen app seeded with CW, MFSK/FT8 and MFSK/FT4 contacts saved and reapplied the FT8 definition and returned one result. The app and focused targets built; CTest activityreport, activity_power_pdf, database and qmlpopups passed 4/4 before the final explanatory text and additional invalid-input assertion, which were rebuilt and the focused test passed afterwards. Translation XML and git diff --check passed.

The UI probe is programmatic and did not exercise Save/Load filters separately, PDF export for this filter, physical input, all mode strings, other platforms or real logs. Older application versions may ignore the new mode property in the shared legacy last-used filter setting; do not use that setting on a downgrade without checking it. Named format-2 definitions are not backward-compatible with old versions. CSV omits filter metadata; preserve the filter context separately. Arbitrary field filters, mode-family expansion and remaining G07 and desktop release requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G07 isolate extended last-used report filters

Last-used custom report filters with a mode are now stored under `activity_report_v2`. The legacy `activity_report_v1` value becomes a marker with an unsupported kind, so the previous reader logic rejects it instead of loading an apparently valid custom report while silently ignoring the mode. The current reader follows that marker to v2; if an older version overwrites v1 with a normal legacy setting, the current reader uses that new v1 setting rather than stale v2 data. Saving an unfiltered or non-custom setting stores v1 and removes v2. Both writes use a SQLite savepoint and roll back together on failure. Previously written v1 settings containing a mode remain readable by the current version until next save; older versions may still ignore that legacy mode. Named definitions are unchanged.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: an actual offscreen app's Save filters probe previously wrote FT8 into a valid v1 custom object; afterwards it writes a v1 marker and the FT8 object in v2, and the current reader returns FT8. In a separate synthetic app scenario, directly replacing v1 with a simulated older version's 2024/40m activity setting made the current reader choose that setting over the stale v2. A SQLite trigger rejecting the marker update caused the save to report failure and preserved the original v1 without leaving v2. These are programmatic QML and synthetic SQLite fault scenarios; the older binary itself was not run. App build and CTest activityreport, activity_power_pdf, database and qmlpopups passed 4/4; git diff --check passed.

This supersedes the previous limitation for newly saved mode-filtered settings only. A rejected old-version setting does not prevent an operator from manually running an unfiltered report, and previously saved v1 mode settings still need a resave with this version to get the marker. The tests do not cover all SQLite failure points, concurrent writers, other platforms, physical input or release acceptance. G07 and the desktop roadmap remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G07 preserve the mode filter in nonempty CSV exports

CSV exports of custom reports with an active recorded-mode filter now append a `filter_recorded_mode` column containing that filter on every data row. Reports without a mode filter retain their existing columns and bytes. This is a conditional schema change for filtered CSV consumers. A report with zero rows has only a header and therefore does not contain the actual mode value; year and band filter metadata are still not included unless represented as grouping dimensions. Retain the report definition or PDF for complete filter context. This narrows, rather than completely resolves, the previous CSV metadata limitation.

A/B on macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2: the new core test initially failed to compile because the CSV function did not accept the filter. After the change, a synthetic FT8-filtered result exports the mode column and value, while an unfiltered export has no added column. In an isolated actual offscreen app with synthetic CW/FT8/FT4 QSO, a saved and reapplied FT8 report exported `20m,1,FT8` under the expected headers. App and focused test targets built; CTest activityreport, activity_power_pdf, database and qmlpopups passed 4/4; git diff --check passed.

The UI interaction is programmatic. No empty-report filter value, complete metadata round-trip, physical UI, PDF comparison, other platforms, large-log CSV export or integrated release acceptance is established. Remaining G07 and desktop roadmap requirements stay open.

## 2026-11-24 — 1.17.67 release gate: background log reads

The Linux aarch64 release gate failed the large-log reload test while SQLite was locked; the background table reader opened a full write-capable LogDatabase and attempted schema setup while a separate write was in progress. The large-log ID reload, category count and field-value count now open existing files read-only, with a 3000 ms busy timeout. This avoids schema migration and optimization writes on those read paths. The primary log connection remains responsible for schema and writes. The fixture now runs normal read/write overlap in WAL mode and switches to rollback journaling only for its deliberate exclusive-lock test; acquiring that test lock is retried with an idempotent helper so the test does not begin a transaction twice.

A/B: the Linux ARM64 runner failed before the change with SQLite lock errors in the large reload test. On macOS arm64/Qt 6.11.2 the updated test passed three successive runs including the deliberate timeout, preserved table and recovery; CTest database, migration, qsomodel, activityreport and qmlpopups passed 5/5 after the initial implementation. The Qt 6.8 Linux x86_64 gate also exposed a missing explicit QJsonDocument include in a database test, now added and the focused test passed locally. Linux ARM64 and x86_64 reruns of the final commit are still required to verify both fixes on their original platforms. The separate legacy CI Windows job failed while installing unavailable MSYS2 packages; the release Windows toolchain is a different workflow.

## 2026-11-24 — 1.17.67 release gate: platform portability

The Windows release gate compiled but failed ten CTest groups. Several diagnostics were platform-specific: test probes formed file URLs from `file://C:/...` or split drive letters at `:`, Python SQLite context managers left connections open during temporary-directory cleanup, some tests used `?` in Windows filenames, and filesystem-replacement tests assumed Windows could rename an open database. The probes now form encoded local file URLs, retain drive letters in path arguments, explicitly close SQLite connections, use legal filenames, and skip only replacement scenarios when the OS refuses the rename. The archive alias test creates a real hard link on Windows. Windows integration subprocesses can request UTF-8 test logging so captured non-ASCII text is not dependent on a console code page; normal logging is unchanged.

A/B: before the changes the Windows runner reported ten failed groups, including missing CSV files from malformed probe URLs and WinError 32 during temporary-directory cleanup. Locally on macOS, the adjusted Windows-drive URL probe produces `file:///C:/logs/My%20%231.adi`; selected contest, import, custom report and POTA UI tests passed 10 cases, the archive protection tests passed, five tracker/rig integration CTest groups passed, and the UTF-8 test logger passed both English and Italian UI probes. A real Windows rerun is required before claiming these changes solve the Windows gate; tests that cannot rename an open database explicitly skip that scenario rather than prove it.

The macOS Apple Silicon runner also failed a QSL responsiveness fixture because it assumed at least five 10 ms timer callbacks during a delayed network response. The fixture now checks that a callback occurs and records/limits the largest observed event-loop gap instead of requiring an arbitrary callback count. Both eQSL and QRZ cases passed locally; the original macOS runner needs a rerun. The previous macOS Intel workflow passed and produced a nonempty DMG, but that artifact belongs to an earlier commit and must be replaced before publication. The final cross-platform release gate and physical hardware tests remain pending.

Further release-check work: Windows integration subprocesses now request a test-only UTF-8 Qt logger, independently of the system code page; a local comparison with this logger passed the English and Italian station-network and Marathon probes. A synthetic drive-letter path was converted by the running app to `file:///C:/logs/My%20%231.adi`. Python tracker/rig fixtures explicitly close read-only SQLite connections before temporary-directory cleanup; five related local CTest groups passed. The local archive hard-link/replacement checks passed on macOS; Windows-specific hard-link creation and the tests skipped when the OS refuses rename still require the runner. The revised large-log async reload test passed three consecutive local runs and the actual app's 20,000-QSO bulk responsiveness scenario passed. QSL selected-upload fixtures passed for eQSL and QRZ with recorded local maximum timer gaps of 23 ms; the former Apple Silicon failure was a callback-count assertion and needs runner validation. These are local/synthetic results, not a completed Windows/Linux release gate or physical radio proof.

The 1.17.67 release was published with all five platform packages. The published packages are not the final G13 integrated acceptance and do not prove external hardware or services.

This exercises programmatic probe interaction, not physical input or visual layout; the revised probe has not yet run on Linux or Windows, and retrying setup does not establish why the original asynchronous change occurred or prove the normal interactive flow immune to it. Remaining G00/G02 and G13 requirements remain open.

A due reminder for the selected radio-net session now displays an in-app banner in the main window when Net Manager is closed. The main-window banner now considers saved reminders of all active radio-net sessions in the currently open log, not just the selected session.
