## 2026-11-24 — G09 filter planned passes by sampled elevation

The PREDICT pass schedule now offers a 0–90 degree minimum sampled-elevation filter, shows the number of matching passes, and hides nonmatching rows without requesting extra predictions. A selected schedule pass is cleared if a higher threshold hides it, and hidden rows cannot be selected via the same action. The filter uses the tracker-provided sampled maximum, not a calculation of true peak or a contact guarantee. The PREDICT guide and Italian labels describe this; no CAT, PTT or rotor action was added. Closing or starting a new plan now clears pass validity before clearing the selected data, avoiding an observed transient invalid-date QML error.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2 with an isolated offscreen app and synthetic read-only PREDICT UDP server: the new integration assertion failed against the previous app because the filter was absent. Afterwards three fixture passes sampled at 30 degrees are all hidden at 31 and restored at 30; a hidden selected pass is cleared and cannot be selected, then can be selected after restoring the threshold. The subsequent pass request uses the selected LOS; the fixture checks the tracker request sequence and zero QSO. The earlier baseline also emitted a RangeError when the dialog closed; the final schedule scenarios reject RangeError/TypeError and pass. CTest predicttracker, predictpass, newqsotuning and predict_ui passed 4/4 before the last selection guard; the final rebuilt predict_ui passed again. Translation XML and git diff --check passed.

The fixture uses programmatic UI, synthetic/old orbital elements and a simulated tracker; it is not current orbital accuracy, physical input, two Decodium sessions, real CAT/PTT, radio, rotor or RF, or Windows/Linux verification. The filter acts only on the bounded requested set, does not fetch replacement passes and is not saved across sessions. Other G09 and integrated acceptance requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G09 explicit UTC pass-planning start

The PREDICT dialog now accepts an optional UTC planning start for both the next-pass and bounded schedule requests. Blank retains the current-time behavior; nonempty input must be an exact valid `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ` timestamp within the existing planner range. Invalid dates, offsets and malformed values are rejected before prediction. Following-pass requests still start after the selected LOS. Changing the field hides the old schedule and invalidates its selected pass. This adds no tracker writes or equipment control; the guide and Italian strings were updated.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2: the new actual-app integration assertion failed against the prior app because there was no start field and it sent the current epoch. With the new field, a synthetic PREDICT server observed the exact `1800000000` epoch for `2027-01-15T08:00:00Z`; a February 30 input was rejected by the UI without extra tracker requests, a non-UTC offset was rejected by the parser, and the following-pass request still used the selected LOS. The fixture also checks zero QSO and permitted prediction/read commands. App build and CTest predictpass and predict_ui passed 2/2; after the final stale-display invalidation change, the rebuilt predict_ui passed again. Translation XML and git diff --check passed.

This uses an offscreen app, programmatic UI and synthetic tracker/orbital elements; it is not live orbital accuracy, physical interaction, two Decodium sessions, CAT/PTT, hardware/RF or Windows/Linux evidence. The final invalidation is compiled and covered by the surrounding app scenario but a dedicated change-after-result UI assertion is not yet recorded. The strict UTC format has no date picker, and future TLE-based results are not guaranteed. G09 and full integration remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G03 electronic pending-request summary

The QSL planner now groups candidate QSO with recorded sent/unconfirmed LoTW, eQSL or QRZ status by service when that service is accepted for at least one potential benefit in the selected award configuration. Each group shows its QSO count and distinct award/item benefits; groups may overlap and are explicitly described as potential. Confirmation and award eligibility are not inferred from a sent status. This is read-only and does not request or transmit a QSL; paper routes and the guarded paper queue remain unchanged.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2: the new core fixture did not compile against the previous plan type. Afterwards two sent/unconfirmed LoTW QSO for the same DXCC produce two QSO and one distinct benefit; an injected received status for one QSO removes the already-confirmed benefit and its pending group from the recomputed plan. The first fixture attempt used the send-only state setter to change received status; it correctly did not update receipt, so the test was corrected to simulate a received status directly in the isolated database. An actual offscreen app with synthetic card and LoTW statuses rendered `LOTW: 1 QSO · 2 benefici potenziali` and completed the existing plan invalidation and guarded paper-queue scenario. Its initial assertion expected English but the app used Italian; the corrected assertion accepts both. App/test builds succeeded; CTest awards, qslplanner and qmlpopups passed 3/3. Translation XML and git diff --check passed.

This summary covers only recorded sent/unconfirmed statuses and configured calculator services, not remote delivery, service account reconciliation, unrecorded opportunities, verified eligibility or action execution. The core fixture simulates receipt with local SQL, not a live LoTW response. The UI probe is programmatic; physical interaction, live services, other platforms and full G03/G13 acceptance remain open. No external QSL was sent.

## 2026-11-24 — G03 inspect pending QSO by electronic service

Each electronic pending-request summary in the QSL planner now has a Show QSO action. It filters the existing candidate list to QSO recorded as sent/unconfirmed for that service and having at least one benefit accepting that service. Show all candidates clears the filter; closing the planner also clears it. The filter works on the current plan, honors existing stale/dirty hiding and does not send or modify QSO. Italian labels were added.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2 in an isolated offscreen app with three synthetic QSO, one pending LoTW: with filtering temporarily disabled the action showed all three (first ID 1) and the new assertion failed; with filtering restored it showed one (ID 2), and clearing returned all three. The same scenario continued through the existing plan invalidation and guarded paper-queue checks. App and test targets built; CTest awards, qslplanner and qmlpopups passed 3/3; translation XML and git diff --check passed. No external QSL was sent.

The UI interaction is programmatic, not physical input or visual-layout verification. The filter is a transient view of the recorded state, not bulk reconciliation, retry, delivery verification or an external account lookup. Concurrent external writes, live services, Windows/Linux and full G03/G13 acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G12 optional similar-call OCR search

The OCR review dialog retains exact-call search as the default and adds an explicit option to include one letter/digit substitution or one adjacent swap within a slash-separated callsign component. A bounded set of valid candidate calls is matched by SQL; UTC date/time filters and the existing 100-result limit apply to the combined result. The dialog labels this as unverified, explains that added/missing characters are not searched and leaves attachment under explicit operator approval. No QSO or confirmation is changed by searching. Italian labels and the OCR guide were updated.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2: the new core fixture did not compile against the previous exact-only API. With the implementation, exact search returns only K1ABC while optional similar search returns K1ABC, K1ABD and K1ACB, excludes an unrelated call and excludes a similar call on a different UTC date. An isolated actual offscreen app used a synthetic QSL image and local Tesseract: the exact-search scenario found one call; the optional similar scenario found two of three synthetic contacts, with no QSO changes or attachment. The existing selected-date/time UI test also passed. App/test targets built; CTest database, tesseractocr and qmlpopups passed 3/3; translation XML and git diff --check passed.

This is a bounded local heuristic, not a ranking or exhaustive fuzzy search, and slash insertion/removal, OCR character omissions and additions are not covered. Results use the existing newest-ID ordering and may be truncated; similar results can displace exact matches within that limit. The UI probe is programmatic and uses a synthetic card, not a physical card or human review. Large-log latency, cancellation during a long SQL statement, Windows/Linux and full G12/G13 acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G12 keep exact OCR matches visible with many similar calls

Optional similar-call search now orders exact calls before similar calls, then by descending QSO ID within each group. The database still limits the combined result to 100 and reports truncation; a result field distinguishes similar from exact, and the OCR dialog marks similar rows for verification. This supersedes the previous warning that similar calls can displace exact calls, though more than 100 exact QSO can still fill the result. The OCR guide and Italian label were updated.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2 with an isolated synthetic file-backed log: one old exact K1ABC QSO followed by 105 newer K1ABD QSO previously returned ID 106 first and omitted the exact match. Afterwards the exact ID 1 is first, the list has 100 rows, truncation is true and subsequent rows are marked similar. The exact-only and UTC-filtered cases passed. Two actual-app offscreen OCR search scenarios passed; CTest database, tesseractocr and qmlpopups passed 3/3; after adding exact/similar assertions the focused test passed again. Translation XML and git diff --check passed. No QSO or attachment was changed.

The 106-row fixture does not establish large-log latency or ordering performance. The app probe uses programmatic interaction and a synthetic card, and does not separately inspect the visual label. Real card review, other operating systems and G12/G13 acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G12 OCR candidates with one missing or added character

Optional similar-call search now includes one letter/digit insertion or deletion as well as the existing substitution and adjacent swap. It does not insert, remove or swap across a slash, retains the callsign syntax and 15-character bounds, orders exact calls first and keeps UTC filters and the combined 100-result limit. Candidate calls are passed through one bound JSON parameter to SQLite `json_each`, avoiding a platform-dependent variable count as the candidate set grows. The dialog and guide explain the updated scope; results remain suggestions requiring explicit review.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2: a synthetic core fixture with K1ABC, K1AB and K1ABCD initially returned only the exact call and an adjacent swap; afterwards it includes both the one-character-shorter and longer calls, excludes unrelated and slash-added calls, and leaves six QSO unchanged. Exact-first/truncation and UTC-filter tests passed. An isolated actual offscreen app with local Tesseract and a synthetic card returned three relevant QSO including the shorter call while excluding an unrelated fourth, without adding an attachment; the existing exact-search probe passed. App/test builds succeeded; CTest database, tesseractocr and qmlpopups passed 3/3. Translation XML and git diff --check passed.

This remains a bounded one-edit heuristic, not full OCR correction or a guarantee that the intended call is in the first 100 results. SQLite JSON support is required and already used elsewhere by the application; large-log latency and cancellation during the SQL statement are unmeasured. The app fixture uses programmatic interaction and a synthetic image, not physical review, live radio or other desktop platforms. Remaining G12/G13 requirements remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G12 synthetic large-log OCR search comparison

Added an opt-in file-backed OCR search fixture with 100,001 synthetic QSO: one older exact K1ABC and 100,000 later K1ABD contacts. The same database is searched with exact mode and optional similar mode under identical UTC date/time filters. The exact query returns one result; the similar query returns 100, marks truncation and retains the exact QSO first. Neither changes the QSO count.

A/B on one macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2 execution: `DECOLOG_TEST_OCR_LARGE_LOG=1 ./build/tst_tesseractocr largeLogSimilarSearchComparison` measured 25 ms exact and 58 ms similar after seeding, including asynchronous dispatch and response. This compares two search modes, not old and new code, and is a single-machine observation rather than a latency guarantee. CTest database, tesseractocr and qmlpopups passed 3/3; the large fixture is skipped by the ordinary suite unless opted in. The test target built and git diff --check passed.

The artificial distribution deliberately concentrates 100,000 rows on one similar call; it is not representative of all logs, storage media, SQLite versions or concurrent writers. It does not measure UI responsiveness, cancellation inside SQL, cold-cache p95, physical cards, Windows/Linux or real user data. No product change was needed on this observation; G12 and integrated acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G06 UTC-day grouping and ADIF timestamps with explicit offsets

Activation partition previews now convert explicitly offset ISO timestamps to UTC before determining the UTC day. Unzoned legacy timestamps retain the existing interpretation as UTC wall time. The shared LogDatabase ISO parser used by ADIF reconstruction now applies the same distinction, keeping exported QSO_DATE/TIME_ON aligned with the instant; it also affects other callers of that parser, including duplicate-distance and enrichment timestamp reads. No stored QSO is rewritten. Existing extra-field overrides remain unchanged.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2 with `TZ=Europe/Malta`: the synthetic activation fixture initially grouped `2027-01-02T00:30:00+02:00` under January 2; after conversion it groups under January 1. A second fixture timestamp `2026-12-31T23:30:00-02:00` and an unzoned legacy `2027-01-01T00:30:00` also group under January 1. Before the shared parser correction, the first reconstructed ADIF record still contained January 2; afterwards actual `exportAdif` output contains January 1 and UTC times 22:30, 01:30 and 00:30 respectively. The app and relevant test targets were rebuilt; CTest database, migration, activation, awards, qslplanner, cloud and qmlpopups passed 7/7. git diff --check passed.

The fixture injects explicit-offset database values directly; normal import/export and all third-party sources were not exhaustively tested. It checks the activation preview and actual ADIF serialization separately, not the asynchronous partition-export UI end to end for these values. Other datetime consumers that operate directly on SQL text may still need separate review, and extra QSO_DATE overrides, physical UI, real activations, Windows/Linux and full desktop acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G06 asynchronous UTC partition export verification

A file-backed controller integration now exercises the asynchronous activation preview and partition export with two synthetic POTA contacts representing the same UTC instant: one stored with Z and one with an explicit +02:00 offset. It reads and parses the actual produced ADIF file and checks that both contacts belong to the January 1 group, retain the POTA reference and contain January 1 at 22:30 UTC. The source log remains at two QSO.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2 with `TZ=Europe/Malta`: temporarily restoring the old preview conversion split the two contacts into two day groups and the new fixture failed; with the UTC conversion restored it produced one group and the ADIF assertions passed. The test target was rebuilt after restoration; CTest database, migration and activation passed 3/3, and git diff --check passed. This narrows the prior milestone's asynchronous export gap for this one offset scenario.

The controller is exercised programmatically against a synthetic log, not through physical UI or an official importer. Negative offsets and unzoned legacy timestamps were covered in the prior direct serialization fixture, not this asynchronous export case. Live activations, external data, other platforms and full G06/G13 acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G06 reject unversioned grouping-data changes before partition export

The activation partition fingerprint now includes each grouped QSO's recorded timestamp, ADIF extra JSON and callsign in addition to UUID and revision. A change to these inputs between preview and the export snapshot invalidates the reviewed group even when an external writer did not increment revision. The existing asynchronous export uses a consistent read snapshot and refuses to replace the destination when the group no longer matches. No QSO is modified by this check.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2: a file-backed synthetic fixture changed a QSO time within the same UTC day directly in SQLite without incrementing revision. Previously the asynchronous export succeeded and overwrote the destination; after the change it failed and preserved the existing file bytes. Separate fixtures changed the callsign and an extra JSON value without incrementing revision; each export was refused without creating a file. The UTC partition-export and existing stale-preview scenarios also passed. The app and activation test target built; CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4; git diff --check passed.

This guard covers the timestamp, call and extra JSON plus normal revision/UUID changes; it does not hash every QSO column or QSL state, so an unversioned external edit to another field can still escape it. It checks the export snapshot, not every possible later filesystem race or a live remote importer. Hashing extra JSON may increase preview cost on large sessions and has not been benchmarked. UI interaction, hardware, Windows/Linux and full G06/G13 acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G06 fingerprint complete QSO and QSL rows for reviewed export

The activation preview fingerprint now includes all QSO database columns and the ordered QSL-status rows read in the same SQLite snapshot, rather than only UUID, revision and grouping inputs. The export's existing snapshot comparison therefore rejects a changed QSO or QSL row even when its writer did not increment the QSO revision. Failure to prepare or read QSL status fails the preview rather than publishing an incomplete fingerprint. This is deliberately conservative: changes to non-exported fields can also require a new preview. An Italian error message was added.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2 with file-backed synthetic logs: before this change a direct unversioned comment edit allowed export to overwrite the destination. Afterwards that edit and a separately tested normal LoTW sent-status update both cause the asynchronous export to fail and preserve existing destination bytes. Existing tests also reject unversioned time, callsign and extra changes, while an unchanged UTC partition exports successfully. A synthetic missing-QSL-table fixture reports an error and no groups. App and activation test targets built; CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4 before the final added fault fixture, and the two affected cases passed after rebuilding. Italian translation XML and git diff --check passed.

This covers QSO and QSL rows in the read snapshot, not changes after that snapshot, other tables, filesystem replacement races or a physical review. It reads QSL rows per session QSO and serializes complete rows, so large-session latency and memory cost need measurement; query errors fail closed. The test uses synthetic local data, not real activations, external importers, hardware or Windows/Linux. G06/G13 remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G06 partition fingerprint cost on a synthetic session

Added an opt-in asynchronous preview benchmark on a file-backed synthetic session with 20,000 QSO, 20,000 LoTW status rows and two POTA reference groups. The fixture checks both groups contain all 20,000 contacts and samples a 10 ms main-thread timer while the worker runs. It does not change product behavior.

A/B on one macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2 machine: using the controller from immediately before the complete QSO/QSL fingerprint, the preview took 236 ms; after restoring the new controller, two runs took 402 and 410 ms. With the final heartbeat fixture the new controller took 405 ms, observed 40 timer callbacks and a maximum observed callback gap of 12 ms. These are single synthetic runs, not p95 or a guarantee. `DECOLOG_BENCH_ACTIVATION_PARTITIONS=1 ./build/tst_activation benchmarkPartitionPreviewWithQslRows` passed; the benchmark is skipped by default. CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4 before the final heartbeat instrumentation; the instrumented benchmark passed after rebuilding. git diff --check passed.

The measurement excludes initial seeding and covers one warm local filesystem, one QSL row per QSO, two groups and a controller event loop, not physical UI responsiveness, slow/removable storage, concurrent writes, memory usage or other platforms. Full fingerprinting added measurable work in this case; larger sessions and operational thresholds remain to be evaluated. G06/G13 remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G06 reduce partition fingerprint query overhead

The activation preview now reads the ordered QSL status representation as part of its QSO snapshot query rather than issuing a separate Qt SQL execution for each contact. The fingerprint still includes the complete QSO row and all ordered QSL status fields; missing/unreadable QSL data fails the query, and stream serialization failures are reported. This requires the SQLite JSON functions already used by activation. A new Italian error string was added.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2: the preceding 20,000-QSO/20,000-QSL-row synthetic benchmark took 402–410 ms with the prior per-QSO query; the revised query took 324 and 330 ms in two runs, with 32–33 heartbeat callbacks and a 12 ms maximum observed gap. These are local single-run observations, not a performance guarantee. Focused file-backed fixtures changed, deleted and added separate QSL service rows after preview and verified that each export was rejected; the missing-table fixture failed closed, and existing unversioned QSO/QSL guard cases passed. App and activation targets built; CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4; translation XML and git diff --check passed.

The benchmark uses one QSL row per synthetic QSO, two groups and one local filesystem. It does not cover all service combinations at scale, slow storage, concurrent writes, physical UI, hardware or other platforms. Changes after the export snapshot and filesystem replacement races remain outside this guard. G06 and G13 remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G04 campaign UTC boundaries with offset timestamps

Campaign matrix and focused watch-goal lookup previously constrained ISO timestamp text to the campaign's nominal date range before parsing offsets. A stored local date on an adjacent day could therefore be discarded despite its instant falling within the UTC campaign. Both queries now widen the indexed text window by one day on either side, then retain the existing exact UTC-date check. Extreme supported campaign dates use open lexical bounds. Naive timestamps remain excluded as before; no QSO is rewritten.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2 with synthetic direct SQLite timestamp updates: for a January 1 UTC campaign, the prior matrix counted zero for two contacts recorded as December 31 -02:00 and January 2 +02:00 even though both instants are on January 1 UTC. The new matrix counts both and excludes two inverse-offset contacts outside the UTC day. The focused worked-goal lookup agrees; a received-card fixture also verifies confirmed-goal lookup and matrix count. The app and relevant test targets built; CTest dxcampaign, database and qmlpopups passed 3/3 before the final confirmed-goal assertion, which passed separately after rebuild. The existing offscreen watched-campaign goal-alert scenario also passed. git diff --check passed.

The test injects offset-bearing database values directly and does not prove every third-party import path, historical malformed timestamp, extreme date, live cluster, real RF or other platform. The widened query can examine contacts from two extra calendar days and its performance on large concentrated logs has not been measured. Existing campaign UTC validation, freshness and stale-result limitations remain; G04/G13 stay open.

## 2026-11-24 — G04 offset boundary in actual alert and synthetic load

An isolated offscreen app fixture now loads a watched campaign whose worked-goal QSO has an offset-bearing timestamp on the next local day but within the campaign's UTC day. It injects synthetic cluster spots and checks the activity trace. A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2: the previous campaign code emitted a false 20m goal alert; after restoring the UTC-window fix, no campaign alert was emitted. The initial A run also attempted to confirm a nonexistent second QSO in the shared probe, causing an unrelated foreign-key warning; the final fixture includes an unrelated second QSO and the B run passed. No live cluster, RF or hardware was involved.

Added an opt-in file-backed 100,000-QSO benchmark with one third of the contacts on each of the UTC campaign day and its adjacent days, all for the same call. Both implementations produce 33,333 worked contacts and an open unconfirmed goal. On this machine, the old narrow window measured matrix 27 ms and goal check 12 ms; the widened UTC-correct window measured 58–59 ms and 14 ms respectively. `DECOLOG_BENCH_CAMPAIGN_UTC=1 ./build/tst_dxcampaign benchmarkCampaignUtcWindow` ran after rebuilding each version; the corrected source and binaries were restored. These are single synthetic observations, not a latency guarantee. CTest dxcampaign, database and qmlpopups passed 3/3 after restoration; the final app scenario passed and git diff --check passed.

The load distribution is deliberately concentrated and uses one local filesystem; it does not cover real logs, cold cache, concurrent work or other operating systems. The app test injects synthetic spots programmatically and does not establish real cluster routing, radio control or RF. G04 and integrated acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G07 custom reports by UTC day and hour

Custom reports now offer UTC day and UTC hour as grouping dimensions alongside existing dimensions. SQLite interprets explicit offsets when deriving the UTC period; invalid timestamps form the existing blank/not-recorded group. The dimensions work with existing filters, aggregations, saved definitions and CSV export. The desktop offers translated labels and explains the interpretation. This groups recorded times and does not infer operator presence or radio activity outside the recorded QSO.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2: the new core fixture initially returned `Unsupported dimension`; after implementation, a +02:00 timestamp and a Z timestamp in the same UTC hour group together, while an invalid timestamp remains in a separate blank group. A 2026 filter selects the two valid QSO; the CSV contains the UTC group, and a named definition reloads from a reopened log. An isolated offscreen app saved and reapplied the day/hour definition and returned the expected two-QSO group. App/test builds succeeded; CTest activityreport, activity_power_pdf, database and qmlpopups passed 4/4 before the final explanatory text, and the focused app scenario passed again after rebuilding. Translation XML and git diff --check passed.

Older application versions that do not recognize these dimensions can reject saved definitions containing them; their report-list behavior may also reject the list until the incompatible definition is removed with a compatible version. The app probe is programmatic; the new dimension was not separately checked in PDF rendering, physical UI, large logs or other platforms. G07 and integrated desktop acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G07 UTC grouping PDF labels and actual-app export

PDF exports of custom reports now label the day and hour dimensions `UTC day` and `UTC hour`, rather than the ambiguous raw keys `day` and `hour`. Added an actual-app offscreen integration probe that creates a synthetic log, saves and reapplies the UTC report, exports PDF, extracts its text and checks both period labels and two independently expected groups. The probe is registered with CTest when Python and pdftotext are available.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2: the generated PDF before the change had the correct rows but the new UTC-heading assertion failed. Afterwards it shows 2026-12-31 23 with two QSO (one stored +02:00, one Z) and 10 with one QSO, under explicit UTC headings. CTest activityreport, activity_power_pdf and activity_utc_pdf passed 3/3. A final PDF page was rendered with pdftoppm and visually inspected: both rows and headings are legible on one landscape page. The application and focused target built; git diff --check passed.

The probe uses synthetic records and programmatic UI calls; it does not establish physical interaction, multi-page layout, every locale or Windows/Linux behavior. PDF headings remain English as in the existing renderer. G07 and integrated desktop acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G07 multi-page UTC report PDF

Expanded the actual-app UTC report PDF fixture from its previous two-row, one-page scenario to include 120 additional synthetic daily groups. The resulting six-page PDF is parsed with pdftotext: every added group appears exactly once and each page repeats the UTC day/hour headings. The two offset-versus-Z contacts still group together as before. A rendered second page was visually inspected and showed legible rows, repeated headings and page number.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2 compares the earlier one-page/two-group fixture with the expanded six-page/122-group output; it is a dataset comparison, not a production-code change. CTest activityreport, activity_power_pdf and activity_utc_pdf passed 3/3. The fixture invokes the real app programmatically with synthetic local data; git diff --check passed.

This checks one grouping layout and one intermediate page visually, not every page, all dimension combinations, large cell contents, locales, physical interaction or Windows/Linux. No real log or service was used. Remaining G07 and integrated desktop acceptance stay open.

## 2026-11-24 — G07 export report context for empty and nonempty CSV

The activity report UI now offers a separate JSON context export for the current completed report snapshot. It records the report kind, UTC year, band, station, mode, dimensions, aggregate, generation time, row count and SHA-256 of the CSV bytes produced from that same snapshot. It omits the log path, log identity and QSO rows. CSV content and existing export behavior remain unchanged; context and CSV are separate explicit writes, not an atomic pair. The report guide documents comparison of the digest and the need to export before running another report. Italian labels were added.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2 in an isolated offscreen app: a synthetic CW-only log produced an empty FT8-filtered CSV whose header contains no FT8 value; the new context contains FT8, year 2026, 20m and zero rows, and its SHA-256 equals the actual CSV bytes. The app rejected the log database as context destination. No QSO data was included in the JSON. The existing report/power/UTC PDF and qmlpopups CTest groups passed 4/4; the final empty-report and protected-destination app probe passed after rebuilding. Translation XML and git diff --check passed.

The digest is not a signature and does not prove authenticity; the operator must export both files from the same snapshot and compare them. The context may contain an operator-entered station filter and is not automatically saved with CSV. Programmatic UI does not prove physical interaction or layout, and hard-link/filesystem race protection, other locales/platforms, real logs and full G07/G13 acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G07 protect report exports from database file aliases

CSV, PDF and JSON context report exports now share a destination guard checking the log database and its WAL, SHM and journal paths by absolute path, canonical path and native file identity where available. This rejects existing hard links and symbolic links to those files before opening an export. As before, it does not make a separate report/context pair atomic. The identity check is a point-in-time check and cannot eliminate later filesystem replacement races.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2 using an isolated temporary log: before the change, a JSON context export accepted a hard link to the database; after the change, JSON, CSV and PDF all rejected it and the link still had the original file identity. A separate app fixture created a symbolic link to the temporary log's active WAL and verified all three exports refused it; the log reopened with its original QSO. The normal empty-report context export still passed. These tests use POSIX links and are skipped on non-Unix platforms. App and test targets built; CTest activityreport, activity_power_pdf, activity_utc_pdf and qmlpopups passed 4/4. git diff --check passed.

The test does not exercise Windows hard-link creation, every SQLite sidecar/link ordering, filesystem attacks between validation and write, physical UI or real logs. The protection applies to the current log and named sidecars, not arbitrary unrelated files. G07 and G13 remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G05 independent contacts during import undo

Added a file-backed two-connection regression test for undo while new contacts arrive. A/B compares the non-mutating preview of 130 imported contacts with the confirmed undo: a second connection inserts independent contacts after the first deletion and at the 128-row journal boundary. The test checks that all 130 imported contacts are deleted, both new contacts remain active, and they survive reopening the log. No production behaviour was changed.

On macOS arm64 with Qt 6.11.2 the focused test passed and CTest database, migration and qmlpopups passed 3/3. The first fixture used repeated calls close in time and imported only 44 records because of duplicate detection; using distinct calls produced the intended 130. This is a deterministic interleaving at progress callbacks, not a sustained simultaneous radio workload, process-crash test, physical UI test or verification on Windows/Linux. Remaining G05 and desktop roadmap acceptance stay open.

## 2026-11-24 — G06 protect activation partition export paths

Activation partition export now checks the resolved parent directory as well as absolute paths and existing-file equivalence against the current log and its SQLite sidecars. It repeats the check in the worker before writing and disables direct-write fallback. This prevents a currently absent sidecar path reached through a symbolic directory alias from being selected as an export destination, while retaining normal partition export.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2: an isolated synthetic activation test selected the absent log.sqlite-journal through a symbolic directory alias. Before the change the export was accepted; afterwards it was rejected, and the same fixture successfully exported normal POTA, WWFF, SOTA and IOTA partitions. CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4; the application and activation test target built.

This is a point-in-time path check, not protection against replacement after validation; the second check narrows but does not eliminate that race. The alias fixture runs on Unix only; physical UI, Windows/Linux, real activations and official importers were not exercised. G06 and integrated acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G06 protect full-session exports

Full-session ADIF and Cabrillo exports now reject the current database and its SQLite sidecar paths using the existing resolved-path and file-equivalence guard. Both use QSaveFile with direct-write fallback disabled, check the write and commit results, and report success only after commit. This prevents direct truncation of the log if it is selected as the output and avoids reporting a successful short write.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2: a file-backed synthetic fixture selected an absent SQLite journal path. Both export methods accepted it before the fix and rejected it afterwards; both also rejected the database path and a symbolic link to it, while producing a nonempty ordinary export. CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4; the application and test targets built. The link case is Unix-only.

The tests call the controller programmatically and do not simulate short writes or a filesystem change between validation and commit. QSaveFile does not guarantee durability across power loss. Windows/Linux, physical UI, real activations and official importers remain unverified; G06 and integrated acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G06 POTA correspondent reference in partition export

When a POTA partition QSO has one locally valid POTA_REF and both SIG and SIG_INFO are empty, the exported copy now supplies SIG=POTA and the park reference in SIG_INFO, omitting any @location suffix from SIG_INFO. POTA_REF itself remains intact, including its location suffix; the source log is not changed. Existing SIG or SIG_INFO is preserved, and multiple or locally invalid references are not inferred into these fields. POTA documents SIG and SIG_INFO for park-to-park contacts: https://docs.pota.app/docs/activator_reference/ADIF_for_POTA_reference.html . ADIF permits the location suffix in POTA_REF: https://www.adif.org/316/ADIF_316.htm .

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2: a synthetic POTA partition containing a correspondent reference previously exported empty SIG and SIG_INFO; afterwards it exports POTA and the park reference while the source QSO stays unchanged. A separate exported four-QSO fixture checks location suffix preservation, an existing WWFF SIG pair, multiple POTA references and invalid input. Application and activation test targets built; CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4.

The local syntax check is not catalogue or POTA acceptance validation. Multiple references and existing partial or conflicting SIG fields are deliberately left for operator review, and the preview does not yet identify these cases. No live POTA uploader, official importer, physical UI, Windows/Linux or real activation was tested. G06 and integrated acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G06 preview correspondent park review

POTA partition preview now counts QSO with a correspondent POTA_REF that cannot be mapped unambiguously: multiple or locally invalid references, or existing SIG fields that do not match the single reference. The group shows a warning and count, with an Italian translation. This is informational; export and stored QSO remain unchanged. A single valid reference with absent SIG fields or an existing matching POTA pair is not counted. The preview follows ADIF extra-field precedence when reading these values.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2: a synthetic four-QSO preview previously returned no review count; it now reports three cases, while the single valid location-suffixed reference remains unflagged. The asynchronous export fixture confirms the corresponding preserved output. An isolated offscreen app fixture displays the warning for one multi-reference QSO. The initial app assertion expected English but the environment rendered the Italian translation; the corrected assertion accepts both. CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4 before the final extra-field precedence adjustment; the focused controller and app tests passed after rebuilding it. App and test targets built.

The warning is a group count, not a per-contact review workflow or catalogue validation, and it does not assess correspondence when POTA_REF is absent. Extra-field override precedence was inspected but not independently injected in the fixture. This programmatic offscreen test is not physical UI, a live POTA upload, real activation or a Windows/Linux test. G06 and integrated acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G06 preview/export extra-field precedence verification

Extended the file-backed POTA fixture with a synthetic legacy-style QSO whose structured correspondent reference is single but whose ADIF extra data overrides it with two references. The preview reports the ambiguity and the asynchronous exported ADIF preserves the two-reference value without inventing SIG fields. No production behaviour was changed.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2: temporarily reading only structured columns made the preview report three cases instead of four and the test failed. Restoring extra-field precedence gave four cases and the exported-record assertions passed. CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4. The test target was rebuilt after restoration.

The fixture directly injects the override into a synthetic database; it does not establish how frequently external imports produce it or cover every override field and malformed value. Live POTA, physical UI, Windows/Linux and full G06/G13 acceptance remain unverified.

## 2026-11-24 — G06 locate POTA correspondent reviews

The activation preview now lists up to 200 distinct QSO whose correspondent POTA fields need review, with call, timestamp and an Open QSO action. It reports how many additional contacts were omitted, displays 100 per page and uses the existing log/UUID/revision check before opening. A QSO belonging to several operator parks appears once in the list, while group counts remain per park. The review remains advisory and does not modify contacts or block export. Italian text was added.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2: the synthetic fixture previously returned no review rows despite four flagged QSO; it now returns their identities and retains the expected exported ADIF. An offscreen app fixture displayed the expected K1ABC review row. App and test targets built; CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4.

The new action reuses the existing guarded opening function but its button was not independently clicked in the POTA fixture; the existing excluded-QSO workflow has separate stale-record tests. The 200-row bound and omission counter have not been load-tested here, and omitted QSO cannot yet be paged from the database. Physical UI, live POTA, Windows/Linux and full G06/G13 acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G06 POTA review navigation A/B

The offscreen desktop POTA fixture now invokes the actual review-row button and observes the matching QSO detail dialog open. It also passes a copy of the row with a different revision to the shared guard and verifies rejection. No functional product logic was changed; the additional probe runs only under the existing test startup mode.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2: temporarily disabling the revision comparison allowed the stale row to open and failed the new assertion; after restoring it, the stale row was rejected and the unchanged row opened QSO 1. App and test targets rebuilt, the focused scenario passed and CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4.

The stale row uses a deliberately altered revision value rather than an actual concurrent edit; a separate existing excluded-QSO fixture exercises an edit. The app is programmatically driven offscreen, not a physical interaction. The 200-row limit, live POTA, Windows/Linux and full G06/G13 acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G06 reach all POTA correspondent reviews

Removed the 200-contact truncation from POTA correspondent review results. The preview retains one row per affected QSO even when it belongs to multiple park groups, and the UI shows 100 at a time with previous/next navigation. The warning now displays the total; Italian text was updated. This trades the old bounded result for an in-memory list proportional to affected QSO, consistent with the existing preview's excluded-contact list; a database-backed paged model remains desirable for large sessions.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2: a synthetic session with 205 flagged contacts and two parks previously returned 200 rows; afterwards it returned all 205 once, with 205 in each park's group count. An offscreen app test navigated to the third page and observed contact K0200; the existing single-row navigation test also passed. App and test targets built; CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4.

The fixture does not measure memory, cold storage or responsiveness on very large sessions, nor does it prove physical UI operation, live POTA or Windows/Linux behaviour. Results still require operator review and can become stale; full G06/G13 acceptance remains open.

## 2026-11-24 — G06 synthetic POTA review load

Extended the opt-in 20,000-QSO, 20,000-QSL-row, two-park preview benchmark to compare no correspondent review rows against every QSO requiring review. Both variants check group counts and expected review-list size. No product behaviour changed.

A/B on one macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2 run with `/usr/bin/time -l`: no review rows took 356 ms with 35 heartbeat callbacks, 11 ms maximum observed gap and 37,519,360 bytes peak process RSS; 20,000 review rows took 370 ms with 37 callbacks, 12 ms maximum gap and 52,215,808 bytes peak process RSS. Peak RSS includes the whole test process and fixture setup, not just the result list. Separate untimed runs measured 357 and 368 ms. Commands used `DECOLOG_BENCH_ACTIVATION_PARTITIONS=1` and additionally `DECOLOG_BENCH_POTA_REVIEW=1` for the second case. CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4.

The approximately 14 MB observed peak RSS difference confirms that storing every review row has a real memory cost on this fixture; database-backed pagination remains open. These isolated warm-cache synthetic observations are not p95, do not measure actual UI rendering or interaction, concurrent writers, slow storage, larger sessions or Windows/Linux. G06/G13 acceptance remains open.

## 2026-11-24 — G06 explain POTA correspondent review rows

Each flagged correspondent QSO now has an explanatory reason: multiple park references, an unrecognized local reference, or SIG fields that differ or are incomplete. When multiple problems coexist, multiple references take precedence, followed by unrecognized syntax; the reason is a prompt to inspect, not a claim of official invalidity. The desktop displays translated Italian labels and leaves the stored QSO unchanged.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2: the synthetic fixture previously returned no row reasons; it now distinguishes four affected QSO including an ADIF-extra override, while the existing exported-file assertions still pass. An offscreen app probe observed the multiple-parks reason and text, and the third-page probe passed. App and test targets built; CTest activation, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4.

Only one reason is shown per QSO even if multiple issues coexist. The test does not cover all malformed reference forms, physical input, live POTA, Windows/Linux or full G06/G13 acceptance. The unbounded in-memory review list remains as previously measured.

## 2026-11-24 — G08 protect Net Manager export aliases

Radio-net JSON export now checks native file identity as well as resolved paths against the current log and SQLite sidecars, both before scheduling and in the worker before writing and committing. It disables QSaveFile direct-write fallback. This rejects a hard link to the open log even when its path differs. The identity checks are point-in-time checks, not a guarantee against all filesystem races.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2: a temporary hard link to the test log was accepted as an export target before the change and rejected afterwards. A separate normal export still produced the complete 207-event history. An initial combined fixture created and removed the hard link while the SQLite connection remained open and subsequently observed a disk I/O error; separating the hard-link fixture from the normal-export fixture avoided that interaction, whose cause was not determined. App and test targets built; CTest radionet, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4.

The hard-link test is Unix-only and does not demonstrate every alias or race ordering. The worker's existing source-log replacement and session-identity behavior was not changed or tested here. Physical UI, Windows/Linux, real radio nets and full G08/G13 acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G08 bind Net Manager export to its source log

Radio-net export now captures the open file identity, persistent log identity and selected session UUID before scheduling. It refuses an already replaced log, opens its worker connection read-only, verifies both log identities and the session ID/UUID, and rechecks the file identity before committing the export. The existing snapshot and destination protections remain. An Italian error message was added.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2: in a temporary fixture, replacing the log path with a different database before scheduling previously allowed export to start; it now rejects the operation without producing an output. Normal history export and hard-link rejection also passed; app and test targets built, and CTest radionet, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4.

The replacement fixture changes the path before scheduling, not between worker checkpoints. These point-in-time checks cannot make live SQLite file replacement safe or remove every race; an already-open snapshot and a later filesystem swap need separate testing. Physical UI, Windows/Linux, actual radio operations and full G08/G13 acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G08 report export after changing log

The asynchronous radio-net export completion now checks the original open generation, file identity, path and selected session. If the current context changed, a successful completion explicitly says the file came from the original log; failure also identifies the original log. The success flag still reflects whether the file was actually saved, and the dialog now displays the success message when supplied. Italian translations were added.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2: the test waited for an actual JSON export to appear without processing its queued completion, opened a different temporary log and then delivered the callback. Previously success had no indication of origin; now it reports the original log and the JSON contains the original session. Normal export also passed. App and test targets built; CTest radionet, database, migration and qmlpopups passed 4/4.

The fixture exercises a log switch after writing, not a session-only switch or every failure ordering; the message branch for session change is not separately tested. The UI success-message binding was built but not observed in physical UI. The result does not make filesystem replacement safe. Windows/Linux, real radio operations and full G08/G13 acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-24 — G10 reject conflicting CQ WW report identities

The CQ WW report reader now rejects conflicting recognized contest or own-callsign headers instead of silently choosing the first. Equivalent repeated headers remain accepted, comparing contest names case-insensitively after whitespace normalization and callsigns case-insensitively. This prevents ambiguous reports from entering the existing review and candidate-search workflow; report data is not modified.

A/B on macOS arm64 / Qt 6.11.2: a synthetic report with two different callsign headers was previously accepted and is now rejected; a conflicting contest header is also rejected, while identical repeated headers and the existing supported-row/unknown-line fixture pass. App and test targets built; CTest contestreport, database, activation and qmlpopups passed 4/4.

The guard only compares headers recognized by the existing CQ WW parser; it is not a general LCR/UBN parser or an authenticity check. Synthetic reports were used; no additional official report, physical UI, Windows/Linux or real contest was exercised. G10/G13 acceptance remains open.
